# BUILD_LOG

## Current state
- Free for everyone (M18, branch `free-for-everyone`, D-2900 to D-2919): the app matches D-869. With no account every local feature is free and unlimited (panes, workspaces, teammates, plugin connections, accounts per engine, six threads, every chat engine, routines, task runs, the Night Shift, session search), the hosted ones say "Comes with Vibezeo accounts", and nothing in the app, demo, docs or site offers a plan. Notes in `CHANGELOG.md`'s `## Unreleased`. See "M18 · Free for everyone".
- Design direction (`design-lead`, D-1890 to D-1899, then D-2700 on): `docs/DESIGN.md` sets the look, feel and journey for the app and the site after the owner's 20 October ask for a premium feel; `app-ui` and `site-motion` build on it, and the design lead reviews their READYs. See "Design direction (`design-lead`)".
- Agent sandbox (M18, F-TL-11, branch `product-research`): Settings › Engines › Sandbox agent panes runs the engine of a local agent pane under bubblewrap on Linux (writes only to its folder, git data, provider home, tasks folder and `/tmp`; `~/.ssh`, the other secrets and Vibezeo's own data hidden; `.git/hooks` read-only; engines without a provider home run unsandboxed and say why); macOS and Windows say "Not available on this system yet". See "M18 checklist (Next features: sandbox)".
- Product research (M18, branch `product-research`, 15 October 2026): `docs/COMPETITIVE_ANALYSIS.md` refreshed (landscape, tracker reaction counts, HN demand signals, and an evidence index in §6), and a new `docs/NEXT_FEATURES.md` added: 21 ranked features with draft acceptance criteria and proposed IDs (F-EN-07…11, F-DB-04, F-AC-08/09, F-TH-17, F-WT-05…08, F-SH-14, F-TL-11, F-TK-05, F-EX-06/07, F-CD-22…24), a build order for the top 10 in eight slices, six bold bets and the owner-only items. The documents only; no product code changed. Reddit couldn't be read from the research environment.
- Repo: github.com/NamanJain-Engineer/tandem, public since 24 October 2026 (the owner switched it, D-870), with no open-source licence. Build kit in `build-kit/`; plan in `docs/PLAN.md`.
- Milestone: **M0–M14 are done** on `product-m0-m5`, and so are M15's six features. M9 was built in parallel on `m9-files-git-browser` (pull request #4), M10 on `m10-teammates-gateway` (pull request #5), M12 on `m12-plugins-approvals` (pull request #34), M8 on `m8-dictate-onboarding` (pull request #37), M11 on `m11-tasks-routines` (pull requests #36, with M16's F-RT-05, and #42, the tasks server as a sidecar) and M14 on `m14-remote-ssh-wsl` (pull requests #6 and #43, the bundled helpers and remote approvals), all merged 10 October 2026. M15's non-functional checks merged as #38 (merge fixes D-823 to D-825); the chain of #42, #38 and #43 passed the gate and 109/109 E2E spec files. 142 of 152 features are done: all 65 P0s and every P1, with F-DI-05 (cloud speech-to-text, D-516) and F-DI-10 (Enhance and Polish, D-517) finishing M13, and M16's F-AG-10, F-AG-13 and F-CN-05 (skill sync, a teammate in a terminal, and Find work's teammates and opt-in hosted ranking; built on `m16d-teammate-extras`, D-1300 to D-1304; pull request #46). The batch merged from `chain4` brings that to **144 of 152** with the phone companion and relay (F-RM-03, F-BE-11; `m16c-phone-companion`, D-1900 to D-1917). It also merges the first slice of Conductor's call, talker/doer, safety, HUD and audio routing (F-CN-01 to F-CN-04, F-CN-08; `m16a-conductor`, D-1100 to D-1113). Calls run end to end against the dev mock, while 23 tools, the floating ring window, the call-log setting and the vendor realtime client are still being built, so those five aren't counted as done yet. The batch also carries M17's five extras beyond the spec (F-EX-01 to F-EX-05; `m17-extras`), M14's live file watching and push, pull and branch switching on hosts, the redesigned website (phases 1–4 and 6, D-1800 to D-1814) and parked background panes (D-139). The batch merged from `chain5` adds the macOS Simulator pane (F-CD-21; M13's session, `m16d-simulator`), which makes **145 of 152**, and four features beyond the spec on `mobile-apps` (F-RM-04 LAN pairing, F-RM-05 the Vibezeo Remote app, F-RM-06 encrypted push, F-RM-07 the Linux store packages; D-2000 to D-2040) and four from the competitive analysis on `competitive-gaps` (F-WT-01 to F-WT-04, the worktree workflow; D-2200 to D-2208): with M17's five extras, 158 of the 165 spec IDs. It also merges Conductor's core slices 2 and 3 (D-1114 to D-1118) and its persona, memory and wake phrase (F-CN-06, F-CN-07; `m16a-persona-wake`, D-1200 to D-1208), M14's worktree lanes, ProxyJump hosts and plugins and task tools on hosts (D-1602 to D-1604), and the Windows and macOS readiness work (`windows-ready`, D-1700 to D-1715; `macos-ready`, D-2100 to D-2110). The batch merged from `chain6` finishes F-CN-06 and F-CN-07: the persona, the wake phrase and the mic owner are wired into calls (D-1119, `m16a-conductor`), which makes **147 of 152** and 160 of the 165 spec IDs. It also brings Best of N to folders on SSH hosts (D-1605) and the tasks server to agent terminals on SSH hosts (D-1606; WSL threads' plan is in `HUMAN_TODO.md`), and the website's version pages answer without a redirect (D-1815). The batch merged from `chain7`, after the owner's go-ahead, brings the performance session's six-agents and cold-start fixes and M11's refusal of SSH and WSL workspaces in Execute… (D-843). The batch on `chain8` brings the home page's largest paint within its 2.0 s budget (D-1816) and makes the docs' links open in the page instead of failing over to a full reload (D-1817); both reach vibezeo.com with the next release. The batch merged from `chain9` finishes Conductor's last five (F-CN-01 to F-CN-04 and F-CN-08; `m16a-conductor`): every acceptance criterion passes against the dev mock, and the vendor realtime client is built from the vendor's published events (D-1120), so **152 of 152** features and all 165 spec IDs are done. Checking the client against a recorded session with the key is what's left (`HUMAN_TODO.md`). The batch merged from `chain10`, after 0.7.1, closes the test gaps Conductor's audit found. Its new tests turned up one bug, now fixed: a floating ring saved on a display that's gone reopened off screen. It also gives a call's mic error F-CN-08's wording (D-845). The batch merged from `chain12` wires F-CN-06's voice memory commands, post-call reflection and call journal into calls (D-1121, with the review's changes in D-846) and adds Conductor's docs page. The batch merged from `chain13` makes a barge-in tell the talker how much of its answer was heard (D-1122). The batch merged from `chain14` stops a 16-pane boot from churning WebGL contexts: all 16 shells come up in about 7.7 s instead of 10.2 s on the build VM (F-SH-07.7's 4 s is still missed). The batch merged from `chain15` fixes Dictate takes started from the mic button or the toggle link: they now stop at 10 minutes even when the global shortcut never started (Wayland without a portal, as in Ubuntu 24.04's default session), and Esc cancels them (D-520). The batch merged from `chain16` keeps Dictate's shortcut status live, and allows the toggle link only while the shortcut is down (D-521, D-847). The batch merged from `chain17` shows Reconnecting while a silent mic is reopened, and the last 5 takes on the Dictate card (D-522). The batch merged from `chain18` adds F-DI-09's opt-in dictionary sync on Pro (D-523), and the batch merged from `chain19` the last two behaviours the 14 October Dictate audit found: on macOS, the Fn → Control+Option fallback and the 🌐 key set to Do Nothing only with consent and undo (D-524, D-848; checking them on a Mac is in `HUMAN_TODO.md`). `chain19` also makes the contact address contact@vibezeo.com (D-1818), records the owner's direction (D-1820) and brings the next-features research (`docs/NEXT_FEATURES.md`). The website's elite pass and the blog (F-WB-06, "M17 · Blog") are live on vibezeo.com since `chain22` (#75, D-851). The batches merged from `chain21` and `chain23` bring M18's first eight features (F-EN-07, F-EN-09, F-WT-05, F-DB-04, F-WT-06, F-WT-09, F-AC-08 and F-AC-09), released in 0.8.0. The batch merged from `chain24` brings eight more (F-WT-07, F-WT-08, F-WT-10, F-EN-08, F-TH-17, F-CN-09, F-RT-06 and F-TL-11), with #78's CI within the free Actions minutes (D-1009 to D-1012). In flight: the rest of M18, the research's top picks, built by four sessions (engines, the review loop, worktrees, and usage with the Night Shift; `docs/REMAINING.md` M18); and the performance budgets still missed (memory, six agents' first frames, then F-SH-14). The coordinating session starts each piece of work in its own cloud session; all of them share one account usage limit. The coordinating session merges each into `product-m0-m5`. `docs/REMAINING.md` lists every feature left. M7 is done (tag `m7`, local; 60/60 E2E spec files at the time; 65/65 with M9's specs). **Deployed:** vibezeo.com (`main`) serves 0.9.0 since 24 October (pull requests #90 and #91). The live checks passed:
  - every page, the new `/changelog/0.9.0/` and `/blog/whats-new-in-0-9-0/` among them, with 0.8.0's changelog page and post still answering;
  - both downloads, matching their SHA-256s;
  - site-info 0.9.0, and the home page's "Released · 0.9.0";
  - Vibezeo is free (D-869):
    - `/pricing` and `/pricing/` answer 308 to `/download/`;
    - site-info says `"free": true`;
    - no price, "per seat" or pricing link on `/`, `/download/`, `/changelog/`, `/docs/`, the limits page or Conductor's page;
    - the FAQ asks "Is Vibezeo free?", and llms.txt says Vibezeo "is free, with no paid plans";
  - the headers, and no framework chunks on marketing pages;
  - D-1815's redirects, the home page's lazy still with `/demo/`'s video loading nothing until it's played (D-1816, D-1841), and D-1817's slash links, whose payloads answer 200.
  Releases are built locally (`TANDEM_HOSTED=0 bash scripts/release-local.sh --dry-run`) and served from vibezeo.com/downloads with SHA256SUMS. The Release workflow (D-105 `early-access` channel, public `tandem-releases` repository, `RELEASES_TOKEN`) waits for Actions minutes, which reset about November 1; the owner chose not to pay for more. CI now runs `quick` on pull requests into `product-m0-m5`, a nightly `checks` on its head and the rest by hand, which should fit the free minutes; its three real failures from 7 October are fixed (D-1009 to D-1012, OPERATING §5a). Tags `m0`–`m7` are local only. The public site at vibezeo.com deploys from `main` (D-102, `docs/DEPLOY_SITE.md`).
- Release: **0.9.0** (Linux, `TANDEM_HOSTED=0`, built with `bash scripts/release-local.sh --dry-run`; `Vibezeo_0.9.0_amd64.deb`, SHA-256 `7c711289…2e1883`, and `Vibezeo-0.9.0-1.x86_64.rpm`, SHA-256 `0e82217f…865df0`, 54.1 MB each). The second M18 release, about bringing parallel agents' work home:
  - Land worktrees: finished worktrees merge into the current branch one after another, each behind the repository's check, with Undo (F-WT-07);
  - a pull request's failing checks and review comments go back to the worktree's agent, through the person's own `gh` (F-WT-08);
  - the intent radar notices when two worktrees change the same functions (F-WT-10, first slice);
  - engine versions, pins, rollback commands and the engines' own background processes (F-EN-08);
  - the session library across Claude Code and Codex, with search in Pro (F-TH-17);
  - agent panes in a bubblewrap sandbox on Linux (F-TL-11).
  Built since 0.8.0 but not in the Free download: F-CN-09's walk-and-review and F-RT-06's Night Shift (both need accounts), the Conductor voice fallback (#83, #85; hosted), and #78's CI changes. The site gains the post "What's new in 0.9.0", six "New in 0.9.0" tiles, a roadmap led by "Everything free" and what's in review, and new figures: 16 milestones, 3,722 automated tests, 145 E2E spec files, 13 engines. Vibezeo is free (D-869): the pricing page is gone (`/pricing/` redirects to `/download/`), no page shows a price, and the notes say the next release removes the download's remaining limits. The demo has no new surfaces (D-868).
  Verified:
  - the gate on the release tree d891f4ff (2,148 Rust and 1,574 TypeScript tests, coverage of 182 spec IDs, clippy on Linux, Windows and both Mac architectures, fmt, protocol drift with 554 types);
  - the full desktop E2E, 145 of 145 spec files in five chunks (36 minutes);
  - the .rpm on clean Fedora 42, 43 and 44 (images from Fedora's registry);
  - the AppImage smoke test offline (the welcome says "Free plan", setup lists all 13 engines);
  - the site from a fresh clone of c5b98702, D-869 included:
    - `build:site` with `strip-runtime` and `check-routes`: 49 pages, no redirects;
    - web vitest 114/114 and site Playwright 25/25;
    - Lighthouse CI on seven URLs, every assertion passing with no warnings (99 or 100 in every category, LCP at most 1.88 s, CLS at most 0.001);
    - the demo walkthrough.
  Deploying is merging `product-m0-m5` into `main` (OPERATING §7).
- Blog (F-WB-06, built under M15 and owned by "M17 · Blog", D-853; D-1850 to D-1864): `/blog/` with posts as files in `apps/web/content/blog`, tag pages and an Atom feed, batched in `chain22` and deployed to vibezeo.com from `main` (D-851). Publishing a post is `docs/BLOG.md` §3. See "M15 · F-WB-06 Blog".
- Website redesign (M15, D-1800 to D-1815): phases 1–4 and 6 of `docs/WEBSITE_BRIEF.md` (`docs-briefs`) are merged with the `chain4` batch: OS light and dark themes, marketing type, the new home page, Pricing, Download, Changelog, the inner pages and the docs, and the performance phase (marketing pages ship no framework JavaScript, D-1813; Lighthouse at 0.95 on five URLs). They reach vibezeo.com with the next release. The signed-in captures of phase 5 wait on the owner (`HUMAN_TODO.md`). See "M15 · Website redesign".
- Website elite pass (M15, `site-elite`, D-1819 and D-1830 to D-1844; `docs/WEBSITE_ELITE.md`). The site and docs are rebuilt on a lit visual system:
  - tokens for light, edges, depth, type, radii and motion;
  - the two-ring backdrop, the product bezel and site buttons;
  - a new header and footer.

  Phase 2 is in too:
  - **Hero:** it leads with ownership ("Your agents. Your machine. Your plan.").
  - **Home:** a tighter story. The release tiles and the roadmap moved to /changelog/, the video to /demo/.
  - **Motion:** the rings draw in, the engines orbit and data packets move, all in CSS.
  - **Docs:** they run no script.

  The real-app captures were retaken from 0.7.2 at 2×. Lighthouse CI scores 1.00 on its five URLs with no warnings: blocking time 0 ms everywhere, largest paint 1.51–1.74 s (fonts trimmed, D-1844). Batched in `chain22` and deployed to vibezeo.com from `main` (D-851).
- M16 Conductor voice API (F-BE-08, and the API halves of F-CN-02 and F-CN-05) is on `product-m0-m5` (pull request #30, built on `m16-voice-api`); see "M16 · F-BE-08 Conductor voice API". The desktop's Conductor client (F-CN-01…04, F-CN-08: calls, the local tool executor, the HUD, the call log) is on `m16a-conductor`; see "M16 · 16A Conductor core". The talker can come from a free primary with a backup, Gemini Live then OpenAI (D-1013 to D-1017, pull request #83): the app tells the API which providers it speaks, and a call whose talker fails before it's ready falls back to the next. The owner chose Gemini's free tier (D-1018): callers in the EEA, Switzerland and the UK, or of unknown country, never use it, and the app and the site say what the free tier means. It's off until the API is deployed with a key (HUMAN_TODO), and `scripts/agent/gemini-live-check.sh` checks it against Google once there is one; see "M16 · Conductor's voice: a free primary and a backup".
- Windows from Linux (M15, branch `windows-ready`, D-1700…D-1710): the gate runs Windows clippy (GNU target, every crate but the app); `bash scripts/windows-check.sh --msvc --wine` (in `ci:local`) checks the app for MSVC through cargo-xwin and runs the platform crates' tests under Wine; `bash scripts/build-windows-installer.sh` cross-builds an unsigned NSIS installer and checks it. Nothing has run on real Windows: Windows stays "Coming soon" until HUMAN_TODO's "M15 Windows first run" passes. Setup in OPERATING §6a.
- Layout matches DECISIONS: 10 crates under `crates/`, Tauri crate `apps/desktop/src-tauri` (lib `tandem_app`, bin `tandem`), `apps/{api,web}`, `packages/{ui,protocol}`.
- macOS from Linux (M15, branch `macos-ready`, D-2100…D-2110; `docs/MACOS.md`): the bundle config for macOS 14 with the hardened runtime, the microphone entitlement and usage string; every bundled executable signed, the app and dmg notarized and stapled, each Mach-O verified (release.yml, `scripts/ci/macos-*.sh`, `codemagic.yaml`, the manual `macos.yml`); the gate runs macOS clippy for both architectures (`scripts/macos-check.sh`). Nothing has run on a Mac yet: HUMAN_TODO "macOS first run". macOS stays "Coming soon" on the site.
- Environment: WebKitGTK, webkit2gtk-driver, xvfb, tauri-driver, dbus, xclip, openbox, cargo-audit and a MinGW cross compiler (`cargo check --target x86_64-pc-windows-gnu`) are installed; `dockerd` must be started by hand in a fresh container (`dockerd &`), then `pnpm infra:up`. When the disk fills, run `python3 scripts/agent/prune-target.py`, delete `target/debug/incremental`, and delete old copies of the invariant test binaries (`target/debug/deps/rule_*`, about 550 MB each, one per threat-model rule per build); gating with `CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0` keeps the 3–4 GB incremental cache from coming back. A full disk shows up as a linker "Bus error" (signal 7). `ort-sys` downloads ONNX Runtime from `cdn.pyke.io` when it builds; where the environment's network policy blocks that host, the app and the invariants don't build (tandem-voice does, without the app's `parakeet` feature; OPERATING §5).
- Gate: `pnpm gate` (lint, vitest, cargo test, desktop build, clippy, fmt, protocol drift) is green. Stage new files first: the drift check diffs the working tree against the index. `gen:protocol` also writes `packages/protocol/shortcuts.json`.
- Desktop E2E: `dbus-run-session -- xvfb-run -a -s "-screen 0 1920x1080x24" pnpm --filter desktop e2e` runs the real Tauri binary via tauri-driver (starts API + web if needed, openbox for WM behaviour, scripted Chromium for sign-in and checkout). 60/60 spec files pass (about 10 minutes). Pass one glob to `--spec` (`m7-{8,10}-*`): the runner keeps only the first `--spec`. Restore specs crash the app with `pkill -9` and relaunch; they sign the same account in again because the test keychain lives in memory. The run starts `scripts/mock-update-feed.ts` on :8790 (scenario `none`). `--suite startup` needs `gnome-keyring` (D-101). `TANDEM_E2E_SKIP_BUILD=1` reuses the last build; `UPDATE_GOLDENS=1` rewrites the TUI goldens (review them). zsh, fish, vim, htop and less must be installed (`apt-get install zsh fish htop`).
- API tests need `pnpm infra:up` (they create `tandem_test`). Web E2E: `PLAYWRIGHT_CHROMIUM=/opt/pw-browsers/chromium pnpm --filter web test:e2e` after `db:migrate`, `db:seed`, `keys:dev`.
- Release harness check: `bash scripts/check-release-harness.sh` (release build, asserts no `test_*` commands or `allow-test-*` permissions in the binary). Until D-1011 it passed by accident (`pipefail` and SIGPIPE) while release builds carried the harness's 32 permissions.
- Open issues: none blocking. Limits a signed token carries are still enforced (D-127, D-2901); none limits anything local. `pnpm gen:docs` writes the docs' generated sources (shortcuts and settings JSON); `gen:protocol` runs it too. The Linux single-instance plugin needs a DBus session bus (E2E runs under `dbus-run-session`).
