## M7 checklist (MILESTONES M7)
- [x] 1. Normalized events and adapter trait (F-TH-03). `tandem_engines::events` (the §6.9 `ThreadEvent` set and envelope, 64 KB tool output cut) and `chat` (`ChatAdapter` / `ChatSession`, `ChatStart`, `TurnInput`, `AdapterEvent`, `InterruptOutcome`). `chat::process::ChatProcess` is the engine process every adapter owns: its own process group, an env it doesn't inherit, a stdout reader that drops lines over 16 MB and reports them, a stdin writer whose writes time out at 3 s (later writes fail at once), a stderr tail for the error card, and Stop: the engine's interrupt, SIGINT at 3 s, the tree killed in time to be over by 3.5 s (AC2, tested with an engine that ignores stdin and SIGINT, children included). `tandem_core::access` holds `Access` and `ThreadMode`. The threads repo (`threads`, `thread_turns`, `thread_messages`, drafts; launch settles running turns to `interrupted`). `ThreadService` runs one turn at a time plus one queued follow-up, drops a finished turn's stragglers (AC3), ends a stopped turn `cancelled` (never failed; a queued follow-up goes back to the draft), shows an error with Retry when the engine exits mid-turn (AC4), stores each text block as one row (partial every 2 s), and streams envelopes; `thread_create/list/get/events/subscribe/send/stop/retry`. The webview's pure reducer (`features/thread/reducer.ts`) has the AC3 test. No engine has an adapter yet: until M7 task 3 a thread's turn ends with "<engine> can't run in Thread mode yet".
- [x] 2. Gateway skeleton (F-PL-01 slice). `tandem-gateway` serves MCP Streamable HTTP (`rmcp` 3.5 on `axum` 0.8) on `127.0.0.1:<random>/mcp`: a guard layer turns away requests without a live lease (401), keeps each MCP session to the lease that opened it, and rmcp only accepts loopback `Host` and a null or loopback `Origin`. Leases are 32 random bytes (base64url) of which only the SHA-256 is kept; `revoke` ends them. The built-in `tandem_approvals__request` takes `{tool_name, input, tool_use_id?}` and answers `{"behavior":"allow","updatedInput":…}` or `{"behavior":"deny","message":…}` from an `ApprovalAsker`. Tests over real HTTP: 401 without a lease and after revoke (AC1), ten sessions on one gateway each kept to its lease (AC2), tool listing by built-in set, the allow/deny replies, and the Host/Origin guard.
- [x] 3. Claude adapter (F-TH-04). `chat::claude`: spawn flags (`--setting-sources user` and `--strict-mcp-config` on every spawn, the gateway config and permission prompt tool, the §6.8.1 access table with unattended runs capped at Edits, `--model`, `--resume`; `invariant_rule_1_repo_settings_cannot_grant`); `ClaudeParser` maps `system/init`, `stream_event` deltas (text, thinking), `assistant` blocks (text, thinking, tool_use with a one-line summary and the input as detail), `user` tool results (ok / error / denied, output cut at 64 KB), and `result` (usage with context fill, cost; limits through the registry's `limit_patterns` with the reset time; `prompt_too_long` with Summarize & continue; other errors with Retry); a subagent's own messages stay in its Task row. Seven stream-json fixtures with reviewed goldens (`tests/fixtures/claude/`, `UPDATE_GOLDENS=1` rewrites them). `ClaudeSession` tags events with the running turn, sends turns as stream-json user messages (files as `@path`, images inline), interrupts with a `control_request` (acknowledged by `control_response` or `result`, else the 3.5 s stop) and reports the exit with its code and stderr tail; tested with `sh` stand-ins. In the app, the launcher starts the gateway on first use, gives each thread session a lease and a 0600 `$DATA/mcp/<thread>.json` (revoking the session before), and the gateway's approvals reach the thread: an `approval_request` row in the turn, the thread Waiting, the Dashboard card (M5 broker), then `approval_resolved` and the answer to Claude; a turn's end cancels what it waited on (tested end to end: gateway HTTP call → card → Allow → `{"behavior":"allow"}`, AC4).
- [x] 4. Codex adapter (F-TH-05). `chat::codex`: `codex app-server` with the gateway as `-c mcp_servers.tandem.*` overrides and the lease in `TANDEM_GATEWAY_LEASE` (never an argument); a JSON-RPC client (requests with ids and timeouts, server requests, notifications); the V2 thread/turn protocol behind `CodexProtocol` (`thread/start|resume` with sandbox and approval policy from the access level, `turn/start` with model and effort, `turn/interrupt`, decisions `accept|acceptForSession|decline`); `CodexParser` maps agent-message and reasoning deltas, command, file-change (per-file diffs), MCP and web-search items (long commands lose their middle on the row, full text in the detail), token usage with context fill against the model's window, and `turn/completed` (interrupted → cancelled; failed → limit with its reset time, sign-in, summarize or retry by `codexErrorInfo`); approval requests become in-band `AdapterEvent::Approval`s that the threads service answers through `resolve_approval`. Version gate: below 0.40.0 or unknown fails closed with "Codex version X isn't supported yet — update Vibezeo or Codex" and the error card offers the terminal (AC3). An app-server that died while idle is restarted and resumed on the next send with "Reconnecting…" (AC2). Six fixtures with reviewed goldens; sessions tested against `sh` stand-ins. Checking the names against a real Codex is in HUMAN_TODO.
- [x] 5. Access (F-TH-09). `tandem_core::access::resolve(level, mode, origin)` is the one place a session's access is decided: Plan mode reads only, and unattended origins (mail, Auto, routines and tasks without their own grant) never get Full (`invariant_rule_4_unattended_never_full`); the launcher resolves it per thread and the adapters map the resolved level (spawn arguments asserted for a mailed Full chat, AC1; Plan + Full sends read-only flags, AC2). An approval nobody answers is denied at 30 minutes and the engine is told so (AC3, mock clock).
- [x] 6. Inline approvals (F-TH-11, UI-23). `tandem_core::allow_rules` (command-prefix rules on normalized commands that never match shell metacharacters, newlines included; tool-name rules; AC2 tested); "Allow for this thread" records the rule, later matching requests are allowed without a card for an attended thread (unattended ones always ask). In the Thread screen the card shows in the transcript at the request and a compact copy is pinned above the composer (the oldest; "+n more waiting" opens the rest), with Allow once / Allow for this thread / Deny / Deny and explain and the card keys; answers go through `approval_resolve` with `via: inline`; resolved cards collapse to one line. Denied tool rows read "Denied"; a turn with denials ends "Completed with blocked actions".
- [x] 7. Transcript (F-TH-12, UI-21). A virtualized list (spacers above 80 rows, sticks to the bottom, "New activity" when scrolled up, keeps its place when older pages load); renderers per event (user bubble with Edit and resend / Copy, reasoning one-liner, ToolCallBlock with output, diff or detail, 3+ calls folded, MarkdownView, approval cards, notes, error banners with their one action, limit callout, divider, plan, "Stopped by you"); "Working {s}s". MarkdownView parses on the main thread with only the streaming tail re-parsed, a 256-message parse cache, and plain text above 200k characters; the threads perf suite found no parse cost worth a worker (D-119).
- [x] 8. Composer (F-TH-07, UI-22). Every toolbar control (Plan/Act, engine with terminal-only engines disabled, model with the "no longer offered" row, effort when the model has levels, access with the one-time Full confirm per folder, attach, token readout); IME-safe Enter and the Send-key setting; Esc stops; ↑ recalls; the draft saves every 300 ms; a failed send restores its text ahead of anything typed since (AC3); attachments by picker (`thread_pick_files`), drop on the thread, paste (PNG) and `@` mentions; a queued follow-up shows as a chip that `thread_unqueue` takes back; remembered defaults per engine.
- [x] 9. Model catalog, threads rail, folderless threads, titles (F-TH-08, F-TH-01, F-TH-02, F-TH-15). `models_list` merges what the engine reports, the remote catalog (refreshed every 10 min) and the bundled one with Default first; a saved model that's gone starts with the default and the note (F-TH-08 AC2). Rail: Pinned, No folder, a group per folder, Other; filter; status dot, title, Working / Waiting on you / Failed / age, unread dot, engine glyph; rename (F2), pin, archive / restore, Show archived, delete from archived only (a 0700 recovery folder with a 0600 `thread.json` and the attachments, swept after 7 days). Header: folder, title (click to rename), branch, engine and model, state, context meter. Folderless: Thread mode opens with a composer that starts a thread under No folder (F-TH-02 AC1); "Add folder…" moves it and keeps the draft. Titles: the first message's first 8 words at once; an AI title only with the Privacy opt-in (D-118). A silent working turn gets "No output for 10 minutes".
- [x] 10. ACP adapter (F-TH-06). `chat::acp` speaks the Agent Client Protocol to every registry engine with `chatAdapter: "acp"` (Cursor Agent, Grok, OpenCode; `acpCmd` after the resolved program): `initialize` (protocol 1, fs read/write, no terminal) → `session/load` when the agent can and the thread has a session, else `session/new` with the gateway as an HTTP MCP server when the agent takes one → `session/prompt` per turn (text, images when the agent takes them, files as resource links) → `stopReason`. Updates map to deltas, reasoning, tool rows (kind → verb, diffs from old/new text, output), and plans to a note; `session/request_permission` becomes the inline card and answers with the matching option (Once → allow_once, Thread → allow_always, Deny → reject); `fs/*` are served inside the thread's folder (`contain`), writes refused at Read access, and at Read access edit/delete requests are declined without a card; Stop sends `session/cancel` and cancels open requests (3 s → tree kill). The agent's models go to the picker; the thread's model is selected, or, when no longer offered, cleared with "Model no longer offered by <engine>" (D-120). Sign-in errors are `stage: auth` with Sign in, which opens the engine's login sheet; the next turn starts a new session that reads the new credentials. Goldens in `tests/fixtures/acp/` (updates, permissions, file requests; sessions: conformance, models withdrawn and selected, auth error, files) run the real session against scripted agents. E2E-M7-12 passes against `fake-engine` as `opencode acp`.
- [x] 11. Plan → implement (F-TH-10). In Plan mode a completed turn's last reply is saved as the thread's next plan version (`plan_revisions`) and the turn shows a `plan_revision` row: the Plan card ("Plan · version {n}") with the plan, Implement this plan, Revise… (pre-fills "Revise the plan: ") and Copy. `thread_implement_plan` switches the thread to Act (at the given access, else the thread's) and sends "Implement plan version {n} exactly as written:" with that version's text; the composer's unsent text stays put (D-121). The header's "Plan versions (n)" lists every version with its time and step count, View, Implement and Copy. E2E-M7-6 passes.
- [x] 12. Changes and Files panel (F-TH-13, UI-24). The thread's first turn records where it starts: the folder's commit (empty for a repository with no commits yet) or, outside git, a manifest of file hashes (`$DATA/snapshots/<thread>.json`, ignored files and files over 5 MB skipped, at most 20k files); moving the thread to another folder starts over. `thread_changes` diffs that commit against the working tree (committed since, staged, unstaged and untracked) within a subfolder workspace's prefix (`Repo::diff_from`), or compares the manifest (added files show their lines; changed ones have no earlier text). The panel (header "Changes" toggle, Mod+Alt+C) lists files with status letter, middle-truncated path and +/− counts, shows a row's DiffView, refreshes at every turn end, and floats over the transcript below 1100 px; Refresh and Copy file list. The Files tab lists the files the thread read or edited (latest first) and shows a text file inline through `thread_file_read` (inside the folder, never a private file, 256 KB). E2E-M7-9 passes.
- [x] 13. Grid (F-TH-14). Threads dragged from the rail onto the thread area (or "Open side by side" in a row's menu) show side by side: up to 6 cells, 1–3 columns by width, each a whole thread view with its own composer, draft and Changes panel; the open thread is the focused cell, and Mod+Alt+Arrow moves between cells (the `pane.focus_*` commands, which act on panes in Code mode and on cells in Thread mode). A seventh says "Grid is full (6)"; on Free (`threadGrid` 1) a second says "Free shows 1 thread at a time" with See plans, and a grid saved on Pro shows one thread after a downgrade. Closing a cell only takes it out of the grid; the layout is the `thread_grid` setting and comes back after a relaunch; archived or deleted threads leave it. E2E-M7-7 passes.
- [x] 14. Engine switch (F-TH-16). Between turns the composer's engine picker switches the thread (`thread_switch_engine`): the session ends, allow rules clear, and the next turn starts the new engine with a summary of the conversation as the first input: the last 20 messages as text plus the names of completed tool actions, never their output, fenced as `<previous_conversation>` data (`services/thread_summary.rs`, D-124). That turn shows the divider "Continued on <engine> (<account>) with a summary" above its message (D-125). Turns record their engine, so the composer's token chip adds up usage per engine. The thread header and composer show "Interrupted — Resume?" for a thread a crash or quit stopped (`thread_retry` sends its turn again; never shown as done), and a pane can hand off to a new Thread in its folder (`handoff_to_thread`, "New thread in this folder" in UI-13, D-110 Thread targets). E2E-M7-8, 10 and 11 pass.
- [x] E2E-M7-*, threads perf suite, G green, tag `m7`. The full desktop regression passes (60/60 spec files; E2E-M7-1…12 included). The perf suite's scroll fps and 2 MB-fence worst frame stay [manual] on this VM (D-119). E2E-M7-1 to M7-4 pass (13 cases): the fake engine speaks Claude's stream-json in Thread mode (`fake-engine/chat.rs`: replies, `run <cmd>` through the gateway's approvals tool with a small MCP client, `/slow`, `/hang`, `/crash`, interrupts). Threads are Dashboard items (F-DB-01, D-6): each thread's state goes to the status bus as `thread:<id>` with its pending approval, and a thread card opens the thread. E2E-M7-5 (Codex) passes too: the fake `codex app-server` speaks the V2 thread protocol, and `TANDEM_FAKE_VERSION` sets the version an engine reports. Threads perf suite (`--suite threads`, perf build, `perf/results/m7.json`): a 5,000-event thread opens to its last message in 221 ms ✓, an older page loads in 43 ms ✓; scrolling runs at 34.8 fps (target 55; this VM idles at 58.9) and the 2 MB fence's worst frame is 165 ms (target 50; a plain thread's open here is 76 ms) [manual], for the reference machines (HUMAN_TODO). P1 tasks 10–14 and E2E-M7-6…12 remain.

## M9 checklist (MILESTONES M9)
Built on branch `m9-files-git-browser` (from `product-m0-m5`), in parallel with M8 (D-200). Owns F-CD-09 (D-1), F-CD-17, F-CD-18, F-CD-19 and F-CD-20; completes F-SH-10 (Files and Browser tabs), F-CD-06 (drops from the Files tree), F-CD-14 (browser seats), F-CD-15 (the lane "Open in Files" item), F-TH-07 (files dragged into a composer) and the status bar branch segment. This section holds M9's state, decisions (from D-200) and log so the M8 session's sections stay untouched. **M9 is done** on this branch: tasks 1–6 and the exit checklist below; it goes into `product-m0-m5` through its pull request.
- [x] 1. Files backend (F-CD-19). `tandem_core::files`: `list_dir` lists one level lazily (folders first, `.git` never), walking down from the root with the `ignore` crate so every `.gitignore` on the way, `info/exclude` and the global excludes apply (also outside a repository); "Show ignored" flags ignored entries, and everything inside an ignored folder is ignored. `read_file` tells text, binary (hex of the first 256 bytes; non-UTF-8 text counts as binary so it's never mangled) and images (a data URL up to 10 MB) apart; over 5 MB is read-only and text is shown up to 20 MB, cut at a line. `write_file` writes a symlink's target (the link stays), refuses with `Conflict` ("File changed on disk") when the file's hash differs from the one the editor opened (a deleted file too), creates new files only when they don't exist, keeps CRLF, a BOM and the mode, and replaces atomically (in place when the folder isn't writable). `create` / `rename` / `delete` never clobber, and deleting a symlink removes the link. `resolve`: the local UI may name any path; every other origin goes through `contain()` (`f_tl_03_…`). `files::watch`: one `notify` watcher per folder, changes batched for 500 ms from the first event; on Linux a non-recursive inotify watch per folder `.gitignore` keeps (new folders are adopted, budget 20k), elsewhere one recursive watch; the repository's index, HEAD and refs (also a lane's, outside its folder) set `git` instead of listing paths; over 500 paths is an overflow. `tandem_store::repo::editor_tabs` keeps each context's tabs, active tab, expanded folders, Show ignored, divider and view (`ws:`/`th:` keys, bounded). In the app, `services::files::FilesService` resolves a context (a workspace, or a thread's folder; a folderless thread has none), makes files outside every workspace read-only (D-201), adds git badges (a folder is Untracked when everything changed in it is new, else Modified) from a libgit2 status cached for 2 s and dropped by any watch event, and fans one watcher per folder out to every `fs_watch` subscriber until `fs_unwatch`. Commands `fs_list`, `fs_read`, `fs_write`, `fs_compare` (`tandem_git::diff_texts`), `fs_create`, `fs_rename`, `fs_delete`, `fs_watch`, `fs_unwatch`, `editor_tabs_get`, `editor_tabs_save` and `app_reveal_path` (ARCHITECTURE §3.2 updated first). `cargo test -p tandem-core files`: 30 pass.
- [x] 2. Files sidebar and editor (F-CD-19). `@tandem/ui` gains `FileTree` (UI_SPEC §2.26: lazy folders, a filter box, type-ahead and arrow-key navigation, git badges, empty, loading and error states) and `CodeEditor` (CodeMirror 6: syntax by file name with the language loaded on first use, undo, search, `Mod+S`, read-only, go to line; colours only from tokens). The sidebar's Files tab (`features/files`) follows the active context: the open thread's folder in Thread mode (a thread with no folder says so), else the active Code workspace or lane. It opens from the "+" menu, `Mod+Alt+F` (`sidebar.files`) and a lane's "Open in Files" (F-CD-15). Panels at least 600 px wide show the tree and the editor side by side with a divider (drag or arrow keys); narrower ones show one column with Back. Editor tabs close with ×, a middle click or Delete and select their left neighbour. Text auto-saves 800 ms after the last edit and on `Mod+S`; a save that finds the file changed on disk opens "File changed on disk" with Reload, Overwrite and Compare (a DiffView of the disk against the editor). A file that changes on disk reloads when it has no edits and shows a banner when it has; binary files show a hex preview, images a preview, and large or outside-workspace files open read-only with a banner. The tree's context menu has New file, New folder, Rename, Delete (confirmed), Copy path, Copy relative path, Show in file manager (Reveal in Finder on macOS), Open in terminal here and, in Thread mode, Attach to composer; the toolbar has Show ignored, New file, New folder, Refresh and Collapse all. Rows drag (pointer events, not HTML5 drag-and-drop) onto a terminal or agent pane, which types the quoted path without Enter (F-CD-06), or onto the open thread, which attaches the file (F-TH-07). Tabs, the active tab, expanded folders, Show ignored, the divider and the narrow view persist per context (`editor_tabs_save`, debounced 400 ms). The browser demo's Files tab browses an in-memory copy of its demo project (D-204). Desktop vitest `features/files` (14), `FileTree` (8) and `CodeEditor` (4) pass; E2E-M9-1 (4 tests, axe in both themes) passes.
- [x] 3. Git panel (F-CD-20). `tandem_git::ops`: stage (`git add -A`), unstage (`restore --staged`, or `rm --cached` before the first commit), discard (tracked files restored, untracked ones deleted without following a link out of the repository; a conflicted file is refused), commit (the message on stdin, the repository's hooks run), push, publish (`push -u` to `origin` or the only remote), pull (`--no-edit`, the user's merge or rebase setting), switch (a remote branch gets its tracking branch) and new branch (before the first commit it renames the branch to be born). They run the user's `git` (`Mode::Write`: their config, hooks and credential helpers, never a repository fsmonitor, never a prompt), with a 120 s limit on the network. A refused push, a diverged pull, conflicts and a missing sign-in come back in words, read from all of git's stderr (`Git::run_verbose`; the shortened stderr can be nothing but hints); a commit without a git identity says how to set one. Reads stay in libgit2: `Repo::diff_file` (staged or not) and `Repo::branches` (local, then remote branches with no local one, with ahead/behind). `invariant_rule_6` now also polls the status three times and reads both diffs and the branches; `f_cd_20_1` proves `/tmp/pwned` is never created (the MILESTONES verification passes). In the app, `GitPanelService` covers the context's whole repository (D-205), runs one action at a time per repository and drops every cached status in it, so the tree's badges follow at once; 11 commands `git_*` (ARCHITECTURE §3.2 first, now taking the Files context). The Files tab gets a Files / Changes switch (persisted per context) and the branch chip: staged and unstaged lists with stage, unstage, discard (confirmed), Stage all, Unstage all and Discard all; the picked change's diff (beside the list when wide, instead of it when narrow); a commit box (`Mod+Enter`); and the chip's ahead/behind with Pull, Push or Publish branch, Switch branch and New branch. Statuses are read again after every action, on each watch batch, when an agent finishes a turn and when the window gets focus, for the contexts on screen. The status bar's branch segment shows the Files context's branch with ahead/behind and opens the Changes view. The browser demo's Changes view says what its terminal's `git status` says, plus page edits, and runs no git (D-204). `cargo test -p tandem-git`: 46 pass (panel 10); app `git_panel`: 6; desktop vitest `changes.test.tsx`: 9; E2E-M9-2.
- [x] 4a. Browser in the sidebar (F-CD-17). Pages are wry webviews the core creates itself, with no Tauri IPC at all (D-206), sharing one WebKit profile at `$DATA/browser-profile`; on Linux they sit in a GTK layer over the main webview that's installed before the window first shows. `browser::BrowserService` (behind `BrowserHost`, a fake in tests) owns the address rules: http and https; `file:` only inside a workspace; a bare host like `localhost:5173` becomes `http://`; anything else searches with the `search_engine` setting (DuckDuckGo by default); `javascript:`, `tandem:`, `data:`, `blob:` and every other scheme are refused, typed or page-made (a link, a redirect, a new window, which opens in the same tab). The address bar shows only what the page committed (WebKitGTK's load commit, and moves within the page's own origin), never what was typed or what a page says. Device preview fits Phone 390×844 or Tablet 820×1180 (rotatable) into the place at a page zoom of at most 1, with the CSS size and zoom shown. Seven commands `browser_*` and the `browser:state` event (ARCHITECTURE §3.2 first). The sidebar's "+" menu has New browser: a tab with Back, Forward, Reload/Stop, the address field (focused), the device menu and ⋯ (Open in system browser, Copy address, Clear browsing data… confirmed); its pill shows the page title and a relaunch reopens the page the first time the tab shows; closing the tab closes the page. Pages hide while a dialog is open or a menu covers them (D-207). Where pages can't be embedded, the tab says so and offers the system browser; the browser demo says it doesn't open pages. `scripts/serve-fixtures.ts` serves the fixture pages on :5173 (the E2E runner starts it). Rust `browser`: 16; desktop vitest `features/browser`: 13; E2E-M9-3.
- [x] 4b. Browser panes and seats (F-CD-17, F-CD-14). The pane picker has a Browser tile; a browser pane runs no process, so the live-pane limit doesn't count it (D-208). It's titled after its page (in the grid and the rail) and its ⋯ has Split, Move to new tab, Open in sidebar and Close. "Open in sidebar" moves the page itself, history and all, into a new sidebar tab (`browser_move` gives it the tab's id), and a sidebar browser tab's ⋯ has "Move to a pane" in Code mode; nothing reloads either way. The core keeps each browser pane's committed URL (`panes.browser_url`, the schema's column), so a relaunch reopens it. The launch sheet's seats can be a Browser URL (`localhost:3000` reads as http); browser seats open in the lineup's shared tab and never get worktrees. A page hides while its pane is covered by Card view or while a pane is dragged. `pane_create` takes `url`, `pane_close` closes the page, and `BrowserState` gained `seq` (D-209). Rust: pane store 1, browser 1 (moves), app 3; desktop vitest: grid 3, browser 1, titles 1, launch 1; E2E-M9-3 now 7 tests (pane ↔ sidebar without a reload, restore after a relaunch, a browser seat), 5 of 5 runs green.
- [x] 5. Element picker (F-CD-18). The browser toolbar's crosshair turns the picker on: the core runs `picker.js` in the page with a fresh 128-bit nonce (D-210). There, hover outlines an element, a click picks it, Shift+click adds one, Alt+click picks its parent and Esc stops; the page's own handlers never see those clicks, and the outlines live in a closed shadow root, out of the page's selectors. Each change sends the whole selection back as a navigation to `tandem-picker://<nonce>/…` from a hidden frame (a new-window request on WebView2 and where the page's CSP refuses the frame), which the page's navigation handler takes and never loads. A report without the session's nonce changes nothing, and a report is held to what a brief uses (50 elements, outerHTML cut at 4 KB, short fields). For each element the page reports a selector that matches it and nothing else (an id only it has, else a path of tags with `:nth-of-type` from the nearest uniquely named ancestor, shortened while it stays unique), the React or Vue component that rendered it, its size and position in page coordinates, its display, position, font, color, background, margin and padding, and its outerHTML. A new page drops the selection and gets the picker again while it's on. The panel under the toolbar lists what's picked and has a note field; Copy brief copies the Markdown (`browser_picker_brief`: the page, the note, then each element; it ends without a newline) and Send to… pastes it into an agent pane (bracketed when the program asked for it) or the open thread's composer (after its draft and what's typed). Neither presses Enter. Commands `browser_picker` and `browser_picker_brief`, the `browser:picker` event and the debug-only `test_browser_eval` (ARCHITECTURE §3.2 first). `scripts/serve-fixtures.ts` serves `/react`, a React page (`apps/desktop/e2e/fixtures/pages/react`) bundled on first use with the desktop's Vite. Rust `f_cd_18`: 5; desktop vitest: picker script 5 (every element of a test page gets a selector that matches only itself), panel 1, composer 1; E2E-M9-4 (4 tests, axe in both themes).
- [x] 6. Links (F-CD-09). Terminal panes get two link providers: URLs through the web-links addon, and the paths programs print (ARCHITECTURE §5.9's pattern: absolute, `./`, `../` or `dir/` paths with `:line[:col]`; a path inside a URL isn't one; a sentence's trailing punctuation is dropped), joined across wrapped rows and mapped to their cells. `pane_resolve_link` resolves a path in the core (`tandem_core::files::links`): relative paths start from the pane's folder, which is its shell's last OSC 7 (so links follow `cd`), else the pane's stored cwd, else its workspace root (D-211); only regular files that exist are links; files outside every workspace come back read-only. The view keeps a resolution for 3 s per pane, text and cwd, so a new cwd asks again at once. Mod+Click (Cmd on macOS, Ctrl elsewhere) opens a URL in a new sidebar browser tab ("Open links in Tandem", on by default) or the system browser, and a file in the Files tab of the context on screen, in the Files view, with the cursor on its line and column (outside every workspace with the read-only banner); a plain click, a hidden pane or a pane being dragged opens nothing. Rust: `tandem-core` links 6, app 1 (bash with shell integration: `cd sub` moves where links start); desktop vitest `links` 6; E2E-M9-5 (5 tests: Ctrl+Click with real WebDriver input opens `src/main.rs` on line 42, a plain click opens nothing, `/etc/hosts` opens read-only, after `cd sub` the same path opens sub's file, a URL opens in a browser tab).
- [x] Exit. G is green. The verification commands pass: `tandem-git` f_cd_20 (panel 10, reads 3) and f_cd_20_1 with no `/tmp/pwned`; `tandem-core files` 38; desktop vitest `F-CD-09|F-CD-1[789]|F-CD-20` 56; E2E `m9-*` 5 of 5 spec files. `trace-tests --milestone M9 --fail-on-missing`: 15 criteria covered, none missing. `invariant_rule_6` covers status polling (task 3). HUMAN_TODO has the manual QA for the browser and the picker on WebView2 and WKWebView, and the WebKitGTK minimum for Linux. `scripts/check-release-harness.sh`, which now also looks for `test_browser_eval`, finds no test harness in the release binary. The full E2E suite (65 spec files) passed 64 on its run; the one failure was a race in E2E-M9-1's test, fixed (M9 log).

### M9 decisions
- D-200: M9 is built in parallel with M8 at the owner's request, although MILESTONES lists M8 as its prerequisite. Nothing in M9 needs Dictate or onboarding: it leaves the voice code, onboarding and `apps/web` alone, and its decisions are numbered from D-200 so they don't clash with M8's.
- D-201: Files inside any workspace are editable and everything else is read-only (PRODUCT_SPEC F-CD-09 "Files inside any workspace are editable"), not just the Files context's own folder as ARCHITECTURE §3.2 put it; a symlink counts by its target. ARCHITECTURE now says so.
- D-202: Files backend details F-CD-19 leaves open. (1) A conflict is decided by the content hash (SHA-256) from when the editor read the file; the mtime is only reported, because two writes within one clock tick keep it. A file deleted since it was opened is a conflict too, and Overwrite reads the current hash and writes with it. (2) Writes keep the file's line endings (CRLF when most of its breaks are), BOM and mode. (3) Text that isn't UTF-8 opens as binary (hex placeholder) rather than being re-encoded on save. (4) Text over 5 MB opens read-only and is shown up to 20 MB, cut at a line; images up to 10 MB preview. (5) The watcher batches for 500 ms from the first event (a trailing debounce would never fire during a long build). On Linux it watches each folder `.gitignore` keeps rather than everything recursively, so an ignored `node_modules` costs no inotify watches. (6) Nothing named `.git` is created, renamed or deleted from the tree, and the context's own folder can't be deleted.
- D-203: Tab strips hold only tabs (ARIA: a `tablist` owns only `tab`s), which the Files tab's axe check found the M5 sidebar strip breaking. In the sidebar strip and the editor's tab strip, a tab's × is a pointer target hidden from assistive technology, and Delete on a focused tab closes it (announced with `aria-keyshortcuts`); the list item around each tab is presentational.
- D-204: The browser demo (D-126) answers the Files commands from an in-memory copy of the `shop` project its terminal shows, so the "+" menu's Files item works there. Edits stay in the page, and New file, Rename and Delete say the demo doesn't do them. The demo shows only what the app does.
- D-205: The git panel's commands take the Files tab's `FileContext`, not a `workspaceId` as ARCHITECTURE first had them, so the Changes view works for a thread's folder and a lane too. `git_status` returns `null` outside a repository (the view's "Not a git repository" state) rather than an error, and `git_diff` returns the `FileDiff` the rest of the app shows. The view covers the whole repository even when the workspace is a subfolder: commits and pushes are repository-wide, so a change staged outside the folder is never hidden; the tree's badges stay scoped to the folder.
- D-206: The browser's pages are wry webviews the browser service creates itself, not Tauri webviews: a Tauri webview with no permissions still gets the IPC bridge (`__TAURI_INTERNALS__`, the `ipc:` scheme), a wry webview gets none, and it runs in its own WebKit context, where the app's schemes aren't registered. ARCHITECTURE's "zero-permission capability file" becomes no capability at all. On Linux the pages go in a `gtk::Fixed` layered over the main webview with a `gtk::Overlay`, set up before the window first shows (a WebKitGTK view moved after it has painted stays blank); macOS and Windows use wry child webviews (manual QA, HUMAN_TODO). The view reports its place in window coordinates and the core fits the page into it. Sidebar tabs and panes use the same layer, so MILESTONES' "fall back to the sidebar only" has nothing to fall back to: when the layer can't be set up, every browser says pages can't open inside the app and offers the system browser. A new tab creates its page with its first address (until then the empty state shows); a restored tab's page loads the first time the tab shows, not at launch. The page policy also allows an iframe's `about:srcdoc` (the page's own content); `data:` and `blob:` stay refused, as PRODUCT_SPEC's "other schemes are blocked" says. Downloads are refused.
- D-207: Nothing the app draws can cover a browser page (it's a native view above the app). So while any dialog is open every page hides, while a menu or list overlaps a page that page hides, toolbar tooltips open upward, and toasts show bottom-left while a page covers their bottom-right corner (UI_SPEC §2.12).
- D-208: Browser panes run no process, so the plan's live-pane limit (F-CD-03, "a fifth spawn is refused") doesn't count them, as the launch service already didn't count browser seats. The picker still shows only the upgrade card at the limit; the sidebar's browser is always there.
- D-209: `BrowserState` carries `seq`, a number that grows with every change in the core, and views keep only the newest state per page. A command's reply and the events that follow it travel separately, and a fast page (a local dev server) can commit, load and name itself before `browser_open`'s reply lands; without it the reply's "loading" state overwrote the loaded page for good. Views also start listening to `browser:state` before they open any page.
- D-210: The element picker runs in the page's own JavaScript and reports through navigations, so pages still get no IPC (D-206). Each time it's turned on, the core runs `picker.js` with a new random nonce. Each change sends the whole selection as a hidden frame's navigation to `tandem-picker://<nonce>/<JSON>`, which the navigation handler takes and never loads. wry hands over only the top frame's navigations on WebView2, so there (`BrowserHost::sees_frame_navigations`) the picker reports through `window.open`, which the new-window handler takes the same way; so does a page whose Content-Security-Policy refuses the frame, which the picker's first report (the empty selection) finds out before the first pick. A report without the session's nonce is ignored, so another page, a frame or an older session can't fill the selection. The page can still shape what the picker reports about it, as it can its own HTML, which the brief quotes anyway: the brief is only ever pasted, never submitted, and is held to 50 elements and 4 KB of HTML each. Component names come from what React and Vue keep on DOM nodes (React's fiber, `__reactFiber$…`, or `__reactInternalInstance$…` before React 17; Vue 3's `__vueParentComponent`, Vue 2's `__vue__`), not from a devtools hook as PRODUCT_SPEC suggests: a hook has to be in place before the page's own scripts run, so it would mean injecting a script into every page all the time, while the nodes give the same names to a picker turned on at any point. The brief puts the note after the page's address and before the elements. The E2E harness reaches into pages with `test_browser_eval` (debug builds with `TANDEM_E2E=1` only), since WebDriver sees only the app's own webview.
- D-211: Terminal links resolve in the core against the pane's live folder: the shell's last OSC 7 (the PTY's state), else the pane's stored cwd, else its workspace root. Agent panes print no OSC 7, so their links start where they started. Only regular files that exist become links (never a folder, a device or a pipe, which an editor tab can't read), and punctuation a sentence put after a path is tried without. A file link opens in the Files tab of the context on screen, by its absolute path when it's outside that folder, so a file in another workspace or outside all of them opens there too (read-only outside every workspace, D-201). A URL opened in the app goes to a new sidebar browser tab.

### M9 log
- M9 task 1 (files backend): `tandem_core::files` (+ `files::watch`), `editor_tabs` repo, `FilesService` and 12 commands; 30 core, 2 store and 9 app tests. Found on the way: `pnpm keys:dev` on a fresh machine rewrites the tracked `resources/keys.json` with new public keys; this session keeps it local (`git update-index --skip-worktree`) and never commits it.
- M9 task 2 (Files tab and editor): `FileTree`, `CodeEditor`, `features/files`, the files store, the demo's files and E2E-M9-1. Found and fixed on the way: the sidebar's "+" menu sometimes ignored a click right after an item was picked (E2E-M5-6 F-SH-10.3 failed 4 of 6 runs; the same happened on the base build). Radix keeps closing content mounted while an animation it hasn't seen start is running, and a popper (menus, popovers, selects, tooltips) starts its entry animation only once it's positioned. A menu closed in that gap stayed mounted for the animation's 180 ms, and its outside-click layer closed the next menu at once. Closed content no longer animates (`base.css`); the new E2E-M5-6 test "F-SH-10 the + menu opens again right after an item is picked" forces the gap and failed 3 of 3 runs before the fix. E2E-M5-6 F-SH-10.1 (at most one reflow per pane when the sidebar opens) also failed about 2 runs in 3 on this VM, on the base build too: its 16 terminal views open one per frame and connect over several seconds on software GL, and the test started counting 1.5 s after the panes appeared, so a late connect's resize was counted with the toggle's. It now waits until every terminal has connected (debug perf marks) and a whole second passes with no resize before it toggles; it passed 9 of 10 runs since. The one failure (a pane reflowed twice) didn't recur in 6 runs instrumented with ResizeObserver logs, which showed one fit per terminal per toggle. Open: look again if it recurs in the M9 regression.
- M9 task 4a (browser in the sidebar): `browser::BrowserService` and its wry host, seven commands, the sidebar's browser tabs and E2E-M9-3, which passed on its first run. WebDriver only sees the main webview, so the pages were also checked on X screenshots of the test display. They found that a page could grow but never shrink: in a `gtk::Fixed` a child is never made smaller than its minimum, WebKit reports its current size as its minimum, and wry's `set_bounds` there only calls `size_allocate`, which the next layout undoes. Pages now keep their frame and get it exactly after the Fixed's layout (its `size-allocate`), with the Fixed's own position and size request kept in step, so Phone and Tablet preview, full screen and sidebar resizes place them right. wry also gave every new page the keyboard focus; restored tabs would have taken it from a terminal, so pages are made unfocused. On this VM a menu's first paint after the sidebar opens can take about a second with or without a page; that's software rendering, not the browser.
- M9 task 4b (browser panes and seats): the picker's Browser tile, `BrowserPane`, pane ↔ sidebar moves, restored pane pages and browser seats. E2E-M9-3's relaunch test failed about one run in three: the restored pane stayed "loading" though the core had loaded it. Temporary stderr tracing showed the page's events arriving before `browser_open`'s own reply, which then put the stale "loading" state back (D-209); with `seq` it passed 5 of 5. The same test also caught the sidebar's 300 ms debounced save landing after an immediate relaunch, so it now waits for the saved sidebar and layout before relaunching. The disk allowance filled twice during app rebuilds; deleting `target/debug/incremental`, leftover `*.rcgu.o` and stale `tandem_app` artifacts freed about 10 GB each time.
- M9 task 5 (element picker): `picker.js` and `browser::picker`, the picker panel, Send to… for panes and composers, the `/react` fixture page and E2E-M9-4 (3 of 3 runs green). The fixture server bundles `/react` with the desktop's own Vite on its first visit (about 5 s here), so the fixtures add no dependency. Found on the way: a brief sent to a thread while its composer was still loading the draft would have been replaced by the draft; the composer now adds sent text only once the draft is in (the new thread test fails without that). Reading wry for the other platforms showed that on Windows it hands over only WebView2's top-frame navigations (`NavigationStarting`), so the picker's frame reports would never arrive there; the host now says whether it sees frames, and on Windows the picker reports through new-window requests (D-210; added to the macOS/Windows manual QA in HUMAN_TODO). E2E-M9-4's axe check once measured the "Brief copied" toast while it faded out (its 5 s timer ran out during the check), so the test waits for the toast to go first.
- M9 task 6 (links): `tandem_core::files::links`, `pane_resolve_link`, the terminal's link providers, the Files tab's pending open, and E2E-M9-5 (5 of 5 tests in 3 of 3 runs). Its first run caught the test, not the app: each link was printed at the same spot, so the pointer never moved and xterm never looked for a link there (a plain click on a link then "opened nothing" for the wrong reason). The pointer now comes in from a few rows below, and the plain-click test first checks that xterm shows the link under it. The launch token reaches a shell only with shell integration, so the app test runs bash with it and checks a real `cd sub`.
- M9 close: the full E2E suite (65 spec files, about 13 minutes) passed 64. E2E-M9-1 F-CD-19.1 failed once, and the app did what F-CD-19 says: the test's "agent" wrote the file before the user's edit, so when typing took longer than the watcher's 500 ms batch (late in a loaded run) the untouched file reloaded first, the edit landed on the agent's text, and there was nothing to conflict. The test now edits, then writes the file, then saves, which is the criterion's own case ("a file that an agent modified meanwhile") without the race, and asserts the same prompt and the same untouched file; it passed 3 of 3, and so did the `m9-*` run. E2E-M5-6 F-SH-10.1 (the open note under task 2) passed in the suite run.

## M18 checklist (Next features: usage)
Built on branch `product-research` (from `chain19`), the coordinator's M18 share for this session: F-AC-08, F-AC-09 and F-RT-06 from `docs/NEXT_FEATURES.md` (§1 #4, #8 and §3 B1). Decisions D-2460–D-2479.
- [x] 1. Usage forecast (F-AC-08). `tandem_engines::forecast`: `UsageHistory` keeps each reading per account and window in memory (2 h, ≤120; a later reset or a fall of 5 points starts a new window) and `forecast()` projects the first-to-last rate of the last 60 min to 100% (not enough data under two readings or 10 min; on track when flat or past the reset). The probe adds `forecasts` to `UsageView`; `UsageBoard::newly_short` warns once per account, window and reset when the new `usage_forecast_notify` setting (off) is on, as a `usage_forecast` notification with `SourceRef::Usage` (opens Settings › Accounts). `usage_log::recent_sessions` (the parser now streams through `visit_file`, shared with the F-AC-07 scan) feeds `usage_sessions`, matched to panes by `StatusService::engine_sessions()`. The popover shows each window's forecast and "Who's using it" (loading, error, unreadable, empty, populated); the launch sheet's agent seats say when their account is at 80% or forecast short and name an account below 50%, text only. E2E-M18-AC08 (`m18-ac08-forecast.e2e.ts`) uses `test_usage_backdate` to stand in for 30 minutes between readings.
- [x] 2. Runaway guard (F-AC-09). `tandem_engines::guard`: `GuardLimits` (from the `runaway_guard` setting, clamped; `null` = off) and `PaneGuard` (time working, subagents since the last prompt, the last three turn-end hashes, one trip per limit per turn, Dismiss until the next prompt). `services::runaway`: `Guardian` reads `StatusService::snapshot()` every 5 s (Working from Done or Idle starts a turn; Working → Done ends one and hashes the pane's last 12 non-empty screen lines), counts `PreToolUse` reports of the registry's new `subagent_tools` (`Task`, `Agent` for Claude Code) through `StatusService::set_tool_sink`, and every 5 min gives each pane its window points (F-AC-08 share × `UsageHistory::hour_rise`). A trip shows UI-170 (`GuardBanner`, `runaway:changed`) and raises a `runaway` notification (a new type row, "Runaway guard"); Pause (or `runaway_action = "pause"`) types the registry's new `interrupt` (Esc by default), and an agent still Working 5 s later is stopped through `tandem_pty::suspend::ProcessControl` (`SignalControl`: killpg SIGSTOP/SIGCONT on Unix, unavailable on Windows) on `PtyManager::foreground_group`. `kill_tree` now continues descendants first, so a paused job is never left stopped. Settings › Teammates › Runaway guard edits the limits (each can be turned off) and the action; threads and teammate chats trip on time only, with a notification. The fake engine's `/subagents <n>` reports n `Task` starts and keeps working. E2E-M18-AC09 (`m18-ac09-runaway.e2e.ts`).
- [x] 3. Night Shift, first slice (F-RT-06). `services::night_shift`: runs in `$DATA/night-shift.json`; `next_reset` (the fullest usage window's reset, else the earliest future limit reset on the account), `due` (start, or wait for a later reset while a window is ≥95%), `summary` and `summary_due` (once a day after `night_shift_summary_at`, 08:00 local by default). The GUI ticks every 30 s; `--run-routines` runs `headless::night_pass` after its routines, within the same cap, when background routines are on. A due run calls `TaskExecService::execute` with its own account and engine, never another; started runs follow their attempt. The summary raises a `night_shift` notification (the Routines type row, `SourceRef::Task`) and queues the same push alert through the new `PhoneService::queue_alert`. UI-171 "Run at reset…" on a task (accounts with their next reset, the plan in words, Execute's Pro block, the note when background routines are off), the task's "Runs at … on …" list with Cancel, and Settings › Notifications › Night Shift summary at. E2E-M18-RT06 (`m18-rt06-night-shift.e2e.ts`) with `test_night_tick`.
- [x] 4. Night Shift review 2 (F-RT-06): the launch settle waits for the app lock before interrupting threads (`settle_threads`), `night-shift.json` is re-read before every change and tick, and the morning push alert waits in the file (`pendingAlerts`) until the GUI hands it to the phone queue. Unit tests F-RT-06.9 to .12 and headless `night_pass` tests (8-hour cap, dead process). D-2540 to D-2542.

### M18 decisions
- D-2460: Forecast samples live in memory only. A forecast needs at most the last hour, a restart costs at most 10 minutes of "Not enough data yet", and nothing new is written to disk.
- D-2461: "Who's using it" names sessions by panes only (the status bus's reported engine session ids). Thread and teammate-chat sessions show as "Another session" until threads report their engine session ids to the same map.
- D-2463: The guard polls the status snapshot every 5 s instead of subscribing to status events. Every source (hooks, heuristics, threads) already lands in the snapshot with its `since`, and a turn's start and end are transitions between two looks; a missed flicker within 5 s changes nothing the limits measure.
- D-2464: Subagents are counted from the engine's own `PreToolUse` reports of the tools named in the registry's `subagent_tools`, not from `SubagentStop`, which reports nothing about which tool ran and comes after the work. Engines without hooks have no subagent count.
- D-2465: "Going in circles" compares a hash of the last 12 non-empty screen lines at each turn end. The screen text is never stored; threads (no screen) don't compare endings.
- D-2466: The guard's limits are stored as a map of numbers, `null` and booleans, under a new `Limits` validator: the `Map` validator allows only strings, booleans and string lists, and the E2E run caught `settings_set` refusing the defaults.
- D-2467 (F-AC-08 fix): a forecast warning is keyed by account and window, with a reset within a minute of the warned one counting as the same window. Codex's app-server reports `resetsAt` as now plus the window's remainder, so it drifts by seconds between readings, and keying by the exact time warned again on the next reading (caught by E2E-M18-AC08 when run after a slower start).
- D-2468: E2E-M18-AC09 checks the runaway item in the notification center, not the OS notifier: with the window focused and the pane on screen, "Only when I can't see the agent" (the default) rightly sends no OS notification.
- D-2469: Night Shift runs live in `$DATA/night-shift.json` (atomic writes) rather than a new SQLite table: the first slice needs no queries, and a migration here would race the other M18 branches' (D-702's renumbering rule).
- D-2470: A run's reset comes from the account's fullest usage window; when the engine reports no windows (Claude Code today), from the last usage limit a pane hit on that account (F-AC-05). An account with neither can't be picked, rather than guessing a time.
- D-2471: At its time a run reads the account's usage again and waits while a window is still at 95% or more with a later reset: reset times are the engine's estimate, and starting on a still-full account would hit its limit at once. It never moves to another account.
- D-2472: The morning summary marks the day sent even when nothing ended, so runs that end later are summarized the next morning rather than with a second notification the same day.
- D-2473 (F-RT-06 review): a run started by the background runner keeps that process and the app lock while it runs, up to 8 hours, instead of ending with routines' 50-minute pass and killing its agent. At 8 hours it's stopped and reported. While a headless pass holds the lock, the GUI neither ticks Night Shift runs nor settles task attempts (`RoutinesService::wait_lock`), so it can't double-start a run or mark the pass's live attempt stopped. The pass settles attempts first, since with the lock held any active attempt belongs to a dead process. A run whose attempt was settled ends as `needs_you` with the attempt's reason, and the summary counts it.
- D-2540 (F-RT-06 review 2): the launch settle no longer interrupts threads at once. `settle_at_launch` pauses agent panes and lists the threads and turns left working; `RestoreService::settle_threads` settles those still working once this app holds the app lock (the GUI right after `wait_lock`, before the attempt settle; the headless pass at its start). Opening the app while a headless Night Shift run works left its thread `interrupted`, so the pass's `wait_turn` failed the run. A thread the pass finished meanwhile keeps its state, and one this app starts before the lock isn't in the list.
- D-2541: `night-shift.json` is re-read before every change and before each tick picks due runs (`due_runs`). The GUI's copy from launch went stale while a headless pass started and finished a run, so the GUI started it again and its save overwrote the pass's record.
- D-2542: the morning summary's push alert is kept in `night-shift.json` (`pendingAlerts`) with the day it marks sent; the GUI's tick hands it to the phone service's queue, dropping alerts over a day old. A headless pass has no push loop, so the in-memory queue lost the alert whenever the app was closed, which is the Night Shift's main case.
- D-2462: The E2E spec moves earlier readings back with `test_usage_backdate` instead of a mock clock: the app's clock can only be mocked from launch (`TANDEM_FAKE_CLOCK`), and the fake app-server's reset times follow the real clock.

### M18 log
- F-RT-06: spec, the planner and its state (6 tests), the dialog's plan and account list and the store (3 vitest cases), docs (Routines › Night Shift) and E2E-M18-RT06 (2 cases). The E2E caught a missing account (the fake Codex has none until one is made; the spec makes one) and nothing in the product.
- F-AC-09: spec, guard core (6 tests), process control (1, a real `sleep` stopped and continued on Linux), the guardian and the service's Dismiss/Resume with a fake `ProcessControl` (6), the settings validator (1), the card and store (4 vitest cases), docs (Checkpoints and workflow tools › Runaway guard) and E2E-M18-AC09 (2 cases, with E2E-M18-AC08 alongside).
- F-AC-08: E2E-M18-AC08 passes (2 cases), and so do E2E-M6-8, E2E-M6-1 and E2E-M5-5 next to it. Spec, forecast core and history (4 tests), per-session tokens (1), the board's warnings, text and shares (3), the popover, the launch hint and the store (6 vitest cases), docs (Accounts and limits › Usage and forecasts) and E2E-M18-AC08. The first gate run ran out of disk (26 GB of debug info in `target/`); gates now run with `CARGO_PROFILE_DEV_DEBUG=0 CARGO_PROFILE_TEST_DEBUG=0`.

## M18 checklist (Next features: sandbox)
Built on branch `product-research`, the coordinator's next M18 share for this session: F-TL-11 from `docs/NEXT_FEATURES.md`, Linux first. Decisions D-2474 on.
- [x] 1. Policy and detection. `tandem_engines::sandbox`: `SandboxPolicy` and `bwrap_args` (`--ro-bind / /`, `/dev`, `/proc`; writable `/tmp`, the pane's folder, the repository's common git dir and the tasks folder; an empty `~/.cache`; `~/.ssh`, `~/.aws`, `~/.gnupg`, `~/.config/gh`, `~/.docker`, `~/.kube`, `~/.netrc`, `~/.local/share/keyrings`, `providers/` and the other worktrees hidden under tmpfs or `/dev/null`; then this account's provider home writable and `run/` read-only; `.git/hooks` and `.git/config` read-only last). The `Sandboxer` trait: `Bubblewrap::detect` finds `bwrap` on the login PATH and probes a one-command sandbox; `NotYet` on macOS and Windows. Nothing is installed.
- [x] 2. Agent panes. With `agent_sandbox = "workspace"` (Settings › Engines, off by default), `TerminalService` wraps a local agent pane's engine argv; SSH and WSL panes never. Each pane's `PaneSandbox` is kept and published as `sandbox:pane`; commands `sandbox_status`, `sandbox_panes`. The engine's argv and environment are unchanged, and nothing under a provider home is read.
- [x] 3. UI and docs. `SandboxChip` on agent panes ("Sandboxed", or why not), `SandboxSetting` with the reason when unavailable, the store `sandbox.ts`, and the docs' Engines › Sandbox agent panes (Linux). The fake engine's `/sh <cmd>` prints a command's output and exit code. E2E-M18-TL11 (`m18-tl11-sandbox.e2e.ts`).
- [x] Sandbox review 2 (F-TL-11): `$DATA` is hidden under a tmpfs (tasks, `run/`, the pane's staging folder, this account's provider home and an engine installed under `$DATA` come back); engines without a provider home start unsandboxed with `OWN_HOME`; Workspace can't be turned on while the sandbox can't run, and an unsandboxed start with it on raises one `sandbox` notification per launch; a closed pane clears its chip. D-2543, D-2544.

### M18 sandbox decisions
- D-2474: The first slice uses bubblewrap's filesystem view only: no new PID, network or session namespace. The network is shared (engines need it), and keeping the PID namespace lets the runaway guard (F-AC-09) stop and continue the engine through the PTY's foreground group.
- D-2475: `/tmp` stays writable and shared. Engines and their tools write there, and a private `/tmp` would break tools that hand files to other processes. The spec says so, and the real-bwrap test checks "outside" on `/var/tmp` instead.
- D-2476: Only the engine's own argv is wrapped (`bwrap … -- <engine argv>`), and `env::agent_wrapper` runs that inside the pane's login shell as before. When the engine exits the shell is back, outside the sandbox, with the same profile, PATH and environment.
- D-2477: Hidden paths are covered with an empty tmpfs (folders) or `/dev/null` (files), from the path's type at spawn. A path that doesn't exist is skipped, so a key folder made after a pane started is hidden from the next agent pane on. The other worktrees are listed from `.tandem/worktrees/` at spawn.
- D-2478: E2E-M18-TL11 prints its marker before the command (`/sh echo M; cmd`) so the reported exit code is the command's own; the first draft's `cmd; echo M` exited with echo's 0. The spec writes the key under the panes' `HOME` (run.ts's `home/`), not `TANDEM_HOME`.
- D-2479 (F-TL-11 review): every hooks folder git would run is read-only, as git resolves it: `.git/hooks` of the folder and of a worktree's common dir, plus `hooks_dir` (`git rev-parse --git-path hooks`, so `core.hooksPath` too). A missing one inside a writable area gets an empty read-only tmpfs, since otherwise the agent could create it; bubblewrap leaves that empty folder behind, as `git init` would. `~/.ssh` was already hidden as a whole folder, not file by file.
- D-2480: Not covered, and said so in the docs and spec: sockets of programs already running for the user (`ssh-agent` through `SSH_AUTH_SOCK`, `gpg-agent`, an unlocked desktop keyring on the session bus), and scripts a hook calls from elsewhere in the folder (they show in the diff). Closing the sockets means hiding the session bus and changing the environment, which F-TL-11.5 rules out and engines' own sign-in may need; that's a product call for the owner. A worktree's own `config.worktree` (read only with `extensions.worktreeConfig`, which `.git/config`, read-only, would have to turn on) stays writable.
- D-2481: Specs that open panes close them in an `after` hook (`closeAllPanes`): spec files share one `TANDEM_HOME` in file order, and panes left open showed up as leftovers in the next launch's "still running" dialog. E2E-M18-TL11 also stops leftovers before it starts (`stopLeftovers`) and fails at once with "needs bubblewrap" when `bwrap` is missing. bubblewrap is in `bootstrap-linux.sh`, OPERATING §3 and the CI E2E job.
- D-2543 (F-TL-11 review 2): Vibezeo's data folder is hidden under a tmpfs before the writable binds; only the tasks folder (writable), `run/` and the pane's `staging/<pane>/` (read-only), this account's provider home (writable) and, when the engine's program resolves under it, that program's folder (read-only) come back. A sandboxed agent could read `tandem.db` with every project's threads and prompts.
- D-2544: engines without a provider home (home strategy `none`: Cursor Agent, Gemini CLI, Copilot CLI, Grok, Droid, Amp, Aider, Kimi) start unsandboxed, their chip saying why (`OWN_HOME`): their sign-in lives in the real home, which is read-only inside, so a token refresh would fail. Binding each engine's own config folders writable needs a registry field with each vendor's documented paths; until then they're left out rather than guessed. `settings_set` refuses Workspace while the sandbox can't run (`may_turn_on`), and the option is disabled with the reason; a pane that starts unsandboxed with the setting on raises one `sandbox` notification per launch (`SourceRef::Pane`). A closed pane, or one started with the setting off, publishes a cleared note, so no chip lingers.

## M18 checklist (Next features: resource governor)
Built on branch `product-research`, after F-TL-11: F-EX-06 from `docs/NEXT_FEATURES.md`, Linux first. Decisions D-2545 to D-2548.
- [x] 1. Governor. `tandem_pty::governor`: `ResourceGroups` (`status`, `place`, `usage`, `freeze`, `thaw`), `SystemdScopes` (a transient user scope `vibezeo-pane-<pane>-<n>.scope` per agent pane through `busctl --user`, `MemoryMax` with `MemorySwapMax=0`, counters from the scope's `cpu.stat`, `memory.current`, `memory.events`) and `Unplaced` (the reason; usage summed over the pane's process tree from `/proc`; freeze by signals, shell first as Pause does). Unit tests with fakes and recorded cgroup files.
- [x] 2. Service and commands. `services::resources`: agent panes on this computer are placed as they start (a failure leaves them running, "Not capped" with the reason when a cap is set), usage every 2 s while the UI asks, a capped scope's rising `oom_kill` marks the pane once ("Stopped: memory cap 512 MB"), `resources:pane` notes. Commands `resources_status`, `resources_list`, `resources_notes`, `resources_freeze`, `resources_thaw`; `settings_set` refuses a cap where it can't apply. `services::check_queue` makes Best of N's and the review queue's checks take turns while `checks_one_at_a_time` is on (a waiting Best of N lane reads `queued`).
- [x] 3. UI and docs. The Ports tab's Panes section (`PaneUsage.tsx`, grouped by worktree, Freeze/Thaw), "CPU 12% · 840 MB" on Dashboard cards, `ResourceChip` and Freeze/Thaw in the pane menu, `MemoryCapSetting` and "One check at a time" in Settings › Engines, the store `resources.ts`, E2E-M18-EX06, and Engines › Resource governor (Linux) in the docs.
- [x] 4. Review fixes. The scope takes the pane's whole process tree, and children that start while it's made follow with `AttachProcessesToUnit` (a fake tree that grows between reads). `OOMPolicy=continue` from systemd 253 (properties tested at 252 and 253). A failed thaw returns its error and the pane stays frozen. Unit names carry a per-launch tag (`vibezeo-pane-<pane>-<launch>-<n>.scope`); `TerminalService::end_pane` thaws, kills, then stops the scope, and pane close, `pty_kill`, tab close, workspace removal and `lanes::stop_panes` all use it; at start, other launches' scopes are thawed. The merge queue's check takes its turn too, and `check_queue`'s state is a `CheckQueue` value (tests make their own). `busctl` and `systemctl` are ended after 5 s; capping reads the user manager's delegated `cgroup.controllers`. The governor's and the service's tests run on every platform (the Linux-only ones in their own module), and `TerminalService` in unit tests gets an inert governor, so no test makes a real scope. A migration test upgrades a v2 database with Best of N lanes to v3.

### M18 resource governor decisions
- D-2545: A pane's scope is made after its process exists, by moving its PID into a transient user scope (`StartTransientUnit` with `PIDs`), not by starting the shell through `systemd-run`. The PTY layer keeps spawning the shell itself (its environment, its controlling terminal, F-AC-09's process groups), and a scope that can't be made leaves the pane running as before. Children started before the move (none, in practice: it happens before the engine is typed) would stay outside. Review: the move takes the whole tree, reads it again afterwards and attaches any child that started meanwhile.
- D-2546: Without a scope, Freeze uses F-AC-09's signals: SIGSTOP to the pane's shell first, then each process group under it; Thaw continues them in reverse, shell last. Stopping the shell first keeps it from seeing its job stop and taking the terminal back. In a scope it's systemd's freezer (`systemctl --user freeze`), which stops everything at once.
- D-2547: E2E-M18-EX06 runs where there's no systemd user session (the CI container), so it covers the unplaced path. Scopes, caps and the OOM notice are tested in Rust against recorded cgroup files and a fake `busctl`; a check on a real desktop is in HUMAN_TODO.
- D-2548: "One check at a time" is one process-wide turn (a semaphore of one) shared by Best of N and the review queue, first come first served. A check's 10-minute limit starts when its turn does, and Stop drops a waiting check's place. Turning the setting off lets checks started from then on run at once; one already waiting keeps its place.

## M18 checklist (Next features: agent-proof secrets)
Built on branch `product-research`, after F-EX-06: the first slice of bold bet B5 from `docs/NEXT_FEATURES.md`, specified as F-TL-12. Decisions D-2549 to D-2554.
- [x] 1. Store and references. `tandem_core::secrets` (names, `.env` parsing with `export`, quotes and comments, `vibezeo-secret://` references, resolution naming a missing secret); the `secrets` table (migration 0005; 0004 is `competitive-gaps`' lane_repos, merged first) for names; `services::secrets` with values in the keychain (`NS.secrets/<name>`), values of 8+ bytes also registered with the global redactor; Delete local data… removes every entry.
- [x] 2. Run and masking. `secrets_run` creates a terminal pane in the source pane's folder and parks a `PendingRun`; its first `spawn_with` runs `env::run_wrapper` (`<command>`, then `unset` the names and `exec` a login shell) with the variables in the PTY environment only. `tandem_pty::mask::LiteralMasker` masks the values in the reader before the screen parser, with a flusher thread for held tails. Commands `secrets_list`, `secrets_set`, `secrets_delete`, `secrets_run_plan`, `secrets_run`.
- [x] 3. UI and docs. Settings › Secrets (`SecretsPage.tsx`: names and references only, add, replace, delete, empty, loading and error states), the Run button and "Run with secrets…" on terminal panes in local folders (`RunDialog.tsx`, naming a missing secret), the store `secrets.ts`, E2E-M18-TL12, and Privacy › Agent-proof secrets in the docs.
- [x] 4. Review fixes. The run wrapper passes the command as `$1` and runs it under `eval` in a subshell with SIGINT trapped, so Ctrl-C or a command that doesn't parse can't skip the `unset`; PowerShell unsets in `finally`; `TANDEM_SHELL_TOKEN` is unset too (real sh, bash, zsh and fish in PTY tests). `.env` is read with `symlink_metadata` (a link must stay inside the folder) through one handle up to the limit; a BOM is dropped, quoted values may span lines, an unclosed quote skips only its variable, and a value that looks like a reference but isn't is flagged in the plan and refused. The secrets row is written before the keychain entry and removed if the keychain refuses. Run checks the tab is in the source pane's workspace, refuses (and hides) where plain terminals open in WSL or the shell is cmd, and a closed pane's parked run is dropped. The masker is aho-corasick leftmost-longest, holds any tail that could still become a longer value, masks a shorter value inside a released tail, and its flusher waits on a condvar. The docs list what isn't masked, and Settings › Secrets warns about values under 4 bytes.

### M18 agent-proof secrets decisions
- D-2549: B5's first slice is F-TL-12 in §4.18 (TL: privacy and security), next to the sandbox. The rest of B5 (a `vibezeo run` command line, approvals for runs an agent starts, secrets for agent panes) is left for later slices and named as not covered in the spec and the docs.
- D-2550: The names live in SQLite and the values in the keychain, one entry per name. The `keyring` crate can't list a service's entries, and the plugin credentials and phone pairings already split the same way. Delete local data… reads the names before the database goes, so no keychain entry outlives it.
- D-2551: A run is a new terminal pane, not text typed into the current shell. Typed `KEY=value cmd` would put the value in the scrollback and the shell's history. The values go only into the PTY's environment; the command line is the user's command; `exec`-ing a fresh login shell after `unset` leaves nothing for what's typed next. cmd.exe can't do that, so Run is refused there, and Flatpak is refused because `flatpak-spawn` carries the environment on its argv.
- D-2552: Masking happens in the PTY reader, before the screen parser and the batcher, so the screen, the ring, snapshots, Auto and Conductor all see `[REDACTED]`. The global redactor only matches token-shaped values of 8+ characters and keeps hashes, so it can't catch passwords with spaces or symbols. Values under 4 bytes aren't masked: they'd hide ordinary output.
- D-2553: A tail that could be the start of a value is held, and a flusher thread releases it after 50 ms of quiet. A prompt that happens to end in the first letters of a value would otherwise wait for the next keystroke. A value split by more than 50 ms of quiet can slip through; the docs' "only the exact value" covers it.
- D-2554: Only the folder's own `.env` (the workspace or worktree root) is read, up to 256 KB. Every variable in it is set for the command, plain values too, so Run behaves like the tools people already use with `.env`. A later line for the same name wins.

### M18 sandbox log
- F-TL-11: spec, policy and detection (7 tests, one against the test machine's real `bwrap` 0.9.0), the worktree policy (1), the chip and setting (3 vitest cases), docs and E2E-M18-TL11 (1 case). Run on its own, the spec passes; run beside E2E-M18-AC09 in parallel workers, it once met that run's leftover pane in the "still running" dialog. The E2E found nothing in the product. Review fixes (D-2479 to D-2481): a real worktree test (commits work; no hook through a missing `.git/hooks` or `core.hooksPath`) and the builder's case (1); the E2E checks the whole `~/.ssh` and a missing hooks folder, and E2E-M18-TL11 and E2E-M18-AC09 pass in both orders (a copy of TL11 run first, then AC09, then TL11). The E2E's `$HOME` lives under `/tmp`, which the sandbox shares, so `$HOME` writes are checked by the real-bwrap Rust test only.

## M18 · Free for everyone (`free-for-everyone`)
Built on branch `free-for-everyone` (from `release-0.9.0`, c5b98702) for the owner's decision of 24 October 2026 (D-869): Vibezeo is free, with no paid plans, and every feature that runs on the person's computer is free and unlimited from the release after 0.9.0. Decisions D-2900 to D-2919 (D-2900 to D-2913 used).
- [x] 1. Limits. `Limits::free()` (`crates/tandem-core/src/entitlement.rs`), `LIMITS.free` (`packages/protocol/src/plans.ts`) and the shared fixture are the local-unlocked set: no limit on panes, workspaces, teammates, plugin connections or accounts per engine; six threads side by side; every chat engine; routines, background routines, task runs and session search on; the hosted features (cloud STT, Enhance, Conductor, Auto, admin, credit packs, dictionary sync, the phone relay) off. `LIMITS.pro` and `LIMITS.team` are never less than `free` (pro's 64-pane soft cap is gone). Routines, task runs and the Night Shift no longer need sign-in (D-2902), so the offline build (`TANDEM_HOSTED=0`) runs them with no account.
- [x] 2. App UI. The plans dialog (UI-120/121), "See plans" (account menu, palette `billing.plans`, gates), Pro badges, upgrade prompts, the account menu's plan badge, the past-due banner and the Account page's plan, billing and credit-pack buttons are gone, with the checkout path (`BillingTarget::Upgrade`, `ApiClient::checkout`) and the Dictate toast's `see_plans` action. The welcome card is "Free · no account · nothing sent to Vibezeo" (DESIGN §9.3) with no callout; the rail footer reads "Free", "Offline" or "{n} credits". Hosted features say "Comes with Vibezeo accounts" (a shared `ComesWithAccounts`, tooltips, Conductor's HUD error); generic limit gates that a signed token could still trip use neutral copy ("This account runs up to {n} live panes", "… is turned off for this account").
- [x] 3. The demo's `ENTITLEMENT` is `LIMITS.free`; routines, task runs and the Night Shift say they work in the app (D-2905).
- [x] 4. Tests: updated to the new spec in Rust, vitest and E2E; new ones prove the old limits are gone (a fifth pane and a fourth workspace, a Cursor Agent thread and session search offline: E2E-M3-7, E2E-M2-2, the new E2E-M18-FREE, and E2E-M11-6 for routines and task runs). Tests whose gate no longer exists are listed in D-2907.
- [x] 5. Docs and site: the "this release keeps limits… the next removes them" wording is gone from the home page (roadmap item, tiles, FAQ), the download page, the docs (accounts-and-limits without `PlanLimitsTable`, routines without its banner, every page that mentioned Pro) and `lib/progress.ts`; `lib/limits.ts` is deleted. PRODUCT_SPEC §3 and UI_SPEC §4.14 (and every gate they described) say what ships now; MILESTONES' E2E-M2-2, E2E-M3-7, E2E-M11-6 and E2E-M18-TH17 follow, with E2E-M18-FREE added.
- [x] 6. CHANGELOG: a `## Unreleased` section describes the unlock (D-2906); the coordinating session cuts the release.
- [x] 7. DESIGN §5.5 and §9.5 (merged from `product-m0-m5`): no plan footer or limit cards, the hosted phrase word for word with "Tell me when accounts open", Settings' one free line, and a test that the download never says "credit" (D-2910 to D-2912).

### M18 · Free for everyone decisions
- D-2900: The limits with no account are the local-unlocked set, in Rust and TypeScript from one fixture. No technical cap replaces the old pane limit: panes are bounded by the machine, and the terminal already caps WebGL contexts at eight (E2E-M3-1 runs sixteen shells). The plan ids `free`, `pro` and `team` stay inside the code and the hosted API's signed entitlement (simplest, and the API keeps signing `LIMITS[plan]`); nothing user-facing names a plan, and `pro`/`team` are never less than `free` (a test checks every field). `phone` stays false: it gates only the hosted relay (`phone_pair_begin` checks it after sign-in), while LAN pairing (F-RM-04) never read it.
- D-2901: Generic limit checks stay as a backstop for a signed token that carries a limit (none does): the core's pane, workspace, teammate, plugin, account, chat-engine, routine, task-run and session-search checks, the pane limit card, the thread-grid toast and the thread's engine gate. Their copy names "this account" and offers no plan or price. UI that only sold a plan is removed (the plans dialog, the routines upsell, the teammate limit banner, the accounts "Upgrade" button). Internal identifiers (`ErrorCode::ForbiddenPlan`, `CloudFailureKind::NeedsPro`, `CallErrorCode::needs_pro`, `paused_by_plan`) keep their names; they're codes, not copy. A unit-test-only `EntitlementService::set_test_limits` lets the generic checks be tested now that no local limit exists.
- D-2902: Routines, task runs (Execute…, `task_execute`) and the Night Shift (`night_schedule`) need no sign-in. They run the person's own engines in worktrees on their computer; the sign-in check only proved the plan. Signing out no longer stops running task attempts; limits that turn task runs off still do. `RoutineHost::signed_in`, `ExecHost::signed_in` and `FailKind::SignedOut` are removed (an old row's `signed_out` kind is still only a string).
- D-2903: Hosted features with no account say "Comes with Vibezeo accounts" and never a price or "Pro": Cloud in Dictate stays on Local with that tooltip; Enhance's controls are disabled with it (gated on `limits.enhance`, no longer on the plan id); dictionary sync shows it in place of "Sync with Pro"; Conductor's call command shows it as the HUD's error (`needs_pro`) instead of opening plans; relay pairing, Create with AI, Auto and Find work's hosted ranking say it in their copy. The Dictate toast's only action is "Use local for this take" (`ToastAction::SeePlans` is removed).
- D-2904: Nothing is sold in the app: `UpgradeDialog` and its test, the `upgrade` UI state, `billing.plans` (registry, palette, Conductor's `get_credits` now voice-only), `BillingTarget::Upgrade`, `ApiClient::checkout`, the account menu's plan badge, the past-due banner and the Account page's plan row, "Manage billing" and "Buy credits" are removed. Settings › Account keeps who's signed in, an organization's name and the credits hosted features use. The hosted API's billing routes and the web portal's billing, credits and team pages stay as they are (not deployed).
- D-2905: The demo runs `LIMITS.free`. Routines, task runs and the Night Shift become visible there, so `routine_save`, `routine_run_now`, `routine_preview_schedule`, `routines_background_set`, `task_execute` and `night_schedule` answer "… works in the app, not in this demo" (D-833) and `night_accounts` lists none.

The 0.9.0 section and the past blog posts keep their words: they describe what those releases did.

Requirement removed: Free threads limited to Claude Code and Codex (PRODUCT_SPEC §3.2, D-127) (D-869). The rest of the spec now runs on a free account; the generic chat-engine narrowing keeps its Rust and vitest coverage.
