## M17 checklist (Extras)
Built on branch `m17-extras` (from `product-m0-m5`) in parallel with the M8, M10–M15 and release sessions, at the owner's request for creative features beyond the spec. Owns F-EX-01…05 (PRODUCT_SPEC §4.19, MILESTONES M17, UI_SPEC §4.16 UI-140–145, ARCHITECTURE §3.2 "Extras"). Decisions D-700–D-799; this section holds M17's state, decisions and log so other sessions' sections stay untouched.
- [x] 0. Spec: PRODUCT_SPEC §4.19 "EX · Extras" (F-EX-01 checkpoints and rewind, F-EX-02 command recall, F-EX-03 ports panel, F-EX-04 Best of N, F-EX-05 prompt library) with numbered acceptance criteria; MILESTONES "M17 · Extras" with tasks, verification and E2E-M17-1…5; UI_SPEC UI-140–145 and two shortcuts; ARCHITECTURE §3.2 rows for every new command and two events (§3.3), §5.8 command lines and §7 checkpoints. `scripts/check-coverage.sh` accepts P2 in M17 (157 spec IDs).
- [x] 1. Checkpoints (F-EX-01). `tandem_git::checkpoint::Store`: a bare store per folder at `$DATA/checkpoints/<key>/store.git` (`<key>`: 16 hex of the folder path's SHA-256) with its own index, so `git add -f --pathspec-from-file` rehashes only files whose stat data changed; `update-index --force-remove` drops what's gone; `write-tree` gives the checkpoint, kept as a ref `refs/checkpoints/<id>` to the tree. The list is the folder's repository's `ls-files --cached --others --exclude-standard` (the user's index only read, `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0`) or, outside a repository, an `ignore` walk with `.gitignore` files, hidden files and a 20,000-file cap (partial past it); `.git` and `.tandem/worktrees` never; files over the size limit are skipped and listed. Bytes are stored as they are (`--attr-source` at the empty tree, an empty `core.attributesFile`, `core.autocrlf=false`). Restore runs in Rust from the store's blobs: atomic replace with the mode, symlinks as links, a link on the way refuses the file (never written through), `.git` refused, deletions only of files the "now" snapshot lists, and never a file either side skipped; a partial checkpoint never deletes. Migration `0002_extras.sql` (checkpoints, command_history, bestof_runs, bestof_lanes; D-702). `CheckpointService`: one queue per folder, dedupe against the folder's newest tree, pruning by count and age after each checkpoint and at startup (a restore's own checkpoint is kept an hour), `gc --prune=now` in the store, usage and delete all. Hooks: `ThreadService::send` takes the turn's checkpoint after the session is up and before `send_turn`; agent panes' input goes through an ordered per-pane queue in which a write with Enter waits for the prompt hook at most 2 s (`TerminalService::write_input`, `before_prompt`; late ones are marked), and `pty_type_prompt` checkpoints before typing. Six commands, `checkpoint:created`, sidebar tab Checkpoints (UI-140: rows against now, file checkboxes, DiffView, restore all or chosen with the confirm, Undo in the toast, the not-a-repository note, no-git, off and empty states), Settings › Checkpoints (UI-117) and `sidebar.checkpoints` in the registry. Tests: tandem-git 13, store 2, app 7 (incl. a thread turn whose engine edits the file the moment it gets the turn), desktop vitest 8, E2E-M17-1 5 (thread turn with the repository's HEAD, refs, stash and index byte-identical; restore one; restore all with Before restore; Enter in an agent pane with the fake engine's new `/write`; a folder without git).
- [x] 2. Command recall (F-EX-02). The bash, zsh, fish and PowerShell scripts put the command line on `133;C` (PowerShell from PSReadLine's buffer) as `cmdline_url=<percent-encoded>`, at most 12,000 encoded characters so the mark stays under `MarkFilter`'s 16 KB; never a line starting with a space; bash sends its newest history entry only when the history number moved (D-703). `MarkFilter` reads it only with the token (`Mark::CommandStart/CommandEnd { command }`) and never re-emits it; the side channel times C → D and adds `Mark::Ran {command, cwd, exit, started_at, duration_ms}`, and tracks `at_prompt`. `RecallService` redacts with the F-TL-02 redactor (`[REDACTED]`, `secret`), stores in `command_history`, prunes at startup and hourly (days setting, 50,000 rows), and `history_run` types the command and Enter only into a terminal pane at its prompt and never a secret. The recall palette (UI-141, `Mod+Alt+J`, `recall.open`) is a new `@tandem/ui` `QuickPicker` (the palette's shell, caller-filtered, Mod+Enter and Mod+Backspace, with a gallery entry); Enter inserts into the focused pane (bracketed paste, no Enter) or the thread's composer, or copies; Settings › Terminal gains "Remember commands I run", "Keep commands for (days)" and "Clear command history…". Tests: tandem-pty 3 (marks, side channel, and real bash, zsh and fish shells), store 2, app 2 (the token is in no database file, WAL included), desktop vitest 7, E2E-M17-2.
- [x] 3. Ports panel (F-EX-03). `tandem_pty::ports`: a `PortProbe` trait with Linux (`/proc/net/tcp{,6}` LISTEN sockets matched to `/proc/<pid>/fd` socket inodes), macOS (`lsof -nP -iTCP -sTCP:LISTEN -Fpcn`) and Windows (`netstat -ano -p tcp|tcpv6`) probes, each tested on recorded output; v4-mapped IPv6 reads as v4, and a server on both `0.0.0.0` and `::` is one row. Pane trees, names and start times come from one `sysinfo` read. `PortsService::list` keeps only ports whose process is in a live pane's tree; `ports_stop` checks again that the PID is in that pane's tree with the same start time (a reused PID is refused), sends SIGTERM (SIGKILL with Force) and waits 3 s. The Ports sidebar tab (UI-142) scans every 2 s only while it shows; Open loads `http://localhost:<port>` in a new sidebar browser tab; Stop and Force stop ask first. `sidebar.ports` in the registry. Tests: tandem-pty 6, app 2 (real process trees, an outsider and a fake start time), desktop vitest 5, E2E-M17-3 3 (a `python3 -m http.server` in a pane listed, one started outside not; Open; Stop with Cancel first, the pane stays).
- [x] 4. Best of N (F-EX-04). `services::bestof` over the launch service (worktree isolation) and lanes: `start` takes 2–3 seats and one prompt, refuses outside a repository or before a first commit, and launches every seat or none (the plan's live-pane check runs before any worktree is made); each lane is a `tandem/<slug>` worktree from the current HEAD, recorded in `bestof_runs`/`bestof_lanes` (migration `0002_extras.sql`), and its agent starts at once without a view (output waits in the ring until the lane is shown) so the first prompt is typed into every agent, not only the one on screen. `bestof_get` returns each lane's diff against the starting commit, committed and uncommitted (untracked files included). `bestof_check` runs the check command in every lane at once with `/bin/sh -c` and the login environment, in its own process group, with a 10-minute limit and the last 200 lines, redacted; Stop kills the groups, and a finished check forgets its group id so Stop never signals a reused one. `bestof_keep` refuses a base with uncommitted changes before asking, asks once in the native confirm with the F-EX-04 copy, stops every lane's agent, commits the winner's changes (`ops::stage_all`, "Best of N: <title> (<engine>)"), merges as "Merge into" does (a conflict keeps every lane), then removes the other worktrees; branches stay. `bestof_discard` asks natively and removes every worktree. Runs and check results survive a relaunch (a check running at quit shows Stopped). UI: the sheet (UI-143, palette "Best of N…": prompt, seats, check command, live summary, the uncommitted-changes note, the not-a-repository state, ⌘↩) and the Compare dialog (UI-144, palette "Compare Best of N": a column per lane with its agent state, files and +/−, check result and Output, DiffView, file filter, Run check / Stop check, Keep this one…, Discard all…, merged and discarded states). Not built (D-710): one shared tab for the agents, a launch-sheet entry, a tab-header chip, per-seat Retry, the account picker per seat. Tests: tandem-git 1, app 8, desktop vitest 9, E2E-M17-4 4.
- [x] 5. Prompt library (F-EX-05). `tandem_core::prompts`: `*.md` files in `<workspace>/.tandem/prompts` and `$DATA/prompts`, front matter (`title`, `description`), at most 64 KB, only files resolving inside their folder; `{{name}}` variables in order of use, `\{{` for a literal; `guard` labels and fences `{{selection}}` and `{{clipboard}}` through `tandem_core::untrusted` (a rule_8 case in `tests/invariants`). `prompts_render` fills `{{branch}}` from the folder's repository and reports what's still missing; the UI supplies the pane's selection, the editor's active file and, only when the prompt uses it, the clipboard. The picker (UI-145, `Mod+Alt+P`, `prompts.open`) is a `QuickPicker`: Enter fills (a form for missing values, with a preview) and inserts into the composer or pane without sending, Mod+Enter edits in the Files editor (`$DATA/prompts` is editable there), "New prompt…" creates from a template, and "Share with the repository" narrows `.git/info/exclude` (D-709). Tests: tandem-core 6 (+1 rule 8), tandem-git 1, app 3, invariants 1, desktop vitest 5, E2E-M17-5 3.

### M17 decisions
- D-700: The extras are a new spec area, EX (PRODUCT_SPEC §4.19), owned by a new milestone M17. They're P2 (differentiators, §0.2), so the coverage rule "P2 only in M16" becomes "P2 only in M16 or M17" (`scripts/check-coverage.sh`, MILESTONES §20). M17 needs only M9's Files editor, browser and git panel, so it's built in parallel with M8 and M10–M15.
- D-701: Checkpoints live in a private bare git store per folder under `$DATA/checkpoints/`, never in the user's repository. Refs in the user's repository (`refs/tandem/…`) would show in `git log --all`, `git for-each-ref` and every GUI client, and get pushed by `--mirror`; an alternates link to the user's objects would save space but break the day the user's own gc prunes an object only a checkpoint still needs. The store keeps its own index so a checkpoint rehashes only changed files, and identical content is stored once across checkpoints. Pruned checkpoints leave the disk at once (`gc --prune=now` in the store, which is ours).
- D-702: The SQLite migration for M17 is `0002_extras.sql`. No other branch had a second migration when M17 started; whichever branch merges second renumbers its file (the list is applied by position, `PRAGMA user_version`), and no released build has run either.
- D-703: The shell reports a command's text on its `133;C` mark as `cmdline_url=<percent-encoded>` (the convention other terminals use for OSC 133), behind the same launch token as every mark. A line starting with a space is never sent (the shells' own "keep out of history" convention), and bash sends its newest history entry only when the history number moved, so bash's HISTCONTROL rules (ignorespace, ignoredups) apply as the user set them: a repeated command bash didn't add isn't recorded again.
- D-705: A selected row's secondary text (ListRow, the thread rail's age) uses `text-fg-muted`: `text-fg-subtle` on `accent-soft` measured 4.3:1 in the dark theme, under AA. E2E-M17-1's axe check found it on the selected thread row.
- D-706: The recall and prompt pickers are a `QuickPicker` with the command palette's shell rather than pages inside `CommandPalette`: their lists come from the core (searched there, not by cmdk), they need Mod+Enter and Mod+Backspace, and the palette's recents and prefixes don't apply. UI_SPEC UI-141/145 say "palette-style picker".
- D-708: Recall's shortcut is `Mod+Alt+J`, not the `Mod+Alt+R` first specified: the Settings › Shortcuts tests that arrived with F-SH-09 rebinding use `Ctrl+Alt+R` as their free combo, and a command already on it made them (rightly) report a conflict.
- D-709: The prompt library's "Share with the repository" replaces `.git/info/exclude`'s `.tandem/` line with `.tandem/*` and `!.tandem/prompts/` (git can't re-include a file inside an excluded folder, so the folder line has to go). Lanes keep F-CD-15's rule (`.tandem/` once) but treat `.tandem/*` as already excluding their worktrees, so a new lane doesn't hide the prompts again.
- D-707: Checkpoints are labelled by what they were taken before ("Before a turn", "Before a prompt", "Before restore", "Checkpoint") with the thread's title or the pane's name; turn numbers aren't shown because a turn that changed nothing makes no checkpoint, so a count would mislead. Commands with secrets keep the redactor's own `[REDACTED]` marker.
- D-704: New UI surfaces: sidebar tabs Checkpoints and Ports (singletons, from the "+" menu), a Settings page Checkpoints (UI-117), a "Command recall" group on Settings › Terminal, palette pages for Recall (`Mod+Alt+J`) and prompts (`Mod+Alt+P`), the Best of N sheet and Compare page.
- D-710: Best of N reuses "Worktree per agent" launching (F-CD-14/15) as it is: each lane shows under its folder in the rail with its own agent pane, rather than all agents in one new tab, so a lane is the same object as any other worktree (diff, merge, discard, PR). Compare is a large Dialog opened after Start and from the palette's "Compare Best of N" (the folder's newest running run), not a page behind a tab chip, since there's no shared tab to hang it on. "Keep this one…" and "Discard all…" ask once, in the native confirm with the F-EX-04 copy built by the core: TL-06 rule 5 makes discarding uncommitted work ask natively, and a second, in-page dialog before it would ask twice; a dirty base refuses before the confirm. The check runs with `/bin/sh -c` (Windows `cmd /C`) and the captured login environment rather than an interactive login shell, so rc-file prompts can't hang it. A lane that failed to start shows its error; there's no per-seat Retry (Discard all and start again). The entry point is the palette only.
- D-711: M17's only change in `apps/web` is one line: "checkpoints" in `DOCS_SETTINGS_PAGES` (`lib/docs/tables.ts`). The /docs settings reference is generated from the app's settings schema, and after merging M15's docs, F-WB-02.2 (every Settings page has a docs anchor) failed on M17's Checkpoints page; the page list is the one hand-kept input. The command recall settings are on the Terminal page, which was already listed.
- D-712: M17's user docs are one page, "Checkpoints and workflow tools" (`apps/web/content/docs/workflow-tools.mdx`, after Code mode), plus a line in Code mode and the privacy page's list of what's stored on the computer; the 0.6.0 release notes are a fragment in `docs/CHANGELOG-0.6.0-M17.md` for the release to take, so `CHANGELOG.md` gets no 0.6.0 section before the release is cut. Both were written against the code: defaults from the settings schema, limits from the constants, labels from `en.json`. Writing them found one claim the code didn't enforce: Best of N in an SSH or WSL folder failed only by accident (a missing repository, or "make a first commit" when the same path is a repository here), so `bestof_start` now refuses non-local folders first ("Best of N works in folders on this computer only"). "Checkpoints aren't available in folders on SSH hosts yet" is D-833's, from the release chain.

### M17 log
- M17 spec: PRODUCT_SPEC, MILESTONES, UI_SPEC and ARCHITECTURE written before any code; `check-coverage.sh` passes with 157 IDs. `trace-tests` found nothing for M17 at first: its `**Owns:**` parser doesn't read `**Owns (P2):**` (M16's line has the same shape and is invisible to it too), so M17's line reads `**Owns:** … (all P2)`.
- M17 tasks 1–2 (checkpoints, recall): E2E-M17-1 failed 3 of 5 on its first run: two clicks landed on toasts over the sidebar (the spec now clicks through the DOM), and axe found the selected thread row's age label at 4.3:1 (D-705, fixed in the app). The bash history parse first matched `[[:space:]]+` after the number, which ate a command's own leading space, so ` echo hidden` was recorded when HISTCONTROL doesn't keep it out; the real-shell test now runs without HISTCONTROL and failed on the old pattern before the fix (the text starts after exactly two spaces).
- M17 merges of `product-m0-m5` (scratchpad; then 0.3.0, M13, shortcut rebinding, invariants): conflicts only where both sides added (the sidebar's panels, registry handlers, Settings pages, the shell's dialogs). The rebinding tests use `Ctrl+Alt+R` as a free combo, so recall moved to `Mod+Alt+J` (D-708). The first full E2E run after the merge failed 51 of 74 spec files with "sign-up failed: 500": the local API's database lacked M13's migrations; after `pnpm db:migrate` and `db:seed`, 74 of 74 passed (about 15 minutes).
- M17 task 3 (ports): E2E-M17-3 passed 3 of 3 on its first run.
- M17 task 5 (prompts): E2E-M17-5's axe check found the Code rail's branch label on the selected workspace at 4.3:1, the D-705 case again (fixed). It also found a real bug: Insert in the fill-in form used the debounced preview, so a click right after typing inserted an empty prompt; Insert now renders with the values as they are (a vitest case pins it).
- M17 task 4 (Best of N): keeping a lane failed in the first unit run because staging passed an empty path list (`ops::stage` rightly refuses one); `ops::stage_all` (`git add -A`) fixed it. E2E-M17-4 found three real bugs: the sheet's seats stayed empty when the engine list hadn't loaded yet (the sheet now loads it and fills empty seats; a vitest case failed before the fix); lane agents never started because a pane's process started only when its view mounted, and lanes sit unseen in the rail (agents now start headless at launch, as restores do); and the UI and the core each asked before keeping, so a person was asked twice (the native confirm is the one, D-710). It also hung on keep's commit in this machine's E2E environment, where the global git config signs commits with a local helper; the spec now sets the repository's own identity and `commit.gpgsign=false`, as E2E-M9-2 does.
- M17 merge of `product-m0-m5` at 039dec5 (0.4.0, M8 Dictate, M10, M11 tasks and routines, M12 plugins, M14 SSH/WSL, M15 NFR): 18 files conflicted, all where both sides added (module lists, AppState fields and constructors, settings keys and specs, the terminal service's fields and methods, i18n, the sidebar's icons, the pty re-exports, a pty test, a rule 8 case); every side kept. M17 still owns the only second migration (D-702). The merged docs test F-WB-02.2 then failed on the Checkpoints Settings page (D-711).
- M17 full E2E regression after the 039dec5 merge: 106 of 114 spec files passed. The other eight: E2E-M14-1…5 need the sshd fixture inside the `with-infra.sh` session and the bundled `tandem-host` (`scripts/build-tandem-host.sh`, which needs `musl-tools`); with both, 5 of 5 passed. E2E-M17-2 found a real test-isolation gap: command history outlived specs (an earlier spec's agent had typed a line into a shell), so the E2E reset now clears it with `history_clear`, as it already clears threads and routines; then it passed. E2E-M17-3 (Cancel in Stop's dialog didn't close it within 5 s) and E2E-M16-2 (a split didn't add a pane) passed on the re-run unchanged; neither failure reproduced.
- M17 full E2E regression after merging 0.5.0 (d1060d3), with the sshd fixture and the bundled `tandem-host`: 112 of 114 spec files passed. E2E-M17-2 read the recall picker's rows the moment it opened, before its list loaded (the spec now waits for them); E2E-M14-5 ("no remote agent spawn") passed unchanged on the re-run, as did the fixed E2E-M17-2.

## M8 checklist (MILESTONES M8)
Built on branch `m8-dictate-onboarding` (from `product-m0-m5`), in parallel with the M10–M14 sessions (D-500). Owns F-DI-01, F-DI-02, F-DI-03, F-DI-04, F-DI-06, F-DI-07, F-DI-08, F-DI-09, F-DI-11, F-DI-12 and F-SH-08; completes F-SH-13 (`dictate/toggle`, the tray), the status bar's "Dictate" segment and F-TH-07 (the composer mic). Decisions from D-500.
- [x] 1. Capture (F-DI-03). `tandem_voice::capture`: `AudioService` keeps one input stream open and paused on its own thread (cpal streams aren't `Send` everywhere); the callback downmixes to mono into an `rtrb` ring, the worker drains it into the take, applies the mic boost (Off/2×/4×/6×/8×, `tanh` soft clip) and resamples to 16 kHz with rubato at the end. A take with no non-zero sample after 1 s rebuilds the stream, then fails over to the backup mic, then reports "Your microphone isn't delivering audio"; a device lost mid-take goes straight to the backup. `devices::choose` never auto-picks loopback or virtual inputs, nor a Bluetooth headset while another mic exists, even as the OS default; an explicit choice wins. `capture_cpal::CpalBackend` is the real mic; `FakeAudioBackend` plays zeros, nothing, a WAV, or disconnects. Chimes are synthesised sweeps played through cpal; "Pause other audio" mutes with `pactl` / `osascript` behind a crash marker. 17 capture/device tests, chimes 1, media 2.
- [x] 2. Local models (F-DI-04). `models::Catalog` is the bundled catalog, now pinned: each file's SHA-256 and size from its Hugging Face LFS record at a fixed commit (D-503; `scripts/pin-models.ts` re-checks). `Downloader` resumes `.partial` files with a range, waits out `Retry-After`, refuses symlinks, and keeps a file only when size and hash match (else deletes it: "Download failed: the file didn't match."). `stt::SttEngine` with `whisper` (whisper-rs: initial prompt from the dictionary, non-speech suppression, entropy 2.4, no-speech 0.6) and `parakeet` (transcribe-rs over ONNX Runtime) features; `EngineSlot` queues a switch until the take ends and keeps the old engine when a load fails. Guards: −50 dBFS silence gate with a softer per-window voiced test, 300 ms "too short", trailing trim, hallucination filter, the 10-minute cap. `scripts/fetch-models.sh` (SHA-checked cache in `~/.cache/tandem-models`) and `scripts/gen-voice-fixtures.sh` (espeak-ng + sox: `hello_world_16k.wav`, `use_tandem_16k.wav`, `silence_2s.wav`). With the real models, "hello world" comes out of both whisper base.en and Parakeet v3 (`--include-ignored f_di_04_2`).
- [x] 3. Dictionary engine (F-DI-09). Four Aho-Corasick automatons (exact/flexible × case), whole words only, leftmost-longest in one pass, links/e-mail/backtick code protected per rule, language and app-profile scope; protected terms wrapped as `⟦…⟧` around a rewrite and the corrected text kept when one changes (`accept_rewrite`, for M13). 10 tests.
- [x] 4. Takes and insertion (F-DI-06). `take::TakeMachine` refuses anything for a take that isn't current. `insert::Platform` per OS (X11 `_NET_ACTIVE_WINDOW` + enigo/XTest, Wayland `wtype`/`wl-copy`, Windows foreground HWND + Alt-tap retry + elevation check, macOS NSWorkspace + AX focus), `FakePlatform` recording every call; the pipeline saves the clipboard, pastes, restores 500 ms later (not on Windows), and fails closed to "Copied". Terminal targets get control characters stripped and Ctrl+Shift+V on Linux. Post-processing: dictionary, then filler/ellipsis clean-up. 13 insertion tests, take 3, post-processing 4.
- [x] 5. Hotkeys (F-DI-01, F-DI-02). `hotkey::Binding` (parse, print, OS labels; toggle refuses modifier-only), `Gesture` (hold arms the mic on key-down and becomes a take at 150 ms, D-502; double-tap latch; toggle; native key-up required; Esc), `Verifier`. Backends: X11 XInput2 raw events (tested for real under Xvfb with xdotool), the GlobalShortcuts portal on Wayland (missing portal → Unavailable with the `xdg-open tandem://dictate/toggle` fallback), a listen-only `WH_KEYBOARD_LL`/`WH_MOUSE_LL` hook on Windows (guarded by `scripts/guards/no-consuming-hook.sh`, a CI step and a test), a listen-only CGEventTap on macOS (self-healing, secure-input pause, Input Monitoring check). Windows and macOS code type-check and pass clippy from Linux (`scripts/agent/check-cross.sh`). The `dictate::Dictate` controller ties gestures, capture, models, the dictionary and insertion together behind a `DictateSink`; 14 controller tests plus `tests/push_to_talk.rs` (fake hold → the WAV → release → real whisper → `FakePlatform` pastes "hello world" and gets its clipboard back).
- [x] 6. Widget (F-DI-07, UI-60). `services/widget.rs` owns a transparent, undecorated, always-on-top window (`?window=dictate-widget`, the same bundle) placed bottom-centre 48 px above the work area, following the cursor's display unless the position is locked; `services/dictate.rs` shows it per "Show widget" (always / while recording, hidden 1 s after insertion / hidden). `@tandem/ui` `WaveformRing` (UI_SPEC §2.30: 48 bars, thinking highlight, error gap, a static thickness ring under reduced motion). The pill shows Listening with a timer (amber "Ns left" from 9:30) and ×, hands-free with the key, Transcribing, "Inserted · N words", and the toasts by code (D-505) with the full diagnostic as the native tooltip (D-512).
- [x] 7. Composer dictation (F-DI-12, UI-68). `Composer` takes a `dictation` prop: the mic records while held (≥300 ms) or toggles on a click, shows the ring, "Listening…" placeholder, ⌥Space hold on macOS; `ComposerHandle.insertAtCaret` puts text at the caret or over the selection, spaced from its neighbours, never through the clipboard. `useComposerDictation(targetId, handle)` wires any composer (threads now; M10 reuses it); the core routes the global key to the focused composer (`dictate_composer_focus`) and sends `dictate:insert`.
- [x] 8. Setup and onboarding (F-DI-11, F-SH-08). The wizard's new "dictate" step (after Accounts) and Settings › Dictate's "Set up" share `DictateSetupBody`: macOS permissions with Quit & Reopen, Recommended / One key / Choose my own, Try it (passes only on a native press and release, `dictate:verified`), a test field, the microphone, the local model download and where text goes.
- [x] 9. History and stats (F-DI-08, UI-65). The sheet: totals (words, speaking time, takes, wpm, time saved vs 40 wpm), FTS search, Copy, Correct… (optionally remembered as a dictionary rule), Add to dictionary, Delete, CSV export with the fixed header, Clear (totals kept), retention, and a 1080×810 share card with totals only.
- [x] 10. Dictionary UI (F-DI-09, UI-64). Words / Corrections / Review / Deleted tabs, the rule editor (phrases, spelling, match case, protect, spacing, language, app profiles), bulk delete, enable switches, the live test box, JSON/CSV import with a preview and export.
- [x] 11. Settings › Dictate (UI-62/108) and the tray. The home card (ready or "Finish Dictate setup", key, engine chip, stats, Try it, History & stats), the model manager sheet (UI-63: progress with speed and time left, verify/load states, Retry, Use, Remove, remove unused), the hotkey status chip, the Wayland deep-link command, the missing-tools banner, and every setting row; the GLOBAL rows in Settings › Shortcuts (D-808); the status bar's "Dictate: {key}" segment; the tray's Dictate status. `HotkeyRecorder` gained opt-in `modifierOnly` and `mouseButtons`. Holding a modifier-only shortcut and pressing another key (Ctrl+Alt+T) cancels the take (D-513).
- [x] Exit. G green on `m8-dictate-onboarding` after merging `product-m0-m5` at ca1e382; E2E 78/78 spec files (E2E-M8-1…8 in `m8-1`…`m8-5`, plus E2E-M5-9 with the new Dictate step). F-DI-05 (cloud speech-to-text) and F-DI-10 (Enhance and Polish) are this session's next slice on M13's clients (ARCHITECTURE §14.11). HUMAN_TODO keeps the manual QA list from MILESTONES M8.

### M8 decisions
- D-500: M8 is built on `m8-dictate-onboarding` in parallel with the M10–M14 sessions; its decisions are D-500–D-599 and its log lives only in this section.
- D-501: Dictate settings follow UI-62 rather than the placeholders M0 shipped: `dictate_mode` gives way to `dictate_latch` (double-tap hands-free) and `dictate_toggle_hotkey` (the optional toggle binding); mic boost is `off`/`2x`/`4x`/`6x`/`8x`; widget visibility is `always`/`while_recording`/`hidden` and style `logo_text`/`logo`/`mono`; "Where text goes" is `paste`/`clipboard`; `dictate_keep_history` and the hidden `dictate_hotkey_verified` and `dictate_widget_position` join. Nothing had read the old keys.
- D-502: Hold opens the mic on the key-down (silently) and only makes it a take at 150 ms, so the first word isn't lost and a shorter tap is dropped with nothing shown, transcribed or stored. A release that beats the 150 ms timer after 150 ms of holding is still a full hold.
- D-503: The model catalog pins each file's SHA-256 and byte size from its Hugging Face LFS record, and each URL to a commit rather than `main`, so an upstream change can't alter what's downloaded (Parakeet's `vocab.txt` isn't in LFS; its hash is of the bytes at that commit). `scripts/pin-models.ts` checks the catalog against upstream; the release scripts' "TBD" guard now passes.
- D-504: Chimes play through a cpal output stream, not rodio: they're synthesised samples, so rodio's decoders would add dependencies for nothing.
- D-505: Dictate toasts cross IPC as a code (plus `app`/`model` arguments) that the UI renders from its catalog, and a technical `detail` for the tooltip; every visible string stays in `en.json`. Details never contain transcript text.
- D-507: Linux pastes with enigo (XTest) on X11 and `wtype` on Wayland; a terminal target gets Ctrl+Shift+V. X11 needs no extra tools, so the missing-tools banner only appears on Wayland (`wtype`, `wl-clipboard`).
- D-508: The Wayland portal only reports the bound shortcut, so Esc can't cancel a take there; the widget's × does.
- D-509: Parakeet runs through transcribe-rs on ONNX Runtime (ort downloads the runtime at build time); the `parakeet` feature isn't built for Intel Macs, where Whisper Turbo is recommended.
- D-511: Dev builds keep only line tables for dependencies (`[profile.dev.package."*"] debug = "line-tables-only"`): with full DWARF for whisper.cpp, ONNX Runtime and WebKit, `target/debug` passed 19 GB and filled the build machines' disk; a clean workspace build is now about 7.5 GB. Workspace crates keep full debug info.
- D-512: The widget's error detail is the pill's native `title` tooltip: the window is 64 px tall, and the OS draws a native tooltip outside it.
- D-513: A modifier-only push-to-talk key held while another key is pressed (Ctrl+Alt+T) was another shortcut all along: the take is cancelled once, as if Esc had been pressed, rather than recording the user's shortcut.
- D-514: E2E downloads models from `scripts/serve-models.ts` (port 8792 since D-817; 8791 before, the local cache) through `TANDEM_MODELS_BASE` (debug builds only); specs steer it through a control file (`normal`, `slow`, or a byte to corrupt).
- D-515: `tandem://dictate/toggle` is honoured only where it's the documented fallback: a Wayland session, or Linux with the global shortcut unavailable (`deep_link_toggle_allowed`, the same rule that shows the command in Settings › Dictate). Elsewhere it logs `dictate.deep_link_refused` and does nothing. Browsers open `tandem://` links (after a prompt the user can tick away for good), so otherwise a web page could start a take and have the transcript pasted into its own focused field. A take the link starts always shows the widget, even with "Show widget: Hidden", so dictation started from outside the app is never invisible. Where the link is the documented trigger it also verifies the setup (F-DI-11): the verifier can only hear keys, so on Wayland without the portal "Try it" would never pass; Try it there names the command to bind instead.
- D-516: Cloud takes (F-DI-05) go to the relay as 16 kHz 16-bit WAV, not Opus: a 10-minute take is about 19 MB, under the relay's 25 MB limit, and it needs no encoder in the app. A Free plan is refused before any audio leaves (the relay would refuse it too, after the upload). A failed cloud take keeps its audio in memory until the next take starts, so "Use local for this take" can transcribe it here; nothing falls back on its own. The consent dialog's answer is the hidden `dictate_cloud_consented` setting (and F-DI-10's disclosure `dictate_enhance_disclosed`). The widget window is now 560 px wide (transparent around the pill) so an error has room for its actions.
- D-517: Enhance and Polish (F-DI-10). Auto-Enhance runs Enhance on every take; formatting instructions without it run as a Polish carrying them, and an app's own instructions (the `dictate_formatting_apps` map, app id → text, ≤2,000 characters each) replace the general ones. Protected spellings go up wrapped as `⟦…⟧` and a rewrite that changes one is dropped for the corrected text. A quick action rewrites the last take typed into another app: it replaces it (undo, then paste) only when that window still has focus, the text was pasted rather than copied, and it isn't a terminal; otherwise the result is copied. An answer identical to the take changes nothing. With the quick actions showing, "While recording" keeps the widget up 4 s after insertion instead of 1 s so they can be clicked. The one-time disclosure appears where the user first acts: inline in the widget for a quick action, as a dialog in Settings for Auto-Enhance or instructions (`dictate_enhance_disclosed`). Free plans are refused before any text leaves.
- D-510: On Windows the Start-menu mask key (0xE8) is tapped when a take starts with Win held, not on its release: injected from inside the hook it could arrive after the Win key-up the shell acts on.
- D-518: A second instance's link is routed once. With its `deep-link` feature the single-instance plugin hands a lone `bin <link>` argument with a configured scheme (`tandem`) to the deep-link plugin, which delivers it through `on_open_url`; the single-instance callback now routes only what that skips (`deeplink::second_instance_links`: more arguments, or debug builds' runtime `tandem-dev`). Before, every such link ran twice: a `tandem://dictate/toggle` where it's honoured (Wayland) started a take and stopped it at once, and sign-in callbacks arrived twice. `CONFIGURED_SCHEMES` is held to tauri.conf.json by a test. The microphone selects use `__default__` for "System default"/"None": Radix shows an empty-string value as no selection, so the trigger was blank.
- D-519: The Dictate widget window is made on first use, not at launch (F-DI-07, NFR §5.1 base memory). Its WebKit web process cost about 122 MB at every launch whether or not anyone dictated (the perf session's bisect: base memory 480 → 604 MB at e1ff9db). `LazyWidget` creates it on the first show (the first recording, or an error), closes it after 10 minutes without one (`IDLE_CLOSE`, checked every 30 s; never while shown), and queues every create, show, hide and close on one thread: a show racing an idle close recreates the window, and neither capture nor the controller waits (capture begins before the Listening event in any case). The first pill after launch can appear a few hundred ms late; the widget page seeds its view from `dictate_status` so a window opened mid-take shows the take. "Show widget: Always" still makes it at launch. `test_dictate_widget` reports `exists`.
- D-520: Dictate slice A (the 14 October audit, D-1121's session).
  - The 10-minute cap and its 30-second warning ran only on the `dictate-keys` thread, which exited when the hotkey backend failed and dropped its sender (a Wayland portal error, no display, an X11 connect failure): exactly when the deep link and the mic button are the way in. The thread now keeps the clock after a disconnect, and each `start_hotkeys` bumps a generation so a restart retires the older thread.
  - `start_take` (the mic button, `dictate_start_take`, the toggle deep link) left the gesture machine Idle, so Esc did nothing and a tap of the hold key re-armed and dropped the take. It now puts the gesture in its hands-free state, as a double-tap does: Esc cancels, the hold key or the toggle stops and inserts.
  - Labels: the tray line and the status bar no longer ask for a model download with Cloud chosen; history's meta says "Cloud" or "Local · <model>" from the take's engine; the home card's Cloud chip names the chosen language; a failed Polish (formatting runs are Polishes) says Polish (`ToastCode::PolishFailed`), not Enhance.
  - Docs (dictate.mdx): the toggle shortcut and backup mic are Settings › Dictate rows, not setup steps; the backup covers an unplugged mic or one silent through the first second after a reopen, and exists only if picked; the link's command shows on Wayland (narrowing it to "portal missing or failed" waits on slice B's live hotkey status); deep-link takes show the widget even with "Show widget: never" (D-515, deliberate); the tray *menu* shows the status.
- D-521: Dictate slice B, a live hotkey status. The service's `hotkey_status` was set only when the shortcut (re)started, so the macOS backend's secure-input pause and a Wayland portal session that died never reached the UI, and `ToastCode::SecureInput` / `HotkeyFailed` had no producer.
  - The Dictate clock thread now reads the backend's own status every 250 ms; a change goes to `DictateSink::hotkey_status` (the service updates its status, the HUD chip and the tray line). Paused toasts SecureInput; a working shortcut (Active or Paused) that becomes Unavailable or NotRunning toasts HotkeyFailed.
  - A restart from the app (a new binding) goes through `Dictate::stop_hotkeys`, which retires the clock thread before stopping the backend, so the gap isn't reported.
  - The toggle link and its command are now the fallback only while the shortcut is down on Linux (Unavailable or NotRunning: no portal, a failed or dead portal session, no display), not on every Wayland session; the setup's link hint follows `deepLinkCommand`. dictate.mdx says so again.
  - "Starting…" stays mostly unseen: the portal's start blocks inside `restart_hotkeys`, before the clock thread exists.
- D-522: Dictate slice C, part 1 (Linux-visible): the widget's Reconnecting state and the home card's last 5 takes.
  - `CaptureEvent::Rebuilt` (the silent stream reopened on the same device) now shows the recording take as Reconnecting with its toast; a new `CaptureEvent::Resumed` (sound after that rebuild) and `SwitchedToBackup` put it back to Listening. Only a shown, recording take changes phase; with none, recovery events show nothing.
  - The Dictate home card lists the last 5 takes (`dictate_history` with `limit: 5`, reloaded as the take count changes); each opens History.
- D-523: Dictionary sync (F-DI-09), Pro and opt-in.
  - `Limits.dictionarySync` (Free off, Pro and Team on; Rust's mirror defaults off). No pricing table lists it.
  - API: `POST /v1/dictionary/sync` takes `{since_rev, changes ≤500}` and keeps the newest `updated_at` per rule, each write taking the user's next rev (one sync per user at a time, an advisory lock). An older change that differs from the stored row is not written; it comes back as a conflict on every sync until the device sends a newer write. `payload.evidence` is dropped. `DELETE /v1/dictionary` forgets the copy on any plan.
  - Desktop: a rule with `sync_rev` 0 changed here (save, delete, restore, import) and waits to go up. A sync sends up to 500 per round with the last rev seen; what went up is marked synced only if it didn't change during the round, and pulled rules never replace a newer local edit. Another account's sign-in starts over from rev 0. The payload is the rule's words and options only (a test asserts the keys); no rule text is logged.
  - Nothing leaves until **Sync dictionary** (`dictate_dictionary_sync`, off, listed in hosted-features.json) is on, the user is signed in and the plan has it; the background loop syncs 3 seconds after a local change and every 15 minutes. Conflicts wait in memory for Keep this computer's (written again as the newest) or Keep the other's. Turning it off asks, and can delete the synced copy.
- D-524: macOS Fn fallback (F-DI-02) and "Press 🌐 key to" (F-DI-11), both behind platform traits and tested with fakes on Linux.
  - Fn fallback: with Fn alone as the hold key, a verify test that hears no native press from it within `FN_GRACE` (8 s) calls `DictateSink::fn_unheard` once. The service switches the hold key to Control+Option (`Binding::fn_fallback`), sets the hidden `DictateFnFallback` to "switched", and `DictateStatus.fnFallback` shows the setup's callout with **Use Fn again**. Picking Fn after that stores "kept_fn", which stops the fallback for good. Picking any other key clears it. The new key is verified on its own.
  - 🌐 key: `hotkey::globe::GlobeKey` (`DefaultsGlobe` runs `/usr/bin/defaults` on `com.apple.HIToolbox AppleFnUsageType`, then `activateSettings -u`; `NoGlobe` elsewhere; `FakeGlobe` for E2E). Reading never writes. `dictate_globe_key_set {doNothing: true}` is sent only from the consent dialog, which names the current value. It first saves that value in the hidden `DictateGlobePrevious` (the first one only, so a second change can't overwrite it). `false` puts the saved value back, or deletes the key if it was never set. Without anything saved it's Invalid, so the app never undoes the user's own choice. Shown in setup (Fn is the hold key) and as a Settings › Dictate row on macOS.
  - The site's dictate.mdx stays Linux-only until a macOS build ships; the on-Mac checks are in HUMAN_TODO.
  - The gate passed: 1,853 Rust tests (7 ignored) and 1,406 TypeScript (desktop 642, API 211), the desktop build, clippy on Linux, Windows and both Mac architectures, fmt and protocol drift (`GlobeKeyView` and `GlobeUsage` added).

### M8 log
- 0.5.0 release-build check (d1060d3, `release-local.sh --dry-run`, after `cargo install cargo-about --features cli`, `cargo-cyclonedx`, `cargo-zigbuild`, `pip install ziglang` and the musl targets in a fresh container): the release binary downloaded Whisper Base, verified it against the pinned SHA-256 and loaded it; Settings › Dictate and onboarding's Try Dictate render; the `dictate-widget` window exists; `tandem://dictate/toggle` is refused on X11 (`dictate.deep_link_refused`). Found: each link from a second instance was handled twice, and the Microphone select's trigger was blank (D-518).
- F-DI-10 (Enhance and Polish): `tandem_voice::cloud::Rewriter`; the controller's rewrite step after the dictionary (the 5,000-character refusal before any call, the raw text and a toast on failure, protected spellings, the Enhancing/Polishing phases) and `quick_action` with `Platform::undo` (Ctrl+Z on Linux and Windows, Cmd+Z on macOS, `wtype` on Wayland). `HostedRewriter` over `auth.with_token(api.ai_rewrite)`. UI: the widget's "Enhance ◈3 · Polish ◈3" after a take and its inline disclosure, "Enhancing…/Polishing… ◈ 3"; Settings rows for the quick actions, Auto-Enhance (with the disclosure) and formatting instructions with a 2,000-character counter and the "Per app…" sheet; Free shows them disabled with "Enhance is included with Pro". Tests: 7 controller, 2 app, 6 UI, and E2E `m8-7-enhance` against the mock rewrite (Auto-Enhance settles 3 credits; a quick action reaches the relay for 3 more and leaves an identical answer alone). With F-DI-10 ticked, the site prices it: /pricing, llms.txt and site-info list "Dictate Enhance and Polish: 3 credits per use" (their tests had asserted it wasn't priced until it shipped). Also made `f_th_03_4` wait for the thread's Failed state, which is written just after the turn end it waited for and raced under the full suite's load.
- F-DI-05 (cloud speech-to-text, after M8 merged): `tandem_voice::cloud` (the `CloudStt` trait, failure kinds with their toast actions, the WAV encoder); the controller sends a voiced take to the cloud when the engine is Cloud, never falls back to local by itself, and keeps a failed take for "Use local for this take" until the next take starts; cloud takes need no local model. `services/dictate_cloud.rs` implements it over `auth.with_token(api.stt_transcribe)` with `HostedFailure`'s words, and refuses Free before uploading. UI: "Transcribe with" (Free opens the plans, Pro asks once), the hints opt-in, the cloud engine chip, toast actions in the widget and the main window. Tests: 6 controller, 2 crate, 3 app, 2 UI, and E2E `m8-6-cloud` against the dev API's mock STT (a Pro take inserts "hello world" and settles 2 credits; a 402 inserts nothing until Use local; Free sends no audio). The widget's new command needed its capability (`allow-dictate-use-local`, and `core:event:allow-emit-to` for See plans).
- M8 exit: the full E2E run showed 18 failing files outside M8; all were the container's API database missing migrations and seed data (sign-up 500, "No user owner@tandem.test"). After `db:migrate` and `db:seed` all 78 pass. WDIO ignores `this.timeout()` inside a test (it reads the timeout first), so the M8 specs set it per suite. OPERATING's E2E lessons have all three.
- M8 tasks 6–11 (app): `services/dictate.rs` (controller, settings, events, history, stats, dictionary, models), `services/widget.rs`, `commands/dictate.rs` and the `test_dictate_*` harness commands; the widget, composer mic, setup, history, dictionary and model manager UIs; 15 desktop and 9 `@tandem/ui` tests named by F-ID; E2E specs `m8-1`…`m8-5` and `scripts/serve-models.ts`. Found on the way: the Files feature already exported a `FileKind` type, so Dictate's is `DictateFileKind`; Settings › Dictate stayed on "Loading" forever when the core answered `null` (the browser demo does), so it now shows an error state and every list load tolerates `null`.
- M8 tasks 1–5 (voice crate): capture, models, dictionary, takes, insertion, hotkeys and the controller, 108 tests. Found on the way: the pinned hash for Parakeet's `vocab.txt` had been taken from a redirect body; `fetch-models.sh` refused the real file and deleted it, and the catalog now pins the real bytes (D-503). The `zsh` package's config prompt stops `bootstrap-linux.sh` on a fresh container; it now installs non-interactively and adds `xdotool` and the MinGW cross compiler.

## M10 checklist (MILESTONES M10)
Built on branch `m10-teammates-gateway` (from `m9-files-git-browser`, which goes into `product-m0-m5` through pull request #4), in parallel with M8 (D-300). Owns F-PL-01, F-AG-01…09, F-AG-11 and F-AG-12; completes F-SH-02 (the Agent surface), F-SH-13 (`teammate/<id>`), F-AC-03 (a teammate's preferred account), F-CD-16 (a teammate as handoff target), F-CD-19 (the teammate Home context) and F-DB-01 (teammate cards). F-DI-12 (the mic in "Create with AI") completes after M8 merges (D-300). This section holds M10's state, decisions (from D-300) and log.
- [x] 1. Gateway (F-PL-01). `tandem-gateway` gains the built-ins registry (`builtins::BUILTINS`: `tandem__session_search`, `tandem__memory_write`, `tandem__read_skill`, `tandem__send_message`, `tandem__list_teammates`, each turned on by a `BuiltinSet` flag, answered by the app's `BuiltinHandler` with the caller's lease), vendor modules (`vendors::VendorModule`: manifest with pinned hosts, tools with risk classes) and the test modules `test_echo`, `test_upper` and the always-unreachable `test_down` behind the `test-vendors` feature. A lease now records its granted connections and conversation. `tools/list` shows the approvals tool, the lease's built-ins and, when it has a connected granted plugin, `tandem__search_tools` + `tandem__call`; "Expose all plugin tools directly" lists each granted tool as `<plugin>__<tool>` instead. A plugin that fails takes only its own tools away (search names it under `unavailable`), and a lease can't call a plugin it wasn't granted ("The test_echo plugin isn't enabled for this session"). A disconnect counts on the next call (F-PL-01 AC4). A watchdog probes the listener every 30 s and reopens it on the same port; `metrics()` reports listeners, leases, MCP sessions, requests, restarts and resident memory. In the app, `services::gateway::GatewayService` starts the one gateway at launch (it was started on the first thread before), writes `$DATA/run/gateway.json`, loads the connections from `plugin_connections` and follows the setting; thread sessions get their grants (the thread's and "all threads"). New store repo `plugins` (connections, grants), commands `plugins_catalog`, `grants_list`, `grants_set` and the harness's `test_plugin_connect` and `test_gateway_metrics` (ARCHITECTURE §3.2 first). The fake Claude gained `mcp <tool> <json>` and `tools`. `cargo test -p tandem-gateway`: 16 (memory after 10 sessions × 20 rounds stays under 80 MB; a killed listener comes back on its port); store `plugins` 1; E2E-M10-8.
- [x] 2. Teammate record (F-AG-01). `tandem_core::teammates` holds the rules: names 1–40 characters, unique by `fold_name` (NFKC, lower case; "Scout" = "scout" = fullwidth "Ｓｃｏｕｔ"), briefs ≤ 8,000, the settings and their defaults (4k memory, Learn Ask, may message on, may schedule off, Edits), the Lead's brief and the avatar hue (`fnv1a32(id) % 12`, the same hash as `@tandem/ui`). Store repo `teammates` (the `teammates` table; a deleted teammate keeps its row for its chats and frees its name) and threads gain `teammateId` / `conversationRef`, `insert_chat` (after the teammate's other chats, so tabs read left to right), `teammate_chats` and `teammate_stats` (working chats, waiting, the latest chat failed, the last thing it said). `services::teammates::TeammatesService`: the Lead is made the first time teammates are listed, pinned, with the default brief and the default chat engine (Claude Code when installed); a deleted Lead isn't made again. Create checks the name, the brief, the Free limit ("Free includes one teammate besides Lead."), that the engine is known and installed (EngineMissing), and makes Home (`$DATA/teammates/<id>/memory/{NOTES,ABOUT_YOU}.md`, `skills/`, owner-only). Terminal-only engines are allowed and say `chatCapable: false`. Delete moves Home to `$DATA/trash/teammates/<id>-<ms>`; duplicate copies brief, engine, settings, memory and skills as "{name} copy"; pinning one unpins the others (one hero); reorder. Teammate chats (`thread_create` kind `teammate_chat` with `teammateId`, `place`) take the teammate's engine, model, preferred account (F-AC-03) and default access, work in Home or a place, and their sessions get a `TeammateChat` lease with the teammate built-ins (mail only with "May message") and the teammate's grants. Commands `teammate_list|get|create|update|delete|duplicate|reorder`, event `teammates:changed` (ARCHITECTURE §3 first). `@tandem/ui` `Avatar` draws from the 12-hue palette, with an optional picked hue (D-302). Rust: core 3, store teammates 2, app teammates 9; ui `Avatar` 3.
- [x] 3. Briefing (F-AG-06). `tandem_engines::briefing::assemble` builds, in order: the preamble (name, date, workspace, access, and a note when the run is unattended), the brief, `<memory>` fenced as data (NOTES.md cut at its cap with a marker, ABOUT_YOU.md at 2k), `<skills>` as an index (one line per valid skill; the bodies stay one `tandem__read_skill` away), the roster when the teammate may message, and tool guidance for the built-ins it has. Memory, skill and roster text is untrusted: credential-shaped strings are redacted, and a line matching the injection pattern (instructions to ignore rules, "system prompt", any opening or closing tag of the briefing's sections) has its angle brackets swapped for look-alikes and is counted (logged by count only). `invariant_rule_8_untrusted_fencing` proves nothing in memory or skills can close or open a fence. Delivery is a 0600 file `$DATA/briefings/<thread>.md`, written for each new session of a teammate chat (`ChatStart.briefing`): Claude gets `--append-system-prompt-file`, Codex gets it as `developerInstructions` over stdin (ahead of Plan mode's own), and ACP agents get it as the first block of the session's first prompt only. `tandem-store` gains the memory and skills file stores the briefing reads (`memory`, `skills`; their commands come with tasks 4 and 5), the six starter skills (original text) and the `skills_seen` setting. Chat-engine spawns now go to `TANDEM_SPAWN_LOG` too, so the harness can check what an engine was started with. Rust: `tandem-engines` f_ag_06 6 (Claude 100 KB by file, Codex developer instructions, ACP first prompt only), store memory 3 and skills 4, app 1; E2E-M10-2 (briefing half).
- [x] 4. Memory (F-AG-07, UI-73). `tandem_store::memory` reads and writes `memory/NOTES.md` (the teammate's cap) and `ABOUT_YOU.md` (2k): a write over its file's cap is refused with "Memory is over its {cap} character budget. Condense it and try again." and one with a credential-shaped line (the log redactor's rules, line by line) with "Memory can't store credentials"; either way the file is untouched. Every accepted write keeps a copy of what it replaced in `memory/.history/<change>.md` and a `memory_changes` row; `memory_undo` puts it back unless the file changed again since. The gateway's `tandem__memory_write` answers for the lease's teammate (`services::teammate_tools`, bound to the gateway as its built-ins handler), as does `tandem__read_skill` (the SKILL.md fenced as data). Commands `memory_get`, `memory_write`, `memory_undo`. UI-73 `MemoryTab` (`features/agents`): Notes / About you, List / Raw, the budget meter "{used} / {cap} characters", notes with a hover × (and Delete on a focused row), Add note (Mod+Enter), Raw with Save / Revert (Mod+S) and "{file} changed outside Vibezeo." with Reload, over-budget and likely-secret states that disable saving (the core's own redactor via `redact_text`), empty, loading and error states. The List view parses top-level `- ` bullets (with their indented lines) and keeps every other byte as it is, so List → Raw → List is byte-exact, CRLF and BOM included. The tab mounts in the teammate page (task 8), where E2E-M10-3 drives it. Rust: store memory 3, memory_changes 1, app 2 (+ `teammate_tools` 1); desktop vitest notes 3, Memory tab 4.
- [x] 5. Skills (F-AG-09, UI-74). `tandem_store::skills` (task 3) validates `SKILL.md`: front matter that parses (key: value lines, quoted strings, folded and literal blocks; lists and objects are refused), `name` kebab-case ≤ 48 and the folder's name, `description` 1–280; an invalid skill is listed with its error and left out of the index. The six starter skills (`concise-diffs`, `test-first`, `release-notes`, `code-review-checklist`, `dependency-audit`, `commit-messages`) are original text in `crates/tandem-store/assets/skills/`, bundled into the binary. Commands `skills_list`, `skill_get` (marks the skill seen), `skill_save` (refuses credentials), `skill_remove` (to the teammate's own trash), `skills_library`, `skill_add_from_library` (Conflict when it's already there). A skill folder the user didn't make, add or open is the teammate's own and shows "New" until opened; with "Allow teammates to write skills" off (Settings › Teammates, a new page) it's listed with the reason and kept out of the briefing. `tandem__read_skill` returns the text fenced as data (task 4). UI-74 `SkillsTab`: rows with name, description or error, New and "Not used" badges, Edit and Remove (confirmed); "Add from library" (a dialog of the six, added ones marked); the editor sheet with Name (kebab-case), When to use, the body and UI-74's checklist (advisory, except "No secrets", which blocks Save); SKILL.md whose front matter isn't the simple shape opens as raw text. Desktop vitest skills 5; Rust as in task 3 (store skills 4).
- [x] 6. Session search (F-AG-11). When a teammate chat's turn ends (whatever its reason), `tandem_store::repo::search::index_turn` puts the turn's user message and the assistant's text blocks into `chat_fts` with the chat's teammate; tool calls, commands, their output and reasoning never go in. `tandem__session_search{query, limit ≤ 10}` searches only the lease's teammate: every word of the query becomes a quoted FTS5 term (all required; no operators, so no syntax errors or injection), results are one per chat, most recent first, `{chatId, title, date, snippet}` with the match in brackets; chats deleted since drop out. One worker delivers the queue oldest first (after a send, a Retry, a change of the pause setting and at launch): an idle chat of the recipient's in the same thread of work (the sender chat's `conversation_ref`, else its id) at no more than Edits gets the mail as a user turn "From {sender}:\n{body}"; otherwise the recipient wakes in a fresh unattended chat titled "{sender}: {first line}" (`title_source` mail) at its default access capped at Edits. A recipient with 20 mail-triggered turns in the last hour keeps the mail queued with "Rate limit reached"; a wake or delivery that fails leaves it failed with Retry, never retried by itself. `tandem__list_teammates` lists the other teammates with engine, status and whether they can take mail.
