### M10 decisions
- D-300: M10 is built in parallel with M8 at the owner's request, although MILESTONES lists M9 (and through it M8) as its prerequisite. M10 leaves the voice code (`crates/tandem-voice`, the Dictate UI), onboarding and `apps/web` alone; its decisions are numbered from D-300. F-DI-12's mic in "Create with AI" needs M8's Dictate, so the card has no mic until M8 merges rather than one that does nothing; it's listed above as completing after M8. "Create with AI" is tested against a desktop API fixture (D-8); the real `/v1/ai/teammate-draft` route comes with M13.
- D-301: Gateway details F-PL-01 leaves open. (1) The gateway starts at launch, not on the first session, so `run/gateway.json` exists as soon as the app runs. (2) A lease's grants are read when its session starts; a grant change marks the subject's sessions stale, so the next turn starts a fresh session (resuming the engine's conversation) with a new lease. (3) The search and call tools are listed only for a lease with at least one connected granted plugin. (4) Plugin approvals (F-PL-06/07) arrive with M12; until then the gateway runs only `read`-class tools and answers every other class with a tool error saying it can't ask for plugin approvals yet. No real vendor module ships before M12, so nothing a user can connect is affected. (5) Settings › Plugins and connecting arrive with M12; `plugins_catalog`, `grants_list` and `grants_set` exist now for the teammate's plugin toggles and nudge, and debug E2E builds connect test vendors with `test_plugin_connect`. (6) Resident memory is reported on Linux (`VmRSS`); macOS and Windows report none until a probe is written for them (the metric is `null`, never a guess).

- D-302: Avatars use F-AG-01's 12-hue palette: `AVATAR_HUES` = 25, 52, … 332 (28° apart, none within 15° of red, as UI_SPEC §1.7 asks), index `fnv1a32(id) % 12` unless the user picked one (`teammates.avatar_hue`, 0–11). UI_SPEC §1.7's continuous `fnv1a32 % 360` hue left nothing for a "pick a hue" control to choose from. The gallery's three Avatar snapshots changed with it (hues only).
- D-303: Teammate record details F-AG-01 leaves open. (1) The Lead is made the first time teammates are listed (not at launch), so engine detection has run and the Lead gets an installed chat engine; a profile that ever had a teammate row never gets a new Lead. (2) The hero tile is the pinned teammate, and pinning is exclusive ("Pin as Lead", UI_SPEC UI-70). (3) A deleted teammate keeps its row (its chats name it "Deleted teammate") and frees its name. (4) The Free limit counts teammates besides the auto-made Lead (`is_lead`), deleted ones excluded. (5) A teammate chat is created by `thread_create` with kind `teammate_chat`; a terminal-only teammate's chat is refused with "Only chat engines carry memory and skills." (EngineUnsupported).
- D-304: Briefing details F-AG-06 leaves open. (1) The date is the machine's local date. (2) Defanged lines keep their text with `‹ ›` in place of `< >`, so the teammate can still read its own note. (3) A memory file over its cap is cut at the cap in the briefing with "[Memory over its budget: the rest was left out.]"; writes through `tandem__memory_write` can't go over. (4) The briefing file is per chat and rewritten whenever the chat starts a new engine session (so memory edits and new skills reach the next session), and removed with the chat. (5) ACP agents take no system prompt, so the briefing opens the first prompt of every new agent session, not every turn. (6) Skills the user made, added from the library or opened are "seen" (the `skills_seen` setting); any other skill folder is the teammate's own and "New", and while "Allow teammates to write skills" is off it's listed with that reason and left out of the index.
- D-305: Teammate settings and creation details. (1) F-AG-05's "May create routines" toggle is absent until routines exist (M11 builds them and the schedule tool; MILESTONES §2 rule 4: unbuilt controls are absent, not disabled). (2) The memory caps are PRODUCT_SPEC's 2k / 4k / 8k / 16k characters with ABOUT_YOU.md at 2k; UI_SPEC UI-76's 3,000 / 6,000 / 12,000 / 24,000 and 1,500 disagree with the feature spec and ARCHITECTURE, which win. (3) UI_SPEC's per-place access select and "Who may I message" list have no backing in PRODUCT_SPEC; places are folders the teammate may work in, and mail goes to any teammate that may receive it. (4) The "Default engine for new teammates" setting isn't added: the sheet starts on Claude Code when it's installed, else the first installed chat engine, the same default as Thread mode. (5) "Learn after each turn" in Settings › Teammates turns learning off for every teammate when off; on, each teammate's own Learn mode decides. (6) Create with AI needs Pro or Team, a sign-in and a connection (F-AG-04); the request carries the description, the installed chat engines and connected plugin ids; the answer is checked before it fills anything.
- D-306: Learn details F-AG-08 leaves open. (1) Only completed turns learn (not cancelled or failed ones). (2) Ask mode's pending suggestion sets live in the running app; a restart drops undecided ones rather than keeping model output on disk. (3) A suggestion set Auto can't write (over the cap) is dropped and logged by code, never applied in part. (4) Engines without a headless mode (ACP agents) don't learn; the follow-up uses the engine's default model when the registry has no `cheap_model`.
- D-307: Mail details F-AG-12 leaves open. (1) Teammates have no pause of their own in the data model, so the "paused" refusal never arises; the global pause queues mail rather than refusing it (AC2). (2) Mail held by the rate limit goes out on the next delivery pass once the hour allows it (a new mail, Retry, resuming, or launch); nothing polls. (3) An idle chat in the same thread of work takes mail only at Read or Edits access; a Full-access chat never runs mail, so the recipient wakes in a fresh chat instead. (4) A woken chat carries the sender's thread of work, so a reply comes back to the sender's chat when that chat is idle. (5) Retry applies to failed mail and mail held by the rate limit; refused mail stays refused. (6) `@name` in a teammate's composer is mail from that teammate (UI-71), so its "May message teammates" applies; `mail_send` without a sender is mail from "You". A teammate can't mail itself. (7) The engine receives "From {sender}:\n{body}" as the turn's message; the transcript shows the mail row in its place. (8) UI-71's "Who may I message" list is left out (D-305 (3)).

### M10 log
- M10 task 1 (gateway): built-ins registry, vendor modules and test vendors, grants on leases, search/call listing, the watchdog and metrics, `GatewayService`, the plugins repo and commands, E2E-M10-8. Found on the way: the gateway was started lazily by the first thread session, so `run/gateway.json` (MILESTONES' verification reads it) would never exist before a thread ran; it now starts at launch.
- M10 task 2 (teammate record): core rules, the teammates repo, teammate chats in the threads repo, `TeammatesService`, commands and `teammates:changed`, the avatar palette. The first gate run of task 1 failed only on the API tests: `with-infra.sh` had started Postgres but it wasn't accepting connections yet; a rerun with Docker already up passed.
- M10 task 3 (briefing): `briefing.rs`, delivery in the three adapters, the memory and skills stores, the starter skills, chat spawns in the spawn log, E2E-M10-2's briefing tests. Found on the way: the memory store's first `write` took its cap from the caller, so a caller passing the notes cap could write a 16k ABOUT_YOU.md; its test caught it, and `write` now picks each file's own cap.
- M10 task 4 (memory): the write checks, the change log and Undo, `tandem__memory_write` and `tandem__read_skill` on the gateway, the memory commands, `MemoryTab` and the byte-exact notes parser.
- M10 task 5 (skills): the skills commands, "New" until seen, the Teammates settings page with "Allow teammates to write skills", `SkillsTab` with its editor and library.
- M10 task 6 (session search): the index on turn end, the scoped search and its built-in, E2E-M10-4. Its first run hit the Free plan's teammate limit in the spec (a second teammate besides the Lead); the spec now uses the Lead as the other teammate.

## M11 checklist (MILESTONES M11)
Built on branch `m11-tasks-routines` (from `m10-teammates-gateway`, since M11 needs M10), in parallel with M10's remaining tasks, M8, M12, M13, M14 and M17. Owns F-TK-01…04 and F-RT-01…04; completes F-SH-10 (the Tasks tab), F-SH-13 (the tray's "Pause routines"), F-DB-02 ("Ready for review" and task-review items), F-BL-04 (routines pause on lapse) and F-AG-05 (the Routines link). Then F-RT-05 from M16 on the same branch. This section holds M11's state, decisions (D-900 to D-949) and log.
- [x] 1. Task store (F-TK-01). `tandem_tasks::TaskStore` keeps the tracker in `$DATA/tasks/`: `meta.json` (next number, projects, labels, the six statuses, actors with the human `me`, the `local` team), `issues/ISSUE-<n>.json` (id `TAN-<n>`, a revision, title, Markdown description, status, priority 0–4, labels, project, assignee, timestamps, archived, result, attempts), and per issue an append-only `ISSUE-<n>.history.jsonl` (one line per changed field: time, actor, MCP connection, from, to) and `ISSUE-<n>.comments.jsonl` (threaded by `parent_id`). Every write takes an exclusive lock file under `locks/` (per issue, `meta`, `idempotency`, `connections`; std `File::try_lock`, 5 s, then `UNAVAILABLE`), so the app and any number of MCP server processes can write at once; issue files are replaced atomically (`tandem_core::atomic`). `save_issue` creates (a title is needed) or patches at `expected_revision` (required for a patch; a stale one is `REVISION_CONFLICT` with the current revision), and a patch that changes nothing writes nothing. Idempotency keys (≤128) return the first recorded result for 24 h without writing again, for issues and comments; older entries are compacted when the store opens. Labels, projects and assignees must exist; `null` clears an assignee or project. `connections.json` holds one connection per engine session with the actor its writes are recorded as; a revoked or unknown connection is `CONNECTION_REVOKED`. `list` filters by status, assignee, label, project and text, newest first, with an opaque cursor at a position (updated, number), pages ≤ 50. Rust `tandem-tasks`: store 9, lock 1.
- [x] 2. MCP server (F-TK-02). `tandem-tasks mcp --dir <tasks dir> --connection <id>` is an rmcp stdio server (`transport-io`) that speaks protocol versions 2025-11-25, 2025-06-18 and 2025-03-26 (a client asking for another gets 2025-11-25), with the instructions text ARCHITECTURE §9.1 gives and the 17 tools of §9.3, in that order, each with a draft 2020-12 input schema that allows no other properties (serde refuses unknown fields too). Outputs are `structuredContent` with a text copy; errors are tool results with `isError` and `{code, message}` (`REVISION_CONFLICT` carries `current_revision`). `get_issue` and `list_comments` wrap descriptions and comment bodies as `{untrusted: …}`; `get_issue` adds the last 20 comments. Issues are also resources (`tandem-tasks://issues/TAN-<n>`). Every call first checks the connection: a revoked or unknown one fails the call with JSON-RPC error -32001, `data.code` `CONNECTION_REVOKED`. At most 8 calls run at once, on blocking threads. `--connection-env <VAR>` reads the connection from the environment instead (terminal agents). In the app, `services::tasks::TasksService` opens `$DATA/tasks`, revokes every connection an earlier run left live, and gives each engine session its own connection (D-901): thread and teammate-chat sessions get the server as `tandem_tasks` next to the gateway (Claude's `--mcp-config` file, Codex's `-c mcp_servers.tandem_tasks.*` overrides, ACP's `mcpServers`), and Claude Code and Codex panes get it in their provider home's own config when "Expose tasks to terminal agents" is on (default), with the pane's connection in `TANDEM_TASKS_CONNECTION`; turning the setting off takes the entry out at the next launch. Connections are revoked when the engine exits, the pane closes, the agent hands the pane back, or the chat starts a new session. The binary is found next to the app (`TANDEM_TASKS_BIN` in debug builds), like `tandem-hook` (D-68). Fixture `tests/fixtures/conformance.jsonl` drives the conformance test. Rust `tandem-tasks` f_tk_02: 4 (conformance at each version: 17 tools, every tool called; an unknown version; revocation; 250 issues in 5 pages with new issues added mid-way); `tandem-engines` 2 (Codex overrides, native config for Claude Code and Codex); app `tasks` 3.
- [x] 3. Tasks UI (F-TK-03, UI-80/81). Commands `tasks_meta` (teammates synced as actors first), `tasks_list` (the store's pages), `task_get` (`TaskDetail`: issue, comments, history), `task_save`, `task_comment`, `task_archive`, `task_restore`, `task_connections_list`, `task_connection_revoke` (ARCHITECTURE §3.2 first; the UI writes as `me`), and `tasks:changed {ids}` from a `notify` watcher on `issues/`, batched 120 ms, so a write by anyone (the UI, an MCP server, another process) reaches the UI well inside 500 ms (D-905). `stores/tasks.ts` loads the meta and every page of issues and refreshes on the event; `features/tasks`: `TasksPanel` (the sidebar's Tasks tab, which only said "arrives later" before, and Agent mode's Tasks page from the rail's new footer and the `tasks.open` palette command): quick add (Enter, into To do), the list grouped by status with ID, title, labels, priority and the assignee's avatar, row × to archive (toast with Undo), "Show archived" with Restore, empty, loading and error states; wide panels show the open task beside the list, narrow ones instead of it with Back. `TaskDetail`: the title edits inline (Enter or blur saves, Esc drops), status, priority, assignee and project save as they change, labels toggle, the description edits with explicit Save / Cancel (Mod+Enter), the result, threaded comments with Reply, and the history. The merge rule (`merge.ts`): unsaved title or description text is a draft on the issue it started from; a newer copy from another writer updates every other field at once, and when it changed the drafted field too the field is in conflict, "Someone else changed the {field} while you were editing." with Keep mine (saves yours on top) and Take theirs; saves always send the revision the draft is on, and a lost race refetches and asks again. The fake Claude gained `tasks <tool> <json>`, which starts the `tandem_tasks` server from its `--mcp-config` and calls one tool. Desktop vitest tasks 9; app `tasks` +2 (ids from file names; a write is announced within 500 ms); E2E-M11-1 (3 tests: an agent's `save_issue` shows within 500 ms; the agent's status change keeps the user's unsaved title and both are saved; the same-field banner and Keep mine; axe in the dark theme).
- [x] 4. Task execution (F-TK-04, UI-82/83/84). `task_attempts` repo (state queued/setup/running/reviewing/in_review/stopped/failed/done, lane, thread, verdict); `services/tasks_exec.rs` `TaskExecService` behind an `ExecHost` trait (`task_host.rs` makes the lane, starts a task thread or an unattended teammate chat with the `tandem_tasks` server bound, removes lanes): Execute needs sign-in and Pro, at most 4 attempts hold a slot and the rest queue with a position; the briefing fences the issue text with `untrusted::fence` (rule 8 case); the agent's progress comments and result go through its tasks server; a finished run moves the issue to In review with its result, then the optional review runs on a different chat engine and posts "Verdict: Approve" or "Verdict: Request changes" (posted from its reply if it didn't comment); Stop during lane creation sets a cancel flag and rolls the lane back, so nothing is spawned; Resume continues the thread with a note, Retry queues a fresh attempt; attempts left mid-way are stopped at launch; sign-out or a plan change stops every run. Commands `task_execute`, `task_attempts_list`, `task_attempt_stop/resume/retry`, event `tasks:attempt` (ARCHITECTURE §3 first), test hook `test_task_lane_delay`. UI: Execute… on the task (`ExecuteDialog`: workspace, runner (teammate or engine), isolation (new worktree by default), access (Edits default, Full only with a checkbox), review, note; the sign-in / Pro callout with Upgrade) and the Runs list with Stop, Resume, Retry and the verdict. App `tasks_exec` 10 tests; vitest tasks 11; E2E-M11-2 (4 tests: assigning spawns nothing; Execute through the UI makes a lane, progress comments, In review with the result and an Approve verdict; a 5th run queues at position 1 and starts when one finishes; Stop during lane creation leaves no lane, no spawn, no worktree).
- [x] 5. Routine model and schedules (F-RT-01). `tandem_routines::cron::CronExpr` parses standard 5-field cron: `*`, numbers, ranges, steps (`*/n`, `a-b/n`, `a/n`), comma lists, month and day names in any case, day of week 0–7 with 0 and 7 both Sunday (`6-7`, `SAT-SUN` and `5-0` all end on Sunday), and the usual OR when day of month and day of week are both restricted. Errors say what's wrong in words ("Step 61 is larger than the minute range (0–59)", "A cron expression has 5 fields …; this one has 4", "The range 5-1 in the minute field runs backwards"). `schedule::ScheduleSpec` (daily times, weekly days and times, an interval of 15 minutes to a week, or cron, plus an IANA time zone) resolves next runs on the wall clock with `chrono-tz` by the documented rule (D-902): a time that doesn't exist that day runs shifted forward by the gap (2:30 → 3:30), a time that happens twice runs once at its first occurrence; intervals run at multiples of the interval since the epoch, unaffected by DST. A schedule that fires more often than every 15 minutes is refused, as is one that never runs (no run in eight years). `words()` turns a schedule into `ScheduleWords` (every day, weekdays, weekends, named days, every n minutes, hourly at :mm, or the cron itself) for the UI's i18n strings, and `describe_en()` says it in English ("Weekdays at 9:00 AM", "Every day at 7:30 AM, 12:00 PM, and 6:45 PM"). `model::RoutineInput::validate` checks the name (1–80), the instruction (≤ 8,000), the teammate, the schedule, and that Full access comes with `fullConfirmed` (the form's "This routine will act without asking."). Rust `tandem-routines` schedule 10: weekdays and weekends with their next runs, spring-forward in New York, Berlin and Sydney, fall-back, the cron table, every refusal message, DOM-or-DOW, Feb 29 and Feb 30, words, intervals, the model.
- [x] 6. Scheduler (F-RT-02). `tandem_routines::scheduler::decide` is the pure rule: paused (by the user or the plan) waits; no next run gets one from now; due within 24 h fires (late when more than 2 minutes after its slot); due longer ago is logged and moved on; a run still going skips the slot with "previous run still in progress". After a fire the next run counts from the slot or now, whichever is later, so any number of missed slots catch up once. Store repo `routines` (`routines`, `routine_runs`, `error_notice_log`): a slot is claimed with `INSERT OR IGNORE` on `UNIQUE(routine_id, due_at)`, so the GUI and a headless pass can't both run it; runs left `running` by a process that went away settle as errors; history keeps the last 100 runs per routine. `services::routines::RoutinesService` ticks every 30 s of the injected clock (a pass at once at start, for catch-up), saves routines (Pro, a live teammate, `RoutineInput::validate`, a schedule that runs), pauses and resumes (from now, no catch-up for the paused stretch), Run now (refused while a run is going), deletes (chats stay), and pauses everything on a plan lapse and resumes on upgrade (F-BL-04, `paused_by_plan`, from the entitlement events). A run checks the teammate (deleted → "The teammate this routine runs as was deleted.", no spawn), the plan, sign-in and the teammate's chat engine, then `AppRoutineHost` makes a fresh unattended teammate chat at the routine's access titled with its name (`title_source` routine, so auto-title leaves it), sends the instruction and polls the chat until the turn ends: Done is "ok" with the notification "Ready for review — {routine}", anything else an error. Failures set `last_error`/`last_error_kind` on the row, with one notification per error type per routine per day. A routine's chat runs with `Origin::Routine { grants_full }` from the routine's own access (rule 4), not the mail cap other unattended teammate chats get. Commands `routines_list`, `routine_get`, `routine_preview_schedule`, `routine_save`, `routine_delete`, `routine_pause`, `routine_run_now`, `routine_runs` and events `routines:changed`, `routines:run` (ARCHITECTURE §3.2 rows updated first). Rust: `tandem-routines` scheduler 3; app routines 9 (fires once at T±30 s with a chat named after the routine; exactly one late run after being closed; a deleted teammate errors with no spawn and one notification; Run now; paused; overlap; Pro, Full and plan lapse; headless runs only background routines; the lock).
- [x] 7. Background runner (F-RT-03). The launchers (first half, above) plus `tandem --run-routines` (`headless::main`): it takes `$DATA/run/app.lock` without waiting (std `File::try_lock`, `app.lock.json` says who holds it) and exits 0 at once when it's held; otherwise it opens the app's services without a window, loads the cached engine report and detects engines, and when the cached plan has background routines runs one pass over the routines whose run mode is background (catch-up included), waits for them (50 min cap) and exits 0; OS notifications go through `notify-rust` (a recording notifier under `TANDEM_E2E`). The GUI's scheduler takes the same lock first (retrying every 5 s while a headless pass has it) and holds it for its life. `routines_background_set` installs or removes the platform launcher at once (Pro; the AppImage path when there is one, else the executable; `TANDEM_HOME` passed on) and `routines_background_status` reports it; saving a background routine installs the launcher when it isn't. `scripts/seed-routine.ts` creates a profile's database with the app's migrations and seeds a teammate and a background routine (`--due-now`). MILESTONES' verification passes (`tandem --run-routines` exits 0; the binary is `tandem`, not `tandem-desktop`). The E2E (E2E-M11-5) comes with the Routines UI.
- [x] 8. Routines UI (F-RT-04, UI-50..53). `features/routines`: `RoutinesPage` (Agent mode's rail footer "Routines" with the active count, the teammate page's header link, the `routines.open` palette command): the header with the background runner chip ("Background runner on" with Turn off, or "Runs only while Vibezeo is open" with Turn on background runs, Pro), the today strip (a 24 h track with done, failed, running and upcoming markers, their tooltips and a now line; hidden under 900 px), a row per routine (status, name, a Full badge, teammate, the schedule in words, Next with the exact time in a tooltip, the last result badge that opens the history, Run now, ⋯ Edit / Pause or Resume / Open last run / Run history / Delete…, and a failure line with its fix: Choose teammate or Run now, and Open run), the row keys (Enter, Space, P, H, Delete, N for new), the empty state with three suggestion chips that pre-fill the editor, loading and error states, the Free upsell and the plan-lapsed banner. `RoutineEditor` (a Sheet with the Esc guard): teammate (or "Routines need a teammate on a chat engine." with New teammate), name, the instruction with the guidance tip and the 20-character minimum, `CronInput`, access with the Full warning and its required checkbox, background runs (Pro), Save and Save and run now (Mod+Enter saves). `CronInput` (UI-52): Daily, Weekly (day toggles, Weekdays / Weekends), Interval and Cron (mono, the field hint and a cheat sheet), the time zone, and the core's preview as you type: the words through i18n (`schedule.ts`, the same rules as `describe_en`), the next 3 runs in the routine's zone and a clock-change note when one falls in the next 60 days; the core's reason replaces the preview when it refuses. `RunHistory` (UI-53): trigger (Scheduled, Run now, Caught up after Vibezeo opened), duration, result, Open chat, the error, All / Failed, older pages and the retention note. `RoutineView.last` gives each row its newest run. F-SH-13's "Pause routines": the `pause_all_routines` setting, toggled from the tray (`tray.rs`: Open Vibezeo, Pause routines, Quit, shown while the background runner is installed), the `routines.pause` palette command and Settings › Teammates; the scheduler fires nothing while it's on and drops the slots that pass. Test hook `test_routines_tick {at}` (ARCHITECTURE §3 first). App tests +4 (pause, `tick_at`, tray); vitest routines 10; E2E-M11-3 (4 tests), E2E-M11-4 (2), E2E-M11-5 (2), E2E-M11-6 (2). The E2E runner puts recording `systemctl` and `crontab` first on the app's PATH and sets `TANDEM_LAUNCHER_ROOT`, and the spec reset deletes routines, turns the background runner off and clears the pause.

The gateway's built-ins gain `tandem__schedule_list`, `_create`, `_update` and `_delete` (`create` takes name, instruction, a 5-field cron, an optional IANA zone and access `read` or `edits`; `update` takes an id and any of those plus `paused`), in a teammate chat's lease only when its "May create routines" is on (`BuiltinSet::for_teammate(may_message, may_schedule)`, now from the teammate's settings in the thread lease and the briefing's tool guidance). `TeammateTools` answers them through `RoutinesService::{made_by, save_for_teammate, delete_for_teammate}`: a routine is the teammate's own (`created_by_teammate_id`, runs as the teammate), Edits unless it asks for Read, Full refused on create and on update, list/update/delete see only its own, and each create or update raises a `routine_changed` notification ("{name} scheduled a routine: {routine}" / "{name} changed the routine {routine}", with the schedule in words). The `tandem-tasks` MCP server ships with the installers: `scripts/build-sidecars.sh <target>` builds it (both arches lipo'd for `universal-apple-darwin`) into `src-tauri/binaries/tandem-tasks-<target>`, and `release-local.sh` and `release.yml` build it and pass `--config src-tauri/tauri.sidecars.conf.json` (`bundle.externalBin`), so Tauri installs it beside the app's executable, where `tasks_bin_from` finds it (`TANDEM_TASKS_BIN` stays the debug override). Vitest `hosted.test.tsx` 4, the registry test sets the plan for plan-gated commands, E2E-M3-9 checks that `routines.pause` runs nothing on its Free profile, app tests +2 (`f_tk_02_*`: found beside the executable; both release paths bundle it).

### M11 decisions
- D-900: The tracker's records have one shape everywhere: the files under `$DATA/tasks/`, the MCP tools and the app's IPC all use the snake_case fields ARCHITECTURE §9.2 and §9.3 give (`expected_revision`, `next_cursor`, …), rather than camelCase on IPC as the rest of the app does, so the UI and agents read the same records and nothing is translated twice. Their TypeScript names carry a `Task` prefix (`TaskComment`, `TaskLabel`, …) so they never collide with other protocol types. A new issue starts in Todo with priority 0 (none); comments don't change an issue's revision, so an agent's comment never makes the user's open edit stale.

- D-901: How agents get the tasks server (F-TK-02 leaves it open). (1) The server is named `tandem_tasks` everywhere (an underscore, since Codex's config keys are TOML). (2) A connection is one engine session: a thread's or teammate chat's session, or an agent pane's run; its writes are recorded as the teammate (teammate chats), the thread's id (plain threads, shown as an agent) or `me` (terminal agents, which act for the user at the keyboard). (3) Terminal agents get it only through provider homes Vibezeo owns, for the engines whose MCP config it knows how to edit (Claude Code's `.claude.json`, Codex's `config.toml`); other terminal engines don't get it yet. Their config is shared by every pane of the account, so it names an env var (`--connection-env TANDEM_TASKS_CONNECTION`, forwarded with Codex's `env_vars`) and each pane's env carries its own connection; a server started without one has every call refused. (4) Engine processes never outlive the app, so at launch every live connection of an earlier run is revoked.

- D-902: Routine schedule details. (1) The DST rule above is the documented one; it holds for cron, daily and weekly schedules alike, and intervals follow real time. (2) Intervals are at least 15 minutes (PRODUCT_SPEC F-RT-01), not UI_SPEC UI-52's 5, and a cron that fires more often is refused the same way. (3) UI-51's "If missed: Skip it / Run once when Tandem opens" isn't offered: F-RT-02 makes a missed run within 24 h run once, late, always. (4) The form's default access is Edits (F-RT-01); the teammate's "Access for unattended work" setting (M10's page) isn't read by routines, which have their own access.
- D-903: Background launcher details F-RT-03 leaves open. (1) The files go under `TANDEM_LAUNCHER_ROOT` when it's set (tests, E2E), else the user's home. (2) On Linux, when `systemctl --user` fails (no user manager, a container), the unit files are removed again and a crontab line tagged `# tandem-routines` is used; installing replaces any earlier line and uninstalling removes only that line, keeping the user's own. (3) Task Scheduler sets no environment of its own, so with `TANDEM_HOME` the task runs `cmd /c "set "TANDEM_HOME=…" && "<exe>" --run-routines"`. (4) A failed `launchctl bootstrap` removes the plist it wrote, so an entry on disk always means it's registered.

- D-904: Scheduler and run details F-RT-02/03 leave open. (1) A routine's instruction is the user's own words and is sent as the turn's message, not fenced as untrusted; a routine a teammate made with the schedule tool (F-RT-05) is its own instruction to itself, sent the same way and marked "created by {name}". Task bodies, which anyone with the files can write, are fenced (F-TK-04). (2) The GUI runs every routine; a headless pass runs only routines whose run mode is background. (3) A deleted teammate's routine stays, with the error on its row (F-RT-02 AC3), not deleted with the teammate as UI-76's copy says. (4) A run that isn't Done when its turn ends (failed, stopped, interrupted, its chat deleted) is an error; a run waiting for an approval keeps waiting. (5) `--run-routines` without background routines on the cached plan (Free, offline) exits 0 without running anything, and a signed-out profile's runs fail with "Sign in to run routines." (6) Run now is recorded as a manual run (a marker in `routine_runs.error`, since the schema has no column) for UI-53's "Run now" trigger.

- D-905: Tasks UI details F-TK-03 leaves open. (1) The UI hears about changes from an `issues/` file watcher (`tasks:changed`), not the `tasks_subscribe` channel ARCHITECTURE first listed: writes come from other processes (the MCP servers), which only the files see. (2) Tasks are one local tracker for the whole app, not per workspace as UI-80's workspace picker suggests (F-TK-01's `$DATA/tasks/` is one store). (3) The list is grouped by status; UI-80's drag-and-drop board, bulk actions and filter chips aren't built (F-TK-03 asks for the grouped list), so the page offers nothing it doesn't do. (4) Agent mode's rail gets a footer with Tasks (and Routines with task 8); while a footer page is open no teammate row is highlighted, and a selected row's age text is fg-muted, since fg-subtle on the accent tint failed axe's contrast check (found by E2E-M11-1, an M10 row style).
- D-906: Task execution details F-TK-04 leaves open. (1) Access Full is offered with an explicit per-task confirmation (PRODUCT_SPEC F-TK-04 allows it when chosen for that task; UI-82's always-disabled Full is the stricter mock) and the unattended session's origin is `Origin::Task { grants_full }`, so rule 4 holds. (2) The review runs on a chat engine other than the runner's (the first installed one) and, when none differs, on the same engine in a fresh session; when the reviewer gave its verdict only in its reply (a `Verdict:` line at a line start), the app posts that reply as the comment; with no such line the run records no verdict rather than guess one. (3) Stop before the session exists is a cancel flag checked after lane creation, which removes the lane: an abort mid-`git worktree add` could leave half a worktree. (4) Attempts are stopped, not resumed, after a relaunch, and sign-out or a plan change stops all runs (Pro feature). (5) `test_task_lane_delay` (test builds only) lets E2E stop a run during lane creation.

- D-907: Routines UI details F-RT-04 and UI-50..53 leave open. (1) Run now while a run is going is refused by the core with F-RT-02's "previous run still in progress" and the row's toast says so; UI-50's "asks first" would offer a second run the scheduler doesn't make. (2) The editor has no "If missed" choice (F-RT-02 gives every routine one late catch-up within 24 h) and no Plugins picker (unattended runs use the teammate's own grants, M10). (3) The interval builder's minimum is the core's 15 minutes (D-902), not UI-52's 5. (4) Each CronInput tab keeps its own values, so switching tabs never replaces a cron expression and nothing asks "Replace your cron expression?". (5) "Pause routines" (F-SH-13) is one setting, `pause_all_routines`, shared by the tray, the palette and Settings; while it's on nothing fires and passing slots are dropped, not caught up (a pause isn't a missed run). The tray shows while the background runner is installed; Dictate, its other reason, isn't built. (6) E2E moves the scheduler's time with `test_routines_tick {at}`, one pass as if the clock said `at`, instead of starting the app on a fake clock, which would also move sign-in tokens and the API's view of time. E2E-M11-4's "closed and reopened" is the first passes at the reopening time: an E2E relaunch comes back signed out (the E2E keychain lives in memory) and routines need Pro; the relaunch path itself (settle, then the first tick) is the unit test `f_rt_02_2`. (7) The rail's Routines count is the active (not paused) routines.

- D-908: Schedule tool details F-RT-05 leaves open. (1) The tool takes a cron expression, not the builder's kinds: every schedule is one, and the row says it in words. The zone defaults to this computer's. (2) A teammate's routine runs as that teammate; an update that doesn't name an access keeps the routine's, including Full when the user chose it in the editor, and a teammate can only set Read or Edits. (3) "Created by" stays when the user edits the routine (the user's edit doesn't take it over), so the teammate can still list and change it. (4) The notification is its own kind, `routine_changed`, in the Routines group of the notification settings; the routine's schedule in words is its body.

- D-909: The download build and plan-gated controls (follow-up). (1) Where `auth.hosted` is false, every "sign in" or "upgrade" promise in M11's screens uses the Upgrade dialog's early-access sentence and offers no button. (2) Plan-gated controls with nothing to act on are absent, not disabled: `routines.pause` gets `EnabledWhen::Routines` and the Settings row is left out, while Open Routines stays (its page explains Pro). (3) The sidecar is in a separate Tauri config passed only by the release paths, because tauri-build checks every `externalBin` exists at each `cargo build`; dev, the gate and E2E keep using `TANDEM_TASKS_BIN`. Built binaries under `src-tauri/binaries/` are ignored.

### M11 log
- M11 task 1 (task store): `tandem-tasks` model, lock files and `TaskStore`, 10 tests; the concurrency test runs ten rounds of two threads saving at the same revision. Found on the way: the container's `apt-get install zsh` stopped at an interactive conffile prompt; `dpkg --configure -a --force-confold` finished it.
- M11 task 2 (MCP server): `tandem_tasks::mcp`, the binary, the conformance fixture, `StdioServer` in `ChatStart` for the three adapters, `ProviderHomes::set_stdio_server`, `TasksService`. rmcp 3.5 negotiates through `supported_protocol_versions`, so the server lists exactly its three versions and falls back to 2025-11-25. serde can't refuse unknown fields through `flatten`, so `list_issues`' filter fields are spelled out in its argument struct.
- Merged `product-m0-m5` (0.3.0, M13, invariants) and M10's memory, skills and session search. Its new invariant tests (rule 1, rule 3) and one threads test were written before M10's `ChatStart`, `GatewayDeps` and `ThreadRecord` fields; fixed in the merge. A first `gen:protocol` after the merge failed on them and left `packages/protocol/src/generated` emptied (the script removes it before generating), so always check its exit status.
- M11 task 5 (routine model): cron, schedules, words and the routine input rules, 10 tests. Its first run caught the weekdays bitmask including Saturday, so `0 9 * * 1-5` read as five named days.
- M11 task 7, first half (launchers): `tandem_routines::launcher` with `LaunchdLauncher`, `TaskSchedulerLauncher`, `SystemdUserLauncher` (with its crontab fallback), `CronLauncher`, the `CommandRunner` trait and a `RecordingRunner` fake; 7 tests (each launcher installs, reports and uninstalls through recorded commands; a failed bootstrap leaves no plist; the user's own crontab lines stay). The headless mode and the app wiring follow.
- M11 tasks 6 and 7 (scheduler, runs, headless): the scheduler rule, the routines repo, `RoutinesService` with `AppRoutineHost`, the routine commands, `headless.rs`, `seed-routine.ts`. Notifications gained `raise_routine` (no window needed: the preferences now come from the store, not `AppState`) and `set_notifier`. The disk allowance filled during the test build (a linker error); `prune-target.py` and deleting `target/debug/incremental` freed 8 GB.
- Merged M10's finished branch, then `product-m0-m5` (M10 merged as #5, M15's site, F-TL-05, Find work). The API's E2E sign-ups failed with 500 until `pnpm db:migrate` ran on the dev database (M13's schema).
- M11 task 3 (Tasks UI): the task commands, the watcher, the tasks store, `TasksPanel`, `TaskDetail`, the merge rule, the rail footer, `tasks.open`, the fake Claude's `tasks` command, E2E-M11-1. Its first run failed two tests for the test's own reasons: WebDriver's `getText()` gave "" for the truncating title spans (read `textContent` instead, OPERATING §5's lesson), and axe found M10's selected-row contrast (D-905 (4)). M10's eight specs pass with the rail changes.

Their first run failed five tests for the specs' own reasons: two WebDriver selectors mixed CSS with `=text` (chain `$(css).$("button=…")` instead), which left the editor open and failed the next two tests behind its overlay, and M11-6 read `.code` off the E2E `invoke` error, which is an `Error` whose message holds the JSON. Axe then found the today strip's now line with an `aria-label` and no role, and a `list` whose items sat inside tooltip wrappers; both are `img`s in a `group` now.
- Merged `product-m0-m5` (0.4.0: M16 voice API, F-SH-05, docs). Clean. The first `gen:protocol` after it ran out of disk and left `packages/protocol/src/generated` empty again; stale copies of the invariant test binaries and rlibs held most of `target/`, so `cargo clean` and a fresh build.

## M16 · F-SH-12 Scratchpad (coordinating session)
Built on branch `m16-scratchpad` (from `product-m0-m5`) by the session that coordinates the parallel milestone sessions, while they build. 16D's Scratchpad only; the rest of M16 is in other sessions' hands. Decisions are numbered from D-800 so they don't clash with theirs.
- [x] F-SH-12 Scratchpad. `tandem_store::scratchpad::ScratchpadStore` keeps each note as `$DATA/scratchpad/note_<ULID>.md`, written atomically (mode 0600), and lists them newest first with a title taken from the first line with words. Four commands (`scratchpad_list`, `_read`, `_write`, `_delete`; ARCHITECTURE §3) run on the blocking pool. `@tandem/ui` gains `NoteEditor` (CodeMirror with the Markdown language the Files editor already loads, `Mod+B/I/U`, list and checklist continuation on Enter, checkboxes that tick with a click) and its formatting commands (`noteCommands`). The sidebar's Scratchpad tab (`features/scratchpad`) has empty, loading, error and populated states: a wide panel shows the list beside the open note and opens the newest; a narrow one shows the list or the note, with a way back. New note puts the cursor in it, Delete asks first, and a failed save keeps the text and offers Try again. The browser demo keeps notes in the page with the same rules (`demo/notes.ts`, as D-204 does for files). Tests: store 4 (`f_sh_12_*`), `NoteEditor` 5, panel and store 5, demo 1, E2E-M16-5 (F-SH-12.1 with a real `kill -9` and timestamped typing, F-SH-12.2).
- M10 tasks 7–8 (rail and page): `AgentRail`, `TeammatePage`, `ChatView` shared with Thread mode, `thread_reorder`, `teammate_suggestions`, the interim surface removed. `product-m0-m5` (Scratchpad, D-804) merged in.
- M10 task 9 (settings and creation): `NewTeammateSheet` with Create with AI and templates, `TeammateSettings` with the Save bar and guard, `teammate_draft_with_ai`, `teammate_templates`, the `teammate.new` palette command. The Upgrade dialog's gate text also covered only workspaces and panes (a thread-grid gate read as the panes one); it now names thread grids and teammates.
- M10 task 10 (learn): `tandem_engines::learn`, `LearnService` on the turn sink, `memory_suggestions_resolve`, the suggestion card and note, headless spawns in the spawn log, E2E-M10-2's learning tests.
- M10 task 11 (mail): the mail repo, `MailService` with its delivery worker and loop guard, the two mail built-ins, `ThreadService::send_mail` and `note_mail`, the mail commands and event, mail rows with their dialog, `@name` in the composer, the pause switch, banner and menu, E2E-M10-5. `product-m0-m5` (Vercel deploy fixes) merged in.
- M10 E2E: E2E-M10-1 (Lead pinned, Create waits for Recheck, "Name already used", the avatar after a relaunch), E2E-M10-3 (memory cap and credentials through the agent's tool, List → Raw → List, a library skill, an invalid skill left out of the briefing), E2E-M10-6 (May message off, tab order after a relaunch, the nudge's Enable, a new engine) and E2E-M10-7 (Create with AI from the fixture, `TANDEM_FIXTURE_TEAMMATE_DRAFT` in `e2e/run.ts`). Found on the way: (1) the New teammate sheet and the teammate's Settings tab never listed the engines themselves, so after a launch straight into Agent mode the engine grid was empty and Create could never enable; both now load the list when nothing has (vitest create 6). (2) On Free at the limit, a taken name was reported as the limit; the name is now checked first. (3) Connecting a plugin didn't reach an open teammate page; connections now publish `plugins:changed` (ARCHITECTURE §3, F-PL-03) and the nudge follows it. (4) Specs share one profile, so the harness's reset now also removes teammates and their chats and puts the Lead and the mail pause back. (5) This container's dev database had never been migrated; `pnpm db:migrate` and `db:seed` fixed sign-up for E2E-M10-7 (environment only).
- F-AG-08.2 and F-AG-12.4 also have focused Rust tests (`f_ag_08_2_*`: the headless args carry no MCP config; `f_ag_12_4_*`: a mail run's access is capped at Edits), so `scripts/trace-tests.ts --milestone M10` finds every criterion.
- `product-m0-m5` merged in again (M13's hosted services and Team, M15's invariants, M16's Auto, usage, SSO, Shortcuts and 0.3.0). M13 brought the real `/v1/ai/teammate-draft` and its client method, so M10's stand-in method is gone and `teammate_draft_with_ai` calls M13's (the answer is still checked by `sanitize` before it fills anything); M13's wire type is exported to the protocol as `ApiTeammateDraft`, since `TeammateDraft` is the app's checked draft the sheet uses. `AppLauncher::bind` (the gateway's thread binding, moved into `GatewayService::bind_threads` in task 1) is gone from M13's wiring; M15's invariant tests build `GatewayDeps` and `SessionDescriptor` with their constructors and `ChatStart` with `briefing: None` (assertions unchanged). E2E-M2-1 takes `product-m0-m5`'s own fix for the spec-order problem (`freshSignedOut`) in place of M10's.
- Rule 8 (F-TL-06, with `product-m0-m5` merged in again for M15's data controls and the demo font fix): teammate-sourced prompt content now goes through `tandem_core::untrusted`. The briefing's memory, skills index and roster each open with `untrusted::label` (in place of M10's own "reference data" sentence), as do `tandem__read_skill`'s answer and the learn prompt; a teammate's mail reaches the recipient as "From {sender}:" then the label and the body in `untrusted::fence` (the user's own mail stays plain). Found on the way: the learn prompt's `<user>`, `<assistant>` and `<learn_request>` tags and `read_skill`'s `<skill>` weren't in the defang list, so a turn or a skill could have closed them; they are now. `tests/invariants/tests/rule_8.rs` gains three cases (briefing, learn prompt, mail); engines learn +1.

## M14 checklist (MILESTONES M14)
Built on branch `m14-remote-ssh-wsl` (from `m9-files-git-browser`), ahead of M10–M13 (D-400). Owns F-RM-01 and F-RM-02; completes F-CD-01 (SSH and WSL workspaces), F-EN-01 (detection per host), F-AC-01.3 (remote spawns get no provider homes), F-CD-06 (WSL drops), Settings › Remote (UI-111) and UI-14. This section holds M14's state, decisions (from D-400) and log.
- [x] 1. `tandem-host` (F-RM-01). The crate is a library (`tandem_host`) and the binary (D-401). `protocol`: newline-delimited JSON-RPC 2.0, `PROTOCOL = 1`, `host.hello` refuses another protocol. `daemon` (Unix): one per user and version on `~/.tandem-host/run/daemon-<version>.sock` (0700 folder, 0600 socket); `serve` pipes a connection to it, starting it under `setsid` when absent. It owns the PTYs (portable-pty): `pty.spawn` (an argv, or the login shell with a command first), `write`, `resize`, `kill` (SIGHUP to the group, SIGKILL 2 s later), `attach{from_seq}` (output numbered by byte offset, 1 MB kept, an exit missed while nobody watched delivered on attach) and `list`; with no client it waits 10 minutes, then hangs up every PTY and exits (D-402). `fs.list/read/write/stat/create/rename/delete` go through `contain()` and then `tandem_core::files` (conflicts by hash, line endings and BOM kept, atomic replace; git's own files never written; raw `data_b64` writes create new files only, for attachments); `fs.dirs` lists folders for the picker. `git.status` (porcelain v2, the repository's top folder with it), `git.diff` and `git.op` (stage, unstage, discard, commit) run the host's git, reads hardened as locally: no fsmonitor, hooks, credential helpers, pager, external diff or textconv, and the repository's own filter drivers emptied (`f_rm_01_a_repo_filter_driver_never_runs_on_reads`). `engines.detect` asks the login shell `command -v` once for every engine and runs each found one's version command (5 s). `run` starts a thread's engine (D-403). The status relay takes hook reports on `status-<version>.sock` and passes them to the pane's client unverified; the desktop verifies (D-406). `env::allowed` is the spawn-env allowlist both sides apply. `manifest`: the bundled binaries' SHA-256 and the platform for `uname -sm`. `ssh`: the transport (task 2's core lives here so the integration test drives the same code, D-401). `cargo build -p tandem-host --release --target x86_64-unknown-linux-musl` gives a 3.4 MB static-pie binary; linux-aarch64 builds statically here with cargo-zigbuild too (not run: no aarch64 machine). CI (`ci.yml` job `tandem-host`, release.yml before `build`) builds linux-x86_64, linux-aarch64 and macos-universal and the release bundles them with `manifest.json` into the app's resources. `cargo test -p tandem-host`: 29 pass.
- [x] 2. SSH transport (F-RM-01). `services::remote::RemoteService` keeps one connection per host: the master (`tandem_host::ssh`), `tandem-host serve` on it and a JSON-RPC client. Connecting (`ssh_connect` → op, progress on `remote:ssh`): `ssh -G` resolves the target (aliases from `~/.ssh/config`, or `user@host:port`); a host with no pinned key is scanned and its strongest key's fingerprint shown in a dialog, and only an approved key is pinned (in `$DATA/ssh/known_hosts` and `hosts.host_key_fp`); then the master, the helper's install (uploaded when its version differs, checked with SHA-256 on the host), `serve` and the protocol handshake. Errors keep the spec's categories as the `IpcError` stage (`unreachable`, `host_key`, `key_file`, `sign_in`, `folder_missing`, `unsupported_arch`, `install_failed`). A changed key is refused ("Host key changed for {host}. Vibezeo won't connect until you verify it.", with the pinned fingerprint); "Trust new key…", after a danger confirm, forgets the pinned key (`ssh_host_reset_key`) so the next connection shows the new fingerprint for approval. Password and passphrase prompts go through the askpass helper (`services::askpass`): ssh runs the app's own executable, which asks a loopback endpoint with the attempt's one-time token, and the dialog's answer goes back to ssh's pipe only (never stored or logged). Remote panes are ordinary `PtyManager` sessions with a remote backend (`tandem_pty::remote`: output and exit through a feed; writes, resizes and kills to the host; a remote PID is never signalled locally, and quitting ends remote panes through their host); `TerminalService` starts a remote workspace's panes on its host with only the allowlisted env (agents run the host's detected engine with the host's own sign-in; remote agents start fresh, never Resume). When the connection drops each remote pane shows "Reconnecting…"; the service reconnects with backoff for up to 10 minutes (as long as the host keeps the PTYs), re-attaches each PTY from the last byte it showed and says "Reconnected" (a PTY the host no longer has ends its pane). Hook reports relayed by the host are applied only with the pane's own secret (`StatusServer::accept_relayed`). Commands `ssh_hosts_list`, `ssh_host_add`, `ssh_host_remove`, `ssh_connect`, `ssh_hostkey_decide`, `ssh_askpass_reply`, `ssh_disconnect`, `ssh_list_dir`, `ssh_host_reset_key`; `engines_list`/`engines_detect`/`engines_report` take a host; `workspace_add` takes `kind` and `hostId` (ARCHITECTURE §3.2 first). UI: Settings › Remote (UI-111 with UI-14's list, D-409): hosts with `user@host:port`, workspaces and state (Connected / Connecting… / Installing helper… / Reconnecting… / Error), Connect, Disconnect, Remove, "Open folder…"; the "Add host" sheet (Name, Host, User, Port, "Use my SSH agent / config" or "Key file…"); the host-key and password dialogs; the remote folder picker; the rail's "Remote hosts" and a cloud glyph with "Remote · {host}" on remote workspaces; a remote workspace's pane picker lists the host's engines. Rust: `tandem-store` hosts 1, `tandem-pty` remote 2, `tandem-engines` relay 1, app remote 2 + askpass 2, and `f_rm_01_app_connect_terminal_and_reattach_after_a_drop` against the fixture (connect with the fingerprint prompt, `echo hi-$$`, no providers in `env`, `ssh -O exit`, "Reconnecting…", "Reconnected", the same PID); desktop vitest `features/remote` 9; E2E-M14-1 (3 tests), E2E-M14-2 (2), E2E-M14-4 (1).
- [x] 3. Remote workspaces (F-RM-01, F-CD-01, F-EN-01, F-AC-01.3). `workspace_add {kind: "ssh", root, hostId}` asks the host for the folder (`fs.dirs`, so it exists and its real path is stored); the rail shows remote workspaces with a cloud glyph and "Remote · {host}", never as missing, and their first tab opens a shell on the host. Panes and agent panes run on the host (task 2); the pane picker of a remote workspace lists the host's engines ("On {host}"), from the detection that runs after each connection or on its own Check again. Files: every `fs_*` command with a remote context (the workspace, or a thread in it) goes to `tandem-host` (`services::remote_files`), with git badges from the host's status; the host's files aren't watched (D-411). Git: the Changes view's status, diffs, stage, unstage, discard and commit run the host's git (a commit returns its sha); the branch chip of a remote folder says push, pull and branches run in a terminal on the host (D-411). Threads: a thread in a remote workspace runs the host's engine through `tandem-host run` over the host's connection (`ChatStart.relocate`, D-403), with the host's own sign-in and no gateway; the org's engine policy and access clamp apply as locally (M13's guard); a new thread there picks one of the host's engines. Local files attached to a remote thread are copied to `<root>/.tandem/attachments/<thread>/` on the host (and `.tandem/` goes in the host repository's `info/exclude`, as locally), and the engine gets that path; files dragged from the host's own Files tab are referenced where they are. The Changes panel of a remote thread says it follows local folders. The `tandem-host` copy on a host is matched by its SHA-256 (not its version), and its daemon's sockets by build (D-412). Invariants (#19): rule 1 (a remote thread's command line carries the same flags), rule 2 (a host's output makes no marks; a relayed report needs the pane's secret) and rule 6 (the host's git reads run no repository helper) gained remote cases. Rust: host `gitops` 3, invariants rules 1/2/6 +4; desktop vitest `changes` +1; E2E-M14-3 (2 tests: `../../etc/passwd` and `/etc/passwd` refused; list, read, save and the host's git status), E2E-M14-5 (2 tests: the + menu lists the host's `claude` and an agent pane runs it; a thread in the remote workspace answers, writes into the host's folder, and gets a local attachment as a copy on the host).
- [x] 4. WSL (F-RM-02). `tandem_core::wslpath` decodes `wsl.exe`'s output (UTF-16LE with or without a BOM, or UTF-8), parses `-l -q` and `-l -v` (the `*` default, WSL 1 or 2), checks a distro name before it reaches `-d`, builds `wsl.exe -d <d> --cd <path> [-- argv]` and translates paths both ways (`C:\x y` ↔ `/mnt/c/x y`, `\\wsl$\<d>\…` and `\\wsl.localhost\<d>\…` → the Linux path, other Linux paths → `\\wsl.localhost\<d>\…`). `services::wsl::WslService` runs `wsl.exe` through the `WslSystem` trait (`WslExe` behind `cfg(windows)`, no console window; `FakeWsl` in tests; none elsewhere, so `wsl_distros` is `Unavailable` with stage `wsl`). `workspace_add {kind: "wsl", root, wslDistro}` checks the distro is listed and the folder exists in it (`cd -- "$1" && pwd -P`, the path an argument, never part of the script), stores the real Linux path and a `hosts` row per distro, and detects the distro's engines in its login shell (the shared `tandem_host::detect` script), kept as that host's report; "Check again" detects again. Panes run `wsl.exe -d <d> --cd <folder>` (an agent first through `sh -lc`, its path from the distro's report, the org's engine policy checked) with `WSLENV` naming only Vibezeo's pane, status and shell-integration variables; drops into a WSL pane become Linux paths (F-RM-02.2). Threads use `ChatStart.relocate`: `wsl.exe -d <d> --cd <folder> -- env K=V … <engine> <args>`, with nothing of Windows' environment beyond what `wsl.exe` needs and an empty `WSLENV` (D-413). Files, the Changes panel, mentions and thread file reads use `\\wsl.localhost\<d>\…`. Settings › Remote has a WSL section (distros with their WSL version and the default; "Open WSL folder…"; "Plain terminals in local folders open in": Windows or a distro, saved as `wsl_default_distro`, which then opens a local workspace's plain terminals in that distro at the translated folder); it is hidden where `wsl_distros` says WSL isn't available. The + menu labels a distro's engines "In Ubuntu"; the rail says "WSL · Ubuntu". Tests: `cargo test -p tandem-core wslpath` (6), `cargo test -p tandem-desktop --lib f_rm_02` (8: distros, folder check, detection, panes, threads, plain terminals, drops), `wsl.test.tsx` (6). WSL itself can't run in this Linux container: the manual pass on Windows is in HUMAN_TODO ([manual] F-RM-02.1: Ubuntu with Claude Code installed only inside WSL shows "Claude Code (in Ubuntu)" and runs there).
- [x] 5. Infra (D-10, D-407). `infra/ssh` (an Ubuntu 24.04 image with OpenSSH and git, user `tandem`, key-only sign-in, a git repository at `/home/tandem/project`, host keys made at start) is the `sshd` compose service under profile `remote` on port 2222; `scripts/gen-ssh-fixture.sh` makes the git-ignored key `infra/ssh/id_ed25519`. `cargo test -p tandem-host --features e2e-ssh -- --include-ignored f_rm_01` runs against it: connect (an unknown host is refused until its key is approved), upload and verify the helper, a terminal running `echo hi`, a pinned key that differs refused as `host_key`, `../../etc/passwd` refused as `PATH_ESCAPE`, no `.tandem/providers` in the remote spawn's `env`, `ssh -O exit` then the same PTY and PID re-attached, and host engine detection plus an engine run through `tandem-host run`: 6 pass.
- [x] Exit (local). G green on a tree with `product-m0-m5` merged in (through F-TL-05, #26). The MILESTONES M14 verification: the `sshd` fixture up, the musl `tandem-host` built and its 29 tests, the SSH integration test 6/6 (`--features e2e-ssh`), `wslpath` 6/6 and `f_rm_02_2` 2/2, `m14-*.e2e.ts` 5/5 spec files, and `~/.tandem-host` present on the fixture. The full E2E regression: 78 of 78 spec files (215 tests). `scripts/trace-tests.ts --milestone M14 --fail-on-missing` passes.
- [ ] Exit (CI). `tandem-host` built for linux-x86_64, linux-aarch64 and macOS universal in CI, and the WSL translation tests on Windows CI. Both are in the workflows (`ci.yml` job `tandem-host` and `test-rust` on `windows-2022`, by hand since D-1009) but GitHub Actions can't run until about 1 November; HUMAN_TODO has them with the manual WSL pass, real macOS and aarch64 hosts, and codesigning the macOS binary.

### M14 decisions
- D-400: M14 is built ahead of M10–M13 at the owner's request, though MILESTONES lists M13 as its prerequisite. It needs only terminals (M3), threads (M7) and Files and git (M9). Nothing M10–M13 adds is assumed: plugin credentials are never forwarded holds trivially until M12 adds plugins, and `tandem_host::env`'s test `f_rm_01_plugin_credentials_never_cross` lists the plugin and gateway variable names that must never reach a host, for M12 to extend. Decisions are numbered from D-400; M10, built at the same time, owns the gateway.
- D-401: `tandem-host` is a library and a binary. The library holds what the desktop shares with the host (the protocol, the client, the env allowlist, the bundled-binary manifest) and the SSH transport, so `crates/tandem-host/tests/ssh.rs` drives the same connect, install and reattach code the app uses. `tandem-core`'s OS keychain (`keyring`, which needs D-Bus on Linux) moved behind a default feature, `os-keychain`, which only the desktop turns on; the workspace dependency is declared without default features. Without it the host links statically for musl.
- D-402: The session daemon does all the work and `serve` is only a pipe to it, so every connection reaches the same PTYs and nothing is lost when one connection drops. Its socket names carry the host's version (`daemon-<version>.sock`, `status-<version>.sock`) so an upgraded helper never talks to an older daemon still lingering. Output offsets are bytes of the PTY's stream; a re-attach replays from the client's last byte, so nothing shows twice. An exit is held until a client sees it. A client that stops reading (4096 queued lines) is dropped rather than stalling every PTY.
- D-403: Thread engines on a host run as `ssh … -- exec ~/.tandem-host/1/tandem-host run --cwd <root> [--env K=V]… -- <argv>` over the host's master connection, instead of `proc.*` methods on the JSON-RPC channel: the chat adapters keep their own process handling unchanged (stdio lines, the 3.5 s stop: closing the channel closes the engine's stdin, and `run` ends its process group 3 s later). `run` starts the engine in the remote folder with the host's own environment plus the allowlisted variables.
- D-404: The SSH master is started on its own (`-M -N -f`, stderr to a file) and every other command joins it with `ControlMaster=no`: a master forked by `ControlMaster=auto` keeps its caller's stderr pipe open, so the first command would never finish. Commands run with `BatchMode=yes` unless the askpass helper is configured, never forward the agent, X11 or ports (`ClearAllForwardings=yes`), and leave Vibezeo's `TANDEM_*` variables behind. Host keys: `ssh -G` resolves the target, `ssh-keyscan` reads the keys, the strongest is shown (ed25519, then ECDSA, then RSA) and, once approved, pinned alone for that host in `$DATA/ssh/known_hosts` (`UpdateHostKeys=no`, no global file).
- D-406: Hook reports from remote panes go through the daemon's status socket to the desktop, which checks their signature with the pane's secret just as for local hooks (the host never decides what's genuine). This build doesn't upload `tandem-hook` to hosts, so remote agent panes report their status from the terminal (titles, waiting patterns, output activity, which run on the desktop on the relayed output); the relay is in place for the hook.
- D-407: The `sshd` fixture is an Ubuntu 24.04 image (the dev machines' own, so a test can copy a binary built here into it) built from `infra/ssh`, with host keys made at container start so E2E-M14-2 can change them. In agent containers whose only way out is an HTTPS proxy, `scripts/agent/build-sshd-image.sh` builds the same image with apt over https through it; the dev machine needs `openssh-client` (`ssh`, `ssh-keygen`, `ssh-keyscan`), which `scripts/bootstrap-linux.sh` now installs (with `musl-tools` for the host binary).
- D-409: Remote hosts live in Settings › Remote (UI-111), which holds UI-14's full list (rows, Add host sheet, errors, folder picker); the Code rail's footer "Remote hosts" opens it, rather than a separate page in the main area. Adding a host connects it at once ("Save and connect"), which is UI-14's "Test connection" and "Install helper" in one step: the dialog asks only what needs the user (the host key, a password). The stored `ssh_target` carries the user (`user@host`), as the schema's comment says, and the port is its own column. Removing a host is refused while it has workspaces.
- D-410: A remote pane is a session in the app's own `PtyManager` fed by the host, so everything that reads a pane (scrollback, the side channel, marks, status heuristics, handoff tails, the view) works unchanged. The app never signals a remote PID locally: kills go to the host and quitting asks the host to end them. Remote agents never resume (no hooks report a session id from the host, D-406), so Resume on a remote agent card says they start fresh.
- D-411: What a remote folder doesn't do yet, said where it shows: ~~the host's file system isn't watched~~ (watched since D-1600), and ~~the Changes view's branch chip says push, pull and switching branches run in a terminal on the host~~ (run by the host's git since D-1601). The Changes panel of a remote thread (its diff since the first turn) says it follows local folders; the thread's Files tab shows the host's git changes. A remote thread's images become references to their copy on the host, so every engine reads them there.
- D-412: The helper on a host is this build's only if its SHA-256 matches the bundled one (`sha256sum`, else `shasum -a 256`), so a different build of the same version is uploaded again; the daemon names its sockets by version and the start of its own SHA-256, so a new helper never talks to a lingering older daemon.
- D-408: Remote engines never reach the local gateway's port: ~~plugins are local-only for remote threads~~ (relayed since D-1604, still with no port), and the transport forwards no ports.
- D-413: A WSL distro is treated as a host, not as a local shell: its engines are its own (detected inside it, signed in inside it), and a thread or agent in a WSL workspace runs there through `wsl.exe` with none of Windows' provider homes, accounts or gateway, as on an SSH host (D-408). "Run agents inside WSL" is therefore a property of the workspace (open the folder as a WSL folder), and the one global switch is which shell a local folder's plain terminals open in (`wsl_default_distro`). Files are read from Windows over `\\wsl.localhost` rather than through a helper in the distro, since Windows can reach them directly.
- D-414: Before SSH workspaces ship (0.5.0), the coordinator's audit of the merged tree found five gaps, decided as follows. (1) Release builds carry `tandem-host`: `release-local.sh` builds Linux x86_64 and aarch64 (cargo-zigbuild cross-builds here) before `tauri build` and checks every bundle with `scripts/check-host-bundle.sh` (`Manifest::verify`); the Linux download names Linux hosts only, since a macOS helper needs a Mac (`release.yml` builds and checks all three). (2) Claude threads on a host or in WSL ask permission in band (`--permission-prompt-tool stdio`, `can_use_tool` control requests), so they get the approval card without any port to the gateway; before, anything Claude asked about was refused with no card. (3) Remote folders don't offer what they can't do: Reveal is hidden for SSH (WSL reveals its `\\wsl.localhost` path), worktrees say they aren't available in remote folders (the launch backend refuses them too), the launch sheet lists the host's engines and the backend checks them there, and the host's Browser tile says it browses from this computer. (4) Settings › Remote shows WSL on Windows only. (5) Hosts reached through ProxyJump or ProxyCommand are refused at `ssh -G`, before any key scan, with words that say so; supporting them waits on scanning the key through the jump.
- D-1600: A host's folder is watched by its helper, not polled by the desktop: `fs.watch` runs the desktop's own watcher (`tandem_core::files::watch`, inotify per kept folder, the same 500 ms batches and git-dir rule) in the daemon and relays each `FsChange` as `fs.changed` to the connection that asked, so the Files tab and the Changes view take remote batches exactly as local ones. A watch belongs to its connection (it ends when the connection closes, so a dropped desktop leaves nothing watching), at most 32 per connection for the host's inotify budget; one helper watch serves every subscriber of the same folder. After a reconnect the desktop watches again and sends each subscriber one "read everything" batch, since what changed during the drop isn't known.
- D-1601: Push, pull, publish, switch and new branch in a remote folder run the host's own git, as the host user, with the host's config, hooks and credential helpers (its SSH keys, its stored tokens): the desktop sends only the action and the branch name, and nothing of this computer's (keys, agent, credentials) is forwarded. They follow the local rules and wording (`tandem_git::ops`): publish goes to `origin` or the only remote, a remote branch is switched to by its tracking branch, names go through `git check-ref-format`, no prompt can open (`GIT_TERMINAL_PROMPT=0`, no tty), and a network call stops after 120 s. A host whose remote needs a password or a passphrase therefore says to push once from its terminal so the credentials are stored there, as locally.
- D-1602: Worktree lanes in an SSH folder live on the host, where the agents run: `lane.*` in the helper makes them under `<repo>/.tandem/worktrees/<slug>` on `tandem/<slug>` with the same slug, exclude, merge, diff and stale rules as locally (the rules moved from `tandem-git` to `tandem_core::lanes` so the helper shares them), and the desktop keeps the lane as a child workspace of the host folder, as for a local one. A lane's pull request pane needs `gh` on the host and says so if it's missing. The sweep looks only at hosts that are connected, so a host that is off never loses its lanes. WSL folders still share their checkout: their git runs through `wsl.exe` paths the lane code doesn't handle yet.
- D-1603: A host reached through `ProxyJump` or `ProxyCommand` in the user's SSH config is supported, not refused. `ssh-keyscan` can only connect directly, so its key is read by ssh itself: one connection that follows the user's config to the jump (which the user's own `known_hosts` and keys check, as for any ssh they run), offers the host no sign-in at all (public key, password, keyboard-interactive, host-based and GSSAPI off, batch mode, no agent or forwarding, no control master) and writes the key the host showed to a throwaway `known_hosts` with `accept-new`. That key is shown for approval and pinned like a scanned one, and every later connection checks it strictly through the jump. The probe signs in to nothing but the jump and pins nothing, so a jump that answers with the wrong host only shows a key the user can refuse. Tests point ssh at a config of their own with `TANDEM_SSH_CONFIG` (`ssh -F`, debug builds only).
- D-1604: A remote thread's plugins and task tools are relayed, not forwarded: the engine on the host starts `tandem-host mcp --channel <id>` as its `tandem` and `tandem_tasks` stdio servers, the helper passes each JSON-RPC line over the host's existing connection, and the desktop answers it with the gateway (served over a stream, bound to the session's lease, which never leaves) or the local `tandem-tasks` process (agreed with M11: the tasks server and its store stay here, unchanged). So no port is opened anywhere, no token, credential or task file is on the host, grants and plugin cards work as for a local thread, and Claude's engine approvals stay in band (D-414). A channel is a random id the desktop opens per session and closes when the session ends; only the connection that opened it hears it, and one server attaches at a time. A dropped connection ends the channels, so the session's tools end with it (the engine's own run ends too). Not covered: agent terminals on hosts (their engines' configs live in the host's provider home) and WSL threads, which still get neither.
- D-1605: Best of N runs in a folder on an SSH host, every step there, as worktrees do (D-1602): the lanes are the host's, the run's base is the commit they started from (each lane records it, since the host's status has no HEAD), Compare reads `lane.diff`, the check runs in each worktree through `tandem-host run` in the host user's login shell (`$SHELL -lc`, output joined), and Keep this one reads the base's and the winner's status on the host, commits the winner with `git.op` stage and commit (the host's git and identity), merges with `lane.merge` and removes the others with `lane.remove`. Stop kills the local ssh, and `run` ends the check's process group on the host once its input closes. A WSL folder still refuses ("Best of N isn't available in WSL folders yet"), as its worktrees do.
- D-1606: An agent terminal on an SSH host gets the tasks server as a local one does, when "Expose tasks to terminal agents" is on, but not through its config: the host's engine uses the host user's own home, which Vibezeo doesn't own and doesn't write. It gets the server for that run on its command line (Claude Code's `--mcp-config` JSON, Codex's `-c mcp_servers.tandem_tasks.*`; other engines get none, as locally), as `tandem-host mcp --channel <id>` on a D-1604 channel answered here by the local tasks server with the pane's own connection. Unlike a thread's, a terminal's channel is opened with `keep`: the agent outlives a dropped connection (its PTY re-attaches), so its server must too. The helper leaves a kept channel's relay attached, holds up to 256 of its lines, and hands them over when the desktop opens the channel again after reconnecting (before the PTYs re-attach); a held channel whose relay goes ends, so a pane closed while away leaves nothing. The channel closes with the pane. Plugins: F-PL-01's "terminal TUI opted into plugins" was never built locally either, and no acceptance criterion covers it, so on hosts too a terminal agent gets the tasks server only. WSL threads still get neither tasks nor plugins; the plan (interop to the Windows tasks exe, a Windows-side bridge to the gateway) is in HUMAN_TODO's "M15 Windows first run", to build and run on Windows with WSL 2.

### M14 log
- Remote parity, open item (c), host half (D-1606): task tools for agent terminals on SSH hosts. `spawn_on_host` opens a kept tasks channel per agent pane (`TerminalService::host_tasks_args`, answered by the local tasks server with the pane's own connection) and adds it to the launch with `homes::inline_stdio_server` (`f_rm_01_f_tk_02_a_hosts_terminal_engine_gets_its_server_inline`: Claude's `--mcp-config` JSON, Codex's `-c mcp_servers.tandem_tasks.*`, none for others, and the channel id isn't mistaken for a secret). `mcp.open` takes `keep` (`f_rm_01_f_tk_02_a_kept_channel_outlives_its_connection_and_is_taken_over`: a drop leaves the relay attached and holds its lines, only an open with `keep` takes it over and gets them, a held channel whose relay goes ends, an unkept one still ends with its connection); `RemoteService` keeps such channels through `mcp_dropped` and takes them over in `mcp_reclaim` before the PTYs re-attach; the channel closes with the pane (`end_tasks_session`). The fake engine's TUI has `/tasks <tool> <json>`, which keeps its server as engines do and prints `tasks[<server pid>]`. E2E-M14-12 on the fixture: the host's claude in a terminal pane is launched with the server inline (`tandem-host mcp --channel`), saves a task that lands in this machine's tracker, nothing is written into the host's `.claude.json` or folder, and after `ssh -O exit` and "Reconnected" the same server process saves another. Runs: the gate (every step), the tandem-host SSH tests (8/8), all twelve M14 specs. WSL threads are held for the coordinator: they have no helper connection to relay through.
- Remote parity, open item (b) (D-1605), on `m14-remote-ssh-wsl` after `bef5627` (chain5): Best of N in folders on SSH hosts. `bestof::start` no longer refuses them: the folder's status is read on the host (not a repository, no commits), the lanes come from the host's git through the launch's worktrees (D-1602), and the run's base is the commit they recorded. `check` runs each lane's check through `tandem-host run` in the host user's login shell (`host_check_argv`; `f_rm_01_f_ex_04_3_a_hosts_check_runs_in_its_login_shell_with_output_joined`), as a local process group so Stop and the 10-minute limit kill the ssh, and `run` ends the check on the host. `keep_question`/`keep` read the base's and the winner's status on the host and commit the winner with `git.op` stage and commit; merge and removal were already the host's. WSL folders still refuse ("Best of N isn't available in WSL folders yet"; `f_ex_04_5_a_folder_without_git_and_bad_seats_are_refused`). E2E-M14-11, on the fixture with two fake claude seats: two worktrees on distinct `tandem/` branches at the host's HEAD; Compare shows the file one agent wrote and nothing for the other; the check (`pwd; test -f done.txt`) passes in one and fails with exit 1 in the other, each printing its worktree's path on the host; a dirty base refuses, Cancel keeps both worktrees, and Keep commits the winner ("Best of N: …"), merges it into the main checkout, removes the other worktree and keeps both branches. PRODUCT_SPEC F-EX-04's states, the `bestof_start` row and the Remote docs say so. Runs: the gate, the tandem-host SSH tests (8/8), all eleven M14 specs, and E2E-M17-4 (local Best of N). Environment notes from the chain5 merge: `pnpm install` for `packages/remote`, `gcc-mingw-w64-x86-64` for the gate's Windows check, and a rebuilt musl helper (the SSH tests compare its version).
- Remote parity, open item (a): two host entries that reach one destination (two aliases for one `user@host:port`, or two hosts behind one jump) shared one ControlMaster, because `ControlPath=run/ssh-%C` hashes the destination and not the entry. Disconnecting or removing one ran `ssh -O exit` on the master both used: the other went "Reconnecting…" (its terminals said so), and had that reconnect failed it would have sat "Disconnected" with no error in the rail. The ControlPath is now `run/ssh-<entry>-%C`, where `<entry>` is 8 hex characters of a SHA-256 over the entry's target, port, user and key file (`f_rm_01_each_host_entry_has_its_own_master`). Against the fixture, `f_rm_01_app_two_hosts_for_one_target_both_connect`: two aliases in a `TANDEM_SSH_CONFIG` for `tandem@127.0.0.1:2222` both connect, and disconnecting one leaves the other with no state change (before the fix: `Reconnecting`, then `Connected`). E2E-M14-4's `dropConnection` now ends every `run/ssh-<entry>-*` master instead of the old fixed path. Not reproduced: the second alias of E2E-M14-9's first draft staying "Disconnected" on its own. With the first alias removed before the second is added, as the spec does, the second connects in about a second; and on OpenSSH 9.6 a ProxyJump host and a ProxyCommand host for one name already had different `%C`. An earlier note blamed a shared pinned key; the key is shared by design and wasn't the cause.
- Remote parity, item 5 (D-1604): plugins and task tools in threads on SSH hosts.
  - Helper: `mcp.open/close/attach`, `mcp.in/line/out/closed` and `tandem-host mcp --channel <id>`. Test `f_rm_01_mcp_lines_pass_between_the_desktop_and_the_engines_relay`: lines in order both ways, bad ids refused, a channel nobody opened refused, another connection can't speak on it, one server at a time, a closed server reported and replaced, and the channel ending with its connection.

Every m14 run therefore shipped the previous run's helper. That was the "Unknown method lane.create" in item 3's first run, and "Unknown method mcp.open" in this item's first E2E-M14-10 run. The helper is now built first.

Merging `product-m0-m5` (0.3.0, M13, #19) brought M13's engine policy and access guard, which the remote launch paths now go through too. The specs are now independent of each other and of the fixture's state (each adds and connects the host if needed and resets `/home/tandem/project`), and a dialog click waits for its zoom (D-52); a rerun had found the "Trust and connect" click dropped once.

Found on the way: Tauri copies bundle resources next to the debug binary at build time, and `target/debug/tandem-host` is the host binary itself, so the resource folder is bundled as `host-binaries/`; and "Save and connect" only connected when the refreshed list already held the new host (the vitest caught it).
