Tests: `f_pl_09_plugin_clients_read_the_apis_config_shape`, `f_pl_09_shipped_configs_hold_only_well_formed_plugin_entries`, `f_pl_09_what_each_module_offers_follows_its_client_and_the_config`, `plugins.test.tsx` (the unavailable card, the token-only sheet). Mail runs are filed under their mail (D-963, `f_pl_08_a_mail_run_is_filed_under_its_mail`).

## M16 · F-BE-08 Conductor voice API
Built on branch `m16-voice-api` (from `product-m0-m5`), in `apps/api` only, while the desktop's Conductor client is built separately against ARCHITECTURE §12.6 and §14.6. Its decisions are numbered from D-1000. The pull request targets `product-m0-m5`, never `main`.
- [x] F-BE-08 voice session minting (P2), with the API halves of F-CN-02 (the doer) and F-CN-05 (Find work).
  - **Routes** (`routes/voice.ts`, `services/voice.ts`):
    - `POST /v1/voice/session` holds 10 minutes of credits (1,200 at the remote-config `voice_10s` price of 20), then mints a realtime session and returns `{session_id, provider, ephemeral_token, ws_url, expires_at, max_seconds: 1800}`. It needs Pro (`conductor`), refuses a Team whose policy has `disableConductor` (403 `FORBIDDEN_POLICY`), and allows 10 calls an hour. A provider failure returns the hold (503 `VOICE_UNAVAILABLE`).
    - `POST /v1/voice/session/:id/settle {seconds}` charges `ceil(s/10)×20`, refunds the rest and is idempotent, also when two settles race. It is never plan- or policy-gated, so a call that loses either mid-way still gets its hold back.
    - `POST /v1/voice/act` runs one doer round during a live call (409 `SESSION_ENDED` after it), on the Claude API through `AiProvider`, with the closed catalog as tools. No credits move: the doer is part of the call's rate. 60 a minute.
    - `POST /v1/voice/resolve` ranks Find-work candidates through `metered()` for 1 credit (the remote-config `resolve` price). 30 a minute.
  - **Jobs.** `jobs/voiceSweeper.ts` runs every 60 s under a Redis lock and settles calls 2 minutes past their cap.
  - **Ledger.** `reserve` takes an `expiresAt`; `topUp` and `settle(…, {topUp: true})` grow a held reservation as far as the balance and a Team member's cap allow.
  - **Providers.** `providers/realtime`: `RealtimeProvider.mint` with `MockRealtime` and `OpenAiRealtime` (the vendor's client-secret endpoint). `ClaudeAi` now sends tools, tool choice, effort, a JSON schema and a cached system prompt when a feature asks, and returns tool calls and the stop reason. `MockAi` maps keyword intents to doer tool calls and scores Find work by shared words (ARCHITECTURE §14.8).
  - **Data.** Migration `0003_m16_voice` adds `voice_sessions.provider_session_id`, `last_act_at` and an index on open calls. `apps/api/voice-tools.json` (written by `pnpm --filter api openapi`, checked by a test) is the doer's catalog for the desktop's ActionRegistry. `openapi.json` has the four routes.
  - **Tests.**
    - API: 27 in `test/voice.test.ts` (F-BE-08 AC1 and AC2, F-CN-01 AC1's 65 s = 140 credits, F-CN-02 AC2, F-CN-05 AC1, the sweeper, top-ups, 402, 403, 409, 429, and a log capture).
    - Catalog: 6 in `src/services/voiceTools.test.ts`. Providers: 4 for `OpenAiRealtime`, 2 more for `ClaudeAi`.
    - The hosted-route guard (`test/hosted.test.ts`) lists the new routes.
  - **Not in this branch.** The dev WebSocket mock `GET /v1/dev/realtime` (F-CN-01, D-1006) and everything in `apps/desktop`.

### M16 · F-BE-08 decisions
- D-1000: This branch works in `apps/api` only, in parallel with the session that owns `apps/desktop`, `apps/web`, `packages/ui` and `packages/protocol/hosted-features.json`. Where a contract needed a reading, it is written down here and in the pull request rather than changed in a client. Decisions start at D-1000 because the D-900s are taken.
- D-1001: A call's hold grows with the call. The mint holds 10 minutes, as F-BE-08 says, but a call may run 30 minutes, and `ledger.settle` never charges more than is held. So:
  - The hold's reservation expires 10 minutes after the call's cap, so the 15-minute reservation sweeper (F-BE-05) never takes a live call's hold.
  - A doer round within 2 minutes of the time the hold pays for takes up to 10 more minutes from the balance (`ledger.topUp`, which takes what there is, within a Team member's cap). A call that has run past its paid time with nothing left gets 402 `CREDITS_EXHAUSTED` from `/voice/act`, which is the "402 → end" of ARCHITECTURE §12.6.
  - Settling charges the full `ceil(s/10)×20`, taking anything above the hold from the balance as far as it goes; a balance never goes below 0.
- D-1002: Settle holds the app's seconds to the call. They are never more than the time since the mint plus 5 s of clock skew, and never less than the time of the call's last doer round less 15 s to connect (`voice_sessions.last_act_at`). Without a usage report the API can't see connected time; the floor stops an app from claiming 0 s after using the doer.
- D-1003: The doer keeps no state between rounds, so no prompt or answer is stored (F-BE-06).
  - Each round is one Messages API call: the request, the current snapshot and the results so far, fenced as data, with the closed catalog as tools. The tools and the system prompt are cached.
  - The API mints each tool call's id as `vc.<round>.<index>.<tool>.<short arguments>`. The short arguments are the call's ids, numbers and choices in base64url; free text is replaced by its length, so an id never carries what the user said.
  - The next round rebuilds its history and its round number from the ids the app sends back. The app should send the results of every call so far in that request, not only the last round's.
  - Round 8 runs with `tool_choice: none` and has to answer in words.
- D-1004: The doer and Find work default to Claude Haiku 5.5 (`claude-haiku-5-5`) at low effort when remote config names no model. They run up to 8 rounds a request, many a minute, inside the 120 credits a minute the call already pays. Remote config's `ai_models.voice_act` and `voice_resolve` still override it. This branch leaves `config/remote-config.json` alone because a Rust test holds it equal to the desktop's bundled copy. `ClaudeAi` sends `output_config.effort` only to models that take it (not Haiku 4.5 or Sonnet 4.5), so an override to one of those still works.
- D-1005: The doer's catalog is F-CN-02's table as 47 typed, closed tools (`services/voiceTools.ts`), written to `apps/api/voice-tools.json` for the desktop's ActionRegistry.
  - Parameter names are this branch's; the spec lists only some.
  - `set_setting` and `get_setting` take the closed list as real `SettingKey`s: `theme`, `notify_system`, `notify_sound`, `dictate_engine`, `dictate_mic`, `pause_all_mail`, `conductor_voice`, `conductor_tone`, `conductor_verbosity`.
  - `set_thread_mode` takes the real `ThreadMode` values `plan` and `act`; the spec's table says plan/agent.
  - The setting keys, settings pages, thread modes, sidebar tabs and isolation choices are checked against the app's generated protocol types (`satisfies`), so a rename in the app fails the API's typecheck instead of a call.
  - Calls outside the catalog or its types never reach the app. When nothing usable is left, the answer is "I can't do that by voice." Forced lane removal, Full access and plugin spend are absent (F-CN-03).
  - The request, snapshot and results pass the server's redactor before the model sees them, on top of the app's own redaction (F-CN-03), and `<` is escaped so data can't close its tag.
- D-1006: Realtime providers sit behind `RealtimeProvider.mint({instructions, voice, maxSeconds})`.
  - `MockRealtime` (dev and tests) returns `ws://<api>/v1/dev/realtime?token=…`.
  - `OpenAiRealtime` uses the vendor's client-secret endpoint (`POST /v1/realtime/client_secrets`, model `gpt-realtime` unless `REALTIME_MODEL` says otherwise). It creates the session with the talker's base instructions, the voice and the one `act` tool; the app adds persona, tone and the workspace summary once connected. The token is good for connecting for 10 minutes.
  - `REALTIME_PROVIDER` and `OPENAI_API_KEY` choose the provider (ARCHITECTURE §17.5; MILESTONES' HUMAN_TODO calls the key `REALTIME_API_KEY`, and the architecture's name wins). Swapping providers through remote config waits for a second vendor and a desktop config update.
  - In production the mock refuses (503 `VOICE_UNAVAILABLE`) rather than hand out a dev socket.
  - The dev WebSocket mock `GET /v1/dev/realtime` is not built here: its protocol is whatever the desktop's MockRealtime speaks, so it belongs with that client.
- D-1007: `expires_at` in the session answer is when the call ends at the latest (`started_at + max_seconds`), the instant `voice_sessions.expires_at` holds. The token's own expiry is the provider's. The voice sweeper settles a call 2 minutes after that: at the provider's reported seconds when it reports usage, else at `max_seconds`, which charges no more than the hold (1,200 credits, or more only if the doer topped it up). OpenAI has no per-session usage report, so an abandoned call pays its hold; the app should keep an open call's id and settle it after a crash.
- D-1008: Find work (`/voice/resolve`) asks for JSON by schema and holds the answer to the offered ids: each once, scores clamped to 0..1, best first, at most 10. An empty candidate list answers `[]` with no model call and no charge. It is gated like the rest of Conductor (plan `conductor` and the Team policy); the app falls back to local fuzzy matching on any failure (F-CN-05). New per-user limits: `voice_act` 60 a minute and `voice_resolve` 30 a minute, next to §14.9's `voice_session` 10 an hour.

### M16 · F-BE-08 log

- The existing guard that every hosted route is plan-gated (`test/hosted.test.ts`) then failed for the new routes, as it should. It now lists session, act and resolve under `conductor`, and settle as the one route that reaches only the caller's own call (with a test that it can't reach anyone else's).
- The whole API suite passes: 24 files, 180 tests. Gate, after merging the latest `product-m0-m5`: green (Rust 818, desktop 362, UI 358, API 180, web 53, protocol 9; clippy, fmt and the protocol drift check).

## CI within the free Actions minutes
Built on `ccr-6d871eba-mxry64` (from `product-m0-m5`) by the voice API's session, which continues its decision range at D-1009. CI's last real runs were #34 and #35 on 7 October. Every run since has failed within 3 seconds: the month's minutes were used up.
- [x] The three failures from those runs, each reproduced and fixed:
  - **test-ts, web and integration** stopped at `pnpm infra:up` with "container infra-stripe-mock-1 has no healthcheck configured". Compose v2 (v2.39.4, as on the runners) fails `up --wait` on a disabled healthcheck. Compose v5.3.1, in these containers, calls the service healthy, and `with-infra.sh` ignores infra:up's exit code, so the gate never showed it. stripe-mock now has a real healthcheck (D-1010). With both versions all four services come up healthy.
  - **test-rust on macOS** failed F-TL-03's corpus: `should accept "/var/folders/…/ws/README.md": that path is outside the folder`. For an absolute path outside the root as written, `contain()` canonicalized the whole path, which fails for a file that doesn't exist yet. It now canonicalizes the deepest folder that exists (D-1012). The new test `f_tl_03_1_new_file_under_a_linked_root_stays_inside` reaches the root through a link, so Linux sees what macOS's /var → /private/var does. It failed before the fix and passes after. Windows' failure (the fixture made no links) was fixed in 5901341.
  - **desktop-build** printed "release binary contains the test harness". tauri-build makes a permission of every file in `permissions/autogenerated`, and the harness's 32 files were committed, so release builds carried `allow-test-*` (0.4.0's .deb did). Release builds now delete the files of commands they don't have, and the harness's files are generated and ignored, like the Simulator's (D-1011). The release ACL manifest went from 32 `allow-test-*` permissions to none.
  - The local check had passed only by accident. With `pipefail`, `strings | grep -q` exits 141 after an early match, which read as clean (3 of 3 runs on a file with a match). It now reads to the end, prints what it found, and still fails when the binary is missing.
- [x] `ci.yml` spends the free minutes where the gate can't (D-1009, OPERATING §5a):
  - `quick` on pull requests into `product-m0-m5`;
  - a nightly `checks` on its head;
  - the rest by hand, with **full** and **platforms**.
  actionlint passes.
- Not run here: the release harness end to end, and the gate's Rust half for the app and the invariants. The app's `parakeet` feature pulls in `ort-sys`, which downloads ONNX Runtime from `cdn.pyke.io` when it builds, and this environment's network policy blocks that host (OPERATING §5).

### CI decisions
- D-1009: CI spends the 2,000 free minutes a month where the gate can't, and the owner doesn't pay for more. October's ran out in a week, at 75–100 Linux minutes a pull request and 250–350 a push to `main` (macOS counts 10×, Windows 2×). A deployed batch ran three times: its pull request, the deploy pull request and the push to `main`. Pull requests here are merged seconds after they open, so no run ever gated a merge: the gate before every commit does.
  - Pull requests into `product-m0-m5` run `quick`: coverage, the hook guard, rustfmt, and every package's typecheck and ESLint. Nothing is compiled and no tests run, so it takes about 5 minutes. The TypeScript tests took 264 s on this 4-core machine, so on a 2-core runner they would double it.
  - Each night `checks` runs the gate's Linux half and the audit on `product-m0-m5`'s head, once per commit (a cache entry marks it). Its Rust cache is saved from `main`'s ref, which every branch can restore.
  - Everything else runs by hand: **full** (web and desktop E2E, the release harness, tandem-host, the Whisper test) and **platforms** (macOS and Windows).
  - Nothing runs for drafts, docs-only changes, deploy pull requests into `main` or pushes. cargo-audit, cargo-zigbuild and tauri-driver are installed prebuilt instead of compiled each run.
  - Estimates from October's pace: 500–750 minutes of `quick` and at most 750 of `checks` a month. Everything the old `ci.yml` ran is still in it (`lint`, `test-ts` and `audit` as steps of `quick` and `checks`); only when it runs changed.
- D-1010: stripe-mock's healthcheck is a real request, `wget` with a test key against `/v1/customers`, rather than none. Removing the block would also pass `--wait`, but a real check waits until the mock answers, like the other three services.
- D-1011: A release build deletes the autogenerated permission files of commands it doesn't have (`build.rs`), and the test harness's files are ignored rather than committed. Builds with debug assertions keep every file, so switching between the gate's builds and the macOS check doesn't re-run the build script. The harness's commands are listed when `CARGO_CFG_DEBUG_ASSERTIONS` is set rather than when `PROFILE` is `debug`: the perf suite's `perf` profile keeps the harness but reports `PROFILE=release`, and until now got the harness's permissions only from the committed files. The release harness check reads the whole of `strings` instead of stopping at the first match. Read to the end, it then found all 32 `allow-test-*` names in a release binary anyway: `lib.rs` adds the harness's capability with `include_str!("../test-capability.json")` under `if e2e`, a runtime flag that is always false in a release build, so the capability's text was compiled in. None of those permissions existed in the release ACL and no `test_*` command was compiled, so nothing was reachable, but the check failed. The `add_capability` call is now also under `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]`, and `release_capabilities_have_no_test_harness` asserts that gate (found by the coordinating session's release check on the merged tree).
- D-1012: `contain()` resolves an absolute path that doesn't exist yet through its deepest existing ancestor, with the missing rest appended as written. When that rest ends in `..`, the path is refused, as before. The part inside the root is taken by component count, so a case-only difference on macOS or Windows can't turn it into the root itself.

### CI log
- The three failures reproduced first: the stripe-mock error with Compose v2.39.4, the macOS containment failure through a linked root on Linux, and the harness's 32 permissions in a release ACL manifest. Then fixed.
- The desktop build script on the final tree: release builds list 0 `allow-test-*` of 596 permissions (32 before) and delete the stale files; debug and `perf` builds list all 32. A first version keyed on `PROFILE` would have dropped them from `perf` builds, which the perf suite's E2E harness needs (D-1011).
- The gate on the final tree, as far as this environment allows:
  - passed: coverage, the hook guard, lint, the desktop build, Windows clippy and fmt;
  - TypeScript 1,493 (desktop 696, UI 411, API 211, web 114, remote 32, mobile 19, protocol 10);
  - Rust 1,220 and clippy: the workspace without the app and the invariants, with tandem-voice built without `parakeet`.
  - Not run: the app's and the invariants' tests and clippy, the protocol drift check and the macOS check. All need the app, so `ort-sys`.
- One test failed, and fails on `product-m0-m5` too: chain23's F-AC-09.8 (`f_ac_09_8_a_paused_agent_keeps_its_pane_and_its_shell_never_hands_it_back`) printed HANDBACK-147 in 3 of 5 runs on this branch and 1 of 6 on a7bac8c, mostly on the first run after a build. The shell took the terminal back while the job was paused, which D-857 meant to prevent. Left to the session that owns F-AC-09.

## M16 · Conductor's voice: a free primary and a backup
Built on `m16-voice-api` (restarted from `product-m0-m5` after #30 and #78) by the voice API's session, with decisions D-1013 to D-1017, and merged as pull request #83. The owner asked for the free model first, with the best backup, then chose Gemini's free tier: D-1018, built on `ccr-6d871eba-mxry64` from #83's merge. It stays off until the API is deployed with a key (HUMAN_TODO): released apps and the API's defaults behave as before.
- [x] **API.** `REALTIME_PROVIDERS` orders the providers (`gemini,openai`). `POST /v1/voice/session` takes `providers`, the realtime protocols the app speaks, and mints with the first of those that mints, under one hold (D-1013). `GeminiLive` mints a Gemini Live ephemeral token (D-1014).
- [x] **Client.** `tandem_voice::realtime::gemini::GeminiLive`, registered as `gemini`, and `realtime::PROVIDERS` for the session request (D-1015).
- [x] **App and call (D-1017).** The app sends `providers`: the session request is `conductor::call::SessionRequest {voice, providers}`, which `services/api.rs` sends as it is. A call is connected when its talker says Ready to the call's configuration; a talker that fails before that is settled at 0 s and left out of the next request. Done here once the Conductor session had nothing in flight on `conductor/call.rs`, `commands/conductor.rs` or `services/api.rs`.
- [x] **The free tier (D-1018).** `GEMINI_TIER` (`free`, the default, or `paid`) and `CLIENT_COUNTRY_HEADER`. On the free tier a caller in the EEA, Switzerland or the UK, or of unknown country, never gets Gemini; with nothing else to offer, the API answers 503 `VOICE_NOT_IN_REGION`, which the app shows as "Conductor's voice isn't available in your country yet." with no Try again. Production with the free Gemini and no header refuses to start. The app's Conductor note, Settings › Privacy, the site's subprocessors and the Conductor docs name Google and say what its free tier means.
- [x] **A live check (D-1018).** `scripts/agent/gemini-live-check.sh` mints a token with the API's own `GeminiLive` and runs `tandem-voice`'s ignored `gemini_live` tests: the app's client, instructions and audio against Google (setup, speech, an act and its answer; `--long` adds a call past the 10-minute reconnect). Not run against Google yet: there's no key here.
- Facts used (Gemini Live, October 2026). Google's docs site is blocked here, so they come from Google's SDK source (`googleapis/js-genai` 2.30.0) and search results:
  - Ephemeral tokens: `POST v1alpha/auth_tokens` with `uses`, `expireTime` (default 30 minutes, under 20 hours), `newSessionExpireTime` (default 60 s), `bidiGenerateContentSetup` and `fieldMask`. "Resuming a Live API session does not count as a use." The socket is `…/ws/google.ai.generativelanguage.v1alpha.GenerativeService.BidiGenerateContentConstrained?access_token=<name>`.
  - A connection lasts about 10 minutes, with `goAway` about a minute before it ends. Audio-only sessions stop at 15 minutes unless context window compression is on. Resumption handles last 2 hours.
  - Output audio is 24 kHz. Input is 16 kHz natively; other rates are resampled when tagged (`audio/pcm;rate=24000`).
  - The Live models are free within rate limits on the free tier. Free-tier content is used to improve Google's products, and paid-tier content isn't. For the EEA, Switzerland and the UK, the terms allow only paid services. These come from third-party summaries of the terms: HUMAN_TODO has checking the original.

### M16 · voice provider decisions
- D-1013: Conductor's talker comes from an ordered list of providers, `REALTIME_PROVIDERS` (`gemini,openai`), or from `REALTIME_PROVIDER` alone as before.
  - The session request names the realtime protocols the app speaks (`providers`). An app that names none speaks `mock` and `openai`, as every app released before this does, so it never gets a session it can't open.
  - Names the API doesn't know are ignored, so a newer app can name a vendor an older API lacks.
  - One hold, then each provider in order until one mints. A provider that fails (a free tier out of quota answers 429) logs its status, and the next one tries.
  - The session records the provider that minted it, and the sweeper asks that one for usage. No migration: `voice_sessions.provider` is text.
- D-1014: Gemini Live is minted with an ephemeral token: `POST v1alpha/auth_tokens`, with the key in `x-goog-api-key`.
  - The token locks the model, spoken answers in the call's voice (one of the 8 prebuilt voices, else Puck), the `act` tool, both transcriptions, and sliding-window context compression, which lets a call pass 15 minutes. The tool is declared without `additionalProperties`, which the Live API's schema lacks.
  - The app's instructions and its resumption handle stay unlocked. The app sends them in its setup, as it already replaces the base instructions with OpenAI.
  - One use, the first connection within 60 s, and messages until the call's cap plus 5 minutes. Resuming isn't a use, so one token carries a 30-minute call across ~10-minute connections.
  - The model (`gemini-3.1-flash-live-preview` unless `GEMINI_LIVE_MODEL` says otherwise) rides in `ws_url`'s fragment, since the grant has no model field and a fragment never leaves the app.
- D-1015: The app's Gemini client has its own relay rather than the shared socket.
  - The token goes in the query, never a header. The setup is the first frame, and every other frame waits for `setupComplete`.
  - A frame can carry several events, in text or binary, and transcripts come in pieces: one TranscriptUser and one TranscriptAssistant per turn, when a piece is `finished` or at `turnComplete`.
  - Audio goes at 24 kHz, tagged, for the service to resample. `interrupted` is SpeechStarted, `turnComplete` ResponseDone, each `functionCalls` entry a FunctionCall, and the act's output a `toolResponse`. Say is a user text turn.
  - SetSpeed and Truncate send nothing: the Live API has no speech-rate setting and tracks barge-in itself. A tool call the service cancels still runs (`toolCallCancellation` is skipped).
  - `goAway` reopens the socket on the same token, with the same setup and the latest resumable handle. An unexpected close tries the same, once per handle.
- D-1017: A call is connected when its talker says Ready to the call's configuration, not when its socket opens. A talker that fails before that (refused at the handshake, closed at its setup, or silent past `CallTiming::ready`, 10 s) is settled at 0 s and left out of the next session request's `providers`. A free tier's rate limits count every user of the API's one key together, and going over them may only show at the setup.
  - The call sends its configuration as part of connecting, then waits for Ready. OpenAI says Ready at `session.created`, before the configuration; the mock and Gemini answer the configuration (`session.ready`, `setupComplete`). So the workspace summary in the instructions is read before the mint now, not after the socket opens.
  - Each failed provider leaves the list, so a call tries each at most once, and the last request, with nothing left the API can mint, is a 503 ("Couldn't connect to Conductor."). An API from before D-1013 ignores the list: a session it mints from a provider already left out is settled at 0, and the call fails rather than trying the same talker again.
  - A hang-up or a failed mic while connecting stops the fallback: the session in hand is settled at 0 s, and no other is asked for.
  - After Ready, nothing changes: a talker that hangs up mid-call ends the call.
  - Each fallback costs one more of the user's 10 session requests an hour (`voice_session` in the API's rate limits).
  - `SessionRequest` lives in `tandem-voice`, next to `ActRequest`, so its JSON is tested where tests can run here; the app only sends it.
- D-1016: Gemini stays off until the owner turns it on, because the free tier changes what a call's audio is used for: free-tier content improves Google's products, and in the EEA, Switzerland and the UK only paid services are allowed. HUMAN_TODO has the choice (a paid Gemini key keeps the same code and lifts both), the key, and checks against a recorded session.

- D-1018: The owner chose Gemini's free tier for Conductor's talker. Google's Gemini API Additional Terms (read through search results, since ai.google.dev is blocked here) allow only Paid Services when an API client is made available to users in the EEA, Switzerland or the UK. So on `GEMINI_TIER=free` the API leaves Gemini out for those callers, by the country the API's edge puts in `CLIENT_COUNTRY_HEADER`.
  - The list: the EEA's 30 countries, the EU's regions with codes of their own (AX, GF, GP, MQ, RE, YT, MF), CH and GB, and names some databases use (UK, EL, EU).
  - An unknown country (no header, XX, ZZ, Tor's T1, or not two letters) counts as one of them: keeping to the terms comes before reach.
  - Such a caller gets the backup. With none, 503 `VOICE_NOT_IN_REGION`, not retryable, so the app offers no Try again.
  - Production with the free Gemini and no header refuses to start, as a provider without its key does. Elsewhere (dev, tests) no header means no country is read and the rule is off.
  - The header must be one the edge always overwrites, with the API unreachable around it: a caller can send any header itself.
  - `GEMINI_TIER=paid` (billing on the key's Cloud project) has no such rule.
  - The same terms say "Do not submit sensitive, confidential, or personal information to the Unpaid Services", and that human reviewers may read inputs and outputs. A call is someone's voice, with names from their workspace, so HUMAN_TODO suggests the paid tier before real users; the free tier suits the owner's own testing.
  - The disclosures say what the free tier means: Google may keep what's said, use it to improve its products and have people review it. The app's note had said "Nothing is recorded after you hang up", which the free tier makes untrue; it now says Vibezeo's servers keep no recording.
  - The Anthropic entry in `subprocessors.json` gains Conductor's planner and Find work, which already used it.

## M16C checklist (Phone companion)
Built on branch `m16c-phone-companion` (from `product-m0-m5`, D-1900). Owns F-BE-11 Remote relay and F-RM-03 Phone companion (MILESTONES M16 §16C) and extends F-TL-06 rule 5 with the live phone path. Decisions are D-1900–D-1949.
- [x] F-BE-11 Remote relay.
  - **Pairings.** `POST /v1/pairings` (desktop bearer) checks the plan includes `phone` (403 `FORBIDDEN_PLAN`) and that a Team admin hasn't set `disablePhone` (403 `FORBIDDEN_POLICY`). It stores only the pairing id, owner and a SHA-256 of a fresh 32-byte token, and answers `{pairing_token, relay_url, expires_at}` (10 minutes). `DELETE /v1/pairings/:id` marks it revoked (404 for anyone but the owner, idempotent) and drops the relay's sockets (`services/pairings.ts`, `routes/pairings.ts`).
  - **Relay.** `routes/relay.ts` serves `GET /v1/relay/:pairing_id` on the API's port with `ws`. The desktop authenticates with its bearer and the phone with `Sec-WebSocket-Protocol: tandem-pair.<token>`, which is echoed back. A live pairing and a plan that still includes the phone are required, or the socket closes with 1008. It holds at most one desktop and one phone socket, and a newer one replaces the older (4000). Binary frames up to 64 KB are forwarded unchanged; larger close with 1009 (`maxPayload`) and text frames with 1003. A token bucket allows 50 frames a second per pairing. It tells each end when its peer comes and goes, and pings every 30 s. It logs pairing id, role and close code only.
  - **No key material (AC2).** `routes/relay.ts` imports no crypto. ESLint `no-restricted-imports` covers `crypto`, `node:crypto`, `jose`, `@noble/*` and `lib/ids` in that file, and a test reads its imports.
  - **Tests.** API: 17, `test/relay.test.ts` (F-BE-11.1 forwarding and the 65 KB close, F-BE-11.2 imports, auth, replace, peer notices, revoke, lapsed plan, rate limit, and a log capture that never holds a sent sentence).

- [x] F-RM-03 Phone companion (Pro).
  - **Protocol (D-1905).** `tandem_core::remote::protocol` and `apps/web/lib/remote/protocol.ts` implement ARCHITECTURE §13.3's wire format v1: the QR hello with `HMAC-SHA256(S, …)`, X25519 and HKDF-SHA256 to a root, per-epoch `d2p`/`p2d` keys, XChaCha20-Poly1305 frames whose nonce starts with the counter, `ctr > last` (replays and out-of-order frames refused, a failed frame changes nothing), plaintext ≤ 61,440 bytes so frames stay under 64 KB, and the desktop-led rekey with its "next" keys. `crates/tandem-core/tests/fixtures/remote_v1.json` holds vectors from fixed inputs; the Rust and TypeScript tests both check every byte against it, so the two sides can't drift.
  - **Messages.** `tandem_core::remote::messages`: `FromPhone`/`ToPhone` (ts-rs), the six phone commands with their grants, `PHONE_COMMANDS`, text that refuses control characters (no escape sequences reach a terminal), and `approval_route` (deny applies, allow applies only while armed, else queued).
  - **Desktop.** `services::phone::PhoneService` behind `PhoneHost` (the app) and `PhoneNet` (API and relay). `phone_pair_begin` makes the identity key (keychain `NS.remote/identity`), a pairing id and a one-time `S` (memory only, 10 minutes), registers the pairing and opens its relay link; the QR carries the `/remote#p=` link. A hello whose tag checks out stores the session keys in the keychain (`pairing:<id>`) and the row in `pairings`; `S` is dropped. Each frame's counters go to `pairings` before its command runs. Commands run as `Origin::Phone`; arming, grants and the queue take a `LocalReview`. Snapshots and transcript pages are capped to fit one frame. Revoke sends `bye`, deletes the keys, marks the row and calls `DELETE /v1/pairings/:id`; sign-out revokes everything first, and a dropped session revokes locally. `commands::phone` holds the app host, the tokio-tungstenite relay socket and ten commands (ARCHITECTURE §3).
  - **UI.** Settings › Remote (UI-111, phone part): Pair a phone opens a dialog with the code (`@tandem/ui` `QRCode`, new), its link and Copy, then "Paired with …"; paired phones with last seen, Connected, four grant switches and Revoke (confirmed); "Allow approvals from phone for the next 8 hours"; the phone's queued allows with Allow and Don't allow; Reset remote access (confirmed). Empty, loading, error and populated states; Free and org policy keep pairing off.
  - **PWA.** `apps/web/app/remote` (portal build, never in the static site export): pairs from the fragment and removes it from the address bar and history, then Agents, Approvals (allow and deny, queued shown as waiting), Threads (paged transcript, send with Chat control) and Routines (Run now with the Routines grant), Type to an agent with Terminal control, Unpair this phone. `lib/remote/client.ts` is the phone side (greets when the relay says the desktop is there, saves the send counter before each frame, answers rekeys, wipes itself on `bye` or a 1008), `store.ts` keeps the pairing in IndexedDB sealed with a non-extractable AES-GCM key. Manifest and service worker are route handlers under `/remote`.
  - **Invariants.** Rule 5: the phone's command set holds no local-review action, allow needs arming, and the live path keeps an allow queued until the main window confirms. Rule 7: no pairing, no relay connection; a revoked pairing gets no answer.
  - **Tests.** Rust: 8 protocol (`tests/remote_protocol.rs`), 4 messages, 2 store, 15 phone service, 4 invariant cases. TypeScript: 12 protocol and 7 client (web), 6 Settings › Remote (desktop), the `QRCode` gallery snapshot.
  - **E2E-M16-4** (`e2e/specs/m16-4-phone.e2e.ts`, 5 tests): a scripted phone running the PWA's own `PhoneClient` in the test process pairs from Settings › Remote over the real relay; Terminal control types a known sentence into an agent pane; a replayed frame is answered `reject: replay` and runs once (F-RM-03.2); an allow without arming is queued and applies when Allow is clicked in Settings (F-RM-03.3); a 65 KB frame closes the socket with 1009 (F-BE-11.1); after Revoke the phone is told, forgets its keys, its next command fails and the relay refuses its token with 1008 (F-RM-03.4). `run.ts` now keeps the API's log as `api.log` (`E2E_RELAY_LOG`) and fails the run if it holds the known sentence (F-RM-03.1); the sentence did reach the agent, as the fake engine's log shows.
  - **Web E2E @m16** (`e2e/m16-remote.spec.ts`, 2 tests): `/remote` with nothing paired explains pairing; a scripted desktop (`e2e/remoteDesktop.ts`) pairs with the real PWA in a fresh browser profile, which shows its agents, queues an allow, sends to a thread, resumes from IndexedDB after a reload (only `{iv, ct}` stored), and wipes itself on `bye`.

### M16C decisions
- D-1900: 16C is built on `m16c-phone-companion`, the branch this session was assigned. The coordinating session asked for `m16-phone-relay` and a pull request per slice; this session pushes each green slice to its own branch, reports the head, and opens one pull request when 16C is complete, as its brief says (the coordinator agreed).
- D-1901: A refused relay connection is upgraded and then closed with 1008, with the offered subprotocol echoed. A browser that gets a refused handshake sees only a generic error, so the phone couldn't tell "revoked" from "offline". Text frames from either end close with 1003. The relay's own messages are the only text frames: `{type:"peer", up}` and `{type:"rate_limited"}`. Ciphertext is always binary.
- D-1902: The rate limit is a token bucket of 50 frames a second with a burst of 50, per pairing, both directions together. Frames over it are dropped, and the sender gets `rate_limited` at most once a second. The socket stays open, because the frame counters tolerate gaps.
- D-1903: The relay runs inside the API process, on its port, and revocation reaches it in memory (`RelayControl`). More than one API instance would need the drop fanned out over Redis, so that's a hosting item in `HUMAN_TODO.md`.
- D-1904: A pairing token lasts until the pairing is revoked. The 10-minute QR lifetime is held by the desktop, which discards the one-time secret `S` after 10 minutes; without `S` no handshake can complete, whoever holds the token. A lapsed plan or a Team's `disablePhone` refuses relay connections as well as new pairings.

- D-1905: The phone protocol's wire format is fixed in ARCHITECTURE §13.3 ("Wire format v1") and pinned by interop vectors that both implementations check. HELLO and SEALED are binary frames with a kind byte; the messages inside are JSON.
- D-1906: Keys live in the keychain and are written at the handshake and each rekey; the frame counters live in `pairings` and are written after every frame (receive counter before the command runs). Loading a session takes the larger of each counter, so a counter never goes back.
- D-1907: Arming ("Allow approvals from phone for the next 8 hours") is held in memory: a restart disarms. It's a step-up a person turns on at the desktop, and it shouldn't outlive the app run they turned it on in.
- D-1908: The grants follow PRODUCT_SPEC F-RM-03 (Chat control, Terminal control, Approvals, Routines; View comes with pairing). UI_SPEC UI-111's "Manage sessions", "Write files" and "Git actions" have no phone command behind them, so they aren't shown. The dialog says "Scan this code with your phone's camera" (there's no app to scan with) and the code lasts 10 minutes, per ARCHITECTURE, not UI-111's 2.
- D-1909: The PWA keeps its pairing in IndexedDB, sealed with a non-extractable AES-GCM WebCrypto key stored beside it. X25519 and XChaCha20 keys can't be non-extractable WebCrypto keys in every browser, so script on the app's origin could still use the pairing; that's ARCHITECTURE's documented residual risk.
- D-1910: The phone says `hi` on every connection and the desktop answers `welcome` with the grants. The relay drops frames while the other end is away, so the phone greets when the relay reports the desktop present. A phone's own `bye` (Unpair this phone) revokes the pairing on the desktop.
- D-1911: `routine.run_now` runs a routine through M11's `RoutinesService::run_now` (overlap and plan checks included), with the Routines grant. Until M11 merged in it answered "unavailable" and the grant wasn't offered; the snapshot now lists routines (name, paused, running) under View, and the PWA has a Routines tab with Run now.
- D-1912: Only the desktop starts a rekey, every hour or 2^20 frames, one at a time. Both sides persist the next keys, so a connection that drops mid-rekey resumes; a stale "next" is replaced by the next rekey.
- D-1913: Sign-out revokes every pairing through the API before the session ends. A session that drops on its own (`auth:changed` with `signedIn: false`) revokes locally, and the relay refuses the desktop until it signs in again. Changing accounts needs a sign-out first, so it's covered by the same path.
- D-1914: The QR code is drawn by a new `@tandem/ui` `QRCode` from the module rows Rust computes (`QrMatrix`, replacing ARCHITECTURE's `qrSvg`), dark on a light panel in both themes because phone cameras read inverted codes poorly.
- D-1915: The desktop's relay socket uses tokio-tungstenite with the bearer in an `Authorization` header, reconnecting with backoff (1 s doubling to 60 s; 30 s after a 1008). It doesn't go through an HTTP proxy.
- D-1916: Desktop E2E runs keep the API's log (`api.log` in the run's home, level info) and start the API with `API_PUBLIC_URL` set to itself. The log is what the relay check greps for the known sentence (MILESTONES M16 verification), and the public URL is what the relay URL in each pairing is built from.
- D-1917: Settings › Remote is one page shared with M14, as UI-111 lays it out: SSH hosts, WSL distributions, then the phone (`RemotePage`). Both sessions had added a `remote` page; the merge keeps M14's entry, adds the phone section to it, and widens its subtitle to "SSH hosts, WSL distributions and your phone" ("SSH hosts and your phone" off Windows, where M14 leaves WSL out).

### M16C log
- F-BE-11: pairings, the relay, the lint rule and 17 API tests. The first run's one failure was in the test: it added a second, canceled subscription instead of lapsing the first.
- F-RM-03 (without its E2E): the protocol in Rust and TypeScript with shared vectors, the desktop phone service and commands, Settings › Remote, the PWA, and the rule 5 and rule 7 cases. The Rust vectors matched the TypeScript ones on the first run. The service's first rekey test was wrong, not the service: with a zero interval the rekey arrives with the welcome, during pairing. Merged `product-m0-m5` (74 commits) on the way; the conflicts were all "keep both" (API deps, test truncation list, module lists, BUILD_LOG).
- F-RM-03 E2E: E2E-M16-4 5/5 and the web @m16 phone specs 2/2; the full web suite 21/21. What the runs found:
  - The PWA's client component imported `lib/site.ts`, which since the merge reads the changelog with `node:fs`, so Next.js failed to build every page in dev. The web typecheck can't see that; the web E2E did. The page now passes the product name down from the server component.
  - E2E runs started the API without `API_PUBLIC_URL`, so pairings got `ws://api.tandem.local:8787` relay URLs that don't resolve on the E2E machine. `run.ts` now sets it to the API it starts, as the web Playwright config already did.
  - The replay test first counted lines in the pane's DOM text, which the terminal doesn't render into; it reads the pane through the test bridge now.
  - The full desktop E2E regression: 90 of 94 spec files on the first run; the 4 that failed were the Dictate specs (m8-1, m8-2, m8-4, m8-5), because this container's model cache was empty (OPERATING §5: `bash scripts/fetch-models.sh base.en parakeet`). With the models fetched, all 5 m8 spec files pass, so 94 of 94.
  - Merged `product-m0-m5` twice more (M11 tasks and routines, then M14 SSH and WSL). After M14, both sessions' Settings › Remote pages became one (D-1917).
  - A gate run was lost to a full disk (test binaries of ~500 MB each pile up); deleting stale test executables in `target/debug/deps` freed 12 GB.

## M16 · 16A Conductor core (F-CN-01…04, F-CN-08)
Built on branch `m16a-conductor` (from `product-m0-m5`), in parallel with F-CN-06 and F-CN-07 on `m16a-persona-wake`, whose seams are agreed: that branch's persona and memory reach a call as a `PersonaBrief` read once at start, and its wake detector starts calls through `ConductorService::start(CallOrigin::Wake)`. Decisions are numbered from D-1100.
- [x] F-CN-01 Call lifecycle, F-CN-02 talker/doer and tool catalog, F-CN-03 delivery rules and safety, F-CN-04 HUD, ring and call log, F-CN-08 audio routing (first slice).
  - **`tandem-core::voice_tools`.** The closed catalog: 47 tools, each mapped to registry commands or marked voice-only, with what it needs on screen (`Review`), and the closed `set_setting` list. Tests hold it equal to `apps/api/voice-tools.json` (names, groups, which tools confirm) and check that every mapped command exists (AC1).
  - **`tandem-voice::realtime`.** `RealtimeProvider` gives normalized `ClientEvent`s and `ServerEvent`s. `MockRealtime` is a tokio-tungstenite client for the dev mock's protocol. There is also the 3 s pre-connect buffer (`buffer::PreConnect`) and the PCM16 24 kHz helpers (a streaming resampler and base64).
  - **`tandem-voice::conductor`.**
    - `CallController` handles start, connect, the talker, doer rounds, mute, the cap, end and settle.
    - `Executor` is the local tool executor. A second closed-schema check uses the same JSON, and it enforces the closed list, the confirmations, the waiting- and shell-pane refusals, control-character stripping, redaction and cancellation.
    - `Confirms` holds questions bound to request ids. A yes on a screen-only question needs a `LocalReview`.
    - The rest: the view types for the HUD, `PersonaBrief`, and `Speaker` (cpal, or silent for tests).
  - **Mic (F-CN-08).** `AudioService::begin_stream` streams a call through Dictate's device policy, gain and silent-mic recovery; recovery events go to the call, not to Dictate. While a call holds the mic, Dictate refuses every take, from the global key and from composers ("Conductor is listening").
  - **Desktop.**
    - `services/conductor.rs` holds the service, the hosted-error mapping, the workspace snapshot (names and states only) and the call-log tidy at startup: unsettled calls settle, and logs past 7 days go.
    - `commands/conductor.rs` has the `AppHost` and the commands `conductor_start_call`, `end_call`, `mute`, `confirm` (main window only), `state`, `call_log` and `call_delete`, plus the debug-only `test_conductor_inject` and `test_conductor_clock`.
    - `ApiClient` gains `voice_session`, `voice_act` and `voice_settle`.
    - `tandem-store::repo::conductor` keeps the call log in the existing tables.
    - `type_prompt_with` adds a `send` flag, and `settings::store_and_apply` is the path `set_setting` uses.
    - Sign-out ends a call first, so it can still settle.
  - **UI.**
    - The title-bar ring (UI-01 item 8): Pro only; a click toggles a call, holding it is push-to-talk.
    - The HUD (UI-61): state, utterance, newest 3 chips plus "+N", the question row with "Confirm on screen", Mute, Call log and End, the credits meter, errors with their actions, and the 3 s receipt.
    - The call log sheet (UI-66), with empty, loading, error and populated states.
    - Palette commands `conductor.call` (Mod+Shift+Space; on Free it opens the plans), `conductor.mute` and `conductor.log`.
    - `WaveformRing` gains `paused`: no animation frames while the window is hidden or the app inactive. `ringStats` counts frames.
  - **Mock and harness.** `scripts/mock-realtime.ts` is a dependency-free WebSocket server with a control API. The API takes `REALTIME_MOCK_URL`, and the E2E runner starts the mock and points the API at it.
  - **Tests.**
