Wiring them to their services is the next slice.
    - Also not built yet: the floating ring window, the "Keep call logs" setting, "talk faster/slower", and the vendor realtime client (D-1113).

### M16 · 16A Conductor core decisions
- D-1100: Conductor's logic lives in `tandem-voice` (`realtime`, `conductor`) behind host traits (`ToolHost`, `CallHost`), so every rule is tested with fakes; the desktop's `AppHost` is the running app. Calls run on a runtime handle the controller keeps, so any thread can start one (the wake detector included).
- D-1101: F-CN-02 AC1 reads "every tool maps to a registry command, or is one of the table's voice-only tools", since half the table (`prompt_pane`, `read_pane`, …) has no palette command and adding one each would put voice-only actions in the palette. The app holds its catalog equal to the API's `voice-tools.json` and checks each call against that file's schemas again before it runs.
- D-1102: The dev mock's protocol is the app's own: JSON text frames, PCM16 24 kHz base64, events `session.update`, `input_audio.append`, `function_call.output`, `response.create` and back `session.ready`, `speech.started`, `transcript.user|assistant`, `audio.delta`, `function_call`, `response.done`, `error`. It is served by `scripts/mock-realtime.ts` (not the API's `/v1/dev/realtime`, which D-1006 left to this branch); the API's `MockRealtime` points there when `REALTIME_MOCK_URL` is set. The token goes in the handshake's Authorization header and is never logged.
- D-1103: The pre-connect buffer keeps the latest 3 s; audio while muted is never buffered, and mute or hang-up empties it.
- D-1104: `prompt_pane` refuses a pane waiting on an approval or a question at once ("waiting for approval", nothing typed) and refuses shell panes; a busy agent gets the prompt once it is back at its prompt, waiting up to 60 s, and checks the call's cancellation right before typing, so a hang-up while it waits delivers nothing (AC2).
- D-1105: A spoken answer is `act("answer <request id> yes|no")`, or a bare yes/no for the question the HUD shows; the app handles it without a doer round and resolves exactly that id. A spoken yes for a shell command is refused; only `conductor_confirm` from the `main` window makes the `LocalReview` that `run_in_shell` takes. The HUD shows one question at a time, oldest first; the talker is told each question's id as it comes up.
- D-1106: A call's id is the API's session id. The call log uses the existing `conductor_calls` and `conductor_call_events` tables, so there is no migration to collide with parallel branches. Connected seconds are saved every 10 s, so a call interrupted by a crash settles at the next start. Settled calls older than 7 days are purged at startup.
- D-1107: (first slice) Tools not yet wired to an app service return a plain failure ("… isn't available by voice in this version yet; it's in the app.") rather than pretending; the doer tells the user so.
- D-1108: Two debug-only harness commands: `test_conductor_inject` runs doer tool calls the mock doer doesn't plan through the same executor, chips and log; `test_conductor_clock` moves only the call's clock, because `TANDEM_FAKE_CLOCK` would freeze the whole app. The 65 s case runs in real time, so the API's settle clamp (D-1002) holds and the credits are the real ones.
- D-1109: Sign-out ends a call and waits up to 3 s for its settle before the tokens go.
- D-1110: While a call holds the mic, Dictate refuses every take: from the global key and from composers. The owner is set through Dictate's `set_mic_owner` until `m16a-persona-wake`'s `MicOwners` registry lands. The composer mic shows "Conductor is listening".
- D-1111: The persona seam is `PersonaBrief {voice, instructions}`, read once per call; the app's rules and the fenced workspace summary follow it. Built-in defaults until F-CN-06's `CallContext` is mapped onto it.
- D-1112: The desktop's balance doesn't show the 10-minute hold while a call runs (the API sends no credits event on reserve); the HUD's minutes-left reads the balance as shown and the settle corrects it.
- D-1113: Only the mock provider is built on the desktop. A session minted for another provider ends with "Couldn't connect to Conductor" and settles at 0 s; the vendor client waits for the key (HUMAN_TODO) Superseded by D-1120.

- D-1114: The second slice wires the remaining tools to the app's own commands.
  - create_teammate opens the New teammate sheet with the spoken description filled in; the user saves it there, so nothing is created without their review.
  - send_to_thread refuses a thread with a pending approval, as prompt_pane does for panes.
  - execute_task uses a new lane, Edits access and review: never Full (F-CN-03).
  - launch_agents opens its agents in their own tab, as the launch sheet does.
  - Tools that name a workspace, thread, teammate or routine take its id or its name; "current" or nothing picks the only workspace.
- D-1115: `panes::list_all_open` sorted panes created in the same millisecond by id, whose ULID suffix is random, so F-CN-05's store test failed now and then in the gate. It now sorts by `created_at`, then insertion order (`rowid`), which is what "oldest first" means.

- D-1116: The speech rate is call-scoped: it starts at the persona's speed (F-CN-06 owns the setting) and "talk faster / slower" moves it for the rest of the call without saving it. Speeds go on the wire rounded to two decimals.
- D-1117: The floating ring is a second Tauri window with its own least-privilege capability (`ring.json`), so a click in it can't confirm anything: `conductor_confirm` isn't in its capability and needs the `main` window anyway. Its Call log button sends `conductor:open-log` to `main`. It reads the theme once when it opens.

- D-1118: F-SH-09.4 (every registry command runs from the palette) does one palette round trip per command, and with Conductor's three commands it went past its 20 s limit under the gate's parallel load. It now finds options with a plain `[role="option"]` selector and looks up its key target once, rather than calling `getAllByRole` and `getByText` per command: 7.5 s alone instead of 12 s, with the same assertions.
- D-1119: The persona, wake and mic-owner seams are wired (F-CN-06, F-CN-07). A call's `PersonaBrief` is `services::conductor_persona::brief` of `ConductorPersona::call_context()`, read once at start: the voice ("default" lets the provider choose), the speed step 1–5 onto `SPEEDS`, and the persona's instructions (memory only while it's on). The wake listener's `CallStarter` is `commands::conductor::WakeStart`, the same plan gate and entry point as the ring (`start_call`, origin `Wake`; a running call is kept). A call claims `MicOwner::Call` before its capture opens and holds it until it lets go of the mic (`services::conductor::CallMic`; a failed open gives it back), so the wake listener steps aside for the whole call. Two debug-only E2E commands, `test_conductor_wake` (the phrase was heard) and `test_mic_owner`, and the mock talker now reports the voice and instructions it was configured with. E2E-M16-1 gains F-CN-06 (voice, name, memory and speed reach the talker) and F-CN-07 (a wake call holds the mic until it ends), in their own `describe` with a second Pro user: the API mints at most 10 voice sessions an hour per user (`voice_session` in `rateLimit.ts`), and the spec's first 11 calls had used them, so the 12th got a 429. E2E-M16-1 13/13, E2E-M16-9 3/3.
- D-1120: The vendor's realtime client is built without the key (CLAUDE.md: stub behind the interface, HUMAN_TODO). `tandem_voice::realtime::openai` follows the vendor's published GA events (read from its SDK's generated types, since the docs site refused this network); `realtime::socket` is the connection both providers share, so the mock and the vendor differ only in their codec.
  - The `act` call is a `response.output_item.done` whose item is a `function_call` with status `completed`: `response.function_call_arguments.done` also arrives for interrupted and cancelled responses, and an interrupted request must run nothing.
  - Something for the talker to say (`ClientEvent::Say`) goes as a system message item then `response.create`: a response's own `instructions` would replace the persona for that turn.
  - `Configure` sets PCM16 24 kHz both ways, input transcription (`gpt-4o-mini-transcribe`, for the call log and HUD), server VAD with `interrupt_response` (the server cancels its answer on barge-in; the app stops playback on `speech_started`), the voice when it's one of the vendor's (otherwise the minted one stays), and exactly one tool, `act`.
  - `session.created` is Ready; `session.updated`, deltas of transcripts and arguments, rate limits and the rest are skipped.
  - Truncating the assistant's item to what was heard on barge-in came after, as D-1122.
- D-1121: F-CN-06 was ticked on its two acceptance criteria (memory off writes nothing; a spoken token is refused) while its call wiring was missing: "remember that…", "what do you know about me", "forget…", post-call reflection and the call journal existed only as `ConductorPersona` methods no call reached, and the memory empty state already told users to say "remember that…". Now built.
  - The three commands are answered in the app, like the speed and yes/no answers (`tandem_voice::conductor::memory::parse_memory`, no planner round, no catalog or API change). The call refuses a credential-shaped fact itself (`check_fact`) before the host sees it; the store refuses it again. Replies say what happened, including "Memory is off".
  - Reflection's proposals come from a local reading of what the user said during the call (redacted transcripts): first-person statements ("I prefer…", "I usually…", "I work on…", "My name is…"), at most 5, no questions, nothing credential-shaped. Nothing leaves the machine for reflection; "After each call" then makes them cards, saves them or drops them. `CallHost::after_call` carries them with the call's length and tools.
  - The journal line is the time, the length and the tools used (`journal_line`), never what was said, so "Don't keep" call logs has nothing to withhold from it; it's written only while memory is on.
  - Neither facts nor journal text is logged; memory reaches the talker only as the persona brief at call start and the reply to "what do you know about me".
- D-1122: Barge-in tells the talker what was heard. `ServerEvent::AudioDelta` carries the provider's item id; the call notes where each item starts in the speaker's queue (`Speaker::queued`) and how much of it arrived, and on `SpeechStarted`, while that item is still playing, sends `ClientEvent::Truncate {item_id, audio_end_ms}` with the milliseconds the speaker really played (`Speaker::played`, never more than arrived, since the vendor rejects a longer one). Nothing is sent when nothing of it is playing (it finished, hasn't started, or the provider names no item).
  - Speakers count queued and played samples; `stop` drops the unplayed ones from the queued count, so the next item's start stays comparable. The cpal queue counts samples as the output stream takes them; `SilentSpeaker` (tests and E2E) by the clock.
  - The vendor gets `conversation.item.truncate {item_id, content_index: 0, audio_end_ms}`, pinned in the fixture's `client_events`; the mock records `{itemId, audioEndMs}` and `/__speak` names its item. Its `conversation.item.truncated` acknowledgement is skipped.
- Lesson (merge of chain4): a merge commit's `git add -A` ran while `gen:protocol` had emptied `packages/protocol/src/generated`, so the commit deleted every generated type. The gate's drift check (`git diff --exit-code`) ignores untracked files and passed. Check `git diff --cached --stat packages/protocol` before committing a merge.

### M16 · 16A Conductor core log
- Tests first from the eight E2E-M16-1 cases, the four F-CN-02 and two F-CN-03 acceptance criteria. The first E2E run crashed the app: `conductor_start_call` is a sync command and spawned on a thread outside the runtime (D-1100's runtime handle fixes it). Then the test-only commands lacked their capability (added to `test-capability.json`), `getText()` read "" while the HUD faded in (read `innerText`), and a key off the closed list was refused by the schema check with a generic reason; the executor now says "Conductor can't change that setting." first. One unit test raced the call's tick, and was fixed in the test rather than slowed.
- Vendor realtime client (D-1120), on `m16a-conductor` after merging `product-m0-m5` (c9adfae). The docs site refused this network, so the event names and fields come from the vendor SDK's generated types (`openai-node`, `src/resources/realtime/realtime.ts`). Tests first: a hand-built fixture of 20 server events pinning every name and field `decode` reads; encode tests for the session (PCM16 24 kHz both ways, one tool), audio, the act result and speech; a local WebSocket server replaying the fixture (connect with the client secret, audio both ways, an act round trip, barge-in, an error, a close); a refused secret; and a whole call over the vendor protocol through `CallController`, where barge-in stops playback. `realtime::socket` now holds the connection both providers share.
- An audit of F-CN-01 to -04 and -08 found every acceptance criterion implemented and tested, all 47 tools of the F-CN-02 table in the catalog and executed, and REMAINING's "to come" list stale. Gaps it found, now closed: the floating ring had no hold for push-to-talk (the spec's "hold it" covers both rings; `features/conductor/hold.ts` is shared now), push-to-talk had no test at any level, and a mic that stops delivering had no call-level test. Those left untested here were closed in the next entry.
- Test gaps closed (tests first, each named with its F-CN ID): Mod+Shift+Space pressed as a key starts a call and ends it (`useShortcuts` mounted, Ctrl on Linux); mic gain carries across three calls in a row (`capture.rs`, 2× through the soft clip); "Don't keep" writes no turns and drops the call's row only once it settles (the record logic moved from `AppHost::record` to `services::conductor::record`, so it runs against a temp store); the HUD's No button answers no to the request it shows, and a refused answer says why. The floating ring's position round-trips through the setting, and a fix the test turned up: a ring saved on a display that's gone (a monitor unplugged) used to open off screen; it now reopens top-right of the main display (`conductor_ring::position`, the ring's centre must lie in some work area). The thread, teammate, task, routine and handoff tools run against the real store and services under Tauri's mock runtime (`tauri` `test` feature, dev-only): `conductor_tools::run` takes `State<AppState>` and a `ToolWindow` (UI actions, focusing a thread) instead of `AppHost`, and the tests record the window side. F-CN-03 AC1's thread variant: `send_to_thread` to a thread with a pending approval sends nothing and leaves its draft as it was. The mic-failure message is now F-CN-08's words, "The microphone isn't delivering audio." (Dictate keeps F-DI-03's "Your microphone…"; UI_SPEC's Conductor error list still says "Your microphone…", so the two specs disagree). Not covered by unit tests: `message_teammate`, `run_routine`, `execute_task` and `handoff` beyond their refusals, since each starts an engine; E2E covers `launch_agents`, `wait_for_pane`, `get_usage`, `check_updates` and `list_routines`.
- E2E-M16-1's F-CN-01.4 failed once in the 0.7.1 release's full run (voicedMs 1,387 of the 2 s tone) and passed alone. The call path was right: it keeps the latest 3 s before connect (F-CN-01, D-1103), and the mic opens before the mint. The test connected a fixed 2.5 s after the mint, so a mint of about 1.1 s under load connected 3.6 s after the mic opened and the tone's first 0.6 s had aged out. The mock can now hold handshakes until released (`/__control {hold}` then `{release}`), and the test releases 2.2 s after the click, so the whole tone is in the window whatever the mint takes; the 1,800 ms threshold is unchanged.
- Docs: `content/docs/conductor.mdx` written out from what's built (starting and ending calls, the tools, the safety rules, the HUD and floating ring, call logs, persona and memory, the wake phrase, credits). Its two tables are generated from `apps/api/voice-tools.json` (`ConductorToolsTable`: all 47 tools by group with their confirm flag; `ConductorSettingsTable`: `set_setting`'s closed list), and `docs.test` pins the tables, the 20-credit rate, the 30-minute cap (`MAX_CALL_SECONDS`), the shortcuts and the setting labels to their sources. Checking it against the code found F-CN-06's voice memory commands, reflection and journal unwired; D-1121 built them and the page describes them.


## M15 · Website redesign (`website-redesign`)
The public site (`apps/web`) rebuilt to the brief on `docs-briefs` (`docs/WEBSITE_BRIEF.md`), in its phases. The owner's §15 answers were taken as defaults: keep the video, labelled as the 0.1.0 recording; Pro is "up to 64 live panes" (what `LIMITS` enforces); Linux offers only the .deb; the site follows the OS theme in CSS only; the unused Next runtime may be stripped if Lighthouse 95+ needs it; product imagery is the labelled live demo and real captures from `site.screens.ts`, never mock-ups.

### Website redesign decisions
- D-1800: one container for the whole site. `container-site` (1200 px, a 16 → 32 px gutter) frames the header, every section and the footer, which fixes the header sitting 64 px inside the content edge. The demo stage alone is 1280 px wide, so on screens of 1344 px and up it's drawn at scale 1.
- D-1801: light and dark follow the OS, in CSS only. `@tandem/ui/site.css` maps the app's light tokens under `@media (prefers-color-scheme: light)` (a copy of `[data-theme="light"]`, which stays UI_SPEC §1.2 verbatim; `site.test.ts` fails if they differ), and the root layout no longer forces `data-theme="dark"`. There's no toggle: it would need a script and storage, both forbidden on the site (F-WB-01 AC2, D-132). Product media sits on a stage that is dark in both themes, because the app's captures and the demo are dark. The site's own colour pairs (text on page, raised and card surfaces, the highlight gradient, text on the stage, the focus ring) are checked for WCAG AA in both themes in `site.test.ts`.
- D-1802: fonts load through `next/font/local` from the workspace's fontsource packages (`app/fonts.ts`): served from `/_next/static`, Inter preloaded with a metric-matched Arial fallback, so the swap moves nothing. Inter is the optical-size cut, so display sizes draw as Inter Display. Vitest aliases `next/font/local` to a stub (`test/next-font-local.ts`): it only runs under Next's compiler.
- D-1803: phones get the header links from a menu built on the Popover API (`popover="auto"`): light dismiss and Esc with no script. A browser without it still has every link in the footer.
- D-1804: lucide icons render through `components/site/Icon.tsx`, which drops `xmlns`. Inline SVG in HTML doesn't need it, and its `http://www.w3.org/2000/svg` reads as a year to the no-year test (`\b(19|20)\d{2}\b` over the markup), whose intent is about visible dates. The test is unchanged.
- D-1805: links in running text are underlined (`site.css`), never told apart by colour alone. The new axe sweep (`e2e-site/site-quality.spec.ts`) found `link-in-text-block` on seven pages of the old site.

- D-1806: the home page is 15 sections, each with its own shape (§4.3), its copy in `lib/home.ts`. Every feature in `FEATURES` appears exactly once: in a mode row, a "New in" tile or the capability index (a test checks). The "New in {version}" tiles are generated from the release's Added entries in CHANGELOG.md (the bold lead phrase is the title, its first sentence the body); a release whose Added title has no icon and docs page in `RELEASE_TILE_MAP` fails the build. Thread mode, Agent mode and the Dashboard have no real capture yet (they need a signed-in engine, `HUMAN_TODO.md`), so their rows point at the live demo or show the app's own status shapes; nothing is mocked up. The tagline's "coding agents" is drawn in the teal → sky highlight; site.test's tagline check still passes unchanged (the footer carries the tagline as one text node too), and `home.test.tsx` checks the h1's text is the tagline word for word.
- D-1807: product images ship as `<picture>` with AVIF and WebP beside the original, written by `apps/web/scripts/optimize-screens.py` (Pillow, which has both encoders here) instead of `sharp` (a native npm dependency the brief suggested). The demo still is AVIF 35 KB (from 88 KB) and loads eagerly with `fetchpriority="high"`; the Code mode capture is AVIF 37 KB (from 186 KB).
- D-1808: the plan comparison on /pricing is generated from `LIMITS`, `MONTHLY_CREDITS` and the engine registry (`PLAN_GROUPS` in `lib/pricing.ts`); the docs' limits table now reads the same rows, so the two can't differ. The "switch on with accounts" rows name their PRODUCT_SPEC feature, and a test checks each is ticked in REMAINING. Team's card gains two facts from `LIMITS`/`MONTHLY_CREDITS` (no limit on live panes; 10,000 credits a month per seat, shared). Pro and Team offer "Tell me when", a prefilled email, as the CTA.
- D-1809: the video loads nothing until played (`preload="none"`), its caption reads the recorded build from `lib/media.ts` (`VIDEO.recordedWith`, 0.1.0), and a test fails if it names the current release while the two differ.
- D-1810: no fallback glyphs. The fontsource Inter subset has no →, ←, ✓, ⌘ or ⇧, so arrows and ticks are lucide icons and keys are generated caps (`Keys`, from shortcuts.json: Ctrl+Shift+E to split right in a terminal). Privacy's hosted-features table draws its "→" as an icon with a screen-reader "to". A test checks every page's text.
- D-1811: the changelog index called 0.5.0 "the first release" and said "the code the first release is built from"; it now says "the latest release" and "the code this release is built from".

- D-1812: the docs share the site's look and legal footer. The sidebar is grouped (Get started, Modes, Features, Plans and privacy, Reference) with Fumadocs separators in `content/docs/meta.json`, and `DOCS_PAGES` follows the same order. docs.test's sidebar-order check now ignores the separators (`---Modes---`): its intent, that the sidebar lists exactly `DOCS_PAGES` in order, holds, since group labels aren't pages. `DocsFooter` gives every docs page the legal links, About and the contact address, which only they lacked (new test). Key caps are styled as caps, the page background is the site's in both themes, and the header carries the mark. Two pages said "Mod is ⌘ on a Mac", a glyph the self-hosted Inter lacks; they now say "Command".

- D-1813: the marketing pages ship no framework JavaScript. `apps/web/scripts/strip-runtime.mjs` runs at the end of `build:site` and removes Next's runtime chunks, their preloads and the inline RSC payload from every exported page except the docs (Fumadocs is a client app) and the demo app; it fails the build if any page then has a script beyond F-WB-01 AC2's two. The home page's HTML went from 280 KB to 103 KB. Nothing on those pages needed it: links are plain, the phone menu is a popover and the FAQ is `<details>`. A site-quality e2e test checks no `/_next/static/chunks/` request happens on a marketing page and that the menu and FAQ still work, and frame.test fails if a site component says "use client". Lighthouse CI now covers five URLs at 0.95 in all four categories, with CLS under 0.02 (LCP and TBT warn above 2.0 s and 100 ms).
- D-1814: `/screens/*` and `/demo/still*` are cached a day (`stale-while-revalidate` a week), appended after the existing header rules in `vercel.json`.

- D-1815: every page answers at its trailing-slash URL with no redirect. On vibezeo.com, `/changelog/0.6.0/` answered 308 to `/changelog/0.6.0`: for a static export, Vercel copies the redirects in `.next/routes-manifest.json`, and Next's own trailing-slash rule (`/:file(…[^/]+\.\w+)/ → /:file`) reads "0.6.0" as a file name. The export now sets `skipTrailingSlashRedirect: true`; `vercel.json` adds back only the other half (a slash-less page URL redirects to its slash form, files untouched, so `/api/v1/site-info` still answers 308 to its slash form, D-135) plus `/changelog/x.y.z` → `/changelog/x.y.z/`. `scripts/check-routes.mjs` ends `build:site`: it fails if any exported page's URL matches a redirect in the manifest or vercel.json, or if its slash-less URL isn't sent to it (it reproduced the live bug with Next's rule put back). New test: `app/routes.test.ts`. "Linux first" now names the release's packages from `DOWNLOADS` (0.6.0: a .deb and an .rpm), and its test reads the notes of every release up to the current one.
- D-1816: before the h1 paints, the home page downloads only its HTML, stylesheet, fonts and the demo loader. Lighthouse warned on `/` (largest contentful paint 2.4 s against the 2.0 s budget). The h1 is that paint and waits only on the stylesheet, but under simulated slow 4G two images shared the connection with it. One was the video's 53 KB poster: a `poster` attribute downloads with the page, `preload="none"` or not, ten sections down. The other was the demo still, eager with `fetchpriority="high"` (D-1807) and below the fold on a phone. Phase 6's note blamed Inter, but without its preload the paint came later and the layout shifted. The video now carries `data-poster`, and `/demo/embed.js`, the page's one script, sets the poster once the video is within 600 px of the screen. A poster image under the player doesn't work: Chromium paints a grey box over a video with no poster. The still loads lazily. On the built site (Lighthouse mobile, median of 7), LCP went from 2.42 s to 1.97 s and performance from 0.97 to 0.99. Tried and dropped: inlined CSS (`experimental.inlineCss`: 1.89 s on `/` but 3.07 s on `/docs/`), a JetBrains Mono preload and `content-visibility` on the later sections, neither of which helped. Tests: home.test's "no image downloads with the page", site.test's poster check, and wb01.spec's "the video's poster loads only as the video nears the screen".
- D-1817: the docs' links end in a slash, so they open in the page. Fumadocs builds the sidebar's URLs without one (`/docs/code-mode`). In a static export, Next's router fetches a link's payload from the link's own path, `/docs/code-mode.txt`, which doesn't exist (the export writes `/docs/code-mode/index.txt`). So each docs page made 11 failed prefetches, and each click fell back to a full page load through a redirect (a 308 on Vercel, D-1815). Fumadocs' loader strips the slash from a custom `url` too, so `lib/docs/tree.ts` (`withSlashes`) maps the tree the layout gets, which also feeds the page footer. The active item still matches, since Fumadocs compares URLs without the slash. Tests: `lib/docs/tree.test.ts`, and `e2e-site/docs-nav.spec.ts`, which checks that the link has its slash, the click keeps the page, and nothing fails or redirects; it failed before the fix.
- D-1818: The contact address is `contact@vibezeo.com` (the owner, 15 October), in place of `hello@vibezeo.com` everywhere: `SITE.contactEmail` (every page, the docs footer, `security.txt` and the early-access mail links), the troubleshooting docs page, the old static page in `apps/site`, the Snap manifest, `DEPLOY_SITE`, `OPERATING` and `HUMAN_TODO`, which also asks the owner to make the new address receive mail. `site.test` checks the address and fails on any docs page that names the old one.
- D-1820: The owner's direction for the site and the product (15 October): "don't restrict yourself… be open and go surprise me with the best". The owner's suggestions are pointers, and the agents make the calls. The website may be restructured freely: sections, pages, navigation and the strings the old brief pinned (its §1.4) change with the design, and the tests follow the new design's intent while keeping their protective checks (privacy, honesty, the legal links and contact on every page, accessibility, performance). D-1813 is relaxed: small first-party scripts may run on marketing pages when they earn their place, each named in strip-runtime's allowlist and the F-WB-01 AC2 test, within the Lighthouse budgets, and the pages still work without them. Unchanged: claims true for the shipped build, nothing from other products, no third-party requests, cookies, storage or tracking, the CSP, WCAG AA. The next-features research (`product-research`) adds bold bets beside the evidence-ranked list.
- D-1821: The home page no longer jumps to the demo when it loads. On a desktop screen the demo's frame loads right away, and the composer's autofocus inside it scrolled the page about 450 px down past the hero and took the keyboard (the live 0.7.2 site does it too). Inside the home page's frame (`?embed=1`), the demo now takes no focus until the visitor clicks or types in it, and after that focus never scrolls the page (`demo/embedFocus.ts`). Tests: `embedFocus.test.ts`, and `demo-embed.spec.ts` "the page stays where the visitor is when the demo loads, and keeps the keyboard", which failed before the change.
- D-851: Site-only updates deploy from a branch based on `main`, and app features reach vibezeo.com only with the release that ships them. The docs' tables (engines, shortcuts, settings) are generated from code, so a site deploy from `product-m0-m5` with unreleased M18 work in it would describe engines and features the download lacks (the engines session flagged it: F-EN-09 makes the registry 13 engines while 0.7.2 has 11). Website branches merge `main` (or each other), never `product-m0-m5`; a site batch goes to `main` by its own pull request and is then merged into `product-m0-m5`. Releases still go `product-m0-m5` → `main` (D-829).
- D-852: The blog's bylines draw an `Avatar`, and the site's stylesheet now scans it. The two website sessions' work met in chain22: the elite pass narrowed the stylesheet to the `packages/ui` components the site renders (D-1844), and the blog added `Avatar` after that list was written. Unscanned, its classes (`rounded-md`, `size-full`, the initials' weight) are missing from the CSS and the avatars draw unstyled. `frame.test` caught it on the combined tree, as D-1844 meant it to; `Avatar` joins the `@source` list in `app/globals.css`.
- D-853: F-WB-06 is owned by a new "M17 · Blog (S)" section in MILESTONES. It's a P2 extra beyond the original spec, and `scripts/check-coverage.sh` lets P2 features belong only to M16 or M17 on `main` (M16 to M18 on `product-m0-m5`); before this the check failed on the blog's branch with F-WB-06 unowned. §20 counts 166 features, 34 of them P2, with M17 owning 10. The spec now names the Copy script that D-1860 allowed on post pages, as the tests already did: index and tag pages carry no script, and a post page only its JSON-LD and `/blog/blog.js`.
- D-854: MILESTONES §20 lists every owned feature again: 170 (65 P0, 67 P1, 38 P2), M17 10 and M18 4. Its table had lacked F-WT-01 to F-WT-04 since they were added on `competitive-gaps`, and chain21's F-WT-06, F-WT-09 and F-AC-08. `check-coverage` reads only the **Owns:** lines, so it never saw the table drift; the rows were checked against those lines and the spec's priorities when they were filled in.
- D-855: The runaway guard's Pause stops a process group only for an agent on this computer (F-AC-09, found reviewing chain23). An SSH pane's local process is `ssh`, so the pane's foreground group was the connection: stopping it left the agent on the host working (and spending) while the card said Paused, and a long stop could let the server drop the session. After the interrupt key's 5 s, `after_grace` now stops the group only when the pane's workspace is `local`; on an SSH host the card says "Couldn't pause an agent on an SSH host: send it a message or stop it.", and WSL panes or an unreadable workspace never get a signal. `f_ac_09_7_only_a_local_agent_is_stopped_after_the_grace` failed on the old rule (it stopped group 42 on an SSH pane) and passes now. The spec gains AC7, and the docs say an SSH agent only gets the interrupt key.
- D-856: `KIRO_HOME` is reserved in a worktree's `worktree.json` (F-WT-06), like every provider home. chain23's gate failed on `f_wt_06_6_every_provider_home_the_registry_uses_is_reserved`: F-EN-09 added Kiro CLI with `providerHomeEnv: KIRO_HOME`, and `tandem_git::setup::reserved_reason` didn't list it. The launch environment already wins over the worktree's (D-2448), so a Kiro pane kept its account's home; the reserved list is the second guard, refusing such a file with the reason. The test is the one D-2448 added for this.
- D-857: Pausing an agent stops the pane's shell first and continues it last (F-AC-09, found by chain23's E2E). An agent pane runs `$SHELL -lic '<engine>; <handback>; exec $SHELL -l'` (F-CD-03), so the agent is a job of an interactive shell, in a group of its own. Stopping only that group let the shell see its job stop: it took the terminal back, ran the rest of its command (the handback mark, status 147) and became a new shell. The pane went idle and Resume continued the agent in the background, where it outlived the pane: the next launch opened the orphans dialog ("1 agent process from your last session is still running"), which covered the next spec's Add folder button (E2E-M18-DB04 failed after E2E-M18-AC09, and passed alone). Checked with bash and dash in a real terminal. Now `suspend::pause` stops the shell, then the job; `suspend::resume` continues the job, then the shell; and closing a pane continues its shell before ending it. Tests: `f_ac_09_8_a_paused_agent_keeps_its_pane_and_its_shell_never_hands_it_back` (a real PTY with `sh -ic`; HANDBACK-147 on the old order), two ordering tests with a fake control, and E2E-M18-AC09.2 now expects the pane still Working after Resume (it read idle on the old order). The spec cleans up after itself and restores the guard's defaults.
- D-858: M18's decision ranges, reassigned while reviewing chain24's candidates. F-TL-11 took D-2480 and D-2481, which REMAINING had set aside for the performance session. So F-SH-14 now starts at D-2560, and each session gets a second range when its first runs out:
  - engines (`m16d-teammate-extras`): D-2500 to D-2519;
  - worktrees (`competitive-gaps`): D-2520 to D-2539;
  - usage (`product-research`): D-2540 to D-2559;
  - reliability (`perf-open`): D-2560 to D-2579.
  The review loop (`m16a-conductor`) still has D-2425 to D-2439. REMAINING's M18 section names each session's next features.
- D-859: A read-only thread never joins the review queue (F-DB-04), found by chain24's review of F-CN-09.
  - **The bug:** the queue accepted any thread in a workspace. A second opinion's reviewer (F-WT-05) is a Read only thread in the worktree it reviews, so when it finished, the worktree's diff made it an item: the review showed another agent's work under the reviewer's name, and Send back (or walk-and-review's) typed "Address it…" into the reviewer while the agent that made the changes got nothing.
  - **The fix:** `reviews_thread` keeps threads that can change their folder (Edits or Full access); Read only threads and teammate chats stay out.
  - **Tests:** `f_db_04_1_a_read_only_thread_never_joins_the_queue` (it didn't compile before the rule existed), and E2E-M18-DB04 gains a Read only thread finishing in the worktree the previous case changed, which never joins.
  - It ships in 0.8.0; the Conductor session leaves `session()` alone so its F-CN-09 fixes don't conflict.
- D-860: Pause waits until the pane's shell has really stopped before it stops the agent (F-AC-09), found by the performance session's gate on a7bac8c1: D-857's PTY test failed about 1 run in 3 to 6 with HANDBACK-147.
  - **The race:** `killpg(shell, SIGSTOP)` only queues the stop; the shell stops when it next runs. Stopping the agent at once woke the shell, still blocked in its wait for the job, and Linux reports a stopped child before it looks at pending signals. So the shell's wait could return "job stopped" before the shell itself stopped. Continued later, it acted on that stale status: it took the terminal back, ran the handback and left the agent behind, which is D-857's bug, just intermittent.
  - **The fix:** `ProcessControl::wait_stopped` (sysinfo's process status, polled every millisecond on Linux and macOS; a no-op in fakes). `suspend::pause` stops the shell, waits up to 500 ms for it to be stopped, and only then stops the agent. A shell that doesn't stop in time is continued, and the pause fails (`NotStopped`), so the card says it couldn't pause. Continuing needs no wait: SIGCONT clears the stop when it's sent.
  - **Tests:** `f_ac_09_8_waits_until_a_stopped_process_has_really_stopped` (a real process: running never counts, stopped does, gone doesn't), `f_ac_09_8_a_shell_that_doesnt_stop_leaves_its_job_running`, and the order tests now include the wait. The real-PTY test passed 30 runs of 30 (it failed 1 in 6 before the fix here, and 1 in 3 for the performance session).
- D-861: Saving "Worktree setup…" keeps the file's `preview` (F-WT-09), found while writing 0.8.0's notes. The dialog has no preview field (the preview is written by hand in `.vibezeo/worktree.json`), so its save sent copy, setup, teardown, ports and env only, and the core rewrote the file without the preview: changing any setup field deleted it. The dialog now sends the loaded file's preview back unchanged. `F-WT-09.1 saving the dialog keeps the file's preview, which it doesn't edit (D-861)` failed before the fix.
- D-862: The blog's seed-post test keeps earlier releases' posts (F-WB-06), found while writing 0.8.0's post. It listed exactly two files, `introducing-vibezeo.mdx` and the current release's post, so adding "What's new in 0.8.0" would have meant deleting 0.7.2's post, breaking its link and its entry in the feed. It now requires those two and lets any other post through only as a release post (`whats-new-in-X-Y-Z.mdx`) for a version the changelog has. The first-party checks on every post are unchanged.
- D-863: A new worktree's tab waits for the pane its trusted setup or preview opens, instead of starting a shell beside it (F-WT-09), found by 0.8.0's E2E run: E2E-M18-WT09-1's Compare previews failed with "beta's preview never answered".
  - **The race:** New worktree… selected the worktree at once, and its empty tab's grid started a shell (`ensurePane`) while `prepareWorktree` was still asking the core for the Preview pane, so the worktree got both. On Free's 4 live panes, with the repository's shell and alpha's shell and Preview already live, the plain shell spawned first and took the last one: the Preview pane said "Free includes 4 live panes" and the preview never started. Whichever pane spawned first won, and chain23's run had started the Preview first. A trusted setup had the same race against its setup pane (E2E-M17-WT1 removes two worktrees before that step for this reason).
  - **The fix:** `useLayout.expect(tabId, until)`. `ensurePane` waits for a pane another flow is placing, and starts a shell only if none came or the flow failed. `createWorktree` starts `prepareWorktree` itself, and the new tab expects it before the worktree is selected. A worktree whose trusted setup or preview runs now opens with that pane alone. One waiting for its review, with nothing to run, or on an SSH host gets its shell as before, one round trip later.
  - **Tests:** `F-WT-09.1 a new worktree's trusted preview is its tab's only pane, with no shell beside it (D-863)` (New worktree…, with the Preview pane arriving 30 ms after the tab loads; a shell was created beside it before the fix), `F-WT-09.2 a new worktree waiting for its review gets a shell meanwhile (D-863)`, and the layout store's `F-WT-09 an empty tab expecting another flow's pane starts a shell only if none came (D-863)`. E2E-M18-WT09-1 now also checks that beta's only pane is its Preview. Alone, the spec passed once on the old code (the race depends on timing, which is why the unit test sets the order) and three times with the fix. The 15 spec files that make worktrees (the launch sheet, New worktree…, Best of N on this computer and on an SSH host, setup, review, overlap, the review queue, instructions, second opinion, runtime and previews) passed in one run.
- D-864: Code blocks in the docs scroll inside a box of their own, and long inline code wraps (F-WB-01, F-WB-02), found by 0.8.0's site check: on a 320 px phone `/docs/code-mode/` was 750 px wide, so "no page scrolls sideways" failed. The docs render shiki's `<pre>` under Fumadocs' typography plugin, which gives `pre` no style at all, so the docs' first code block (the preview's `worktree.json`, added with F-WT-09) had no box and pushed the page sideways at every width; so did the unbroken `DATABASE_URL=postgres://localhost/app_{{slug}}` in F-WT-06's text. The site's browser tests run only in a release's site check, so the batches that added them didn't catch it. `docs.css` now gives `pre` the blog's code box (the `surface` edge, fill and shadow, the mono type at 13 px, shiki's colours by the OS theme) with `overflow-x: auto`, and inline code `overflow-wrap: anywhere`. The 320 px check and axe in both themes pass on the rebuilt site.
- D-865: A desktop's plan and credit updates no longer stop about 25 s after it reconnects (F-BE-04), found by 0.8.0's second full E2E run: E2E-M8-7's Polish charged its 3 credits, but the desktop never fetched `/v1/me` again. An SSE stream on `/v1/me/events` stops twice: on its abort, and again when its heartbeat loop next wakes, up to 25 s later. When a desktop reconnected (after signing in, or a plan change), the old stream's abort emptied the user's listener set and removed it; the new stream made a new set; then the old stream's second stop, still holding the empty set, removed the user's entry again, which was now the new stream's. Events went nowhere until the desktop's next reconnect. In the failing run the Enhance's event (22:06:11) arrived, and the Polish's (22:06:25) came 2 s after that second stop. `EventHub.listen`'s stop now removes the user's entry only while it is still that stream's own set. `F-BE-04 a closed stream's late cleanup never silences the stream that replaced it (D-865)` failed before the fix. The hosted API isn't deployed yet, so no user met it. With the fix, the API's 212 tests pass, the seven E2E specs that wait on plan or credit updates over the stream passed, and E2E-M8-7 passed three runs in a row.
- D-866: Dictate's Fn fallback notice has its own string (F-DI-02), found by checking chain24's merged catalog for keys written twice. `en.json` held `dictate.setup.fnFallback` twice: M8's label for the macOS shortcut section's "Fn not working? Use Control+Option" button, and chain19's notice after Fn went unheard (D-524). JSON keeps the last, so on a Mac the button read "Fn didn't reach Vibezeo, so your Dictate key is now Control+Option. Press it to try." The notice is now `dictate.setup.fnFellBack`. `i18n.test` fails on any key written twice, and `F-DI-02 Use Control+Option and the notice after Fn went unheard have their own words` renders both; each failed on the old catalog.
- D-867: M18's decision ranges, extended while reviewing chain25's candidates.
  - `competitive-gaps` used all of D-2520 to D-2539 (F-CD-23 took D-2536 to D-2539), so it continues at D-2580 to D-2599. F-CD-23's repository-trust decision is D-2580. It was first suggested as D-2540, which F-TL-11 already holds.
  - `product-research` finishes D-2540 to D-2559 with B5's second slice, and continues at D-2800 to D-2819 from F-EX-07.
  - Both new ranges were unused on every branch.
- D-868: 0.9.0 ships without new demo surfaces (D-128).
  - The rule's first half holds: everything the demo shows is in the download, and the demo's plan (`ENTITLEMENT`) is the download's Free plan.
  - None of the six new features is in the demo. Landing, pull-request results and the intent radar need worktrees with real history, engine versions and the session library read the engines' own files, and the sandbox needs bubblewrap. A simulated core can show them only as scripted scenes.
  - They are queued for the demo with the website's product scenes (`site-motion`, DESIGN §5.1), tracked in REMAINING's website section.
- D-869: Vibezeo is free (the owner, 24 October 2026): "Remove the costing page. I need it free."
  - **No paid plans.** No prices, plan comparisons or credit prices anywhere on the site. `/pricing/` and anything under it redirect to `/download/` (vercel.json). The home page's plan teaser, the nav and footer links, `lib/pricing.ts`, llms.txt's price lines and site-info's `pricing` block are gone, and site-info says `free: true`. DECISIONS' business model records it.
  - **0.9.0 ships as built.** Its download still has the earlier Free plan's limits (4 live panes, 3 workspaces, one thread at a time, threads with Claude Code and Codex, one account per engine). The notes, the post, the download page and the docs' limits table say so, and say the next release removes them.
  - **The next release unlocks every local feature** for everyone: panes, workspaces, engine accounts, six threads, every chat engine, teammates, routines, tasks in their own lanes, the Night Shift and session search. Hosted features (cloud dictation, Enhance and Polish, dictionary sync, Conductor's calls, the phone relay) need servers. They stay off until Vibezeo accounts open, and the site never prices them.
  - **Docs:** the routines page opens with "In the next release", and Conductor's with "Comes with Vibezeo accounts". Conductor's credit costs and Dictate's credit prices are gone.
  - **Tests:** the site's pricing tests (F-WB-01 AC3, the comparison table, llms.txt's and site-info's prices) checked a requirement this decision removes. They now check that no page shows a price or links to `/pricing/`, that the redirect is there, and that site-info and llms.txt say Vibezeo is free.
- D-870: The repository goes public (the owner, 24 October 2026: "make that repo public"), with no open-source licence until the owner picks one.
  - **Visibility** is a repository setting that agents can't change. The owner switched it on 24 October, after these checks.
  - **Checked first:**
    - A scan of the 87 branches and 93 pull requests (873 commits, 10,103 files) looked for keys and tokens: AWS, Stripe, GitHub, Slack, Google, OpenAI, Anthropic, Sentry, PostHog, npm and Vercel keys, private keys, JWTs, minisign secret keys, and secret-shaped assignments. It found 43 matches, all made-up values in tests: the redaction tests' samples, patterned keys such as `sk-ant-api03-AAAA…`, AWS's documented example key, and the phone protocol's test vectors.
    - No `.env`, key, keystore or service-account file was ever committed.
    - Once the repository is public, GitHub's secret scanning may flag some of those test values. They can be closed as used in tests.
    - Commits are signed with Claude's address or the owner's GitHub noreply address, and the files hold no personal address (only test fixtures).
    - `build-kit/reference/` was never committed.
    - The workflows run outside code only on `pull_request`, where the token is read-only and no secrets are passed. `macos.yml` offers a self-hosted runner only through `workflow_dispatch`.
  - **Licence.** Three declarations disagreed. Cargo.toml said MIT, an M0 default nobody chose. The Flatpak and Snap metadata say proprietary. The app's third-party notices listed Vibezeo's own crates under MIT. A licence can't be taken back from code already published under it, and the owner hasn't chosen one: GO_TO_MARKET says "Don't promise a licence", and as with D-1711, an agent doesn't grant or accept a licence for the owner. So the code is all rights reserved:
    - Cargo.toml says `LicenseRef-proprietary`, like the store metadata.
    - about.toml leaves Vibezeo's own crates (`publish = false`) out of the notices, since they aren't third-party. That leaves 629 crates listed, none of them `tandem-*`, and the SBOM's component reads `LicenseRef-proprietary`.
    - The README says that no licence has been granted. Choosing one is in HUMAN_TODO.
  - **For visitors:**
    - The README opens with what Vibezeo is and that it's free, then the download, the docs, the working name Tandem and the licence.
    - SECURITY.md says how to report a vulnerability privately (contact@vibezeo.com) and what Vibezeo promises.
  - **CI:** public repositories get GitHub Actions minutes free on standard runners, so `ci.yml` runs again once the repository is public.
- D-871: The Dictate widget can no longer send events to the main window. This was found by the release review of `free-for-everyone`.
  - **Why it had the permission:** its capability kept `core:event:allow-emit-to` only so that the widget's See plans could open the plans dialog (D-825).
  - **The risk:** with See plans gone, any script in the widget could still send the main window events such as `app:deep-link`, which adds a workspace.
  - **The fix:** `widget.json` drops the permission, and lib.rs's capability test now pins the shorter list. The floating ring keeps emit-to for its call log (D-1117).
  - **Docs and comments:** ARCHITECTURE §10.2 no longer lists a sign-in check or `signed_out` for routines (D-2902), and four comments stop describing plans.
  - **Smoke test:** `scripts/agent/smoke-appimage.sh` clicks Start where the welcome's button now is (y 451). The card lost its plan line, so the old y 508 missed the button. The app was fine: a click on the button opens setup.
  - **Follow-ups after the release:** the same review's other notes (dead billing code, two dead telemetry events, the hosted build's sign-in countdown, stale spec lines, test gaps).

### Website redesign log
- Merged product-m0-m5 (#46, D-829's `git.deploymentEnabled`) into website-redesign; vercel.json keeps both that block and D-1814's cache headers. The home page's proof band adds "Linux first", only facts the build ships: the .deb's distributions (from `lib/release.ts`), Dictate on X11 and Wayland, SSH hosts on x86-64 and arm64 (a test checks the 0.5.0 CHANGELOG says them). No claim about price or markup.
- Phase 1 (foundation): `@tandem/ui/site.css` (OS light theme, display type, containers, motion), `buttonClass()` with `xl` and `outline`, fonts through next/font, the sticky header with the Popover phone menu, the four-column footer, `Section`, `Keys`, `CommandLine`, `ProductShot`, `PageHero`. New: `site.test.ts` (light mirror, contrast in both themes), `frame.test.tsx`, `e2e-site/site-quality.spec.ts` (axe in dark and light on every page, no sideways scroll at 320 px, reduced motion).
- Phase 2 (home): the 15 sections; `lib/home.ts`, `lib/media.ts`; download size from the served file (`releaseAssets().bytes`). New: `home.test.tsx`.
- Phase 3 (inner pages): Pricing (plan columns, generated comparison, credits, billing facts), Download (icon, version, size, install and verify commands, steps with generated keys, previous versions), Changelog (release rail, milestone grid) and version pages (meta column, prev/next, Added as cards), Demo, About, prose pages, 404. New: `pages.test.tsx`.
- Phase 4 (docs): grouped sidebar, DocsFooter, the site's typography and caps (D-1812). One test edit, docs.test's sidebar order (separators ignored, intent kept).
- Phase 6 (performance): the runtime strip (D-1813), media caching (D-1814), the five-URL LHCI config. Lighthouse mobile, median of 3 (performance / accessibility / best practices / SEO): `/` 97/100/100/100 (LCP 2.4 s: the h1 waits on Inter under simulated slow 4G), `/pricing/`, `/download/` and `/changelog/` 100/100/100/100, `/docs/` 95/100/100/100 (it keeps its runtime); CLS 0 everywhere. Before the redesign: `/` 95/96, `/pricing/` 99/100.
- Checks on the phase 1–3 tree: web vitest 73/73, `build:site`, site Playwright 13/13 (the 9 existing plus 4 new), axe clean in both themes. No existing test changed.
- `chain8` (coordinating session, D-1816 and D-1817): Lighthouse CI on its five URLs passed every assertion (median of 3). Performance: `/` 99, with LCP at 1.97 s and no warnings; `/pricing/`, `/download/` and `/changelog/` 100; `/docs/` 97 (93 to 97). `/docs/` still warns on LCP (2.3 s) and total blocking time (140 ms), from hydrating Fumadocs' layout. Accessibility, best practices and SEO are 100 everywhere, and CLS is 0. Web vitest 85/85, site Playwright 16/16 (two new).

## M15 · Website elite pass (`site-elite`)
The owner (15 October): the site looks basic for a product we sell; make it feel premium. This pass studies 14 sites and records the findings and Vibezeo's direction in `docs/WEBSITE_ELITE.md`, then rebuilds the visual system and every marketing page and the docs on it. Every claim stays true for 0.7.2.

### Website elite pass decisions
- D-1819: the docs' largest paint is inside its budget. The coordinating session's reading held. Locally the docs' first paint came after Next's and Fumadocs' chunks had downloaded and run, and Lighthouse's simulation (Lantern) counts every request finished before the observed paint toward the largest paint. Deferring the chunks by hand in a copy of `/docs/` took the largest paint from 2.72 s to 1.82 s, and performance from 0.96 to 0.99 (lhci's server, 3 runs each).
  - **The deferral, built:** `scripts/defer-docs.mjs` (in `build:site` after `strip-runtime`) moves each docs page's chunk tags and preloads into one first-party loader, `/docs-boot.js`, which adds them once a frame has painted. The inline RSC payload stays.
  - **Less JavaScript (the blocking time):** the docs no longer use Fumadocs' client layout (`DocsLayout`, `RootProvider`, its page components; about 200 KB of uncompressed JavaScript). `DocsShell` renders the frame on the server (D-1839), and only Next's router remains, for D-1817's in-page links. The docs' JavaScript is 108 KB gzip, down from about 180.
  - **The docs stylesheet:** it now loads only Fumadocs' typography plugin. Its `preset.css` scanned Fumadocs' whole `dist`, which made 64 KB of utilities (now 15 KB), and its `.hidden` came after globals.css and hid the site header's links.
  - **Result:** `/docs/` over 5 runs gives median largest paint 1.82 s, blocking time 80 ms and performance 0.99.
  - **Tests (`app/docs.test.tsx`):** the deferral, the loader, the server-only docs, the sidebar's groups and the shell. `docs-nav.spec.ts` still passes.
- D-1830: light, depth and edges are tokens in `site.css`, each with a light-theme value: blooms (`--site-glow-a/b`), the rings' line and lit line, grain, gradient edges (`--site-edge-top/bottom`) with a 1 px inner highlight, fills with light at the top, and three layered elevations (cards, lifted cards, product frames). The `surface`, `surface-raised` and `surface-hover` utilities and the `Card` component draw cards with them. `site.test.ts` checks every such token has a light value, and that the primary button's label passes AA on each stop of its gradient.
- D-1831: motion uses an expo-out curve: 700 ms reveals (opacity, a 1.5 rem rise, a 6 px blur), a slower `rise` for product frames, 180–260 ms hovers, and the phone menu's sheet sliding down with `@starting-style`. Everything that moves sits under `prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference`; a new test cuts those blocks out of `site.css` and fails if any animation remains.
- D-1832: marketing type grows a step. The home h1 is 44 → 76 px at −0.042 em, page h1s 36 → 56 px, h2s 30 → 48 px, figures 40 → 64 px mono. Headings use `text-sheen` (ink at the cap height, slightly softer at the baseline; monochrome, unlike the gradient headlines studied). The site turns on Inter's `cv01 cv09 cv11 ss03`. Heading balance lives in `@layer base`, so utilities can override it.
- D-1833: radii nest. The app's 6 px controls stay (no pills); cards are 12 px, product frames 16 px and the screen inside a frame 10 px.
- D-1834: Vibezeo's own backdrop is the two-ring mark at page scale (`Orbit`, inline SVG): hairline rings, a fainter echo pair and light gathered where they overlap. The light sits on the page, never on a frame, which keeps clear of the glowing demo frame and the light beam in the brief's §1.2. Product captures and the live demo sit in a neutral bezel (`frame`). The bezel carries no transform, filter or backdrop-filter, and `#try` no stacking context, because the demo's full screen is `position: fixed` at z-index 1000 and must rise over the sticky header (a first try with `isolate` trapped it; the site E2E caught it).
- D-1835: site buttons come from `siteButton()` in `packages/ui` (`btn-*` utilities in `site.css`): the primary is the accent as a gradient with a lit top edge, a dark ring and a soft accent glow; the secondary is glass with a hairline. Class names are whole strings so Tailwind finds them.
- D-1836: the header is glass with the links centred and a hairline that appears on scroll; the phone menu is a sheet from the top with large rows and a full-width Download. The footer has a lit horizon, the brand with the current release (linking its notes), four columns and the owner line.
- D-1837: sections take three header shapes (left, centred, split title/lead) and an optional horizon hairline. Page heroes and the closing call to action share `PageHero` and `CtaBand` (the mark on a lit tile at the centre of slowly turning rings).
- D-1838: product captures are the real 0.7.2 app at 2× (`code-mode` 2880×1800, `launch-grid` 1088×1220), taken through the desktop E2E harness under Xvfb with `GDK_SCALE=2`. `code-mode` shows four real terminals over a clone of the repository; `launch-grid` shows Claude Code detected (never started). The debug build's DEV badge, which release builds don't show, was hidden before each shot. They ship as AVIF and WebP at 1× and 2× with a PNG fallback, and each names its build (`SCREENS` in `lib/media.ts`; a test checks the build is in CHANGELOG.md and every file ships). Dashboard and Thread captures were possible only with the fake engine, so they aren't used.
- D-1839: the inner pages and the docs take the system. Pricing: plan cards side by side, Free lit and marked "Available now" (the only plan on sale), Pro and Team "With accounts", full-width actions, the generated comparison in a lit card with accent group labels, and the closing band. Download: the app icon on a lit tile, one Linux card with both packages as buttons, Install and Verify side by side (checksums in wells). Changelog: a glowing current-release node on the rail, each release in a card. Version pages, About, Demo (the still in the bezel, at 2×), the prose pages and the 404 use the same hero, surfaces and hairlines. The docs (`docs.css`) map Fumadocs' card and border colours to the site's, light the top of the page, give the sidebar glass with mono group labels and a lit active item, set titles at display size with the sheen, and make key caps and inline code match the site; every docs page ends with a lit download card above the legal footer.
- D-1840: no film grain. Lighthouse CI warned on `/`: largest paint 2.05 s against its 2.0 s budget, up from 1.96 s. The soft-light grain layers over the lit bands made the first paint slow enough that the lazy demo still and the mono font finished before it. Lantern counts every request that finishes before the observed paint, so both joined the largest paint. Measured on copies of the built page: without the grain layers the largest paint is 1.96 s. Removing the scroll reveals or the h1's sheen changed nothing measurable. The grain tile, its utility and its token are gone. The home h1 is drawn in solid ink rather than `text-sheen`: a transparent fill took it out of the largest-paint candidates, so a container div was measured instead. With both changes `/` measures 1.97 s (5 runs), with the h1 as its largest paint as D-1816 intends.
- D-1841: phase 2, after the owner widened the brief (via the coordinating session): restructure freely, add motion, and lead with neutrality and ownership.
  - **Hero:** the h1 is `HERO.title` ("Your agents. Your machine. Your plan."). The lead counts the engines from `ENGINES`. Four `OWNERSHIP` facts follow, each true of 0.7.2, and no other product is named. `SITE.tagline` stays for titles, the share image and the footer.
  - **Home order:** hero, demo, how data moves, engines, modes, capabilities, Built with Claude, figures, founder, pricing, FAQ, CTA.
  - **Moved off the home page:** the "New in" tiles and the roadmap to /changelog/ (`#new`, `#roadmap`), the video to /demo/ (`#video`, with its poster as an attribute below the fold). Dictate, Plugins, Tasks and SSH workspaces join the capability index.
  - **Tests rewritten to the new intent:** the h1 and ownership facts, the section ids, the video and its honest caption on /demo/, the tiles on /changelog/, every feature placed once, no video or poster on the home page (`wb01.spec.ts`), and the changelog's version order read from its Releases list.
- D-1842: motion that tells the story, in CSS with transforms and a stroke dash only, all off under reduced motion.
  - **Rings:** the hero's two rings draw in on load (`pathLength` 100).
  - **Engine orbit:** the engines' monograms orbit them once every 180 s (`EngineOrbit`: one composited layer; each monogram counter-turns to stay upright; hidden on phones). Axe flagged their letters at 70 % opacity, so they're drawn at full opacity under the mask.
  - **Data flow:** packets move on the data-flow lines that carry data, and the line to Vibezeo's servers stays still.
  - **Largest paint:** the hero's lead became the page's largest paint and lost its fade-in. A phone gets an 800 px demo still (`still-800`, written by `optimize-screens.py`), because the still loads before the largest paint. Without it `/` measured a 1.96 s median with runs up to 2.04 s; with it the median is 1.89 s.
- D-1843: the docs run no script. A full Lighthouse CI run gave `/docs/` a blocking-time warning (119 ms; earlier runs gave 67–104 ms). Its only script was Next's router, kept so docs links opened in the page (D-1817), and evaluating it is the blocking task. The docs are now stripped like the marketing pages (`strip-runtime` no longer skips them), and `DocsShell` uses plain links, each at its slash URL. Pages change through the site's cross-document view transition. D-1819's loader (`defer-docs.mjs`, `/docs-boot.js`) is removed, since there's nothing left to defer. `/docs/` now has blocking time 0 and largest paint 1.81 s. `docs-nav.spec.ts` now pins the new intent: slash links, no redirect or failed request, no script on the docs, and the active link after navigating.
- D-1844: headroom, after the coordinating session's Lighthouse runs on chain20 (`/` 1.96–2.01 s against the 2.0 s budget; it asked for under 1.8 s here).
  - **Fonts:** trimmed to the weights the site draws. `scripts/trim-fonts.py` (fonttools) instances Inter to weights 400–650, keeping its optical-size axis, and JetBrains Mono to 400–500. Inter goes from 73 KB to 52 KB and the mono from 40 KB to 29 KB; both load before the home page's largest paint. They live in `app/fonts/` beside their OFL licences, and `fonts.ts` points at them.
  - **Result:** `/` measures a 1.75 s median over 5 runs (worst 1.82 s), down from 1.89 s.
  - **The stylesheet:** it scans only the 12 packages/ui component files the site and the portal render, not the whole package. The desktop app's utilities went into every page's CSS: 87 KB raw down to 78 KB, which matters because lhci's static server sends it uncompressed. A `frame.test` check fails if a page imports a component the list misses. With both changes `/` measures 1.73–1.75 s over 6 runs.
  - **Ruled out on copies of the built page:** an inlined favicon and `content-visibility` on the sections below the fold moved nothing. The spread in first paint (1.21 s or 1.36 s) comes from elsewhere.
  - **Tests:** `frame.test` checks the trimmed files, their licences and ranges, and that the site's CSS and markup ask for no weight outside them.
  - **Anchors:** the coordinating session saw them land under the sticky header. They now land with the section's eyebrow 2 rem below it (`scroll-mt-section`: html's scroll padding clears the header, and the section's top padding is taken back). A new `site-quality` spec checks five anchors.

### Website elite pass log
- Study: three parallel agents read 14 sites (BridgeMind, Linear, Vercel; Raycast, Warp, Zed, Cursor; Resend, Supabase, Ghostty, Tailwind CSS, Factory). Findings, the signature looks to avoid and the direction are in `docs/WEBSITE_ELITE.md`.
- Foundation: tokens (D-1830 to D-1833), `siteButton`, `Orbit`, `Card`, `ProductShot` in the bezel with its build label and 2× sources, `Section`/`SectionHeader`, `PageHero`, `CtaBand`, the header and footer. Home restyled on it, with the 0.7.2 captures. Checks: web vitest 89/89, `packages/ui` 411/411, `build:site`, site Playwright 16/16 (the privacy table now wraps at 320 px). The gate's Rust half isn't affected by these TypeScript and CSS changes; the full gate runs before READY.
- The docs' chunks start after the first paint (D-1819); the docs render on the server in the site's frame. `/docs/` median largest paint 1.82 s (was 2.72), blocking time 80 ms, performance 0.99.
- Pages: pricing, download, changelog and its version pages, about, demo, privacy, terms, security, 404 and the docs on the system (D-1839). Web vitest 89/89.
