- D-2415: "Continue with another engine" writes the brief from the transcript with §6.11's fallback (the last 200 lines, redacted) instead of asking an engine: the source session isn't running, and a headless run would spend a turn the user didn't ask for. The sheet offers the session's folder only; no worktree, thread or "close the original".
- D-2416: Session text goes through the global redactor (`Redactor::global()`, F-TL-03's patterns and registered secrets) as the library parses it, so a title, the transcript view, the Continue brief and `session-index.db` never hold a token a user pasted into a session. The engine's own file is untouched; the library just never copies a secret out of it (CLAUDE.md: secrets live only in the keychain). The test searches a session by its other words and checks the token is in none of the index's files.
- D-2500 (review of F-EN-08, 16 October): Stop's identity is the pid, the engine and the process's start time from the OS. The list sends the start time; Stop lists again and refuses unless all three still match, so a process that exited and whose pid went to another engine process isn't signalled. A pidfd would close the remaining gap between the check and the signal on Linux only; with the start time the gap is a few milliseconds, so it isn't used.
- D-2501: Only a Thread turn on this computer moves "last good", and only the engine's own failure counts against a version: an error event with stage Spawn, or the code `engine_exited` (its process died mid-turn), `ErrorStage::engine_fault`. An engine's generic error result (Claude Code's non-success `subtype`, Codex's unclassified `turn_failed`, ACP's `prompt_failed`), being offline, signed out, a model the account can't use, a tool's own failure, a stop or a usage limit record nothing: the engine answered, so it works. The thread service remembers whether a turn had an engine fault and checks the thread's workspace is local (or none) before calling the health sink (re-review, 18 October).
- D-2502: Install and Roll back from Settings and the thread banner open their pane in a local workspace's tab: the active tab when it's local, else the first local workspace's, else none, and the sheet then offers Copy only. Detection, which rechecks the result, reads this computer.
- D-2503: A Pin may not set any engine's provider-home variable, `HOME`, `PATH`, `SHELL`, `NODE_OPTIONS`, `NODE_PATH`, `BASH_ENV`, `ENV`, `ZDOTDIR`, `LD_*`, `DYLD_*` or `TANDEM_*` (registry validation), and its environment is set before the provider home and the status hook, which win. Version regexes keep a pre-release suffix (`0.47.0-alpha.3`). npm's `node_modules` under Bun's, Yarn's or pnpm's global folders is "installed another way" (no npm rollback), the home is canonicalized before it's compared with the engine's own versions folder, and `npm view` runs in the data folder so a project's `.npmrc` can't steer it.
- D-2504 (review of F-TH-17): The index is format version 2 (`PRAGMA user_version`): any other version is deleted and built again, so an index from before redaction doesn't keep a token. `secure_delete` is on, so deleted rows are overwritten. Turning search off through any path (the switch, `settings_set`, `settings_reset`, Conductor's `set_setting`) deletes it, and `settings_set` refuses turning it on without Pro. A search re-checks the setting after indexing and deletes the index if it was turned off meanwhile. Index writes take one lock and a 5 s busy timeout, and the UI gives listing and the first search 2 minutes.
- D-2505: The library's reads are bounded: a file is opened with `O_NOFOLLOW | O_NONBLOCK`, must be a regular file of at most 64 MB after opening, and is read through a 64 MB limit. A line is read at most 4 MB at a time and a longer one is skipped to its newline without being held. A transcript keeps its last 2,000 turns. `projects/` and `sessions/` must be real folders, not links; the home itself may be a link. A listing serves the commands that follow it for 30 s instead of every command reading every home again.

### M18 · Next features: engines log
- F-EN-07: the vendor pages changed the draft: Gemini CLI's `GEMINI.md` takes an `@AGENTS.md` import, so no settings file is edited and no symbolic link is needed (D-2400). The UI's Undo toast sits under the open dialog's modal layer, so the test finds it with `hidden: true`. Building `chain19` filled this container's disk twice (test binaries are kept per build); removing all but the newest of each in `target/debug/deps` freed 8.5 GB and 6.7 GB.

## M16 mobile-apps checklist (LAN pairing, Vibezeo Remote, push, Linux stores)
Built on branch `mobile-apps` (from `m16c-phone-companion`, with `product-m0-m5` merged). Owns F-RM-04 LAN pairing, F-RM-05 Vibezeo Remote app, F-RM-06 Encrypted push and F-RM-07 Linux store packages (new spec entries, PRODUCT_SPEC §4.14; MILESTONES M16 owns them). Decisions are D-2000–D-2099.
- [x] F-RM-04 LAN pairing.
  - **Desktop relay.** `services::phone_lan`: `LanServer` binds one socket per private IPv4 address (RFC 1918 as `lan`, 100.64.0.0/10 as `vpn`), never `0.0.0.0`, loopback, a public address or IPv6, and re-scans every 30 s. `accept_one` closes connections from outside the LAN unread; `serve_connection` reads the request head itself (httparse), refuses a `Host` that isn't the socket's IP (421), and either upgrades `/v1/relay/:pairing_id` (tokio-tungstenite on the raw stream, 64 KB limit) or serves the phone web app. `LanHub` is the relay's pairing table with F-BE-11's semantics (D-2003); the desktop's end is in-process (`attach_desktop` gives the same `RelayLink` the hosted relay socket does), so `PhoneService` didn't change beyond `pair_begin_via(PairVia::Lan)` and the `PhoneNet::{create_lan_pairing, forget_local}` defaults. `LanNet` makes a random 32-byte token per pairing and keeps only its SHA-256 (keychain `NS.remote / lan:<pid>`), restoring it after a restart.
  - **Commands.** `phone_pair_begin {via: "lan", address?}` (no account or plan; org policy still applies), `phone_lan_state`, `phone_lan_set {enabled, port?}` (main window). ARCHITECTURE §3, §13.3 and §16.1 (threat 16).
  - **UI.** Settings › Remote › Local network: the switch (off), where it listens, the address the code uses (LAN or VPN), "Pair a phone on this network", the port error, and the Tailscale note; the pairing dialog says the phone must be on the same network.
  - **Phone web app on the LAN.** `apps/web/remote-lan` builds the same `RemoteApp` with Vite into `resources/remote-web` (`sh scripts/build-remote-web.sh`; not committed). Served at `http://<ip>:<port>/remote` with a CSP whose `connect-src` is that one `ws://` socket. Without WebCrypto (not a secure context) the IndexedDB store seals with XChaCha20 instead (D-2004).
  - **Shared code (D-2008).** `apps/web/lib/remote/*` moved to `packages/remote` (`@tandem/remote`), with `session.ts` (the screen logic: phase, connection, snapshot, notices, `relayAllowed`, `linkFromUrl`) and `demo.ts` (a simulated computer speaking the real protocol).
  - **Tests.** Rust: 15 `phone_lan` (F-RM-04.1–.5 over real sockets on loopback with the real `PhoneService`: pairing and a command, replay, 1008/1003/1009/4000, revoke, restart, the web app and rebinding, a non-LAN peer, turning it off) and `invariant_rule_7_lan_mode_is_off_by_default_and_never_public`. TypeScript: 3 Settings › Remote (F-RM-04), the LAN store without `subtle`, `relayAllowed` and a refused internet `ws://` code (F-RM-04.6).
- [x] F-RM-05 Vibezeo Remote app (`apps/mobile`, Expo SDK 57, React Native 0.86).
  - **Screens.** Welcome (scan, paste a link, Try the demo; pairing, failed and unpaired states), the camera scanner (expo-camera), and Home: Agents (status, Type to this agent with Terminal control), Approvals (Allow/Deny, a queued allow says it waits at the computer, the step-up note), Threads and teammate chats (paged transcript, send with Chat control), Routines (Run now), Settings (computer, connection, the phone's grants, notifications, push, Unpair with a confirm). Tablets and wide screens get two columns (list beside the open thread or agent). Every list has empty, loading, error and populated states. Strings live in `lib/strings.ts`; colours, radius and spacing in `ui/tokens.ts`, checked against `packages/ui`'s tokens.css.
  - **Logic.** `packages/remote/src/session.ts` (`RemoteSession`) holds the screen logic for both the app and tests; `linkFromUrl` reads the desktop's web link, a LAN link and `vibezeo-remote://pair#p=…`; `relayAllowed` refuses `ws://` to anything but private, VPN or loopback IPv4 (D-2006). The pairing is in the Keychain / Keystore (`lib/secureStore.ts`, expo-secure-store, `WHEN_UNLOCKED_THIS_DEVICE_ONLY`), split into parts with a two-generation pointer so an interrupted save keeps the previous pairing (D-2010). `lib/runtime.ts` gives Hermes `crypto.getRandomValues` (expo-crypto) and a UTF-8 `TextDecoder` (D-2011).
  - **Demo mode.** `packages/remote/src/demo.ts`: a made-up computer inside the app that speaks the real protocol over an in-memory socket, labelled on every screen; "Simulate activity" finishes an agent and raises an approval (D-2012).
  - **Notifications.** New approvals and agents that finish, need you or fail become an in-app banner while the app is open, or an OS notification while it's in the background (`lib/notify.ts`), once each; never sent anywhere.
  - **Native config.** `app.config.ts`: `com.vibezeo.remote` on both platforms; camera permission (no microphone); `NSAllowsLocalNetworking` and Android cleartext for LAN mode (D-2013); the `vibezeo-remote` scheme and App Links / Universal Links for `app.vibezeo.com/remote` (D-2026); the privacy manifest (no data collected, no tracking, the required-reason APIs; D-2024); `ITSAppUsesNonExemptEncryption = true` (D-2025); target and compile SDK 36, min SDK 24, iOS 16.4. `plugins/withReleaseSigning.js` signs release builds with the owner's upload key from outside the repo; `eas.json` builds and submits both stores.
  - **Android build (done here).** `bash scripts/mobile/build-android.sh` runs `expo prebuild` and Gradle and writes `dist/mobile/VibezeoRemote-<version>.apk` and `.aab` with SHA256SUMS, refusing a debug-signed result, and checks 16 KB alignment (`zipalign -P 16`) and the AAB's signature. This container's build was signed with a throwaway key (HUMAN_TODO). Maven Central answers 429 through the agent proxy, so `~/.gradle/init.d/central-mirror.gradle` points Gradle at Google's mirror (container setup, not repo).
  - **iOS (prebuild done here).** `expo prebuild --platform ios` writes the project with the push extension target, the shared keychain group, the privacy manifest and associated domains. Compiling needs a Mac or EAS (HUMAN_TODO has the steps).
  - **Store.** `apps/mobile/store/LISTING.md` (true claims only, review notes for the demo, privacy answers), `PLAY_CLOSED_TEST.md`, and `scripts/mobile/screenshots.mjs`, which renders demo-mode previews with react-native-web in Chromium (no emulator here: no KVM).
  - **Tests.** Mobile (Vitest): 19: secure store (round trip in keystore-sized parts, interrupted save, clear), runtime polyfills, notifications and prefs, strings, tokens against tokens.css, the signing plugin, the push extension plugin against Expo's template project, push on the phone. `packages/remote`: session and demo (11), push vectors.
- [x] F-RM-06 Encrypted push.
  - **Format (D-2021).** `tandem_core::remote::push` and `packages/remote/src/push.ts`: ChaCha20-Poly1305 (RFC 8439, so CryptoKit opens it), `0x03 ‖ 0x01 ‖ seq ‖ nonce ‖ ct`, AAD binding the pairing and sequence; pinned by `tests/fixtures/remote_push_v1.json` (the Rust bytes matched the TypeScript ones on the first run).
  - **Desktop.** Phone commands `push.register {platform, token, key}` (hosted pairings only) and `push.unregister`; the registration lives in the keychain and goes on revoke or a 410. `PhoneService::push_alerts` (after each coalesced bus burst) seals new approvals and agent changes for phones that registered and aren't connected, saving the sequence number before sending, through `PhoneNet::push` → `POST /v1/pairings/:id/push`.
  - **API.** `POST /v1/pairings/:id/push`: the caller's live pairing, Pro, 60 a minute; forwards and stores nothing; logs pairing, platform and outcome only; imports no crypto. Providers: `FcmPush` (HTTP v1, service-account JWT), `ApnsPush` (HTTP/2, ES256 provider token, generic alert + `mutable-content`), `MockPush`; `PUSH_PROVIDER=live` picks each platform by its credentials.
  - **Phone.** `lib/push.ts`: turning Push on (Settings, off by default) makes a fresh key, stores it with the pairing id in the keychain (`AFTER_FIRST_UNLOCK_THIS_DEVICE_ONLY`, shared group on iOS), gets the device token and registers; off unregisters. Android opens data messages in a background task; iOS's Notification Service Extension (`plugins/ios-push-extension`, Swift + CryptoKit) does it before display.
  - **Tests.** Rust: 2 vectors, 4 phone-service push tests (nothing until registered; sealed and opened only with the key, sequence 1, 2; unregister, revoke and a dead token stop it; bad registrations refused). API: 4 route tests (forwarding, no payload or token in logs, ownership, revoke, 410, size), 2 provider tests (APNs against a local HTTP/2 server with a verified ES256 token; FCM's message). Mobile: opening alerts, replay, wrong pairing.
  - **Unverified here:** the Swift extension has never been compiled (no Mac), FCM and APNs have never been called for real (no accounts). HUMAN_TODO.
- [x] F-RM-07 Linux store packages (as far as this container allows).
  - **Flatpak.** `packaging/flatpak/com.vibezeo.Vibezeo.yml` repackages the release .deb on GNOME 51 with Flathub's libappindicator shared module and git built in; `com.vibezeo.Vibezeo.metainfo.xml` validates with `appstreamcli` (pedantic: only the uppercase id note); a desktop file and launcher. Built here with flatpak-builder from 0.5.0's .deb and run under Xvfb: the welcome screen came up inside the sandbox. GNOME 48 is end-of-life, which the first build showed, so the manifest uses 51.
  - **Host commands (D-2032).** `tandem_core::sandbox`: inside a Flatpak, terminal panes (`tandem-pty`), threads (`tandem-engines` chat process), login-env capture and engine detection start through `flatpak-spawn --host --watch-bus --directory=… --clear-env --env=…`, with exactly the environment they'd get outside. Nothing changes outside a Flatpak. Not yet exercised inside a real Flatpak (0.5.0 predates it); HUMAN_TODO has the check.
  - **Snap.** `packaging/snap/snapcraft.yaml`: classic confinement (D-2031), core24, from the release .deb with its libraries staged and patchelf. Not built (no snapd in the container).
  - **Tests.** `f_rm_07_3_host_commands_carry_their_folder_and_exact_environment`.

### mobile-apps decisions
- D-2000: Phones get **Vibezeo Remote**, a native companion, built with **Expo / React Native** in `apps/mobile`: the owner-approved exception to DECISIONS' Tauri-only stack (the desktop stays Tauri 2). Phones can't run the coding CLIs (iOS forbids spawning processes; Google Play forbids downloaded executables), so the phone app is the F-RM-03 companion. PRODUCT_SPEC §6 items 3 (no native apps) and 8 (no store distribution) are superseded for the phone companion and the Linux stores by the owner's direction.
- D-2001: LAN mode is the free path: the desktop is its own relay on the local network. It needs no account, plan or hosted service, so it isn't Pro-gated (the hosted relay stays Pro, F-RM-03); a Team's `disablePhone` still turns it off. Remote access away from home is a VPN (Tailscale's free plan), documented, not built.
- D-2002: LAN mode listens per private IPv4 address only, never `0.0.0.0`, loopback, public addresses or IPv6 (home networks hand phones IPv4; telling ULA from global IPv6 with privacy addresses isn't worth it yet). Peers outside private, VPN, link-local and loopback ranges are closed unread, and `Host` must be the socket's IP literal.
- D-2003: The LAN relay keeps F-BE-11's wire behaviour (subprotocol token, 1008 after upgrade, 1003, 1009, 4000, `peer` notices, 50 frames/s with a burst of 50). The rate limit counts phone frames only: the desktop end is in-process. Tokens last until revoke, as D-1904; the 10-minute QR lifetime is still the one-time secret `S`.
- D-2004: The desktop serves the phone web app itself on the LAN, because a page from an HTTPS origin can't open a `ws://` socket (mixed content). It's the same `RemoteApp`, built by Vite (`apps/web/remote-lan`) into `resources/remote-web`. Plain HTTP on a LAN address isn't a secure context: no service worker (Add to Home Screen still works) and no `crypto.subtle`, so the pairing in IndexedDB is sealed with XChaCha20 under a random key kept beside it. That's D-1909's residual risk without the non-extractable key; the origin serves only this page.
- D-2005: Turning LAN mode on is `phone_lan_set` (main window, `LocalReview`), kept in `$DATA/remote/lan.json`, not a settings key: `settings_set` has no window check, and opening a listening socket is a choice made at the desktop. Default port 47800.
- D-2006: The phone apps follow a `ws://` relay only to private, VPN, link-local or loopback IPv4 addresses (`relayAllowed`); `wss://` anywhere. A crafted code can't point a phone at a plain-text server on the internet (the frames would still be sealed).
- D-2007: `PhoneThread` gains `teammate`, and the phone's thread list includes teammate chats, which F-RM-03's Chat control covers ("send to threads and teammate chats").
- D-2008: The phone's shared TypeScript lives in `packages/remote` (`@tandem/remote`): protocol, client, stores, the session controller and the demo computer. The web PWA, the LAN web app, desktop E2E, web E2E and Vibezeo Remote import it; nothing is copied.
- D-2009: `commands::phone::start` starts the phone service in every build (it returned early without hosted endpoints), because LAN pairings exist without them.
- D-2010: Vibezeo Remote keeps the pairing in expo-secure-store (iOS Keychain, Android Keystore-backed), readable only while unlocked and never backed up. Values are split into 1,800-character parts behind a generation pointer, so a crash mid-save leaves the old pairing.
- D-2011: The app uses the same audited @noble libraries as the web app (one implementation, checked against the shared vectors) rather than a native crypto module. Hermes gets a CSPRNG from expo-crypto and a UTF-8 decoder polyfill where missing.
- D-2012: Demo mode is a simulated computer that speaks the real protocol, so App Review and curious users exercise every screen without a desktop; it is labelled on every screen and reaches no network (`demo: true` allows only the demo relay).
- D-2013: Android allows cleartext and iOS `NSAllowsLocalNetworking`, for LAN mode's `ws://`. The app's own `relayAllowed` limits plain `ws://` to private and VPN addresses (D-2006), and every frame is end-to-end encrypted either way.
- D-2014: The Android release is signed by `plugins/withReleaseSigning.js` from Gradle properties the build script reads from `~/.vibezeo/android` (outside the repo); a release signed with the debug key is refused. The key made in this container is throwaway: updates must be signed with the owner's key, so nothing built here is for publishing.
- D-2015: Screens are React Native components drawn with `ui/tokens.ts` (packages/ui's values, test-checked); packages/ui's DOM components can't run in React Native.
- D-2020: Push is opt-in per phone, needs a hosted-relay pairing (LAN mode has no server to push from), and the API is a stateless forwarder: the desktop sends the phone's token with each alert, so the API stores no tokens.
- D-2021: Push alerts use ChaCha20-Poly1305 (RFC 8439) with a per-registration random key the phone makes, not the session's XChaCha20 keys, so iOS's CryptoKit can open them in an extension without the session state, and a rekey never strands an alert. A sequence number (saved before sending) lets the phone drop replays.
- D-2022: iOS gets a generic visible alert ("Vibezeo · Something on your computer needs you.") with `mutable-content` and the sealed bytes; the Notification Service Extension replaces the text after opening it with the key from the shared keychain group. If it can't, the generic text shows: no readable content ever passes through the API or Apple.
- D-2023: The desktop pushes only to phones that aren't connected, and only on new approvals and on agents turning waiting, done (from working) or failed, with redacted, clipped text.
- D-2024: The privacy manifest declares no collected data and no tracking, plus the required-reason APIs the Expo and React Native modules use (UserDefaults CA92.1, file timestamps C617.1, boot time 35F9.1, disk space E174.1).
- D-2025: `ITSAppUsesNonExemptEncryption = true`: the app's own encryption is standard but beyond Apple's exempt categories. The owner files the self-classification report (HUMAN_TODO).
- D-2026: Universal Links and App Links cover `https://app.vibezeo.com/remote*`; the portal serves `apple-app-site-association` and `assetlinks.json` from `APPLE_TEAM_ID` and `ANDROID_CERT_SHA256` and answers 404 until they're set.
- D-2030: The Flathub id is `com.vibezeo.Vibezeo` (the desktop bundle id is `com.vibezeo.tandem`, which store ids would freeze; Flathub wants the product name last). The Flatpak repackages the signed release .deb rather than building from source, since the source repository is private.
- D-2031: The Snap uses classic confinement: Vibezeo starts the person's own shells and coding CLIs in their folders, which strict confinement forbids. That needs the Snap Store's manual approval.
- D-2032: Inside a Flatpak, everything that runs the person's tools runs on the host through `flatpak-spawn --host` with an explicit environment (terminals, threads, the login env, engine detection); git is bundled in the sandbox instead. Environment values pass as `--env=` arguments, so a same-user process can read them from `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` as it already can from `/proc/<pid>/environ` (ARCHITECTURE §16.1's residual risk).
- D-2040: In a build without Vibezeo accounts (`TANDEM_HOSTED=0`, D-833), Settings › Remote keeps the Pro callout for the hosted relay only, worded "Pairing through the Vibezeo relay is part of Pro … pair over your local network below", never asks to sign in, and offers the Local network section in full: F-RM-04 needs no account, plan or hosted service (D-2001). The 8-hour arm switch shows only with a paired phone (D-833), which a LAN pairing can be.

### mobile-apps log
- F-RM-04: the LAN relay, its commands and UI, the LAN web app, `packages/remote`. The 15 Rust LAN tests passed on the first run; clippy wanted `from_phone` renamed (`from_*` takes no `self`). The UI test found `Callout` doesn't pass `data-testid` through. The first LAN web app build linted as source: its output is now ignored by ESLint.

- F-RM-05/06: the Expo app, demo mode, push end to end, the iOS extension, store files. What the runs found: Expo SDK 57 needs iOS 16.4 at least (prebuild refused 15.1); Gradle's Maven Central downloads got 429 through the proxy (Google's mirror fixed it); the release JS bundle needs `babel-preset-expo` declared in a pnpm workspace; the first push tests failed because a phone that just sent a frame counts as connected (the tests now disconnect it, as a closed app does); the LAN hub could close a revoked phone before the desktop's `bye` reached it (the close now queues behind the desktop's frames, through a weak sender so a dropped link still ends); merging `product-m0-m5` duplicated a `tandem-store` line in `tests/invariants/Cargo.toml`.
- F-RM-07: the Flatpak (built and started here), the Snap YAML and host commands in a Flatpak. A full gate run caught the LAN revoke race again under load: once the desktop's link is gone, the weak sender can't carry the end marker, so the forwarding task now finishes a revoked slot itself when its queue closes. Four full desktop test runs after the fix: 528/528 each.
- Android release built here: `VibezeoRemote-0.1.0.apk` (73 MB, arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a, x86_64) and `.aab` (49 MB), 16 KB aligned, signed with a throwaway upload key.

## Design direction (`design-lead`)
The owner (20 October): the product and the site should feel premium, "not something cheap and just another product", so people stay, try it, use it and pay; "the features are primary but the look and feel is equally important". Branch `design-lead` (from `product-m0-m5`) sets the direction for both surfaces in `docs/DESIGN.md`, builds the shared token layer and reviews the two implementing sessions' work: `app-ui` (D-2600 on) and `site-motion` (D-1870 on). Decisions D-1890 to D-1899, then D-2700 on (the coordinating session, 20 October): D-1870 to D-1889 are the website session's and D-1900 on the phone companion's.

### Design direction decisions
- D-1890: `docs/DESIGN.md` is the design direction for the app and the site: five brand attributes (calm, precise, fast, trustworthy, crafted; when they conflict, trustworthy > fast > calm > precise > crafted), principles from the design books, what makes premium products feel premium, the shared system, the journey from first visit to paying with concrete changes and owners, and how a READY is reviewed (must-fix or polish). It starts from an audit of vibezeo.com 0.8.0 and its live demo at 1440×900 (§0): the product starts below the fold, the home page is 12,984 px, teal does six jobs, the demo opens on an empty thread under a six-step coach card, and the app's surfaces don't share the site's light and depth.
- D-1891: one token source. `packages/ui/src/styles/brand.css` (new) holds the brand layer both surfaces use: edges, fills, elevation e1–e3, the rings' light, card and frame radii, the expo-out curve. `site.css` keeps its `--site-*` names as aliases and mirrors the light block under `prefers-color-scheme`, as D-1801 does for `tokens.css`. The app imports it but changes nothing until a slice uses it. Migration slices T1–T7 are in DESIGN §4.1.
- D-1892: one accent, three jobs: the primary action, focus and selection, and a control's active state. On the site at most three teal elements per viewport (eyebrows, inline links and ticks go neutral); in the app one accent-filled button per view. Status colours stay status only, each with its shape.
- D-1893: the path to paying is honest by rule (DESIGN §5.5): no plan prompt during first run; a limit is explained at the control that hit it, free way out first; Pro lists only what `LIMITS` gives it and the download runs, with hosted features apart under "Comes with accounts"; buttons that can't work today say "Tell me when accounts open"; no countdowns or repeated nags. Two lines in 0.8.0 break it and are on `app-ui`'s list: "Unlimited panes & threads" and "upgrade for unlimited panes" (Pro is 64 panes, 6 threads).

- D-1894: the neutrals are `ink`, a true neutral near-black, for the app and the site; slate leaves every semantic token. `app-ui` built it in its slice 1 (D-2600) from the coordinating session's first brief, which predates DESIGN.md, and the coordinating session asked the design lead to choose between ink and slate. Compared at 1:1 in the demo and on the site, slate's blue cast washed teal and the status colours toward one cool tint; every premium dark product measured grounds on a neutral near-black (#08090A, #07080A, #0D0E11), a convention rather than anyone's signature. Kept from DECISIONS: 6 px controls (the site's buttons and the nesting depend on them); adopted from D-2600: 10 px panels, panes, cards and popovers, so a 6 px control nests in 4 px of padding. One elevation set: `brand.css`'s `--tn-edge-hi` and `--tn-elev-1…3`, with the app's `--shadow-1…3` as aliases. DESIGN §4.2, §4.5, §4.7 and the T8 slice (the site on ink) follow. DECISIONS' visual identity is amended in `app-ui`'s branch, worded as this decision.

- D-1895: engine marks are our own monograms on small tinted tiles, after `app-ui` and the coordinating session raised telling engines apart at a glance in a grid of four or more panes. The letterform carries identity and the tint is a quiet second cue: low-chroma OKLCH tokens in `brand.css` (`--tn-engine-<id>`, dark and light, for the app and the site), letters in `fg`, hues chosen away from each vendor's brand colour and checked against the vendor's site before shipping, never a vendor logo, colour or gradient. The status bar follows the coordinating session's call: quiet by default, items only while non-zero, at most four, waiting first (DESIGN §4.6, §5.4.6).

- D-1896: with Vibezeo free (D-869), the journey's last step is no longer paying. DESIGN §5.5 becomes "the moments that make someone stay, tell a friend and come back": the first agent working (first run under a minute, the first finish as the peak), the first worktree landed (Best of N and the review queue get the craft budget after the Dashboard; we earn the recommendation and never ask for it inside the work, with no share prompts, referral codes or tracking), and coming back to exactly where you left off. Rules: no plan UI anywhere once the release that lifts the limits ships (until then a limit is stated as a fact of that version, with no way to pay past it); free said once, plainly, where it answers a question. Hosted features carry one phrase, "Comes with Vibezeo accounts", as the 0.9.0 docs already say, always with "It uses Vibezeo's servers and switches on when Vibezeo accounts open." (or that sentence as its tooltip), and the action "Tell me when accounts open"; never Pro, premium, paid, upgrade, plan, trial, credits, a price or a date. DESIGN §9 retires the plan and limit strings (§9.5 lists the keys) and keeps the voice rules.

### Design direction log
- Read the brief's sources, captured vibezeo.com and its live demo (Playwright, Chromium, 1440×900, dark and light), inventoried the journey as built (welcome, five-step wizard, launch grid, empty states, every upgrade prompt and its copy, raw colours and arbitrary values) and studied premium product sites for principles only.
- `docs/DESIGN.md` written (D-1890 to D-1893).
- T1, the shared token layer (D-1891): `packages/ui/src/styles/brand.css` with the edges, fills, glow, rings, highlight ink and elevation e1–e3 (dark and light), `shadow-e1…e3`, `rounded-card`, `rounded-frame` and `ease-out-expo`. `site.css` aliases its `--site-*` names to them, mirrors the light block as its section 1b, and no longer defines the shadows, radii or curve itself; the app and the site import `brand.css` after `tokens.css`. No component changed. `site.test.ts` checks the new mirror, and that every themed site token either has a light value or aliases a brand token that has one. Checked unchanged: `build:site` on this branch and on `product-m0-m5` (ea582544), served side by side, give identical computed styles (box shadow, background, radius, colours, easing, size) on every element of eight pages and the demo app, in dark and light; ten of twelve full-page captures are pixel-identical, and the home page in light differs between two captures of the same base build too.
- The gate on T1: lint; TypeScript 1,564 tests (ui 412, desktop 747, api 225, web 116, remote 33, mobile 21, protocol 10); Rust 2,147; the desktop build; clippy on Linux, Windows and macOS; fmt; no protocol drift. The fresh container first needed `pnpm infra:up` (images through `mirror.gcr.io`) and `scripts/bootstrap-linux.sh` (`libdbus-1-dev` and the rest).
- Design QA of `site-motion` slice 1 (dark mode, 73b6a6b5), sent to the coordinating session: not yet. Must-fix: the commit replaces the app's bundled `resources/keys.json` (a `keys:dev` run); keyboard focus on the theme control draws nothing (the ring sits on the hidden input); merged with T1, the forced-Light block must drop the 15 tokens that now alias `brand.css` (site.test fails otherwise); the privacy page should name the `vibezeo-theme` key. The theming itself passes: forced themes match the OS themes on six pages at two widths, the control is neutral, media stay dark in a bezel that follows the theme.
- DESIGN §9, the copy deck: the exact words for every string §5 changes (home, pricing, download, welcome and first run, launch grid, empty states, the demo's hints, the upgrade dialog and the limit prompts), each with the `en.json` key or `lib/*.ts` constant it replaces and its values read from code (`LIMITS`, `MONTHLY_CREDITS`, `ENGINES`). Checking them against the build caught one claim of DESIGN's own: §5.3's welcome line said "nothing leaves this computer", but the engines talk to their vendors; it now says "nothing sent to Vibezeo".
- `scripts/agent/design-shots.mjs`, the design self-check (DESIGN §6): captures pages at 1440×900 and 390×844 in four theme states under reduced motion, and fails a capture that scrolls sideways or shows more than three teal elements in its first viewport (the rings' radial blooms don't count), naming them. On the 0.8.0 site it counts 7 on the home page (both Download buttons, the highlight, "What's new", the release pill's dot, the demo's eyebrow and its dot) and 5 on pricing and download: the work §4.2 gives `site-motion`.
- Design QA of `app-ui` slice 1 (tokens and primitives, 42367ede), with the ink-or-slate call (D-1894): ink adopted; must-fix are merging `product-m0-m5`, 6 px controls and 4 px chips, one elevation set (drop `--tn-edge`, alias `--shadow-1…3` to `brand.css`), DECISIONS worded as D-1894, and the site's stage and lines moved off slate (T8, `site-motion`).

## Decisions
- D-1: F-CD-09 owned by M9. It needs the Files editor and the Tandem browser.
- D-2: F-SH-08 framework slice in M5, Accounts step in M6, owned by M8.
- D-3: F-DI-09 owned by M8 so dictionary actions exist before the Enhance interplay.
- D-4: M7 builds the `tandem-gateway` skeleton (loopback MCP, leases, approvals) as a slice of F-PL-01.
- D-5: M7 ships `/v1/ai/title` with a mock provider; the real provider lands in M13.
- D-6: Dashboard approvals are tested on the core ApprovalBroker in M5, again with a real thread in M7.
- D-7: Thread Running → Interrupted is tested on the sessions table in M5, in the UI in M7.
- D-8: "Create with AI" is tested against an API fixture in M10; the real route lands in M13.
- D-9: Threat-model rule tests land beside each mechanism; M15 owns F-TL-06 and audits completeness.
- D-10: Test-only dependencies (wiremock, fast-check, vt100, …), the `sshd` compose profile and the `tandem-invariants` crate don't change the runtime stack.
- D-11: One repository with independent deployables (desktop, API, web, sidecars). The user asked whether to split into several repos; DECISIONS fixes a monorepo, and the generated protocol types tie the desktop, API and web to one commit. Revisit only when a deployable gets its own release cadence.
- D-12 (consistency pass, CONSISTENCY_REPORT "Decisions made in this pass"): composer dictation uses the global Dictate key (⌥Space also on macOS; Alt+Space unbound on Windows/Linux); light accent teal-700 #0F766E; meter thresholds 80/95%; Free = 1 teammate besides Lead; thread modes stored `plan`/`act`, access `read`/`edits`/`full`; one main window; Plan/Act `Mod+Shift+P`, model picker `Mod+Shift+O`; global gate G is the 3-line MILESTONES gate.
- D-13: The earlier session's cloud sync log (`/v1/sync/events`, local `events` table) was removed. It has no feature ID, it isn't in the ARCHITECTURE §14.6 endpoint catalogue, and uploading local events by default would break DECISIONS rule 4.
- D-14: Unit-only Rust enums serialize as plain snake_case strings (`"forbidden_plan"`), not `{type: …}`. ARCHITECTURE §3.1's `tag = "type"` rule is applied to enums that carry data.
- D-15: The whole ARCHITECTURE §4.2 DDL ships as `0001_init.sql` now (MILESTONES M0 lists only four tables). No user data exists yet, and one shipped schema avoids migration churn across milestones.
- D-16: Redaction corpus lines mark each fake secret with «…» and split it with `{{}}` so repository secret scanners don't flag fixtures; the test strips the marker. The benign corpus is 1,248 real lines sampled from Python stdlib, C headers, npm/eslint sources and this repo. Two sampled lines that held a real-format token and a password-bearing URL were removed: they aren't benign.
- D-17: The desktop E2E injects axe-core into the webview with `browser.execute` because `@axe-core/webdriverio` opens helper windows, which a Tauri webview cannot. WebdriverIO runs with `wdio:enforceWebDriverClassic` (WebKitWebDriver has no BiDi).
- D-18: Vite 7 across the desktop app so vitest and the app share one Vite major.
- D-19: `GET /v1/config` sends `payload` as the RFC 8785 canonical JSON *text* and signs its UTF-8 bytes, so the desktop verifies without re-canonicalizing. The bundled fallback is `resources/config.json`, a copy of `apps/api/config/remote-config.json` (a Rust test keeps them equal).
- D-20: API tests run against the compose Postgres and Redis (a fresh `tandem_test` database per run) instead of fakes; `pnpm gate` therefore needs `pnpm infra:up`. CI's test-ts job starts the compose services.
- D-21: Added the dev-only `POST /v1/billing/mock/portal` (declared in ARCHITECTURE §14.6) so the mock portal can cancel at period end through the same webhook handler, and test-only `/__test/user/delete` and `/__test/subscriptions` for re-runnable E2E.
- D-22: The API serves better-auth with `baseURL = WEB_PUBLIC_URL`; browsers reach it through the web app's `/v1/*` rewrite so session cookies are first-party. SSE responses set `Cache-Control: no-cache, no-transform` so the proxy doesn't gzip-buffer the stream.
- D-23: The rate limiter's refill clamps negative elapsed time to zero (found when the test clock moved backwards and locked a client out).
- D-24: Stripe fixture event ids derive from the user, so replays are no-ops (M1 verification); E2E passes a nonce for fresh events.
- D-25: The analytics id lives in its own file, `$DATA/telemetry/analytics-id` (MILESTONES E2E-M2-7 checks "the analytics id file"), instead of a settings row. Opting out deletes the file.
- D-26: Crash reports use a minimal Sentry envelope client over `Net` instead of the `sentry` crate, so every request shows in `TANDEM_NET_LOG` and nothing else can send; the panic hook only writes scrubbed reports under `$DATA/crashes/`.
- D-27: `TANDEM_FAKE_PICK_FOLDER` (debug only, declared in ARCHITECTURE §17.5) makes `workspace_pick_folder` return a path, or the contents of a file it names, because WebDriver can't drive the native dialog. `pane_list` added to §3.2 until `layout_get` lands in M3.
- D-28: ts-rs 12 with `TS_RS_LARGE_INT=number`: JSON over IPC carries i64/u64 as numbers, so the generated TypeScript says `number`.
- D-29: Each PTY IPC call gets its own JS `Channel`: when Rust drops a channel (a failed `pty_attach`) the JS side closes it for good, so reusing it for `pty_spawn` lost every frame.
- D-30: Status badges keep their §1.3 `*-text` colour on the surface with a tinted border instead of a soft tinted fill: the text tokens pass AA on surfaces (UI_SPEC §7.3) but the light amber/green/rose text fell to ~4.2:1 on their own tints. Found by the both-themes axe pass.
- D-31: The E2E keychain is in memory (`TANDEM_FAKE_KEYCHAIN=1`), so a relaunched app is signed out by design; specs that relaunch use "Use Tandem offline" (persisted without secrets) instead of a file-backed fake keychain, which would put secrets on disk.
- D-32: Signing out also leaves offline mode (back to UI-03). The window state is also saved 400 ms after each change so a crash or kill keeps it, and a restored window that isn't fully on one display is re-centred (the plugin alone only checks overlap).
- D-33: Panes render into host elements the grid moves between layout slots (`paneHosts.ts`), so swap, move and maximize never remount xterm: the WebGL context and scrollback stay (F-CD-02 AC2, AC3).
- D-34: A fresh tab starts a Shell (E2E-M2-1 expects "a tab with a live shell"); a tab whose last pane closed, or that replaced the last closed tab, shows UI-10's launch tiles instead.
- D-35: `pane_move {paneId, tabId}` (declared in ARCHITECTURE §3.2) moves a pane to another tab of its workspace with the same process: the tab-strip drop and "Move to new tab".
- D-36: The command registry carries the three binding columns of UI_SPEC §5 (`mac`, `pc`, `pcTerminal`); titles are `cmd.<id>`. It lists only commands that have a handler and grows each milestone; a test checks every `pcTerminal` binding against the terminal intercept list.
- D-37: Code mode's rail order is `workspaces.sort_key` / `pinned` (`rail_move` with `mode: "code"`); `rail_items` holds the Agent and Thread rails when they land. `rail_width` defaults to 248 within 180–360.
- D-38: Terminal queries are answered in Rust for every pane, visible or hidden, and xterm's own replies are switched off with parser hooks (ARCHITECTURE §5.7). MILESTONES M3 describes xterm answering while visible; ARCHITECTURE outranks it, and both give exactly one reply. CPR uses the vt100 shadow cursor as of the query.
- D-39: Orphans are found with `sysinfo`: a record matches when its PID is alive with the same start time, and on Unix any process in the pane's session (the session id is the recorded PID) also counts, so a `nohup` child of a shell that died with the app is found. Windows relies on the Job Object.
- D-40: `scripts/gate.sh` runs one command per line. Under `set -e` a failure inside an `a && b` list doesn't stop the script, so a red `pnpm -r test` once let the gate exit 0 (found when Postgres was down after a container restart; the earlier gated commits had run every step).
- D-41: The E2E axe helper waits for finite animations to finish before running, so a dialog mid-fade isn't measured as low contrast.
- D-42: A minimal engine registry lands in M3 (`crates/tandem-engines/registry.json`: id, label, vendor, commands, provider home variable, resume, newline sequence) with PATH-only detection behind `engines_list`, because E2E-M3-5 runs an agent pane. M4 adds the rest of §6.1, the detection report and install commands; `EngineView` keeps its shape.
- D-43: The handback mark (`OSC 7770;handback;<token>;<code>`) carries a per-launch token passed as `TANDEM_SHELL_TOKEN`; the wrapper copies it into an unexported shell variable and unsets it before the engine starts, so it's in neither argv (`ps`) nor the engine's environment (§5.8's rule for shell marks).
- D-44: Engines resolve on the login PATH, then the app's own PATH: Linux login profiles reset PATH (`/etc/profile`), which would hide installs the app can see. The working directory is never searched.
- D-45: Each run records its children in `run/children-<app pid>.json` stamped with the app's own PID and start time, and deletes it on a clean exit. The startup scan runs in the background, collects only files whose owner has exited and waits up to 8 s for one still shutting down, so a quick relaunch doesn't report children the old instance is stopping, and two overlapping instances don't overwrite each other's records (both were seen in E2E). `orphans_list` waits for the scan.
- D-46: Find highlights use two new terminal theme tokens (`searchMatch`, `searchActive`, `#RRGGBB` as the search addon requires) so the bar adds no ad-hoc colours. A first search starts from the top of the scrollback, so the first match reads "1 of n" (F-CD-08 AC1).
- D-47: `terminalHandles` and the WebGL pool only drop an entry its owner registered, so a late unmount of an earlier view can't remove the live one.
- D-48: E2E specs share one `TANDEM_HOME`, so each launch restores the previous spec's workspace. The reset removes workspaces through IPC and then reloads the webview, so the UI never shows a pane the backend has closed; failures also save `__tandemTest.debug()` (stores, panes, focus, dialogs) next to the screenshot.
- D-49: Shell-integration marks are filtered out of the PTY stream in Rust before the ring, the side channel and the view see them (`MarkFilter`, split-safe across reads). Valid OSC 133 marks go back into the stream without the token, so the view can decorate commands while the token never reaches the webview, the ring or snapshots; marks without the token are dropped. "Copy last command output" is captured as plain text between C and D by the side channel (vte-stripped, `\r` and `ESC[K` applied) rather than from ring offsets, which the ring's eviction would invalidate. ARCHITECTURE §5.8 updated.
- D-50: bash runs `--rcfile <shim> -i` with login emulation (`/etc/profile`, then the first of `~/.bash_profile`, `~/.bash_login`, `~/.profile`); zsh files are sourced at the top level of the shim (never inside a function, so `typeset` stays global) and ZDOTDIR goes back to the user's value when startup ends; fish leaves the user's prompt alone, so it has no B mark; PowerShell writes C before PSReadLine echoes the line break and tags it `nl=1` so that break isn't captured. Agent wrappers never get the integration environment (a zsh wrapper would load the shim and take the token); the shell they hand back to does.
- D-51: A plain shell's status dot follows its marks: working between C and D, idle once nothing has printed for 2 s after D. Agent status arrives with M4.
- D-52: Under a full E2E run, WebKitWebDriver occasionally lost the click on a picker tile right after the dialog opened (3/3 isolated runs and an event trace showed normal delivery). The split helper waits for animations, then clicks once more only if the picker is still open and its tiles are enabled (they are disabled while a pick runs, so the retry can't launch twice). E2E-M3-1 builds its 4×4 grid over IPC (`pane_create`, `layout_save`, reload): splitting stops at the 240×120 minimum before 16 panes fit the test window, and the spec checks rendering, not splitting.
- D-53: A paste into a program with bracketed paste on sends its text between the markers with line breaks as LF (F-CD-05 AC2 spells `ESC[200~a\nb ESC[201~`) and removes any marker inside the text, so pasted text can't end the bracket early and run the rest. Without bracketed paste, line breaks are typed as CR, as terminals do.
- D-54: The view measures its character cell again once it is in the document and whenever the font set finishes loading, and every refit goes through the PTY resize; a resize sent before the spawn lands is forgotten and reconciled once connected. Found by E2E-M3-2: xterm measured a fallback font in the detached pane host, so the PTY kept 42 rows while the view showed 36 and TUIs drew off-screen. E2E-M3-2 now checks `stty size` against the view.
- D-55: PTY resizes wait for the layout to settle (80 ms with no size change) after a divider drag or becoming visible, then throttle at 50 ms. Panel groups land over a few frames after pointer-up, which gave some panes two resizes (E2E-M3-3 asserts one per affected PTY and none elsewhere).
- D-56: TUI goldens are keyed by terminal size (`apps/desktop/e2e/goldens/<name>.<cols>x<rows>.json`, from the serialize addon), written with `UPDATE_GOLDENS=1` and reviewed by hand; htop keeps only its column header and key bar (its numbers move). E2E-M3-1 and E2E-M3-3 put a Pro user in place through the API's `/__test/plan` route and build their grids over IPC.
- D-57: Output pacing (F-CD-04): the focused pane writes at once; every other pane's output waits in one scheduler that hands xterm at most 256 KB per 16 ms tick, round-robin, only after the previous tick has parsed, and visits each pane at most every 33 ms. Unparsed output isn't acked, so the Rust ack window holds the programs back. Timers, not frames, so hidden panes keep draining. The perf suite runs a `--profile perf` binary (release with debug assertions).
- D-58: The WebGL pool batches every visibility change of one task and attaches one context per frame. Panes shown together tie and keep the contexts they hold, so a mode switch back to 16 panes attaches nothing (it used to evict and re-attach 8 contexts synchronously: 1.2 s). "GPU rendering: Off" never applied: the setting stores `"off"` and the view compared with `false`; it now resets the pool.
- D-59: New terminal views open one per frame, focused first (`mountQueue`); one view takes 60–100 ms here. The cell is measured again only when the web font was still loading at open — a re-measure costs a full re-render (200–300 ms) and every new pane did one, twice. A font face that loads later (bold, italic) clears the WebGL glyph atlas instead.
- D-60: Pane `LANG` defaults to `C.UTF-8` on Linux (`en_US.UTF-8` elsewhere) when it's unset or empty; `en_US.UTF-8` isn't installed on many Linux systems, and programs then fall back to ASCII.
- D-61: zsh breaks its own command line with real newlines at the pane width (program output wraps softly and `paneText` joins it). E2E assertions about typed command lines join lines (`waitForPaneText(…, { commandLine: true })`).
- D-62: Remote engine entries come from the config's `registry_overrides` (ARCHITECTURE §14.10), in the registry's camelCase field names.
- D-63: The detection probe runs the login shell with the captured login environment (not the app's), so what it finds is what a terminal finds. Debug builds read `TANDEM_DETECT_SYSTEM_DIRS`, `TANDEM_DETECT_NO_NPM` and `TANDEM_DETECT_DEADLINE_SECS` so tests don't see engines installed on the machine.
- D-64: Cargo can't set `panic = "abort"` per package; `tandem-hook` has no panicking paths, ignores every error and always exits 0.
- D-65: An engine's default account is checked and inserted in one write, so agents launched together share it (six at once raced into the unique index); provider-home files go through unique temp names.
- D-66: Added `status_list` (ARCHITECTURE §3.2) for the rail and pane headers after a webview reload; `status:changed` also lists `removed` keys when an agent leaves its pane. The full Dashboard snapshot (`status_snapshot`) stays with M5.
- D-67: F-CD-11's auto-arrange (⌈√n⌉ columns, row-major) backs the Workspace menu item and Mod+Shift+T; PRODUCT_SPEC wins over UI_SPEC's preset table where they differ (3 panes: 2+1, not 1+2).
- D-68: `tandem-hook` is looked up next to the app binary (`TANDEM_HOOK_BIN` in debug builds). Without it, panes get status from heuristics only. Bundling it as a Tauri sidecar is part of M5's release pipeline.
- D-69: Hook-reported status wins for a pane from its first verified report; heuristics stay quiet for it from then on.
- D-70: The E2E app runs with a desktop-session environment (HOME, DISPLAY, XDG_*, TANDEM_*, PLAYWRIGHT_*, …) and a minimal PATH, not the test runner's. Panes inherit the app's environment, and the runner's CI/agent variables showed up as token-shaped values in the spawn log (E2E-M4-4); engines installed on the build machine also leaked into detection.
- D-71: Check again captures the login environment again but keeps the previous capture when the new one is degraded (a shell that's slow right now doesn't lose a good PATH).
- D-72: Added `engines_report` (the cached last report) so the launch grid's diagnostics survive a webview reload, and the test-only `test_account_create` (a second engine account with its own home; the sign-in flow is F-AC-02 in M6) and API `POST /__test/config` (re-signs the remote config with new `registry_overrides`).
- D-73: `PaneRecord` carries `accountId`; `pane_create {accountId}` pins an agent pane to an account of its own engine, otherwise the engine's default account.
- D-74: Where UI_SPEC and PRODUCT_SPEC/MILESTONES differ on the sidebar, the latter win: it floats below 1,100 px (not 1,280) and its tabs are closable, reorderable pills. The close confirmation uses PRODUCT_SPEC's title ("Stop {name}? It's working.") with UI_SPEC's body and buttons.
- D-75: An agent pane's first name sits beside its title instead of replacing it; the title chain (F-EN-06) is unchanged. Names are drawn at random from the free ones inside the pane's write transaction, so panes created together can't collide; when all 200 are in use a number is added ("Ada 2").
- D-76: `SourceRef` gains a test-only `fixture` variant (E2E fixture agents). The `approvals` table doesn't record fixture sources (its CHECK allows thread, teammate_chat and gateway).
- D-77: `ui:focus` carries the pane's `{workspaceId, tabId}` so a card or notification can open a workspace whose layout hasn't loaded yet.
- D-78: A real system notification's click isn't routed to its source yet: the notification plugin has no click callback on desktop. Clicking one brings Tandem forward on macOS and most Linux desktops; the jump to the source is covered through the recording notifier (`test_notifier_click`) and the bell. HUMAN_TODO lists the manual check; wiring the click per OS goes with M15's hardening.
- D-79: `notify_types` keys are flat, `"<type>.<channel>"` (`"done.system": false`): settings maps hold scalar values only.
- D-80: Notification paging uses `"<createdAt>:<seq>"` (SQLite rowid) so items created in the same millisecond keep their order and none is skipped.
- D-81: The approval broker makes its expiry timer inside the spawned task and needs a runtime only when one exists: a request opened from a sync command once crashed the app ("no reactor running").
- D-82: The E2E axe check waits for the theme to land, colours to stop changing and every open dialog to be fully opaque; WebKit doesn't list every fade in `getAnimations()`, and a half-faded sheet read as low contrast (E2E-M2-2 failed intermittently under load). Failures now print the measured colours and ratio.
- D-83: `restore_get_snapshot` returns only what restore adds (paused cards with `canResume`/`reason`, interrupted threads, the auto-resume flag); workspaces, tabs, layouts and the sidebar keep loading through their own commands, and `PaneRecord` carries `restoreState`. `restore_resume` / `restore_start_fresh` take `{paneId, cols?, rows?}` and start the PTY with no view; the pane's view then attaches with `pty_attach` and replays. Clicks and auto-resume share that one path. The saved scrollback shows (dimmed, ending in "— Restored output —") in a read-only view under the card; once resumed, the live view starts with the new process's output rather than prepending the old (§5.6 asks for the same xterm; revisit with the Files/terminal work in M9).
- D-84: Auto-resume is started by the UI once the app shell shows (`restore_auto_resume`, once per launch), so agents never run behind the sign-in screen. An agent holds one of the two queue slots from launch until it is ready (§6.5) or 10 s pass.
- D-85: Each agent session stores its launch configuration as JSON (`{extraArgs, home}`) and Resume uses it, never the current settings. A configuration that no longer parses, or a provider home that's gone, reads "Launch configuration unavailable" and only Start fresh is offered.
- D-86: Engine session ids from hooks are written to the running session row as soon as they are reported, so a kill -9 can still resume. The fake engine reports `fake-<pid>` and honours `--resume <id>` ("Resumed session <id>").
- D-87: Quit stops every PTY tree in parallel (SIGHUP → SIGTERM → SIGKILL, 2 s apart) with a 5 s deadline, after which whatever is left is force-killed. The menu's Quit stores pending layout saves first.
- D-88: Kill-fuzz (`scripts/killfuzz.ts`) kill -9s the real app at random points (during startup, or with its shells live) and the store's own writer mid-transaction, then checks integrity and foreign keys, layouts against open panes, paused agents, orphans and crash reports. `TANDEM_SNAPSHOT_EVERY_MS` (debug builds) shortens the 5-minute scrollback snapshot for it and the E2E.
- D-89: The splash says "Restoring your workspace…" (UI_SPEC copy; MILESTONES says "session"), then "Checking your plan…", bounded to 3 s. Any key or click skips the wait. It only fades in (no other motion), and with Reduce Motion it appears at once.
- D-90: Threads arrive in M7, so E2E-M5-3 seeds a bare `threads` row through `test_thread_seed` and reads it back with `test_thread_state` (D-7).
- D-91: The agent session row is written before the engine process starts (and dropped if the spawn fails): with four agents starting at once a SessionStart hook could beat the insert, and the session id Resume needs was lost (E2E-M5-2 resumed 3 of 4).
- D-92: The child registry writes its file while holding its lock. Two shells starting in the same millisecond could leave an older list on disk, so a crash at that moment would hide an orphan from the next scan (found by kill-fuzz, reproduced by `f_sh_07_concurrent_spawns_all_reach_the_registry_file`).
- D-93: Updates sit behind an `UpdateBackend` trait (Tauri's updater in the app, a fake in unit tests) so the state machine (stages, anti-rollback, min-version grace, channel, deb) is tested without a window. A failed check never shows the pill (only download, verify and install failures do); "Check now" waits for a check already running.
- D-94: Release builds only trust `TAURI_SIGNING_PUBLIC_KEY` compiled in from CI; debug builds trust the dev key `uk-1` (`pnpm keys:dev`, public half in `resources/keys.json`). Plain-HTTP feeds are allowed only in debug builds and only on 127.0.0.1 (the plugin's config is patched at startup). Under the E2E harness the install step records the version in `$DATA/test-update-installed` instead of replacing the running debug binary, and the app doesn't restart; check, download and minisign verification are the real plugin's.
- D-95: The minimum-version notice starts when a response first names a higher minimum (persisted, so relaunching doesn't reset the 72 h); a lower minimum clears it. The banner sits first in the UI-133 priority order. The modal overlay is re-read on focus and every minute; E2E moves the notice into the past (`test_min_version_shift`) instead of mocking the whole clock. The API's test routes can now set `min_app_version`.
- D-96: The release workflow builds Linux on ubuntu-22.04, not in a `debian:bullseye` container (ARCHITECTURE §17.4 updated): Tauri 2 needs webkit2gtk-4.1, which bullseye doesn't ship. Notices and SBOMs are generated once in their own job and every platform build embeds the same notices.
- D-97: npm licences and the npm SBOM come from `pnpm --filter "desktop..." licenses list --prod` (the app plus its workspace packages, production only). `license-checker` and `@cyclonedx/cyclonedx-npm` read `npm ls`, which under pnpm reports missing dependencies and lists dev packages (2,089 components instead of 106), so `scripts/gen-sbom-npm.ts` writes the CycloneDX 1.5 file itself. Rust uses `cargo-about` (`about.toml`) and `cargo-cyclonedx`.
- D-98: About › Licenses reads notices embedded at build time (build.rs copies `resources/THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` into `OUT_DIR`, else a placeholder). It isn't a bundle resource, so dev builds without the generated file still build. The release script and workflow generate it with `--resources` before building.
- D-99: `crates/tandem-voice/models/catalog.json` is the model catalog and the attribution source; its sha256 values stay `TBD` until `scripts/pin-models.ts` (M12), so `release.yml`'s verify step refuses real tags until then and `release-local.sh --dry-run` only warns.
- D-100: The onboarding wizard (UI-04) is, for this slice, the MILESTONES list: Welcome (the existing sign-in screen, UI-03), Engines, Privacy, First workspace. UI-04's theme step and F-SH-08's Accounts and Dictate steps join `STEPS` with their milestones (Accounts M6, Dictate M12; theme is already in Settings). Installs and sign-ins from the Engines step stay in Settings › Engines until the install sheet can run without a workspace. The E2E reset marks the wizard done; `freshProfile()` starts it, and `signInDesktop` skips it on a brand-new profile.
- D-101: The startup perf suite relaunches five times signed in (16 live shells need a paid plan), so `e2e/run.ts --suite startup` runs the real Secret Service keychain: a private session bus and an unlocked gnome-keyring inside the run's HOME (`apt-get install gnome-keyring`). Cold start is measured from the app process's start time (/proc) to the 16-pane layout with the focused shell's prompt. Results on the build VM are in `perf/results/m5.json`; the misses are [manual] for the reference machines (HUMAN_TODO).
- D-102: The public site (an F-WB-01 slice, pulled ahead of M15 so the product has a home at vibezeo.com) is a static export of `apps/web`. Public pages use the `.site.tsx` suffix; `SITE_EXPORT=1` (`pnpm --filter web build:site`) builds only those, with `output: "export"` and trailing slashes, into `apps/web/out`, hosted on Vercel (`apps/web/vercel.json`: Next.js preset with the export, response headers, and a skip rule so desktop-only commits don't redeploy). Vercel's Next.js builder is used even with `framework: null` once `next` is a dependency, so the config doesn't set an output directory (checked with `vercel build`). The normal build serves both the public pages and the portal, so nothing moves when M15 adds pricing, downloads and docs. The site states only what M0–M5 shipped, loads nothing but its own first-party files: no cookies, analytics or third-party resources (F-WB-01 AC2), and its contact, founder and optional legal details (registered name, CIN, address, city: shown only when set) live in `apps/web/lib/site.ts`. The About page follows the usual small-company layout: company, founder, how we work, company details, contact. Server pages import `@tandem/ui` components by path (`@tandem/ui/components/Badge`), because the barrel also exports hook-based client components. Deploy steps: `docs/DEPLOY_SITE.md`.
- D-103: Tandem's hosted AI features run on the Claude API (first-party), not OpenRouter: thread titles, Enhance/Polish rewrites, "Create with AI" teammate drafts, the Auto supervisor and the Conductor's doer (F-BE-06, M13). `AiProvider` gets a `ClaudeAi` implementation selected by `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (`AI_PROVIDER=claude`), with per-feature model ids still in remote config and `MockAi` when the key is absent. Speech-to-text and the realtime voice stay as planned (the Claude API offers neither). The public site lists these as planned, not built (ARCHITECTURE §14, MILESTONES M13).
- D-104: Hardened git reads (TL-06 rule 6) differ from ARCHITECTURE §7's flag list in two ways, both found by the rule-6 test, which first proves its traps fire with plain git. (1) `-c diff.external=` is dropped: in git 2.43 an empty external diff (or `diff.<driver>.command`) makes `git diff` try to run "" and fail, so diff-family reads (`diff`, `log`, `show`, `whatchanged`) get `--no-ext-diff --no-textconv` instead, and `blame` gets `--no-textconv`. (2) Filter drivers defined in the repository's own config (`filter.<d>.clean/smudge/process`, local and worktree level) are overridden with empty commands for every CLI read, since `git status` and `git diff` run clean filters; the user's global drivers (git-lfs) still run. Reads that need no CLI (status, diff vs base, head, branches, ancestry, conflicts) go through libgit2, which runs no helpers at all. Lanes are created under the main checkout's root even from a lane, and removing a lane keeps its branch.
- D-105: Early-access builds ship before the hosted service exists. They're compiled with `TANDEM_HOSTED=0` (debug builds also take `TANDEM_HOSTED` or `--test-no-hosted` at run time). `NoHostedApi` answers every API call as offline without a network request; the welcome screen (UI-03) offers only "Start using Tandem" (Free limits, local); `auth_sign_in` returns Unavailable; the 14-day offline deadline (PRODUCT_SPEC §F-SH-08 step 1) doesn't apply; Account & Plan and the plans sheet say Pro and Team open at launch and can't start a checkout. Release builds now default to `api.vibezeo.com` / `app.vibezeo.com` (`tandem.dev` isn't ours) and the bundle id is `com.vibezeo.tandem`; in-app links go to vibezeo.com. The Release workflow's `early-access` channel builds every OS with `TANDEM_HOSTED=0`, ad-hoc signs macOS when there's no Developer ID (Apple Silicon won't run unsigned arm64 code), and publishes a prerelease to the public `NamanJain-Engineer/tandem-releases` (secret `RELEASES_TOKEN`); the voice-model hash check applies once the app depends on `tandem-voice`. E2E: `ea-1-early-access`.
- D-106: A slice of F-BE-06 is pulled ahead of M13 so Tandem's hosted AI runs on the Claude API now: `POST /v1/ai/title` (free, 60/h) and `POST /v1/ai/rewrite` (Enhance/Polish, Pro, 3 credits, 300/day) on `ClaudeAi` (Messages API, 30 s, one retry on 5xx/529, errors carry the status only) when `AI_PROVIDER=claude` with `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`, else `MockAi`. Models come from remote config `ai_models` (Claude Haiku 4.5 for both). User text goes to the model between `<text>` tags under a system prompt that calls it data (TL-06 rule 8). The ledger gains `reserve` / `settle` / `refund` (ARCHITECTURE §14.5; F-BE-05 AC1 concurrency test), a failed call refunds the credits and the daily count, and `ai_requests` keeps metadata only (F-BE-06 AC2 log-capture test). The desktop callers arrive with Thread mode (titles, M7) and Dictate (Enhance/Polish, M8); teammate drafts, Auto and voice stay in M13. Pricing note: at Haiku 4.5 list prices a full 5,000-character Enhance can cost more than its 3 credits; revisit before launch (HUMAN_TODO).
- D-107: Worktree details F-CD-15 leaves open. (1) `lane_remove` without `force` checks for uncommitted or untracked files before it stops the worktree's agents, so a refusal costs nothing (ARCHITECTURE §7 lists the stop first). (2) A worktree created from a worktree starts at that worktree's HEAD and is registered under the repository's top-level workspace: the rail nests one level. (3) An empty slug source gives `tandem/session` (UI-12's fallback), not "lane", which is an internal word (UI_SPEC §6.2). (4) "Create pull request" types `gh pr create` into a new shell pane once the shell has been quiet for 1.5 s after its first output; shell-integration prompt marks aren't kept as pane state, and the quiet rule is the spec's alternative. (5) Removing a repository's workspace now stops its worktrees' panes too (their rows were already soft-removed with it).
- D-108: Launch sheet details F-CD-14 leaves open. (1) Built-in presets live in code with fixed ids `pst_builtin_{solo,pair,bench,fleet}` and can't be overwritten; Fleet is four agents (Builder, Builder, Tester, Reviewer) with a worktree each. (2) Seats that don't get a worktree open together in a new tab named after the task's first words ("Agents" without one), so a launch never rearranges the tab you were in. (3) Browser seats are refused with "Browser panes come in a later version" until the browser pane exists; the sheet doesn't offer them. (4) The Account column joins the lineup with F-AC-02 (task 5); until then every agent uses its engine's default account. (5) An agent's first prompt is the worktree briefing (when it has one), then "Your role: {role}. {brief}" and "Task: {task}", typed as one bracketed paste after the terminal is live (90 s at most); without a task nothing is typed. (6) Without a task, each worktree is named after its agent's role.
- D-109: UI_SPEC's z-index scale puts popovers (100) under sheets (200) and dialogs (300), so a select, menu or tooltip opened in a sheet or dialog was drawn behind its overlay and couldn't be clicked (found by E2E-M6-3). `--z-popover` is now 350: above sheets and dialogs, below toasts (400). A test in `theme.test.tsx` keeps that order.
- D-110: Handoff details F-CD-16 leaves open. (1) UI-13 writes the summary when the dialog opens and lets the user edit it, so `handoff_start` became `handoff_brief` + `handoff_start` (ARCHITECTURE §3.2); the spec's optional extra instruction is the editable summary, a teammate target waits for Teammates, and Thread targets for M7. (2) The source is asked when it's a live agent pane of an engine with a chat adapter (Claude Code, Codex) whose screen doesn't match the engine's `limit_patterns`. The request asks for the brief between `--- handoff brief <nonce> ---` and `--- end of brief <nonce> ---`; a reply counts when at least four of the six headings stand on their own lines, and an empty or malformed reply moves on at once instead of waiting out the 120 s. (3) The registry gains `headless_cmd` (Claude Code `-p --strict-mcp-config --model {model}`, Codex `exec --skip-git-repo-check -c mcp_servers={} --model {model} -`, `{model}` = `cheap_model`), run with the prompt on stdin and exactly the env a pane gets (sanitized login env plus the account's home); the session is fenced as data in the prompt. Debug builds also pass `TANDEM_FAKE_*` so the E2E fake engines can be steered. (4) The note on the source pane lives for the session, not in the database. (5) The target uses its engine's default account until the account picker arrives with F-AC-02/03.
- D-111: Engine-account details F-AC-02/03 leave open. (1) The sign-in is a PTY that isn't a pane: `accounts_begin_add {engineId, label}` returns `LoginSession {opId, paneId, accountId, action, reports}`, the sheet attaches to `paneId` with a small xterm (MiniTerminal), and the end arrives as `accounts:login-finished` (the event ARCHITECTURE already named, with a `state`). Sign in again and Sign out reuse it through `accounts_begin_session`; engines that can't report their sign-in (no `signed_in_file` or `status_cmd`) show "I've signed in" instead (`accounts_finish_login`). (2) The registry gains `signed_in_file` (Claude Code: `.credentials.json`, checked for presence only). The login runs as `exec <engine> [login_cmd]` under the user's login shell, in the account's home; a slash command (`/login`, `/logout`) is typed once a ready pattern shows, after 30 s at most. (3) An agent pane is pinned to its account when it's created (explicit pick, else the workspace override, where a worktree falls back to its repository's, else the engine default), so Make default changes new panes only and a restarted pane keeps its account. The teammate preference joins with Teammates; the resolver already takes it. (4) The AccountChip's choice is the workspace override for that engine (the repository's for a worktree); "Use the default" clears it, and a running pane never switches. (5) Remove deletes the home only when it is exactly `<providers>/<engine>/<id>`, frees the nickname, makes the engine's oldest remaining account the default and drops overrides that named it. Not in this slice: "Open provider folder", the override Edit dialog (the chip sets, × removes) and usage meters (F-AC-04, task 8).
- D-112: Limit details F-AC-05 leaves open. (1) Terminal limits come from the screen checks the status service already makes (at most every 300 ms per pane) plus a trailing check on its 1 s tick, so the last lines of a burst count; a line is reported once, and the same line still on screen after Resume or Stop isn't reported again. The reset parser reads an epoch after `|`, a delay ("in 2h 3m", "in 45 minutes", "in 30s") or a time of day in the user's zone ("resets 4pm", "at 16:12"; tomorrow when already past). (2) In a Code pane UI-36 is a banner under the header, and Choose account… opens the dialog; continuing elsewhere uses the brief fallback (the last 200 redacted lines under the brief's headings) through `limit_continue`, split right of the source. No OS notification on resume yet; threads and `kind` come with M7. (3) E2E-M6-7 uses a reset seconds away on the real clock, because the E2E app runs on the system clock (the status heuristics are time-based); Resume at reset under the FakeClock is unit-tested (`f_ac_05_2_resume_at_reset_acts_at_the_reset_time_and_not_after_a_cancel`). (4) The AccountChip reloads the account list once when its pane's account isn't in it (a default account created with the pane).
- D-113: Usage and context details F-AC-04/06 leave open. (1) Only Codex reports usage to other apps today: `usage_get`/`usage_refresh` start a short-lived `codex app-server` in the account's provider home (its login env, `env_clear`) and read `account/rateLimits/read` (primary/secondary → "5h"/"week", 15 s cap); Claude Code and other engines are `unavailable` with the reason in the chip's tooltip ("Claude Code doesn't report usage to other apps."). Stream rate-limit data (M7) and the local-log estimate (`estimated`, F-AC-07, M16) plug into the same `UsageView`. (2) The 5-minute and after-turn refreshes are driven by the webview (an agent pane's turn ending refreshes its account, at most every 30 s per account), and an account with no reading is probed when the chip first shows it; readings live in memory (`usage_snapshots` stays unused until the dashboard). (3) With no agent pane focused the chip follows the default engine's default account (UI_SPEC §3.2); the spec's "hidden if the engine doesn't report usage" yields to F-AC-04 AC2, which shows "Usage unavailable". (4) Context fill comes from the Stop hook's `transcript_path`, read only when it resolves inside the pane's provider home (links out are refused), from the file's last 256 KB: the last `usage` object's input + cache-read + cache-creation tokens, with no message content kept; the window is 200K, or 1M when the model id says so. Codex terminals have no transcript hook yet, so they show no meter. (5) The chip's two xs meters show from 1536 px, not UI_SPEC's 1280: between the two the title bar overflowed and clipped the window controls (seen in E2E-M6-8's screenshot). The omnibox label truncates instead of wrapping. A reset that isn't today names the day ("Sun 9:00 AM", or "Oct 20, 9:00 AM" beyond six days), in the limit card too. (6) The probe creates the account's provider home when a pane's launch hasn't yet (the chip asks as soon as the pane appears).
- D-114: Onboarding Accounts step (F-SH-08 step 3, MILESTONES M6 exit). The wizard is now Engines, Accounts, Privacy, First workspace; Welcome is the sign-in screen, so AC2's "step 3" is Accounts, and E2E-M5-9 quits and resumes there. The step lists installed engines with a chat adapter whose accounts Vibezeo can keep apart (a home strategy other than `none`), so Cursor Agent, which signs in globally, isn't offered. A signed-in default account reads "Signed in". Otherwise "Sign in to {engine}" opens the Settings › Accounts sheet: Sign in again for the engine's default account, or Add account with the nickname "Personal" filled in when the engine has none. Skip and Continue both move on; nothing is required.
- D-115: Thread event details F-TH-03 leaves open. (1) Text streams as blocks: deltas carry their block's `seq`, and the block closes when the adapter sends its own final text, when any other event arrives, or at `turn_end`; its final text then replaces the partial row (saved every 2 s) in place. So a turn's rows are final at block granularity, not only at `turn_end` (§6.9 said "at turn end"). (2) A subscriber gets the running turn with the open block as an `assistant` event of its text so far, then live deltas; the reducer replaces by `seq` and appends deltas to that item, so a resync after falling behind never doubles text. (3) Stop: a turn the engine reports as an error after a stop is `cancelled`; an acknowledged stop the engine doesn't end within 3 s is ended `cancelled` by the app. A queued follow-up never runs after a stop: it goes back to the thread's draft. (4) The tree kill comes at 3.4 s, so the whole stop is over by AC2's 3.5 s. (5) Windows has no process-group signals: there the stop is the engine's interrupt, then ending the engine process; a Job Object for the whole tree comes with the adapters (M7 tasks 3–4). (6) `thread_events` returns `ThreadEventPage {items, nextBeforeId}`; `thread_subscribe` returns nothing and streams until the webview drops the channel; `threads:changed` and `thread_list` carry the thread rows (there is no separate `ThreadSummary`). (7) A thread without a folder starts in `$DATA/scratch/<thread id>` with Read access; one in a workspace starts with Edits. Remembered per-engine defaults replace both with the composer (M7 task 8).
- D-116: Gateway and Claude adapter details F-PL-01/F-TH-04 leave open. (1) The gateway starts on the first thread session, not at app start (§1 table says "main at startup"): it costs nothing until Thread mode is used. (2) One lease per thread session: a new session (restart, resume) revokes the previous lease; the MCP config is rewritten at `$DATA/mcp/<thread>.json` with mode 0600 (the bearer is a Vibezeo lease, never a vendor token). (3) Origin: absent, `null` or `http://127.0.0.1|localhost:*`; Host: loopback names only (rmcp's checks, 403/400). (4) A bare limit line in an assistant message ("…usage limit reached|<epoch>") isn't shown as text: the result's limit card says it, without the epoch. (5) Context fill on `usage` is a percentage with one decimal against 200K, or 1M when the model id says so. (6) A tool's result row keeps the input detail, since the view replaces the running row with it. (7) Changing the model mid-thread is a new (resumed) session with `--model`; the adapter's `set_model` does nothing for Claude. (8) Approvals offer Once and Thread scopes; "Allow for this thread" rules and inline cards are M7 task 6, until then the Dashboard card answers. The thread is set Waiting before the card exists, so no view shows a card on a Working thread.
- D-118: Thread titles from the hosted AI are opt-in. F-TH-15 asks for `/v1/ai/title` after the first reply, but that sends the first message off the machine, and CLAUDE.md and the product principle ("Nothing leaves the machine by default") forbid that without an opt-in. So every thread gets the local title at once (the first 8 words of its first message, `title_source = fallback`), and the AI title replaces it only when Privacy › "Name threads with AI" (`ai_thread_titles`, default off) is on and the user is signed in; a user rename is never replaced. HUMAN_TODO asks the owner whether to make it default-on, with a consent step, instead.
- D-117: Codex and access details F-TH-05/09 leave open. (1) Only the V2 thread/turn app-server protocol is spoken: Codex versions with only the legacy conversation API are below the registry's `>=0.40.0` and fail closed (the version check uses the detected version, else `initialize`'s `userAgent`; unknown fails closed). (2) The protocol names follow the vendor's documented app-server API and are frozen in fixtures; checking them against a real install is in HUMAN_TODO, since no Codex is available to the build. (3) The gateway lease goes to Codex through `bearer_token_env_var`, so it's in the process env, never in argv or a file; Codex sessions don't get the approvals tool (they ask in-band). (4) In-band approvals: the adapter raises `AdapterEvent::Approval` with its own id and an `ApprovalSubject`; the service shows the same card as for Claude and answers through `ChatSession::resolve_approval`. (5) `thread/tokenUsage/updated`'s `last` request gives `usage`; context fill is its input tokens against `modelContextWindow`. (6) "Reconnecting…" is a note in the turn that restarts an engine process that went away on its own; an idle exit fails nothing. (7) Access is resolved once, in `tandem_core::access::resolve`; adapters map what they're given and no longer cap unattended runs themselves, so a routine's explicit Full grant (M13) isn't overridden. Unattended thread rows count as routine runs without a grant until routines and tasks pass their own.
- D-119: Threads perf suite details (UI-21, ARCHITECTURE §17). (1) The first thread opened in a session pays a one-time paint warm-up whatever its size (1.4 s on this software-GL VM, 0.8 s even for a 60-event thread), so the suite opens a small thread first and records that as `baseline.coldFirstOpenMs`; the targets then measure what a thread's size costs.
