The idle frame rate and a plain thread's worst open frame are recorded beside them, so a miss can be read against the machine. (2) MarkdownView's parse stays on the main thread (MILESTONES M7 task 7 says a worker): with the parse cache, mounting rows while scrolling costs a few ms of script per frame; what's left is layout and paint. (3) Rows mount and unmount a screenful at a time (the rendered range is kept while it covers the viewport by 400 px, then replaced by one covering it by 1,200 px), unchanged rows keep their objects while a turn streams, and an oversized message is line-aligned blocks in the transcript's own scroll: a nested scroller cost a 430 ms first paint here.
- D-120: ACP details F-TH-06 leaves open. (1) Models: an agent reports them on `session/new|load` (`models.availableModels`, `currentModelId`) and the thread's model is chosen with `session/set_model`; both are marked unstable in the protocol, so a refused `set_model` counts as a withdrawn model. Until an agent has reported models, its saved model is passed through instead of dropped by the catalog. The spec's "Automatic" is the catalog's first row, which UI_SPEC calls "Default". (2) ACP has no `delete`/`move` verbs on our rows: they show as edits (the card says Delete or Move). (3) Read access: ACP has no sandbox, so edit, delete and move permission requests are declined at Read (and Plan) without a card, and `fs/write_text_file` is refused; commands still ask. (4) "New credentials on the next turn": a sign-in error marks the session stale, so the next turn starts a new agent process with the account's current env (loading the old session when the agent can); an agent that reads its own config per request needs nothing more. (5) The gateway's tasks server (`tandem_tasks`, §6.8.3) joins with Tasks (M10). (6) Agents that only take stdio MCP servers get none; their approvals still come in-band.
- D-121: Plan → implement details F-TH-10 leaves open. (1) Implement keeps the composer's unsent text in the composer (UI_SPEC §4.3) rather than queueing it as a separate message (PRODUCT_SPEC, MILESTONES): text the user hasn't sent is never sent for them. (2) The message is PRODUCT_SPEC's "Implement plan version {n} exactly as written:" followed by the plan (UI_SPEC's "with the plan attached"). (3) A plan is the turn's last non-empty assistant reply, saved only when a Plan-mode turn completes (not stopped or failed). (4) "Revisions are listed in the thread inspector": until the inspector arrives with the changes panel, the header's "Plan versions (n)" button opens the list (UI_SPEC's ⋯ menu entry).
- D-122: Changes panel details F-TH-13/UI-24 leave open. (1) The start is recorded just before the thread's first turn runs, not at creation: a thread can be created long before it's used, and a folder can be added to it later. (2) The non-git manifest hashes with SHA-256 (ARCHITECTURE §7 said blake3, which isn't in the stack). (3) Shown now: the list, a row's diff, Refresh, Copy file list, the empty, loading, error and snapshot states, the narrow-window float, and the Files tab. Not yet: the "All uncommitted" scope, Commit…, Discard all…, Open diff in the main area, file-watch refresh (turn ends refresh it) and dragging the panel's width; they come with the git write actions (F-CD-20's commit flow).
- D-124: Engine switch summary limits F-TH-16 leaves open: each message is cut at 2,000 characters, the latest 40 completed tool actions within the kept messages are named (verb and argument summary, 120 characters), reasoning and failed or denied actions are left out, and the fence can't be closed from inside it. The summary is sent once: it stays pending until a turn's input reaches the new engine, so a failed start sends it again.
- D-125: A turn starts with its message (the reducer drops anything else at seq 0 as a straggler), so the switch divider is stored right after the message and the transcript draws it above. The seeded summary is the engine's input, never a transcript row.
- D-126: The browser demo (vibezeo.com/demo, user request after the second startup-program rejection). The desktop interface is built for the browser (`pnpm --filter desktop build:demo`, `vite.demo.config.ts` → `apps/web/public/demo/app/`, part of `build:site` on Vercel) against `src/demo/backend.ts`, a simulated core behind the existing `setInvokeBackend` seam: a seeded workspace with two agent panes and a shell, two threads, a scripted agent turn (read, edit, an approval before the tests, the result, Changes), a small line-editing shell, and quiet defaults for everything else. App boot now runs on a fake backend too (only the demo installs one before render). The demo bar and the /demo page say the agents are simulated and nothing leaves the browser; the marketing pages stay script-free (F-WB-01 AC2), the demo is a separate page with first-party scripts only and makes no network calls (CSP `connect-src 'self'`).
- D-123: Grid details F-TH-14 leaves open. (1) Mod+Alt+Arrow belongs to the `pane.focus_*` commands (bindings are unique in the registry), so those commands gained the `pane_or_cell` predicate and move between grid cells in Thread mode. (2) The plan's `threadGrid` decides how many cells show, but the saved grid isn't trimmed on a downgrade: it shows one thread on Free and comes back on Pro. (3) "Open side by side" in the rail row's menu is the keyboard path to dragging. (4) In a cell the Changes panel always floats (cells are narrow).
- D-127: The Free plan's chat engines (PRODUCT_SPEC §3.2 "Chat engines in Thread": Free runs Claude Code and Codex, Pro and Team all; UI_SPEC UI-11 Engine select). (1) The core enforces it from the entitlement's signed `chatEngines` (`Limits::allows_chat_engine`) where a thread is made, switched to another engine, sends (a queued follow-up too) and implements a plan, with `forbidden_plan` "Free threads run Claude Code and Codex. Pro runs every chat engine." Handoff to a Thread goes through the same create. (2) It applies to Thread mode only, as the matrix row says; teammate chats follow their own limits (M10 decides). (3) After a lapse, a thread made on Pro keeps its history and stays readable; it can't start a turn until it's switched to Claude Code or Codex (the composer shows an accent gate card with "See plans" and keeps the engine picker usable). (4) A new thread doesn't start on a remembered engine the plan no longer runs; it takes Claude Code, else the first allowed chat engine. (5) The engine picker disables the others with "Pro"; the handoff dialog's Thread target says "needs Pro". The early-access build (`TANDEM_HOSTED=0`) is Free, so it runs ACP engines in terminal panes only; the site and CHANGELOG say so. The plans dialog's gate line was the panes sentence for the grid gate too; each gate now has its own.
- D-128: The interactive demo on the home page (docs/FEATURES_TO_BUILD.md "The interactive demo on the website"). (1) Site rule: the home page loads exactly one first-party script, `/demo/embed.js` (no framework, no other origin, no storage), which frames `/demo/app/?embed=1` in a same-origin iframe; the F-WB-01 AC2 test now allows exactly that script on `/` and still forbids frames in the pages' HTML and any third-party resource. Vercel serves `/demo/app/*` with `frame-ancestors 'self'` and `X-Frame-Options: SAMEORIGIN` (a rule after the global one, which keeps `DENY`/`'none'` everywhere else). (2) The static export still hydrates with Next's runtime, and hydration replaced the section the loader had changed (the frame vanished, found by the site E2E), so the frame's stage is inner HTML (`dangerouslySetInnerHTML`), which hydration leaves alone, and the loader touches nothing outside it. (3) The loader frames the demo when the section is within 200 px of the viewport, after the page's own `load` (or 2 s); under 1024 px it waits for "Tap to explore". Full screen is the same frame made `position: fixed` (never moved in the DOM, so nothing reloads); Esc closes it from the page, and from inside the demo through a same-origin `postMessage` when the app didn't use the key. (4) The demo runs the Free plan the 0.3.0 download runs (it said Pro with mixed limits before), so it never shows more than the download. (5) Limits live in the page's memory: 12 messages a visit and 500 characters a message; past that the core answers "Demo limit reached…" and the bar shows it with Download for Linux. Reset the demo reloads the page, which rebuilds the sample world. Browser storage holds only `vibezeo-demo-hint-dismissed`. (6) The sample world: three workspaces (shop, api, docs-site), Claude Code, Codex and Gemini CLI panes plus a shell (the Free plan's four), the Dashboard with Working, Waiting for you and Done, a finished thread, a thread switched from Claude Code to Codex with its divider, the scripted fix, Plan-mode turns that make plan versions (Implement works), `git diff` in the shell, and the Files tab, git panel and Scratchpad as before.
- D-129: 0.3.0 includes the Scratchpad (F-SH-12, merged into `product-m0-m5` before the release build) and D-804's release-build shortcut fix. The CHANGELOG lists only what the build does; it has no "Fixed" list, because neither the plans dialog's grid-gate line nor the release build's Ctrl+S were ever in a published build (0.2.0 was never deployed).
- D-130: The desktop E2E runner gives the app a zsh login shell (`SHELL`), whatever shell runs the suite. The specs were written for zsh (E2E-M4-1 writes `~/.zshrc`), and some containers put a real `claude` on bash's login PATH through `/etc/profile.d`, which made E2E-M4-1 find it as a shell install instead of the nvm folder under test.

## Log
- M0 (earlier session): first slices of core, engine registry, local store, desktop shell frame, API health, CI.
- M0: build kit and plan committed (`build-kit/`, `docs/PLAN.md`).
- M0: realigned to DECISIONS: crate rename to `tandem-engines`, seven new crates, Tauri crate with capabilities and CSP, ts-rs protocol generation, UI_SPEC tokens, ICU i18n, rewritten core (redactor, containment, settings schema, keychain, logging, store with writer thread and full schema), API restructured to ARCHITECTURE §14.1, web on the shared tokens, dev/gate/coverage/trace/CI scripts, desktop E2E harness. Both M0 E2E scenarios pass. M0 closed.
- M1: backend accounts, entitlements, remote config, mock billing, edge limits, desktop auth server side, account portal pages. 37 API tests, 6 Playwright scenarios, Rust verifiers with an interop fixture. M1 closed.
- M2: desktop shell, design system and sign-in. `tandem-pty` (reader/batcher/flow control/ring/query responder/tree kill/login env), core `net` + `telemetry`, store repos for workspaces/tabs/panes, desktop services (api, auth, entitlement, deeplink, workspaces, terminal, telemetry, events), the M2 command set and plugins, `@tandem/ui` kit with gallery, and the desktop screens. Fixed on the way: boot waited on an animation frame a hidden window never gets; channel reuse lost PTY output; Settings Esc could miss a non-element target; the terminal stole focus back from Settings' opener. M0's boot spec now asserts the first real screen (the splash is transient since M2). All M2 E2E scenarios pass. M2 closed.
- M3 (in progress): pane grid, picker and drag and drop; command registry, dispatcher and palette; rail, tab strip and missing-folder flow; `tandem-pty` side channel and child registry. Fixed on the way: a new pane took focus twice and could steal it back from Settings' opener; Escape in an inline rename still committed through the blur; the rail tree owned list markup (axe); `gate.sh` could pass with a failed step.
- M3 (cont.): handback and Saved sessions, engine registry slice, Windows Job Object, WebGL pool and rendering options, clipboard/paste/drops/keys, find bar, per-run orphan registry, E2E-M3-4…8, 12, 14…16 specs. Fixed on the way: a quick relaunch reported the old instance's dying children as orphans and could overwrite the new run's registry; the E2E reset left the UI showing the previous spec's closed pane; zombies were counted as orphans; a closed pane could still be spawned.
- M3 closed: output scheduler, batched WebGL pool and staggered mounts (mode switch 1,947 → ~150 ms, six agents framed 4,176 → 670 ms here), perf suite on a `--profile perf` build, UTF-8 pane locale; full desktop suite green; tags `m3a`/`m3`.
- M4: registry with remote overrides, detection, provider homes and accounts, launch specs, HMAC status bus with `tandem-hook` and heuristics, live status in panes and the rail, launch grid, install sheet, Settings › Engines, title chain, readiness and `pty_type_prompt`, auto-arrange, Card view, collapsed sessions; `fake-engine`; E2E-M4-1…7. Fixed on the way: a status overflow panic on the first output of a pane, the install sheet dying with the empty tab's grid, auto-arrange widths off by half a gutter, the E2E app inheriting the runner's environment. Restored this Log section and ARCHITECTURE's tail after a scripted edit cut them short.
- M5: approval broker, right sidebar, pane names and header, Dashboard with a fixture source, notification center, OS notifications with a recording notifier, Settings › Notifications; session restore with paused cards, a two-slot restore queue, launch configurations, auto-resume, bounded parallel quit, splash phases and kill-fuzz; the updater with a signed mock feed, pill, restart dialog, Settings › Updates and the minimum-version notice; the release workflow, local dry run, notices and SBOMs; the onboarding framework; the startup perf suite; E2E-M5-1…9. Fixed on the way: an approval opened from a sync command crashed the app (no runtime), notification paging skipped same-millisecond items, the E2E-M2-2 axe check read a half-faded sheet, a SessionStart hook could beat its session row, and the child registry could lose a child to two simultaneous spawns. M5 closed.
- Site (M5 pause, F-WB-01 slice): static public site for vibezeo.com (home, about, privacy, terms, security, 404, robots, sitemap, security.txt), `build:site`, site tests; checked under the production CSP at desktop and phone widths. Moved from Cloudflare Pages to Vercel at the owner's request: `vercel.json` replaces `_headers`, verified with `vercel build` on a clean copy, and its skip rule tested against first-deploy, desktop-only, web and unknown-SHA cases.
- Site on `main`: so Vercel deploys from `main`, the site went there on its own (`5943c8c`: the site pages, the tokens, fonts, Badge and StatusDot from `@tandem/ui`, `vercel.json`). The rest of M0–M5 reaches `main` through a pull request from `product-m0-m5`, which merges `main` back. `apps/site` (an earlier one-page draft from another session) is kept but not deployed.
- Public site for the Claude startup program: a Built with Claude section (Claude Code hooks and `--resume`, built with Claude Code, hosted AI features planned on the Claude API), real app screenshots (`apps/desktop/e2e/screens/site.screens.ts`), real progress figures and a changelog, a Request early access email, founder and company facts. Removed a claim the app doesn't support yet: approving tool requests from Dashboard cards (no real approval source until Thread mode). Then, after looking at a selected applicant's site (onemana.dev): `/pricing/` with the launch plans from PRODUCT_SPEC §3.1 (marked "Pricing at launch", planned features badged), and an unedited screen recording of the real app (45 s since the re-capture) (`apps/desktop/e2e/screens/demo.screens.ts`: Claude Code detected, four terminals, kill -9, restore), served as VP9 WebM and H.264 MP4.
- Linux release and rename (site on `main`, merged back here): vibezeo.com names the product Vibezeo, says 0.1.0 is released for Linux and serves the .deb and SHA256SUMS from `apps/web/public/downloads/`, links no code repository, shows no years, and drops wording that read as unfinished (site tests pin all of it). The desktop app's visible name is Vibezeo too (window title, catalog, sign-in page, diagnostics); internal names (`~/.tandem`, crates, bundle id) stay. Screenshots and the 45 s demo were re-captured from the renamed app.
- Credibility pass for the startup-program review: the founder's name links to their X profile; same-day features on every platform is stated as a goal; the progress strip counts the code 0.1.0 was built from (862 automated tests, 42 E2E suites); the welcome screen, account menu, plans sheet, Account & Plan and the sign-in refusal say "Free plan" and "Pro and Team start with Vibezeo accounts" instead of "early access" and "at launch". This branch then merges into `main` (pull request #2, `97b7ae6`), so `main` is the code that shipped, and the .deb on vibezeo.com is rebuilt from it (welcome screen: "Free plan").
- Site: the home page's video is now a 1-minute product video (`public/screens/product.{webm,mp4}`, poster `product-poster.jpg`) in place of the 45 s unedited recording, at the owner's request. Its app shots are one screen recording of the released 0.1.0 build on a 2x display (driven with xdotool), with motion graphics, an original synthesized soundtrack and sound effects (Remotion); the terminal part is marked 2.3× speed and the live-status scene is labelled an illustration, and the caption says both. htop's command column was hidden for the take so the internal binary name doesn't show.
- Fix (M5 notifications, found by the M6 E2E regression): an agent's "finished" notification was skipped when its "working" and "done" arrived in the same 50 ms status batch (a quick turn), since the service saw done → done. It now also compares when the status began (`since`), so a done that began after the last one seen notifies; `notification_for` is unit-tested, and E2E-M5-5 passes again. E2E-M6-4 reads the conflict list with `textContent` (WebKit's `getText` is empty while a dialog fades in).
- Fix (M5 status, found by the M6 E2E regression): a hooked agent's first report is SessionStart, which switched the output heuristics off without giving the agent a status. Depending on which arrived first, a just-started agent had no status (missing from the Dashboard and from the update dialog's live agents: E2E-M5-7 failed 2 of 3 runs) or stayed "working" from its startup banner. The first SessionStart now sets Idle (`f_en_05_a_started_hooked_agent_is_idle_whichever_arrives_first`); E2E-M5-7 then passed 3 of 3.
- M6 usage and context (F-AC-04, F-AC-06): the title-bar usage chip reads Codex's windows through `codex app-server` and says "Usage unavailable" with the reason for engines that don't report usage; agent pane headers show the context meter from the engine's own transcript at each Stop. E2E-M6-8 passes 3 of 3. Its screenshots found three layout faults, now fixed: the chip wrapped, the omnibox label wrapped, and with the meters the title bar clipped the window controls at 1440 px (D-113).
- M6 closed: the onboarding Accounts step (D-114) completes the exit checklist, and the full E2E regression passes 48 of 48 suites. The first full run failed E2E-M5-9 only: earlier suites had already created Claude's default account, so "Sign in to Claude Code" opened Sign in again instead of Add account. A fresh test profile now also removes engine accounts, as a real first run has none (reproduced with m5-8 then m5-9 failing; passes after the fix).
- M7 task 1 (F-TH-03): normalized thread events, the chat adapter traits, the engine process that every adapter will own (Stop is over within 3.5 s even for an engine that ignores stdin and SIGINT: 3.40 s, five runs), thread storage, and the turn service with its eight IPC commands, plus the webview's reducer. No engine adapter yet, so no UI; the Claude adapter is task 3. While building, the disk filled with 9.8 GB of stale `tandem*` build artifacts in `target/debug/deps`; deleting them freed it.
- M7 tasks 2–3 (F-PL-01 slice, F-TH-04): the loopback MCP gateway with leases and the approvals tool, and the Claude Code adapter (flags, stream-json parser with reviewed fixture goldens, session with interrupt and exit reports). A Claude thread now runs end to end in the core: the launcher starts the gateway, leases a session, writes its MCP config, and Claude's permission requests become approval cards. The disk filled twice from build caches; builds in this session now run with `CARGO_INCREMENTAL=0`.
- M7 tasks 4–5 and task 6's core (F-TH-05, F-TH-09, F-TH-11): the Codex adapter over JSON-RPC with in-band approvals, the version gate and resume after an idle exit; access resolved in one place (Plan reads only, unattended never Full without a grant); approvals expire into a deny at 30 minutes; "Allow for this thread" rules per §6.10. Codex's protocol names couldn't be checked against a real install here (HUMAN_TODO).
- M7 task 9 core and task 8 backend (F-TH-08, F-TH-01, F-TH-02, F-TH-07, F-TH-15): the model catalog, the remaining thread commands (update, archive, delete with a recovery copy, move to folder, switch engine, drafts, attachments, `@` search, `models_list`), unread, local titles with AI titles behind a Privacy opt-in (D-118), and the silent-turn note. A test caught the `@` search escaping the folder through `../` (a lexical `starts_with` check); it now refuses `..` and absolute parts and compares canonical paths.
- M7 tasks 6–9 UI (F-TH-01, F-TH-02, F-TH-07, F-TH-11, F-TH-12): the Thread mode screen replaces the interim one: rail, header, virtualized transcript, the composer wired to drafts, attachments, mentions and the toolbar, and inline approval cards. Every surface stays mounted, so the composer takes focus only while Thread mode shows (it would otherwise pull focus from Code mode's terminal at launch).
- E2E-M7-1…4 (F-TH-02, F-TH-03, F-TH-07, F-TH-11, F-TH-15, D-6): 13 cases pass. They found two product bugs, both fixed: a turn that ended before the webview subscribed never showed (the view now subscribes first, then reads history and replays live events on top, with a unit test), and a pasted screenshot did nothing on Linux (WebKitGTK doesn't list a copied image as a file item; a paste with an image type or no text now asks the core to read the clipboard). Threads now also appear on the Dashboard.
- E2E-M7-5 (F-TH-05): a killed idle app-server restarts on the next send with "Reconnecting…", resumes its thread and keeps the history; Codex 0.31.0 fails closed with the version message. It found that `engines_list` still reported every engine as not chat-capable (a placeholder from before the adapters), so once the engine list loaded Thread mode said it needed a chat engine; `chat_capable` now follows the adapters Thread mode has (Claude Code, Codex).
- Threads perf suite (UI-21): a 5,000-event thread opens in 221 ms and pages back in 43 ms on the perf build. Profiling in the page found four costs, all fixed: the list re-rendered on frames where its visible rows hadn't changed and mounted rows one per frame while scrolling (now a screenful at a time: 15.5 → 34.8 fps), rows re-rendered on every streamed delta (new row objects each time; unchanged rows now keep theirs), messages re-parsed whenever they scrolled back into view (a parse cache), and a 2 MB reply's preview sat in a nested scroller whose first paint took 430 ms (now line-aligned blocks in the transcript, revealed a block per frame: 384 → 165 ms). Scroll fps and the fence's worst frame stay [manual]; this VM's software GL is the floor (D-119).
- M7 task 10 (F-TH-06): the ACP adapter for Cursor Agent, Grok and OpenCode, with golden fixtures for the protocol and for whole sessions against scripted agents, the agent's models in the picker with the withdrawn-model fallback, sign-in errors with Sign in, and E2E-M7-12 (4 cases) against a fake ACP agent. Running every M7 spec together found that an earlier spec's remembered engine leaked into later specs (they run in name order, `m7-12` before `m7-2`); the E2E reset now clears the thread defaults.
- M7 task 11 (F-TH-10): plan versions from Plan-mode turns, the Plan card with Implement / Revise / Copy, "Plan versions (n)" in the header, and `thread_implement_plan`; E2E-M7-6 passes, and all seven M7 specs pass together (20 cases).
- M7 task 12 (F-TH-13): the Changes and Files panel with its start point per thread, `thread_changes` and `thread_file_read`; E2E-M7-9 passes and all eight M7 specs pass together (22 cases). A unit test caught the Files list forgetting that a file was edited when it was read again later.
- M7 task 13 (F-TH-14): the thread grid with drag from the rail, the plan limit, the saved layout and cell focus; Thread mode's view became a per-thread `ThreadView` that a cell or the single view shows. E2E-M7-7 passes (relaunching signs in again: the E2E keychain lives in memory), and all nine M7 specs pass together (25 cases). A unit test caught the Free limit losing to "Grid is full" when a six-cell grid was saved on Pro.
- M7 task 14 (F-TH-16): engine switch with the summary seed, the divider and per-engine usage; "Interrupted — Resume?"; handoff to a Thread. E2E-M7-8, 10 and 11 pass. E2E-M7-8 found that a divider stored before the message hid the whole turn live (the reducer starts turns from the message), so it now follows the message (D-125); its screenshot also showed short user messages wrapping a letter or two per line (the bubble's 72% cap measured against a shrink-to-fit wrapper), fixed by capping the wrapper.
- Browser demo (D-126): vibezeo.com/demo explains it and opens /demo/app/, the real interface on a simulated core; walked through in Chromium (send, approve, result, Changes, Code mode, shell) with no console errors.
- M7 regression: 59/60 spec files passed on the first run; E2E-M3-9 F-SH-09.6 ran every registry command from the palette in Code mode, where `thread.changes` (enabled only with a thread open) can't run. The spec now opens a thread first for `thread_open` commands; 60/60 pass. Tag `m7` (local).
- 0.2.0 (M6 + M7): version bump, CHANGELOG section, and the site's release, changelog (M6, M7), features, roadmap and progress figures (8 milestones, 1,235 automated tests, 60 E2E spec files).
- Handover: `docs/OPERATING.md` (setup, the work loop, commands, E2E lessons, release, site deploy and checks, monitoring, non-negotiables), `scripts/agent/` (infra wrapper, target pruning, offline AppImage smoke test, demo walkthrough) and the product video's sources in `media/product-video/`, so another session or person can run the whole project from the repo. `CLAUDE.md` points to the handbook.
- Free plan chat engines (F-TH-06, D-127, release-0.3.0): Free threads run Claude Code and Codex only, enforced in the core from the signed limits (create, switch, send, implement), with the engine picker's "Pro" rows, the composer's gate card for a lapsed ACP thread, the handoff dialog and the plans dialog's own gate line; the site's plan copy says it (pricing, home, download). E2E-M7-12 now runs on a Pro account and adds the Free case. The tests found that the plans dialog showed the panes sentence for the grid gate.
- E2E environment (D-130): this container's bash login shell put a real `claude` on PATH (`/etc/profile.d/nodejs.sh`), and its `$SHELL` is bash, so E2E-M4-1 failed (claude found by the shell, not the nvm folder; no slow-shell diagnostic from `~/.zshrc`). The runner now gives the app zsh; 65/65 spec files passed with it. Docker Hub answered pulls with 429 and `with-infra.sh` hid it (sign-up 500s); the images come from `mirror.gcr.io` now and the script says when `infra:up` fails (OPERATING §3). `release-local.sh` also needs `cargo-about --features cli` and `cargo-cyclonedx`.
- Interactive demo on the home page (D-128): `/demo/embed.js`, the home page's frame with its still image (`public/demo/still.jpg`, a capture of the demo's first screen), Explore full screen and Tap to explore, the `/demo/app/*` frame headers, and in the demo the first-steps hint, the 12-message and 500-character limits, Reset, the Free plan, three workspaces with a Gemini CLI pane, the Dashboard's three states, a switched thread, Plan-mode turns and `git diff`. 15 demo unit tests and 5 browser tests (`e2e-site/demo-embed.spec.ts`, acceptance 1–6). The browser tests found two bugs: Next's hydration replaced the section the loader had changed (the frame vanished), so the stage is now inner HTML; and on phones the Tap to explore overlay stayed over the open demo.
- Two bugs found while preparing 0.3.0, both fixed with tests: (1) the layout store's `load()` copied every tab before its awaits and wrote the copy back, so workspaces loading at the same time wiped each other's panes and the app then opened a stray shell in the emptied tab (the demo's three workspaces showed it; `stores/layout.test.ts`); it now merges only its own workspace's tabs into the latest state. (2) E2E-M2-1 assumed it ran right after E2E-M0-1, but `m16-5-scratchpad` sorts before it and leaves an offline profile, so the full regression failed it; it now starts from `freshSignedOut()`.
- Release 0.3.0: version bump, CHANGELOG, the .deb in `apps/web/public/downloads` with SHA256SUMS, release.ts, progress figures, home features (Files, git and a browser; Scratchpad) and roadmap. Gate green; full E2E regression on the final tree (see the pull request).
